===Original===

> “Of **Jurgen** eke they maken mencioun,

That of an old wyf gat his youthe agoon,

And gat himselfe a shirte as bright as fyre

Wherein to jape, yet gat not his desire

In any countrie ne condicioun.”

===Chinese===

> “他们亦曾提及朱尔根（Jurgen），
>
> 他从一老妪处重获青春，
>
> 又得一件明丽如火的衬衣，
>
> 穿上它纵情嬉游，却终难如意，
>
> 无论身处何邦何境。”

===Original===

> … amara lento temperet risu

===Chinese===

> ……以从容一笑消释苦涩

## A Foreword: Which Asserts Nothing / 无所断言的前言
===Original===

> “Nescio quid certè est: et Hylax in limine latrat.”

===Chinese===

> “定是有何事端：海拉克斯在门槛上狂吠。”

===Original===

In Continental periodicals not more than a dozen articles in all would seem to have given accounts or partial translations of the Jurgen legends. No thorough investigation of this epos can be said to have appeared in print, anywhere, prior to the publication, in 1913, of the monumental *Synopses of Aryan Mythology* by Angelo de Ruiz. It is unnecessary to observe that in this exhaustive digest Professor de Ruiz has given (VII, p. 415 *et sequentia*) a summary of the greater part of these legends as contained in the collections of Verville and Bülg; and has discussed at length and with much learning the esoteric meaning of these folk-stories and their bearing upon questions to which the “solar theory” of myth explanation has given rise. To his volumes, and to the pages of Mr. Lewistam’s *Key to the Popular Tales of Poictesme*, must be referred all those who may elect to think of Jurgen as the resplendent, journeying and procreative sun.
Equally in reading hereinafter will the judicious waive all allegorical interpretation, if merely because the suggestions hitherto advanced are inconveniently various. Thus Verville finds the Nessus shirt a symbol of retribution, where Bülg, with rather wide divergence, would have it represent the dangerous gift of genius. Then it may be remembered that Dr. Codman says, without any hesitancy, of Mother Sereda: “This Mother Middle is the world generally (an obvious anagram of *Erda es*), and this Sereda rules not merely the middle of the working-days but the midst of everything. She is the factor of *middleness*, of mediocrity, of an avoidance of extremes, of the eternal compromise begotten by use and wont. She is the Mrs. Grundy of the Léshy; she is Comstockery: and her shadow is common sense.” Yet Codman speaks with certainly no more authority than Prote, when the latter, in his *Origins of Fable*, declares this epos is “a parable of﻿ ﻿… man’s vain journeying in search of that rationality and justice which his nature craves, and discovers nowhere in the universe: and the shirt is an emblem of this instinctive craving, as﻿ ﻿… the shadow symbolizes conscience. Sereda typifies a surrender to life as it is, a giving up of man’s rebellious self-centredness and selfishness: the anagram being *se dare*.”
Thus do interpretations throng and clash, and neatly equal the commentators in number.

===Chinese===

在欧洲大陆的期刊上，叙及或部分翻译朱尔根（Jurgen）传说的文章，统共恐怕也不过十来篇。在这部史诗付梓印刷之前，世间任何角落都未曾出现过哪怕一篇详尽探究——直至1913年，安杰洛·德·鲁伊斯那部皇皇巨著《雅利安神话纲要》面世。无须多言，在这部包罗万象的辑要中，德·鲁伊斯教授（第七卷，第415页及后文）将韦尔维尔与比尔格所搜集的这些传说作了大体概述，并以渊博学识详加探讨了这些民间故事的秘传寓意，以及它们与神话“太阳论”所引发的诸多争议之间的关联。凡是倾向于将朱尔根视作光芒万丈、巡行周天且滋生万物的太阳之人，皆当去参阅教授的宏卷，以及刘易斯塔姆先生所著《波伊捷斯民间故事要略》的篇章。

同样，明达的读者在阅读后文时，也会明智地摈除一切寓言式附会，单单是因为迄今为止提出的种种解释未免过于五花八门。例如，韦尔维尔将涅索斯之衫（Nessus shirt）视为天道报应的象征；而比尔格则大相径庭，认为它代表天才那份凶险的禀赋。此外想必有人记得，科德曼博士谈及谢列达母亲（Mother Sereda）时曾毫不犹豫地宣称：“这位‘中庸之母’大体便是这尘世（显然是Erda es的易位变位），而这位谢列达所掌管的不仅是劳作日的正中，更是世间万物的正中。她是‘折中性’与庸碌凡常的化身，是规避极端的代表，是习惯与风俗所孕育的永恒妥协。她是森林精怪中的格伦迪太太；她是康斯托克主义道德审查的化身；而她的阴影便是常识。”然而，科德曼的权威性断然不会高于普罗特——后者在《寓言之起源》中断言，这部史诗实乃“一则寓言……喻示人类徒劳奔波，苦苦探寻其天性所渴望、却在宇宙间无处觅得的理性与公正；而那件衬衣便是这种本能渴求的象征，正如……阴影象征着良知。谢列达则体现了对现实生活的屈从，是对人类叛逆的自我中心与自私自利的放弃：其异序变位词正是se dare（自我献出）。”

种种释义便如此蜂拥相撞、互不相让，其数目恰好与阐释者的人数相当。

===Original===

Yet possibly each one of these unriddlings, with no doubt a host of others, is conceivable: so that wisdom will dwell upon none of them very seriously.
With the origin and the occult meaning of the folklore of Poictesme this book at least is in no wise concerned: its unambitious aim has been merely to familiarize English readers with the Jurgen epos for the tale’s sake. And this tale of old years is one which, by rare fortune, can be given to English readers almost unabridged, in view of the singular delicacy and pure-mindedness of the Jurgen mythos: in all, not more than a half-dozen deletions have seemed expedient (and have been duly indicated) in order to remove such sparse and unimportant outcroppings of medieval frankness as might conceivably offend the squeamish.
Since this volume is presented simply as a story to be read for pastime, neither morality nor symbolism is hereinafter educed, and no “parallels” and “authorities” are quoted. Even the gaps are left unbridged by guesswork: whereas the historic and mythological problems perhaps involved are relinquished to those really thoroughgoing scholars whom erudition qualifies to deal with such topics, and tedium does not deter.﻿ ﻿…
In such terms, and thus far, ran the Foreword to the first issues of this book, whose later fortunes have made necessary the lengthening of the Foreword with a postscript. The needed addition﻿—this much at least chiming with good luck﻿—is brief. It is just that fragment which some scholars, since the first appearance of this volume, have asserted﻿—upon what perfect frankness must describe as not indisputable grounds﻿—to be a portion of the thirty-second chapter of the complete form of *La Haulte Histoire de Jurgen*.
And in reply to what these scholars assert, discretion says nothing. For this fragment was, of course, unknown when the High History was first put into English, and there in consequence appears, here, little to be won either by endorsing or denying its claims to authenticity. Rather, does discretion prompt the appending, without any gloss or scholia, of this fragment, which deals with
*The Judging of Jurgen.*
Now a court was held by the Philistines to decide whether or no King Jurgen should be relegated to limbo.

===Chinese===

然而，上述每一种解读，连同其余无数未及备载的阐释，或许皆自成一理；因此智者断不会将其中任何一种太当回事。

至于波伊捷斯（Poictesme）民俗的源起与幽微隐义，本书至少是全然不予挂怀的：它并无宏愿，不过是想为了故事本身，让英文读者领略朱尔根的传奇史诗。鉴于朱尔根神话格外典雅纯洁，此一古老故事极尽幸运，几乎可以不作删节便呈献给英文读者：为了剔除几处可能冒犯过分拘谨之人的零星、无关紧要的中世纪直白流露，全书通共仅作了不过半打权宜性的删节（且均已妥善标明）。

本书纯粹是作为供人消遣的故事而呈现，故后文既不推演道德寓意，亦不牵扯象征符号，更不援引任何“参照文献”与“权威定论”。纵使留有缺漏，亦不作主观臆断的补缀；至于其中可能牵涉的历史与神话难题，则尽数留给那些真正博古通今的学者去料理——唯有他们的渊博学识堪配探究此类议题，且耐得住枯燥繁琐。……

本书初版之前言，辞句止于此，概貌亦如是。嗣后的际遇，却使得此前言不得不添上一篇补记。此项补遗——至少在幸事一端尚称相符——极为简短。自本书首度面世以来，便有若干学者据理力争——若秉持全然之坦诚，其依据实难称无可辩驳——断言此乃《朱尔根正史》全本第三十二章之一节遗篇。

对于这些学者的断言，审慎之态唯有保持缄默。盖因昔日将《正史》译为英文之际，此一残卷尚无人知晓；故而无论在此认可抑或否认其真伪，皆无甚裨益。倒不如出于谨慎，将这一残卷不加按语、不置评注地附于文末。残卷所叙如下：

《朱尔根受审》

话说非利士人（Philistines）设下一处法庭，以裁决是否应当将朱尔根国王贬入幽冥虚空。

===Original===

And when the judges were prepared for judging, there came into the court a great tumblebug, rolling in front of him his loved and properly housed young ones. With the creature came pages, in black and white, bearing a sword, a staff and a lance.
This insect looked at Jurgen, and its pincers rose erect in horror. The bug cried to the three judges, “Now, by St. Anthony! this Jurgen must forthwith be relegated to limbo, for he is offensive and lewd and lascivious and indecent.”
“And how can that be?” says Jurgen.
“You are offensive,” the bug replied, “because this page has a sword which I choose to say is not a sword. You are lewd because that page has a lance which I prefer to think is not a lance. You are lascivious because yonder page has a staff which I elect to declare is not a staff. And finally, you are indecent for reasons of which a description would be objectionable to me, and which therefore I must decline to reveal to anybody.”
“Well, that sounds logical,” says Jurgen, “but still, at the same time, it would be no worse for an admixture of commonsense. For you gentlemen can see for yourselves, by considering these pages fairly and as a whole, that these pages bear a sword and a lance and a staff, and nothing else whatever; and you will deduce, I hope, that all the lewdness is in the insectival mind of him who itches to be calling these things by other names.”
The judges said nothing as yet. But they that guarded Jurgen, and all the other Philistines, stood to this side and to that side with their eyes shut tight, and all these said: “We decline to look at the pages fairly and as a whole, because to look might seem to imply a doubt of what the tumblebug has decreed. Besides, as long as the tumblebug has reasons which he declines to reveal, his reasons stay unanswerable, and you are plainly a prurient rascal who are making trouble for yourself.”
“To the contrary,” says Jurgen, “I am a poet, and I make literature.”
“But in Philistia to make literature and to make trouble for yourself are synonyms,” the tumblebug explained. “I know, for already we of Philistia have been pestered by three of these makers of literature. Yes, there was Edgar, whom I starved and hunted until I was tired of it: then I chased him up a back alley one night, and knocked out those annoying brains of his.

===Chinese===

待到法官们升堂齐备，准备断案之时，堂前走入一只巨大的屎壳郎（tumblebug），当先推着它那宝贝备至、妥为安置的一窝幼虫。伴随着这造物而来的是黑白相间的侍从（亦或是黑白印就的篇页），携着一柄剑、一根权杖与一杆长矛。

这甲虫看向朱尔根，一对螯钳因惊骇而直直竖立起来。甲虫冲着三位法官高声叫道：“奉圣安东尼之名！必须立时将这朱尔根贬入幽冥，因他冒犯失礼、淫邪下流、放荡不羁、伤风败俗！”

“这从何谈起？”朱尔根问道。

“你冒犯失礼，”甲虫答道，“因为这书页侍从佩着一柄剑，而我偏要说它不是剑。你淫邪下流，因为那书页侍从握着一杆长矛，而我宁可认为它不是长矛。你放荡不羁，因为那边的书页侍从执着一根权杖，而我执意断言它不是权杖。最后，你伤风败俗，其缘由令我作呕而不屑形诸笔墨，因而我拒绝向任何人透露。”

“唔，听来倒颇有逻辑，”朱尔根说，“不过若是能掺上几分常识，断不至于这般糟糕。诸位大人只需秉公通观全书全篇，便可亲眼得见，这些篇页侍从所携者，不过一剑一矛一杖，别无他物；我更指望诸位能推论得出，一切下流淫邪，全在乎那急不可耐要把这些物事唤作别样名目的虫豸心思里。”

法官们尚且不发一言。然而押解朱尔根的守卫，以及所有其余的非利士人，却分立左右两侧，紧紧闭着双眼，异口同声叫道：“我们拒绝秉公通观这些篇页，因为看上一眼，便仿佛是对屎壳郎大人的裁定心存疑窦。况且，既然屎壳郎大人拒绝吐露其缘由，他的缘由便不可辩驳；显而易见，你是个心术不正的无赖，纯粹自讨苦吃。”

“恰恰相反，”朱尔根说，“我是个诗人，我是在创作文学。”

“可在非利士地，创作文学与自讨苦吃乃是同义词，”屎壳郎解释道，“我再清楚不过，因为我们非利士人此前已被三个文学创作者搅得鸡犬不宁。不错，先是有个埃德加（Edgar），我把他逼得饥寒交迫、四处碰壁，直到我自个儿都厌烦了：终于有一夜，我把他逼进一条暗巷，彻底敲碎了他那令人厌烦的脑瓜。

===Original===

And there was Walt, whom I chivvied and battered from place to place, and made a paralytic of him: and him, too, I labelled offensive and lewd and lascivious and indecent. Then later there was Mark, whom I frightened into disguising himself in a clown’s suit, so that nobody might suspect him to be a maker of literature: indeed, I frightened him so that he hid away the greater part of what he had made until after he was dead, and I could not get at him. That was a disgusting trick to play on me, I consider. Still, these are the only three detected makers of literature that have ever infested Philistia, thanks be to goodness and my vigilance, but for both of which we might have been no more free from makers of literature than are the other countries.”
“Now, but these three,” cried Jurgen, “are the glory of Philistia: and of all that Philistia has produced, it is these three alone, whom living ye made least of, that today are honored wherever art is honored, and where nobody bothers one way or the other about Philistia.”
“What is art to me and my way of living?” replied the tumblebug, wearily. “I have no concern with art and letters and the other lewd idols of foreign nations. I have in charge the moral welfare of my young, whom I roll here before me, and trust with St. Anthony’s aid to raise in time to be God-fearing tumblebugs like me, delighting in what is proper to their nature. For the rest, I have never minded dead men being well-spoken-of. No, no, my lad: once whatever I may do means nothing to you, and once you are really rotten, you will find the tumblebug friendly enough. Meanwhile I am paid to protest that living persons are offensive and lewd and lascivious and indecent, and one must live.”
Then the Philistines who stood to this side and to that side said in indignant unison: “And we, the reputable citizenry of Philistia, are not at all in sympathy with those who would take any protest against the tumblebug as a justification of what they are pleased to call art. The harm done by the tumblebug seems to us very slight, whereas the harm done by the self-styled artist may be very great.”
Jurgen now looked more attentively at this queer creature: and he saw that the tumblebug was malodorous, certainly, but at bottom honest and well-meaning; and this seemed to Jurgen the saddest thing he had found among the Philistines.

===Chinese===

“随后又来了个沃尔特（Walt），我把他从一处追逐毒打到另一处，直打得他半身不遂：对他也一样，我给他扣上了冒犯失礼、淫邪下流、放荡不羁和伤风败俗的帽子。再往后又来了个马克（Mark），我吓得他换上小丑戏装掩人耳目，免得教人看出他是个文学创作者：说真的，我把他吓得将大半心血之作深藏起来，直到他死后、我都奈何他不得之时才肯公之于世。依我看，跟我耍这等花招实属卑劣下作。不过，托老天有眼和我老人家警惕之福，在非利士地被揪出来的文学创作者也就只有这三个；若非这两条，我们这儿恐怕也要像别的国度一样，让搞文学创作的人泛滥成灾了。”

“哎，可正是这三个人，”朱尔根大喊道，“才是非利士地的无上荣光！非利士地产出的万千物事之中，唯独这三位在世时被你们视若草芥的人，如今在一切尊崇艺术之地皆受敬仰，而在那些地方，根本无人会在意非利士地如何。”

“艺术于我和我的生活之道算得什么？”屎壳郎厌倦地答道，“我与艺术文章，乃至外国蛮邦那些下流的偶像毫无瓜葛。我肩负着身前滚着的一窝幼虫的道德福祉，且仰仗圣安东尼的保佑，假以时日把它们抚育成跟我一样敬畏上帝的屎壳郎，各安其分、怡然自得。至于死人嘛，我向来不在意别人夸他们好话。不，不，我的好小子：只要凡我所为之事再不能动你分毫，只要你当真烂透了，你便会发现屎壳郎相当友善。眼下，有人付工钱让我抗议活着的人冒犯失礼、淫邪下流、放荡不羁、伤风败俗，人总得糊口嘛。”

于是分立两侧的非利士人义愤填膺地齐声附和：“而我们，身为非利士地声誉卓著的良好市民，绝不同情任何胆敢借攻击屎壳郎大人来为他们所谓的艺术开脱之人。屎壳郎大人造成的损害在我们看来微乎其微，而自命为艺术家之徒造成的祸害却可能大如倾天。”

朱尔根此刻更为审慎地打量起这奇异的造物：他看清了，这屎壳郎周身恶臭固然不假，但骨子里却实诚得紧，且满怀善意；在朱尔根看来，这实乃他在非利士人当中所撞见的最为可悲之事。

===Original===

For the tumblebug was sincere in his insane doings, and all Philistia honored him sincerely, so that there was nowhere any hope for this people.
Therefore King Jurgen addressed himself, as his need was, to submit to the strange customs of the Philistines. “Now do you judge me fairly,” cried Jurgen to his judges, “if there be any justice in this mad country. And if there be none, do you relegate me to limbo or to any other place, so long as in that place this tumblebug is not omnipotent and sincere and insane.”
And Jurgen waited.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

只因这屎壳郎在其疯癫行径中怀着十二分的真诚，而全非利士地亦真诚地推崇着它，以至于这方土地上的黎民已无药可救。

于是，朱尔根国王不得不遵从情势，屈尊俯就非利士人的奇风异俗。“如今且请秉公审断于我，”朱尔根对法官们高声呼吁，“若这疯人国中尚存半分公道。若已无半分公道，便随你们将我流放至幽冥虚空或任何去处，只要在那个地方，这只屎壳郎并非无所不能、并非既真诚又疯癫便好。”

朱尔根静立以待。……

## XVII About a Cock That Crowed Too Soon / 第十七章 论一只啼得过早的公鸡
===Original===

Next the tale tells of how Jurgen and the ghost of Queen Sylvia Tereu came into the White Turret. The Lady of the Lake was in bed: she slept unaccompanied, as Jurgen noted with approval, for he wished to intrude upon no more tête-à-têtes. And Dame Anaïtis did not at first awake.
Now this was a gloomy and high-paneled apartment, with exactly the traditional amount of moonlight streaming through two windows. Any ghost, even an apprentice, could have acquitted himself with credit in such surroundings, and Jurgen thought he did extremely well. He was atavistically brutal, and to improvise the accompanying dialogue he did not find difficult. So everything went smoothly, and with such spirit that Anaïtis was presently wakened by Queen Sylvia’s very moving wails for mercy, and sat erect in bed, as though a little startled. Then the Lady of the Lake leaned back among the pillows, and witnessed the remainder of the terrible scene with remarkable self-possession.
So it was that the tragedy swelled to its appalling climax, and subsided handsomely. With the aid of Caliburn, Jurgen had murdered his temporary wife. He had dragged her insensate body across the floor, by the hair of her head, and had carefully remembered first to put her comb in his pocket, as Queen Sylvia had requested, so that it would not be lost. He had given vent to several fiendish “Ha-ha’s” and all the old high imprecations he remembered: and in short, everything had gone splendidly when he left the White Turret with a sense of self-approval and Queen Sylvia Tereu.
The two of them paused in the winding stairway; and in the darkness, after he had restored her comb, the Queen was telling Jurgen how sorry she was to part with him.
“For it is back to the cold grave I must be going now, Messire Jurgen, and to the tall flames of Purgatory: and it may be that I shall not ever see you any more.”
“I shall regret the circumstance, madame,” says Jurgen, “for you are the loveliest person I have ever seen.”
The Queen was pleased. “That is a delightfully boyish speech, and one can see it comes from the heart. I only wish that I could meet with such unsophisticated persons in my present abode. Instead, I am herded with battered sinners who have no heart, who are not frank and outspoken about anything, and I detest their affectations.”
“Ah, then you are not happy with your husband, Sylvia? I suspected as much.”

===Chinese===

故事接着讲到，朱尔根（Jurgen）与西尔维娅·特鲁王后（Queen Sylvia Tereu）的幽灵一同来到了白塔（White Turret）。湖中夫人正自卧榻而歇：她是独榻而眠，朱尔根见状颇表赞许，盖因他再也不愿撞破任何两相私语的幽会了。起初，安娜伊蒂斯夫人（Dame Anaïtis）并未醒转。

这原是一间幽暗而墙面镶板高耸的居室，两扇窗牖间恰到好处地倾泻进合乎鬼魅传说的清冷月辉。置身于这般光景之中，莫说老练幽魂，便是个初出茅庐的学徒鬼魅也能大显身手；朱尔根自忖表现得极为体面。他流露出一种隔代遗传的凶残蛮霸，临场现编的对白也拿捏得轻车熟路。一切进展得顺遂无比，且气势昂扬，不多时安娜伊蒂斯夫人便被西尔维娅王后凄切动人的求饶声惊醒，在床上直起身来，神色微显诧异。旋即，湖中夫人又悠然靠回枕上，打了个哈欠。

“当真可惜得很，”她说，“你们两位便不能放轻些动静？这般胡闹，把合府上下都吵醒了。”

“我亲爱的年轻娘子，”朱尔根语带委屈地说，“合情合理地讲，你总不能指望我行凶杀人时连半点动静都不弄出来吧。这般要求全然不合鬼怪现身作祟的通行规矩；况且，我们这便要告辞了。”

随即，朱尔根做出一副凶神恶煞的模样，一把揪住西尔维娅幽灵的头发，将她一路拖出狭窄的楼梯道。然而房门一旦合拢，他便松开了手，如释重负地长叹一声：

“好啦，总算完事了，我也踏实了。在自家亲眷头上克尽这等凄苦职守，着实令人心酸。既如此，西尔维娅，我谨祝你晚安，且回炼狱（Purgatory）一路顺风。”

“等等我呀！”她凄楚可怜地哀求道，“噢，朱尔根，莫要把我独自撂在黑魆魆的暗处！我怕黑，我又迷了路，在这般穿堂阴风乱吹的甬道里，我怎生寻得回炼狱的路途呢？”

“哎，我的好姑娘，”朱尔根说，“你总不成还指望我送你回府吧？且体谅体谅我的处境：在格拉西昂（Glathion）我不过是个异乡客，明儿清早还得早起去向年轻的吉尼薇尔（Guenevere）献殷勤，而且说当真话，我实在不惯于充当鬼魂的向导向导伴游。不成，西尔维娅，这般作为成何体统。你丈夫若知晓了该作何感想？”

===Original===

“I see very little of Smoit. It is true he has eight other wives all resident in the same flame, and cannot well show any partiality. Two of his Queens, though, went straight to Heaven: and his eighth wife, Gudrun, we are compelled to fear, must have been an unrepentant sinner, for she has never reached Purgatory. But I always distrusted Gudrun, myself: otherwise I would never have suggested to Smoit that he have her strangled in order to make me his queen. You see, I thought it a fine thing to be a queen, in those days, Jurgen, when I was an artless slip of a girl. And Smoit was all honey and perfume and velvet, in those days, Jurgen, and little did I suspect the cruel fate that was to befall me.”
“Indeed, it is a sad thing, Sylvia, to be murdered by the hand which, so to speak, is sworn to keep an eye on your welfare, and which rightfully should serve you on its knees.”
“It was not that I minded. Smoit killed me in a fit of jealousy, and jealousy is in its blundering way a compliment. No, a worse thing than that befell me, Jurgen, and embittered all my life in the flesh.” And Sylvia began to weep.
“And what was that thing, Sylvia?”
Queen Sylvia whispered the terrible truth. “My husband did not understand me.”
“Now, by Heaven,” says Jurgen, “when a woman tells me that, even though the woman be dead, I know what it is she expects of me.”
So Jurgen put his arm about the ghost of Queen Sylvia Tereu, and comforted her. Then, finding her quite willing to be comforted, Jurgen sat for a while upon the dark steps, with one arm still about Queen Sylvia. The effect of the potion had evidently worn off, because Jurgen found himself to be composed no longer of cool imponderable vapor, but of the warmest and hardest sort of flesh everywhere. But probably the effect of the wine which Jurgen had drunk earlier in the evening had not worn off: for now Jurgen began to talk wildishly in the dark, about the necessity of his, in some way, avenging the injury inflicted upon his nominal grandfather, Ludwig, and Jurgen drew his sword, charmed Caliburn.
“For, as you perceive,” said Jurgen, “I carry such weapons as are sufficient for all ordinary encounters. And am I not to use them, to requite King Smoit for the injustice he did poor Ludwig? Why, certainly I must. It is my duty.”

===Chinese===

“我极少见得着斯莫伊特（Smoit）。说来也是，他在同一团烈焰里还居留着其余八位妻子，总不好厚此薄彼。不过他的王后里倒有两位径直升入了天堂；至于他的第八任妻子古德伦（Gudrun），我们不得不心怀忧惧地认定，她生前必是个怙恶不悛的罪人，因她至今未曾抵达炼狱。但我自己打小便信不过古德伦：若非如此，我当年也断不会向斯莫伊特进言，教他将她缢杀好立我为后。你看，朱尔根，当年我还只是个不谙世事的娇俏丫头，觉得做王后是件风光无限的体面事。而那时候的斯莫伊特，朱尔根，说话抹蜜、周身飘香、一身锦缎，我哪里料得到后来竟会落得那般惨酷的下场。”

“确实，西尔维娅，被那只原本起誓看顾你福祉、理当跪在你膝前伺候的手所谋害，确是件令人悲叹的凄凉事。”

“我介怀的倒不是这个。斯莫伊特是因一时妒火攻心才杀了我，而嫉妒以其笨拙的方式而言，倒也不失为一种恭维。不，朱尔根，落在我身上的还有一件比这更糟透的事，教我这肉身一世皆浸在苦涩之中。”说罢，西尔维娅掩面啼哭起来。

“究竟是何事，西尔维娅？”

西尔维娅王后悄声吐露了那骇人听闻的隐情：“我丈夫不懂我。”

“天哪，”朱尔根暗忖，“一旦有女人对我说出这等话，哪怕这女人是个死鬼，我也明白她心底期盼我做些什么了。”

于是朱尔根伸出臂膀，揽住了西尔维娅·特鲁王后的幽灵，细语宽慰起来。见她颇为顺从地领受抚慰，朱尔根便在阴暗的台阶上坐了片刻，一只胳膊依旧揽着西尔维娅王后。那药剂的效力显然已然退去，因为朱尔根发觉周身各处已不再由清冷缥缈的气雾凝就，而是重新化作了温热坚实、实实在在的凡人血肉。不过，朱尔根入夜时分喝下的烈酒多半酒力未消：因为此刻朱尔根竟在暗处胡话连篇起来，絮叨着他无论如何须替他名义上的祖父路德维希（Ludwig）雪耻复仇，讨还所受之辱；朱尔根拔出了他的佩剑，那柄施过魔法的王者之剑卡利班（Caliburn）。

“因为，正如你所见，”朱尔根说，“我随身携带着足以应付寻常交锋的趁手利刃。难道我不该拔刀出鞘，为斯莫伊特国王强加在可怜的路德维希头上的不公报仇雪恨么？这自然是理所应当的。此乃我的分内之事。”

===Original===

“Ah, but Smoit by this is back in Purgatory,” Queen Sylvia protested, “And to draw your sword against a woman is cowardly.”
“The avenging sword of Jurgen, my charming Sylvia, is the terror of envious men, but it is the comfort of all pretty women.”
“It is undoubtedly a very large sword,” said she: “oh, a magnificent sword, as I can perceive even in the dark. But Smoit, I repeat, is not here to measure weapons with you.”
“Now your arguments irritate me, whereas an honest woman would see to it that all the legacies of her dead husband were duly satisfied﻿—”
“Oh, oh! and what do you mean﻿—?”
“Well, but certainly a grandson is﻿—at one remove, I grant you﻿—a sort of legacy.”
“There is something in what you advance﻿—”
“There is a great deal in what I advance, I can assure you. It is the most natural and most penetrating kind of logic; and I wish merely to discharge a duty﻿—”
“But you upset me, with that big sword of yours, you make me nervous, and I cannot argue so long as you are flourishing it about. Come now, put up your sword! Oh, what is anybody to do with you! Here is the sheath for your sword,” says she.
At this point they were interrupted.
“Duke of Logreus,” says the voice of Dame Anaïtis, “do you not think it would be better to retire, before such antics at the door of my bedroom give rise to a scandal?”
For Anaïtis had half-opened the door of her bedroom, and with a lamp in her hand, was peering out into the narrow stairway. Jurgen was a little embarrassed, for his apparent intimacy with a lady who had been dead for sixty-three years would be, he felt, a matter difficult to explain. So Jurgen rose to his feet, and hastily put up the weapon he had exhibited to Queen Sylvia, and decided to pass airily over the whole affair. And outside, a cock crowed, for it was now dawn.
“I bid you a good morning, Dame Anaïtis,” said Jurgen. “But the stairways hereabouts are confusing, and I must have lost my way. I was going for a stroll. This is my distant relative Queen Sylvia Tereu, who kindly offered to accompany me. We were going out to gather mushrooms and to watch the sunrise, you conceive.”
“Messire de Logreus, I think you had far better go back to bed.”
“To the contrary, madame, it is my manifest duty to serve as Queen Sylvia’s escort﻿—”
“For all that, messire, I do not see any Queen Sylvia.”
Jurgen looked about him.

===Chinese===

“哎呀，可斯莫伊特眼下早就回了炼狱，”西尔维娅王后抗辩道，“况且冲着一个妇道人家拔刀弄剑，未免太没男子气概。”

“我迷人的西尔维娅，朱尔根这柄复仇之刃虽令心怀嫉恨之徒闻风丧胆，却向来是天下俏佳人的贴心慰藉。”

“这柄剑确乎大得很，”她说，“哪怕是在暗处我也辨得真切。但我再说一遍，斯莫伊特并不在这儿与你比试兵刃。”

“如今你的这番说辞教我好生不耐，要知晓一个本分的妇人自当设法成全亡夫所遗留的一切遗泽——”

“哎呀，哎呀！你这话是何用意——？”

“唔，隔了一代也罢，无论如何孙儿辈总归算得上一笔遗产嘛。”

“你这话倒也不无道理——”

“我向你担保，此言大有深意。这乃是天地间最自然、最精辟透彻的至理名言；而我不过是想略尽本分——”

“可你挥舞着那柄大剑教我心烦意乱，令我心惊胆战；只要你这般舞弄个不停，我便没法同你讲理。好啦，快把剑收起来！哎，谁能拿你有什么法子呢！这儿便是盛你那柄宝剑的剑鞘呀，”她娇嗔道。

正值此刻，他们被打断了。

“洛格鲁斯公爵（Duke of Logreus），”安娜伊蒂斯夫人的声音传来，“在我寝门前闹出这等荒唐动静引来丑闻之前，你莫非不觉得及早退下更为明智么？”

原来安娜伊蒂斯将寝门半掩半开，手里端着一盏灯盏，正从门缝探头望向幽窄的楼梯道。朱尔根神色颇为尴尬，在他看来，与一位死去了63年的贵妇在此厮磨亲昵，委实难有体面的托辞。于是朱尔根站起身来，匆忙收起了向西尔维娅王后亮出的那件兵刃，决意将整桩事体轻描淡写地遮掩过去。外头传来一声公鸡啼鸣，天已破晓。

“向你道一声清早好，安娜伊蒂斯夫人，”朱尔根说道，“不过此处的楼梯拐弯抹角委实教人眼花，我怕是迷了路。我原是出来散步消遣。这位是我的远亲西尔维娅·特鲁王后，承蒙她美意为我引路伴行。你大可想见，我们本是出来采拾菌子、顺带瞧瞧日出的。”

“洛格鲁斯大人，依我之见，你还是速速滚回榻上去妥当得多。”

“夫人此言差矣，担当西尔维娅王后的护花使者实乃我不可推卸之责——”

“即便如此，大人，我怎的连半个西尔维娅王后的影子都瞧不见呢？”

朱尔根举目四顾。

===Original===

And certainly his grandfather’s ninth wife was no longer visible. “Yes, she has vanished. But that was to be expected at cockcrow. Still, that cock crew just at the wrong moment,” said Jurgen, ruefully. “It was not fair.”
And Dame Anaïtis said: “Gogyrvan’s cellar is well stocked: and you sat late with Urien and Aribert: and doubtless they also were lucky enough to discover a queen or two in Gogyrvan’s cellar. No less, I think you are still a little drunk.”
“Now answer me this, Dame Anaïtis: were you not visited by two ghosts tonight?”
“Why, that is as it may be,” she replied: “but the White Turret is notoriously haunted, and it is few quiet nights I have passed there, for Gogyrvan’s people were a bad lot.”
“Upon my word,” wonders Jurgen, “what manner of person is this Dame Anaïtis, who remains unstirred by such a brutal murder as I have committed, and makes no more of ghosts than I would of moths? I have heard she is an enchantress, I am sure she is a fine figure of a woman: and in short, here is a matter which would repay looking into, were not young Guenevere the mistress of my heart.”
Aloud he said: “Perhaps then I am drunk, madame. None the less, I still think the cock crew just at the wrong moment.”
“Some day you must explain the meaning of that,” says she. “Meanwhile I am going back to bed, and I again advise you to do the same.”
Then the door closed, the bolt fell, and Jurgen went away, still in considerable excitement.
“This Dame Anaïtis is an interesting personality,” he reflected, “and it would be a pleasure, now, to demonstrate to her my grievance against the cock, did occasion serve. Well, things less likely than that have happened. Then, too, she came upon me when my sword was out, and in consequence knows I wield a respectable weapon. She may feel the need of a good swordsman some day, this handsome Lady of the Lake who has no husband. So let us cultivate patience. Meanwhile, it appears that I am of royal blood. Well, I fancy there is something in the scandal, for I detect in me a deal in common with this King Smoit. Twelve wives, though! no, that is too many. I would limit no man’s liaisons, but twelve wives in lawful matrimony bespeaks an optimism unknown to me. No, I do not think I am drunk: but it is unquestionable that I am not walking very straight. Certainly, too, we did drink a great deal.

===Chinese===

果不其然，他祖父的那位第九任王后早已踪影全无。“不错，她已然隐遁而去了。不过鸡鸣时分原该如此。然而，那只公鸡委实啼得不是时候，”朱尔根懊恼不迭地叹道，“这未免太不近人情了。”

安娜伊蒂斯夫人说道：“戈吉尔万（Gogyrvan）的酒窖里储藏甚丰，你同乌里恩（Urien）与阿里伯特（Aribert）通宵达旦畅饮；想必他们也交了好运，在戈吉尔万的酒窖里淘出了一两位王后。不过，我瞧你眼下酒劲儿尚未全消。”

“安娜伊蒂斯夫人，你且答我一句：今夜你门前难道未曾有过两位幽魂登门拜访？”

“哎，这话随你怎么说，”她答道，“不过白塔闹鬼向来声名远扬，我在此歇宿极少能有清静安枕的良夜，盖因戈吉尔万合家上下没几个善类。”

“奇了怪哉，”朱尔根暗自称奇，“这位安娜伊蒂斯夫人究竟是何等人物？我犯下这般残虐弑逆之罪，她竟无动于衷；视这般幽冥鬼怪，竟如我拂拭飞蛾般等闲视之？听闻她通晓巫术媚道，身段体态亦着实妖娆诱人：总而言之，若非年轻的吉尼薇尔占据了我整个心扉，这等妇人倒真值得探究一番。”

他随即朗声说道：“夫人，或许我当真是醉了。然而我依旧觉得，那只公鸡当真啼得不是时候。”

“改日你定要向我细细解说此中深意，”她说，“眼下我且回榻安睡，我也劝你趁早照办。”

言毕房门紧闭，门闩落下，朱尔根抽身离去，心头犹自翻腾着难以按捺的躁动。

“这位安娜伊蒂斯夫人倒是个有趣得紧的人儿，”他忖度着，“若是机缘凑巧，此刻向她当面分辩我对那只公鸡的满腹怨尤，倒真是一桩乐事。嗨，世间比这更荒诞离奇的事亦屡见不鲜。况且，她撞见我时我宝剑已然出鞘，因而她心知我所操持的兵器堪称可观。有朝一日，这位无夫相伴的俏丽湖中夫人，说不定也会深感需要一名得力剑客呢。所以且耐下心来。再者，看来我身上当真流淌着王家贵胄之血。唔，我想那段风流秘闻绝非空穴来风，因为我察觉自身与那位斯莫伊特国王颇有相通之处。不过，12位妻子！不成，这委实过甚了。我不愿对任何男人的露水情缘横加置喙，但若在明媒正娶的纲常里纳下12位正妻，未免流露出一种我未曾领略的盲目乐观。不，我自忖并未酩酊大醉：但我步履蹒跚、走不成直线倒亦是无可抵赖的实情。想来的确，我们今夜委实灌了太多陈酿。”

===Original===

So I had best go quietly back to bed, and say nothing more about tonight’s doings.”
As much he did. And this was the first time that Jurgen, who had been a pawnbroker, held any discourse with Dame Anaïtis, whom men called the Lady of the Lake.

===Chinese===

“因此我最好悄没声儿地回榻安歇，对今夜的荒唐勾当再莫吐露半字。”

他依计而行。而这，便是曾为当铺柜员的朱尔根，头一遭同世人唤作湖中夫人的安娜伊蒂斯夫人打交道。

## XI Appearance of the Duke of Logreus / 第十一章 洛格鲁斯公爵登场
===Original===

So Jurgen brushed from the table the chessmen that were set there in readiness for a game, and he emptied the silver flagons upon the ground. His reasons for not meddling with the horn he explained to the Princess: she shivered, and said that, such being the case, he was certainly very sensible. Then they mounted, and departed from the black and silver pavilion. They came thus without further adventure to Gogyrvan Gawr’s city of Cameliard.
Now there was shouting and the bells all rang when the people knew their Princess was returned to them: the houses were hung with painted cloths and banners, and trumpets sounded, as Guenevere and Jurgen came to the King in his Hall of Judgment. And this Gogyrvan, that was King of Glathion and Lord of Enisgarth and Camwy and Sargyll, came down from his wide throne, and he embraced first Guenevere, then Jurgen.
“And demand of me what you will, Duke of Logreus,” said Gogyrvan, when he had heard the champion’s name, “and it is yours for the asking. For you have restored to me the best loved daughter that ever was the pride of a high king.”
“Sir,” replied Jurgen, reasonably, “a service rendered so gladly should be its own reward. So I am asking that you do in turn restore to me the Princess Guenevere, in honorable marriage, do you understand, because I am a poor lorn widower, I am tolerably certain, but I am quite certain I love your daughter with my whole heart.”
Thus Jurgen, whose periods were confused by emotion.
“I do not see what the condition of your heart has to do with any such unreasonable request. And you have no good sense to be asking this thing of me when here are the servants of Arthur, that is now King of the Britons, come to ask for my daughter as his wife. That you are Duke of Logreus you tell me, and I concede a duke is all very well: but I expect you in return to concede a king takes precedence, with any man whose daughter is marriageable. But tomorrow or the next day it may be, you and I will talk over your reward more privately. Meanwhile it is very queer and very frightened you are looking, to be the champion who conquered Thragnar.”
For Jurgen was staring at the great mirror behind the King’s throne.

===Chinese===

于是，朱尔根（Jurgen）将案上摆设停当准备对弈的棋子一扫而空，又将银壶里的美酒尽数倾倒于地。至于缘何不去碰那只号角，他向公主细加解释；吉尼薇尔（Guenevere）听罢打了个寒颤，言道既是这般情由，他此举委实明智之至。二人随即跨上坐骑，离开了那座黑银相间的凉亭。一路再无波折，他们平平安安地抵临了戈吉尔万·高尔（Gogyrvan Gawr）统辖的卡梅利亚德城（Cameliard）。

且说百姓闻知自家公主安然归来，满城欢声雷动，钟声齐鸣；家家户户张灯结彩，悬挂彩旗锦缎，号角齐奏。吉尼薇尔与朱尔根一同进谒，来到国王的正义大殿。这位统领格拉西昂（Glathion）、兼为埃尼斯加斯（Enisgarth）、卡姆威（Camwy）与萨吉尔（Sargyll）领主的戈吉尔万走下宏阔的宝座，先是紧紧拥抱了吉尼薇尔，继而拥抱了朱尔根。

“向我索求你心仪之物吧，洛格鲁斯公爵（Duke of Logreus），”戈吉尔万在知晓了这位勇士的名号之后说道，“凡有所求，但说无妨。因你将一位至尊国王平生最引以为傲的爱女交还给了我。”

“陛下，”朱尔根通情达理地答道，“心甘情愿效劳之功，本就是其自身的酬劳。故而我所求者，乃是陛下将吉尼薇尔公主赐予我，明媒正娶，永结秦晋之好，陛下当能明鉴：我大抵算是个形单影只的鳏夫，这点我颇有把握；但我更十二分确信，我全心全意地钟爱着令媛。”

朱尔根这般陈辞，因心绪激动，言辞间不免有些颠三倒四。

“我委实瞧不出你一颗真心如何，同这般不近人情的非分之请有何相干。况且，如今统领不列颠全境的亚瑟王（King of the Britons）的使臣正候在门外，求娶小女为妻，你挑这时候向我提亲，实在大失分寸。你自称洛格鲁斯公爵，我承认公爵确乎尊贵不凡；但我料想你也该懂得，在任何家有待嫁闺秀的父亲眼里，国王总归要居于公爵之先。不过明日抑或后日，你我二人不妨私下细细商量你的酬赏。眼下嘛，身为荡平斯拉格纳（Thragnar）的盖世英雄，你的神色瞧来怎生这般古怪、又这般惶恐不安？”

只因朱尔根正呆呆凝望着王座背后悬着的那面硕大明镜。

===Original===

In this mirror Jurgen saw the back of Gogyrvan’s crowned head, and beyond this, Jurgen saw a queer and frightened looking young fellow, with sleek black hair, and an impudent nose, and wide-open bright brown eyes which were staring hard at Jurgen: and the lad’s very red and very heavy lips were parted, so that you saw what fine strong teeth he had: and he wore a glittering shirt with curious figures on it.
“I was thinking,” says Jurgen, and he saw the lad in the mirror was speaking too, “I was thinking that is a remarkable mirror you have there.”
“It is like any other mirror,” replies the King, “in that it shows things as they are. But if you fancy it as your reward, why, take it and welcome.”
“And are you still talking of rewards!” cries Jurgen. “Why, if that mirror shows things as they are, I have come out of my borrowed Wednesday still twenty-one. Oh, but it was the clever fellow I was, to flatter Mother Sereda so cunningly, and to fool her into such generosity! And I wonder that you who are only a king, with bleared eyes under your crown, and with a drooping belly under all your royal robes, should be talking of rewarding a fine young fellow of twenty-one, for there is nothing you have which I need be wanting now.”
“Then you will not be plaguing me any more with your nonsense about my daughter: and that is excellent news.”
“But I have no requirement to be asking your good graces now,” said Jurgen, “nor the good will of any man alive that has a handsome daughter or a handsome wife. For now I have the aid of a lad that was very recently made Duke of Logreus: and with his countenance I can look out for myself, and I can get justice done me everywhere, in all the bedchambers of the world.”
And Jurgen snapped his fingers, and was about to turn away from the King. There was much sunlight in the hall, so that Jurgen in this half-turn confronted his shadow as it lay plain upon the flagstones. And Jurgen looked at it very intently.

===Chinese===

在这面镜子里，朱尔根望见了戈吉尔万头戴王冠的后脑勺；而越过王冠，朱尔根瞧见了一个神色古怪且惊惶的年轻后生：生着一头油亮柔顺的黑发，一副神气活现的翘鼻子，一双睁得大大的清亮褐色眸子，正目不转睛地死死盯着朱尔根；小伙子那两瓣殷红厚实的嘴唇微微张着，露出一口洁白整齐的坚固好牙；身上则裹着一件流光溢彩、织着奇异花纹的衬衫。

“我方才在想，”朱尔根开口说道，只见镜中的那个小伙子亦在一道张口说话，“我方才在想，陛下这面镜子委实非比寻常。”

“它同世间凡百镜子并无二致，”国王答道，“无非是照出物事的本来面目罢了。你若瞧上了它充作酬赏，只管拿去，我欣然奉送。”

“到了这步田地，陛下难道还同我絮叨什么酬赏不成！”朱尔根喜不自胜地大叫道，“天哪，倘若这面镜子当真能照出物事原貌，那我从借来的那个星期三脱身而出，竟然依旧只有21岁（二十一岁）！哎呀呀，我真是个绝顶聪明的妙人儿，竟能这般精巧地奉承谢列达母亲（Mother Sereda），把她哄得这般宽宏大度！我倒真纳了闷了，凭你这么个区区老国王，王冠底下耷拉着一双昏花老眼，王袍底下腆着一个松垮肚腩，竟也配夸口酬赏一位芳华正茂的21岁倜傥后生？凡你所拥有的俗物，眼下没一样是我瞧得上的。”

“这么说来，你往后便再不会拿我女儿那些混账话来纠缠我了；这可是天大的好消息。”

“我如今哪里还用得着求取陛下的青睐，”朱尔根说，“更无须仰仗世间任何家有俏女儿、美娇娘的凡夫俗子之鼻息！因为眼下我身后，站着一位新晋升为洛格鲁斯公爵的威猛俊后生；仰仗他的雄姿容貌，我大可自立门户，在全天下所有女子的绣榻深闺之中，我皆能替自己讨还公道！”

言毕，朱尔根打了个响指，正欲拂袖背过身去。大殿里阳光普照，朱尔根这半转过身来，赫然撞见了自己的阴影清晰无误地投映在石板地上。朱尔根不由自主地死死盯住了那道阴影。

===Original===

“Of course,” said Jurgen presently, “I only meant in a manner of speaking, sir: and was paraphrasing the splendid if hackneyed passage from Sornatius, with which you are doubtless familiar, in which he goes on to say, so much more beautifully than I could possibly express without quoting him word for word, that all this was spoken jestingly, and without the least intention of offending anybody, oh, anybody whatever, I can assure you, sir.”
“Very well,” said Gogyrvan Gawr: and he smiled, for no reason that was apparent to Jurgen, who was still watching his shadow sidewise. “Tomorrow, I repeat, I must talk with you more privately. Today I am giving a banquet such as was never known in these parts, because my daughter is restored to me, and because my daughter is going to be queen over all the Britons.”
So said Gogyrvan, that was King of Glathion and Lord of Enisgarth and Camwy and Sargyll: and this was done. And everywhere at the banquet Jurgen heard talk of this King Arthur who was to marry Dame Guenevere, and of the prophecy which Merlin Ambrosius had made as to the young monarch. For Merlin had predicted:
“He shall afford succor, and shall tread upon the necks of his enemies: the isles of the ocean shall be subdued by him, and he shall possess the forests of Gaul: the house of Romulus shall fear his rage, and his acts shall be food for the narrators.”
“Why, then,” says Jurgen, to himself, “this monarch reminds me in all things of David of Israel, who was so splendid and famous, and so greedy, in the ancient ages. For to these forests and islands and necks and other possessions, this Arthur Pendragon must be adding my one ewe lamb; and I lack a Nathan to convert him to repentance. Now, but this, to be sure, is a very unfair thing.”
Then Jurgen looked again into a mirror: and presently the eyes of the lad he found therein began to twinkle.
“Have at you, David!” said Jurgen, valorously; “since after all, I see no reason to despair.”

===Chinese===

“当然，”朱尔根旋即改口道，“陛下，在下不过是借题发挥、打个比方罢了；无非是在转述索尔纳蒂乌斯（Sornatius）那段虽被陈词滥调玷污却依旧精妙绝伦的篇章——想必陛下对此耳熟能详。他在后文中说得那般透彻优美，远非我拙劣文笔所能描摹，唯有原汁原味引其原话：方才这番言论纯系戏谑之辞，绝无半点冒犯之意，噢，天地良心，绝无冒犯天下任何人之心，陛下尽可安心。”

“甚好，”戈吉尔万·高尔说道，嘴角泛起一抹笑意，朱尔根却揣摩不透其深意，因他此刻仍在用眼角余光不住斜睨着自己的阴影。“我重申一句，明日我必与你私下详谈。今日我要大宴宾朋，设下一场此方水土前所未见的盛筵，只因小女失而复得，更因小女即将加冕为全不列颠的王后。”

这位格拉西昂国王、埃尼斯加斯、卡姆威与萨吉尔的领主戈吉尔万一锤定音；盛宴随即铺排开来。席间四处，朱尔根尽皆听闻宾客们津津乐道于迎娶吉尼薇尔夫人的那位亚瑟王，以及梅林·安布罗休斯（Merlin Ambrosius）对这位年轻君王所作的宏伟预言。盖因梅林曾预言道：

“他必施行拯救，践踏仇敌之项颈；重洋诸岛尽归其统辖，高卢之林莽尽入其版图；罗慕路斯之后裔亦将畏惧其威怒，其丰功伟业必成后世吟游叙事之谈资。”

“嗨，这么说来，”朱尔根暗自思忖，“这位君王从头到脚无一处不像极了以色列的大卫王（David of Israel）——在古昔岁月里，那大卫是何等威严显赫、名扬四海，却又是何等贪得无厌。因除了这诸般林莽、海岛、项颈与旁人物业之外，这位亚瑟·潘德拉贡（Arthur Pendragon）偏偏还要横刀夺爱，夺去我唯一的独生母羊羔；偏生我又缺个先知拿单（Nathan）去劝他痛改前非。哎，平心而论，这委实太不公道了。”

随后朱尔根再度探头瞧向明镜：不多时，他在镜中照见的那位俊后生的眼眸里，便渐渐泛起了狡黠的光芒。

“且放马过来吧，大卫！”朱尔根意气风发地叫道，“横竖思量，我还没到绝望的田地呢。”

## XXII As to a Veil They Broke / 第二十二章 论他们破开的一层面纱
===Original===

So it was that Jurgen came into Cocaigne, wherein is the bedchamber of Time. And Time, they report, came in with Jurgen, since Jurgen was mortal: and Time, they say, rejoiced in this respite from the slow toil of dilapidating cities stone by stone, and with his eyes tired by the finicky work of etching in wrinkles, went happily into his bedchamber, and fell asleep just after sunset on this fine evening in late June: so that the weather remained fair and changeless, with no glaring sun rays anywhere, and with one large star shining alone in clear daylight. This was the star of Venus Mechanitis, and Jurgen later derived considerable amusement from noting how this star was trundled about the dome of heaven by a largish beetle, named Khepre. And the trees everywhere kept their first fresh foliage, and the birds were about their indolent evening songs, all during Jurgen’s stay in Cocaigne, for Time had gone to sleep at the pleasantest hour of the year’s most pleasant season. So tells the tale.
And Jurgen’s shadow also went in with Jurgen, but in Cocaigne as in Glathion, nobody save Jurgen seemed to notice this curious shadow which now followed Jurgen everywhere.
In Cocaigne Queen Anaïtis had a palace, where domes and pinnacles beyond numbering glimmered with a soft whiteness above the top of an old twilit forest, wherein the vegetation was unlike that which is nourished by ordinary earth. There was to be seen in these woods, for instance, a sort of moss which made Jurgen shudder. So Anaïtis and Jurgen came through narrow paths, like murmuring green caverns, into a courtyard walled and paved with yellow marble, wherein was nothing save the dimly colored statue of a god with ten heads and thirty-four arms: he was represented as very much engrossed by a woman, and with his unoccupied hands was holding yet other women.
“It is Jigsbyed,” said Anaïtis.
Said Jurgen: “I do not criticize. Nevertheless, I think this Jigsbyed is carrying matters to extremes.”
Then they passed the statue of Tangaro Loloquong, and afterward the statue of Legba. Jurgen stroked his chin, and his color heightened. “Now certainly, Queen Anaïtis,” he said, “you have unusual taste in sculpture.”
Thence Jurgen came with Anaïtis into a white room, with copper plaques upon the walls, and there four girls were heating water in a brass tripod.

===Chinese===

正是在这般机缘下，朱尔根（Jurgen）步入了乐土科凯恩（Cocaigne），时间（Time）的寝殿便坐落于此。传闻时间亦与朱尔根一同入得此境，盖因朱尔根终是凡胎肉身；世人言道，时间终于得以从一砖一石剥蚀城池的漫长苦工中脱身喘息，因常年细细雕琢皱纹而疲惫不堪的眼眸亦得以稍歇，遂在这六月下旬（late June）的一个晴朗傍晚，于日落初刻便欢天喜地钻入寝榻，酣然入梦：于是此间气候长留晴暖、终古不迁，四海无灼烈刺目的骄阳，唯有一颗硕大星辰在朗朗清明中独放异彩。那便是“工巧维纳斯之星”（star of Venus Mechanitis），嗣后朱尔根瞧见这颗大星竟是由一只名唤凯布利（Khepre）的硕大甲虫在苍穹穹顶上骨碌碌推着巡行周天，不由大感快慰。遍处林木长葆初生嫩绿，鸟雀在枝头慵懒地唱着晚歌，在朱尔根驻留科凯恩的整段光景中无不如此，因时间恰在一年中最怡人的时节里最怡人的那一刻陷入了沉睡。故事便这般流传着。

朱尔根的阴影亦紧随其后随他而入，然而在科凯恩恰如在格拉西昂，除了朱尔根自己，似乎无人介意这道如今无论走到何方皆寸步不离的古怪阴影。

在科凯恩，安娜伊蒂斯王后（Queen Anaïtis）筑有一座宫寝，无数穹顶与尖塔在一片古老微暗的暮色森林顶梢之上泛着柔和的冷白辉光；林中生长的草木全然不同于凡俗土壤所滋养的林木。举例而言，林中竟能见得一种教朱尔根望之毛骨悚然的怪苔。安娜伊蒂斯与朱尔根循着宛若低语绿洞般的幽狭小径一路前行，踏入了一座以黄色大理石砌墙铺地的庭院；庭院中别无长物，唯立有一尊色泽黯淡的神像，生着10个头颅与34条臂膀（十首三十四臂）：神像被塑造成正与一名女子如胶似漆、缠绵难解的模样，而那几只空闲的手上，还攀揽着其余若干名女子。

“这位乃是吉格斯比德神（Jigsbyed），”安娜伊蒂斯说道。

朱尔根言道：“我倒无意置喙。不过，我自忖这位吉格斯比德未免有些做过了头。”

随后他们走过了坦加罗·洛洛匡神（Tangaro Loloquong）的雕像，复又行经莱格巴神（Legba）的立像。朱尔根摩挲着下颌，面色渐渐泛红。“平心而论，安娜伊蒂斯王后，”他说，“你在雕塑品味上当真非同凡响。”

自此，朱尔根随安娜伊蒂斯步入一间素白居室，四壁嵌着铜饰铭牌，4位少女正在铜制三脚鼎前烧煮热水。

===Original===

They bathed Jurgen, giving him astonishing caresses meanwhile﻿—with the tongue, the hair, the fingernails, and the tips of the breasts﻿—and they anointed him with four oils, then dressed him again in his glittering shirt. Of Caliburn, said Anaïtis, there was no present need: so Jurgen’s sword was hung upon the wall.
These girls brought silver bowls containing wine mixed with honey, and they brought pomegranates and eggs and barleycorn, and triangular red-colored loaves, whereon they sprinkled sweet-smelling little seeds with formal gestures. Then Anaïtis and Jurgen broke their fast, eating together while the four girls served them.
“And now,” says Jurgen, “and now, my dear, I would suggest that we enter into the pursuit of those curious pleasures of which you were telling me.”
“I am very willing,” responded Anaïtis, “since there is no one of these pleasures but is purchased by some diversion of man’s nature. Yet first, as I need hardly inform you, there is a ceremonial to be observed.”
“And what, pray, is this ceremonial?”
“Why, we call it the Breaking of the Veil.” And Queen Anaïtis explained what they must do.
“Well,” says Jurgen, “I am willing to taste any drink once.”
So Anaïtis led Jurgen into a sort of chapel, adorned with very unchurchlike paintings. There were four shrines, dedicated severally to St. Cosmo, to St. Damianus, to St. Guignole of Brest, and to St. Foutin de Varailles. In this chapel were a hooded man, clothed in long garments that were striped with white and yellow, and two naked children, both girls. One of the children carried a censer: the other held in one hand a vividly blue pitcher half filled with water, and in her left hand a cellar of salt.
First of all, the hooded man made Jurgen ready. “Behold the lance,” said the hooded man, “which must serve you in this adventure.”
“I accept the adventure,” Jurgen replied, “because I believe the weapon to be trustworthy.”
Said the hooded man: “So be it! but as you are, so once was I.”
Meanwhile Duke Jurgen held the lance erect, shaking it with his right hand. This lance was large, and the tip of it was red with blood.
“Behold,” said Jurgen, “I am a man born of a woman incomprehensibly. Now I, who am miraculous, am found worthy to perform a miracle, and to create that which I may not comprehend.”
Anaïtis took salt and water from the child, and mingled these.

===Chinese===

少女们为朱尔根沐浴更衣，其间奉上了诸般令人惊异的爱抚——以舌尖、发丝、纤甲与乳尖款款摩挲——又以4种香油（four oils）涂抹其周身，复为他重新换上那件光华夺目的璀璨衬衣。安娜伊蒂斯言道，卡利班宝剑（Caliburn）眼下暂无用武之地：于是朱尔根的佩剑便悬挂在了墙上。

这几位少女端来盛满蜂蜜调和美酒的银碗，又奉上石榴、禽卵、大麦粒以及赤色三角面饼，并以庄重典雅的姿态在上面播撒芬芳细籽。于是安娜伊蒂斯与朱尔根一同进膳开荤，4位侍女在旁侍奉周全。

“既如此，”朱尔根说道，“既如此，我的好娘子，我意欲提议，我们此刻不妨动身去探求你早先对我提及的那些奇趣妙乐了。”

“我欣然应允，”安娜伊蒂斯应道，“盖因凡此种种妙乐，无一不是靠移易人之天性换取得来。然而在此之前，想来无须我多言，总有一套仪典须得恪遵。”

“敢问究竟是何等仪典？”

“嗨，我们管它叫‘破开面纱之仪’（Breaking of the Veil）。”安娜伊蒂斯王后随即解说了二人所行之事。

“唔，”朱尔根说，“凡是佳酿，我都乐意浅尝一遭。”

于是安娜伊蒂斯引朱尔根步入一间颇似小堂的秘所，四壁布满了全无半点教门气象的怪诞壁画。堂内设有4座神龛，分别供奉圣科斯莫（St. Cosmo）、圣达米亚努斯（St. Damianus）、布雷斯特的圣吉尼奥莱（St. Guignole of Brest）以及瓦赖耶的圣富坦（St. Foutin de Varailles）。小堂之中立有一名蒙头男子，身裹白黄相间条纹的长袍；另有两名赤身女童侍立在侧。一名女童手捧香炉，另一女童右手托着盛有半罐清水的蔚蓝瓷罐，左手托着一撮食盐。

首先，蒙头男子为朱尔根拾掇停当。“且看这杆长矛，”蒙头男子说道，“在此番历险之中，唯它能为你效力。”

“我愿承领此番历险，”朱尔根答道，“因我笃信此件兵刃堪称趁手得力。”

蒙头男子言道：“愿如所期！然尔今朝之态，亦我昔日之形。”

其间朱尔根公爵右手高擎长矛，直立挺举，微微震颤。此矛粗硕修长，矛尖因饮血而殷红夺目。

“且看，”朱尔根宣示道，“我乃妇人以不可名状之妙所生之男子。今我这夺造化之身，亦配行此神迹，去缔造我所不能参透之奇功。”

安娜伊蒂斯自女童手中取过盐与水，将二者相融相和。

===Original===

“Let the salt of earth enable the thin fluid to assume the virtue of the teeming sea!”
Then, kneeling, she touched the lance, and began to stroke it lovingly. To Jurgen she said: “Now may you be fervent of soul and body! May the endless Serpent be your crown, and the fertile flame of the sun your strength!”
Said the hooded man, again: “So be it!” His voice was high and bleating, because of that which had been done to him.
“That therefore which we cannot understand we also invoke,” said Jurgen. “By the power of the lifted lance”﻿—and now with his left hand he took the hand of Anaïtis﻿—“I, being a man born of a woman incomprehensibly, now seize upon that which alone I desire with my whole being. I lead you toward the east. I upraise you above the earth and all the things of earth.”
Then Jurgen raised Queen Anaïtis so that she sat upon the altar, and that which was there before tumbled to the ground. Anaïtis placed together the tips of her thumbs and of her fingers, so that her hands made an open triangle; and waited thus. Upon her head was a network of red coral, with branches radiating downward: her gauzy tunic had twenty-two openings, so as to admit all imaginable caresses, and was of two colors, being shot with black and crimson curiously mingled: her dark eyes glittered and her breath came fast.
Now the hooded man and the two naked girls performed their share in the ceremonial, which part it is not essential to record. But Jurgen was rather shocked by it.
None the less, Jurgen said: “O cord that binds the circling of the stars! O cup which holds all time, all color, and all thought! O soul of space! not unto any image of thee do we attain unless thy image show in what we are about to do. Therefore by every plant which scatters its seed and by the moist warm garden which receives and nourishes it, by the comminglement of bloodshed with pleasure, by the joy that mimics anguish with sighs and shudderings, and by the contentment which mimics death﻿—by all these do we invoke thee. O thou, continuous one, whose will these children attend, and whom I now adore in this fair-colored and soft woman’s body, it is thou whom I honor, not any woman, in doing what seems good to me: and it is thou who art about to speak, and not she.”
Then Anaïtis said: “Yea, for I speak with the tongue of every woman, and I shine in the eyes of every woman, when the lance is lifted.

===Chinese===

“愿大地的咸盐令此薄液承继浩瀚丰饶之汪洋的神力！”

言毕，她屈膝跪地，轻抚长矛，极尽怜爱地摩挲起来。她对朱尔根言道：“愿你身心如火如荼！愿永恒蛇环作你的华冠，愿太阳滋生万物的烈焰作你的气力！”

蒙头男子再度尖声应和：“愿如所期！”因其所受之创刑，其音调尖细凄厉宛若羊羔悲鸣。

“凡我们肉眼凡胎不能参透之玄妙，我们亦在此虔诚祈请，”朱尔根说道，“仰仗这高擎长矛的神威”——他左手顺势握住了安娜伊蒂斯的手——“我，这不可名状由妇人所生之凡男，今朝执掌了我全副身心唯一渴求之物。我引你向东。我将你高高举起，超拔于大地及大地上一切凡俗尘埃之上。”

于是朱尔根将安娜伊蒂斯王后高举而起，教她端坐于祭坛之上，原先坛上供奉之物滚落满地。安娜伊蒂斯拇指与其余诸指指尖相对相抵，双手合扣成一处敞开的三足形虚空，静候于此。她头上戴着一顶垂挂下垂枝杈的赤红珊瑚网冠；身上那件轻绡薄裙共有22处开口（二十二处开口），足纳一切凡人能想象之爱抚，裙身织就两色，黑红相间，奇诡莫测；她深邃的双眸闪烁灼灼，气息渐趋急促。

此刻，蒙头男子与两名赤身女童依例各行其仪式职分，此间细微曲折自无形诸笔墨之必要。朱尔根见状，心头亦不免颇感惊骇。

即便如此，朱尔根依然高吟道：“噢，维系群星运转之灵索！噢，容纳万古光阴、万千色相与无尽意念之宝盏！噢，虚空之元魂！非借由我们行将所成之功显化其象，凡胎肉身断无由得见汝之真容。故而，凭藉每一株播撒种籽之草木，凭藉接纳滋养种籽之温润沃野；凭藉流血之痛与极乐之欢的交织难分，凭藉以叹息战栗模仿苦楚之狂喜，凭藉以静寂模仿死亡之餍足——借由这凡百显象，我们向汝祈请。噢，永续不绝的独一主宰，这幼童侍奉汝之意志，而我今于这温香软玉的丽人肉躯中向汝俯首赞美；我行随心所欲快慰之事，所尊崇者乃是汝而非任何凡间妇人；行将发声宣示者亦是汝而非此女子。”

于是安娜伊蒂斯宣示道：“然也，当长矛高举之时，我以全天下妇人之舌代言，我于全天下妇人之眸闪烁。”

===Original===

To serve me is better than all else. When you invoke me with a heart wherein is kindled the serpent flame, if but for a moment, you will understand the delights of my garden, what joy unwordable pulsates therein, and how potent is the sole desire which uses all of a man. To serve me you will then be eager to surrender whatever else is in your life: and other pleasures you will take with your left hand, not thinking of them entirely: for I am the desire which uses all of a man, and so wastes nothing. And I accept you, I yearn toward you, I who am daughter and somewhat more than daughter to the Sun. I who am all pleasure, all ruin, and a drunkenness of the inmost sense, desire you.”
Now Jurgen held his lance erect before Anaïtis. “O secret of all things, hidden in the being of all which lives, now that the lance is exalted I do not dread thee: for thou art in me, and I am thou. I am the flame that burns in every beating heart and in the core of the farthest star. I too am life and the giver of life, and in me too is death. Wherein art thou better than I? I am alone: my will is justice: and there comes no other god where I am.”
Said the hooded man behind Jurgen: “So be it! but as you are, so once was I.”
The two naked children stood one at each side of Anaïtis, and waited there trembling. These girls, as Jurgen afterward learned, were Alecto and Tisiphonê, two of the Eumenidês. And now Jurgen shifted the red point of the lance, so that it rested in the open triangle made by the fingers of Anaïtis.
“I am life and the giver of life,” cried Jurgen. “Thou that art one, that makest use of all! I who am a man born of woman, I in my station honor thee in honoring this desire which uses all of a man. Make open therefore the way of creation, encourage the flaming dust which is in our hearts, and aid us in that flame’s perpetuation! For is not that thy law?”
Anaïtis answered: “There is no law in Cocaigne save, Do that which seems good to you.”
Then said the naked children: “Perhaps it is the law, but certainly it is not justice. Yet we are little and quite helpless. So presently we must be made as you are for now you two are no longer two, and your flesh is not shared merely with each other. For your flesh becomes our flesh, and your sins our sins: and we have no choice.”
Jurgen lifted Anaïtis from the altar, and they went into the chancel and searched for the adytum.

===Chinese===

“侍奉我胜过世间万般物事。当你以一颗燃起灵蛇之焰的心向我祈请，哪怕唯有刹那光景，你亦能彻悟我这园囿中的无上妙趣，领略其中何等难以言表的欢愉在不住搏动，领略那独占男儿全副精气的心魔何其雄浑沛然。为侍奉我，你将甘心情愿舍弃生命中其余一切凡俗挂碍；至于旁样欢愉，你不过左手顺道拈来，断不会全神挂怀：因我乃是独占男儿全副精魂之渴念，故而绝不虚掷半点春光。我接纳你，我渴求于你——我这太阳之女、且远甚于太阳之女的神明。我，这集天下欢愉、天下沉沦与心髓深处酩酊于一身者，渴求于你。”

此刻朱尔根在安娜伊蒂斯身前高挺长矛，笔直如削。“噢，潜藏于众生万物之中的万象秘钥，如今既然长矛赫然高耸，我亦无惧于你：因你居于我身，而我便化作了你。我是跳动的心房与最遥远星辰核心深处燃烧的烈火。我亦是生机，是生机的赋予者，而死亡亦伏于我身。你又何尝胜过我分毫？我独步天地：我心即是公道；凡我所立之处，别无其余神祇容身之所。”

朱尔根身后的蒙头男子沉声道：“愿如所期！然尔今朝之态，亦我昔日之形。”

两名赤身女童分立安娜伊蒂斯左右两侧，在原地战战兢兢静立等候。这两名女童，朱尔根嗣后方得知乃是阿勒克托（Alecto）与提西福涅（Tisiphonê），欧墨尼得斯（Eumenidês）复仇女神中的两位。此刻朱尔根移转了长矛那赤红的尖端，教它恰好安歇在安娜伊蒂斯双手指尖搭就的那处敞开的三角虚位之中。

“我乃生机，是生机的赋予者！”朱尔根高声喝道，“合而为一的至尊，役使万物的造化！我乃妇人所生之男子，在此位格上尊崇于你，亦是尊崇这役使男儿全副身心的本能渴念。开辟造化之路吧，鼓荡我们心头如火如荼的炽烈尘泥，助我们令此神火延绵不绝！难道这不正是汝之律法么？”

安娜伊蒂斯答道：“科凯恩并无旁样律法，唯有一条：凡你自忖快意之举，但行无妨。”

赤身女童随即言道：“这或许是律法，却断然不是公道。然而我们弱小无依、无可奈何。不多时我们亦必被塑造成你们这般模样；因如今你们二人已不再是分立的两人，你们的血肉不再仅仅彼此相属。你们的血肉化作了我们的血肉，你们的罪孽化作了我们的罪孽：而我们全无抉择。”

朱尔根将安娜伊蒂斯从祭坛上抱扶而下，二人迈入圣所后庭，探寻内殿至圣密室（adytum）。

===Original===

There seemed to be no doors anywhere in the chancel: but presently Jurgen found an opening screened by a pink veil. Jurgen thrust with his lance and broke this veil. He heard the sound of one brief wailing cry: it was followed by soft laughter. So Jurgen came into the adytum.
Black candles were burning in this place, and sulphur too was burning there, before a scarlet cross, of which the top was a circle, and whereon was nailed a living toad. And other curious matters Jurgen likewise noticed.
He laughed, and turned to Anaïtis: now that the candles were behind him, she was standing in his shadow. “Well, well! but you are a little old-fashioned, with all these equivocal mummeries. And I did not know that civilized persons any longer retained sufficient credulity to wring a thrill from god-baiting. Still, women must be humored, bless them! and at last, I take it, we have quite fairly fulfilled the ceremonial requisite to the pursuit of curious pleasures.”
Queen Anaïtis was very beautiful, even under his bedimming shadow. Triumphant too was the proud face beneath that curious coral network, and yet this woman’s face was sad.
“Dear fool,” she said, “it was not wise, when you sang of the Léshy, to put an affront upon Monday. But you have forgotten that. And now you laugh because that which we have done you do not understand: and equally that which I am you do not understand.”
“No matter what you may be, my dear, I am sure that you will presently tell me all about it. For I assume that you mean to deal fairly with me.”
“I shall do that which becomes me, Duke Jurgen﻿—”
“That is it, my dear, precisely! You intend to be true to yourself, whatever happens. The aspiration does you infinite honor, and I shall try to help you. Now I have noticed that every woman is most truly herself,” says Jurgen, oracularly, “in the dark.”
Then Jurgen looked at her for a moment, with twinkling eyes: then Anaïtis, standing in his shadow, smiled with glowing eyes: then Jurgen blew out those black candles: and then it was quite dark.

===Chinese===

后庭圣所各处看似全无门户；然而不多时朱尔根便撞见了一处以粉红色面纱（pink veil）遮蔽的幽深洞口。朱尔根执长矛猛力刺去，挑破了这层面纱。他听闻一声短促凄切的幽幽低泣；紧随其后的却是一阵轻柔吃吃的娇笑。于是朱尔根步入了内殿密室。

黑色的蜡烛在室内静静燃烧，一架红色的T形十字架前硫磺亦在滋滋冒火，十字架顶端围成环状，上面活活钉着一只犹自蠕动的蟾蜍。此外，朱尔根亦瞥见了其余种种诡谲莫测的物事。

他失笑出声，转过脸看向安娜伊蒂斯：如今蜡烛全落在了他身后，她恰好娉娉婷婷立在他的阴影之中。“好哇，好哇！不过操持这诸般语焉不详的荒唐把戏，你未免有些抱残守缺了。我还当真不知晓开化文明的体面人，至今竟还留存着足够的盲信，靠亵渎神明来榨取战栗快感呢。不过，妇人家总得顺着哄着，真是拿她们没辙！总而言之，依我之见，追寻奇趣妙乐所需的一切繁文缛节，眼下我们大体算得上功德圆满了。”

安娜伊蒂斯王后美艳不可方物，纵然置身于他那遮蔽光芒的沉沉暗影之中亦然。在那奇特珊瑚发网底下，她那骄矜的玉容洋溢着胜券在握的得意，然而这张丽人容颜之上，却隐隐浮动着一缕幽怨凄楚。

“痴顽的呆子，”她说，“当初你为森林妖灵莱希（Léshy）作歌之际，胆敢折辱星期一，原是不智之举。如今你却早抛在脑后了。此刻你发笑，无非因我们方才所成之事你浑然不解；而至于我究竟是何方神圣，你同样一无所知。”

“任凭你是何方神圣，我的好娘子，我笃信你待会儿自会一五一十全盘托出。因我认定你向来打算公平待我。”

“我自会行恪尽我本分之事，朱尔根公爵——”

“正是这话，我的好娘子，半点不差！无论世事如何，你总打算不负本心。这等志向教我肃然起敬，我自当倾力相助。且说我向来觉察得出，凡天下妇人最能展露本来面目之所，”朱尔根故作玄虚地宣示道，“唯在暗室之中。”

随后朱尔根目不转睛凝视了她片刻，眼眸中闪烁着狡黠的星芒；站在他阴影中的安娜伊蒂斯，一双晶莹美眸亦泛着灼灼火光，嫣然一笑：旋即朱尔根一口气吹熄了那些黑蜡烛：四下里便陷入了一团浓黑的幽暗。

## XXXVIII As to Applauded Precedents / 第三十八章 论博得喝彩的先例
===Original===

Afterward Jurgen abode in Hell, and complied with the customs of that country. And the tale tells that a week or it might be ten days after his meeting with Florimel, Jurgen married her, without being at all hindered by his having three other wives. For the devils, he found, esteemed polygamy, and ranked it above mere skill at torturing the damned, through a literal interpretation of the saying that it is better to marry than to burn.
“And formerly,” they told Jurgen, “you could hardly come across a marriage anywhere that was not hallmarked ‘made in Heaven’: but since we have been at war with Heaven we have quite taken away that trade from our enemies. So you may marry here as much as you like.”
“Why, then,” says Jurgen, “I shall marry in haste, and repeat at leisure. But can one obtain a divorce here?”
“Oh, no,” said they. “We trafficked in them for a while, but we found that all persons who obtained divorces through our industry promptly thanked Heaven they were free at last. In the face of such ingratitude we gave over that profitless trade, and now there is a manufactory, for specialties in men’s clothing, upon the old statutory grounds.”
“But these makeshifts are unsatisfactory, and I wish to know, in confidence, what do you do in Hell when there is no longer any putting up with your wives.”
The devils all blushed. “We would prefer not to tell you,” said they, “for it might get to their ears.”
“Now do I perceive,” said Jurgen, “that Hell is pretty much like any other place.”
So Jurgen and the lovely vampire were duly married. First Jurgen’s nails were trimmed, and the parings were given to Florimel. A broomstick was laid before them, and they stepped over it. Then Florimel said “Temon!” thrice, and nine times did Jurgen reply “Arigizator!” Afterward the Emperor Jurgen and his bride were given a posset of dudaïm and eruca, and the devils modestly withdrew.
Thereafter Jurgen abode in Hell, and complied with the customs of that country, and was tolerably content for a while. Now Jurgen shared with Florimel that quiet cleft which she had fitted out in imitation of her girlhood home: and they lived in the suburbs of Barathum, very respectably, by the shore of the sea.

===Chinese===

嗣后，朱尔根（Jurgen）留居地狱（Hell），并遵从了该国风俗。故事讲道，在同弗洛里梅尔（Florimel）相遇约莫一周或十天之后，朱尔根便迎娶了她，丝毫未因自己另有三位妻子（3位妻子）而有所窒碍。盖因他发觉恶魔们极推崇多妻之制，甚至将其置于折磨受罚亡魂的纯熟技艺之上——他们拘泥字句地曲解了圣书所言“与其欲火攻心，倒不如嫁娶为妙”。

恶魔们对朱尔根言道：“往昔岁月里，你在凡间几乎寻不着一桩不曾打着‘天堂缔造’（made in Heaven）烙印的婚事；然而自打我们同天堂开战以来，我们便彻底将这桩营生从仇敌手里夺了过来。故而在此处你想结多少回婚，便可结多少回婚。”

“既如此，”朱尔根说，“我自当草草成婚，再从容重蹈覆辙。不过此处可准予和离离异么？”

“哎呀，使不得，”他们答道，“我们早先也做过一阵子这门营生，却发现凡凭我们效劳得以离异之人，转头便感恩戴德谢天谢地，庆幸终于重获自由。面对这等忘恩负义之徒，我们早早收手歇了这桩折本买卖；如今在昔日法定离异的旧址上，已然建起了一座专制男士成衣的工场。”

“然而这等权宜之计委实难尽人意，我私下里倒想讨教一句，地狱里若是到了委实无法忍受自家妻子的地步，又当如何处置？”

恶魔们尽皆涨红了脸。“我们宁可不对你讲，”他们说，“免得走漏风声传进她们耳朵里。”

“如今我方算看透了，”朱尔根说，“地狱同世间任何别处原本别无二致。”

于是朱尔根与娇俏的吸血女妖如期举行了大婚。起初先是修剪了朱尔根的指甲，将碎甲赠予弗洛里梅尔。一柄扫帚横置在二人身前，他们双双跨越而过。随后弗洛里梅尔三度高呼“特蒙！”（Temon!），朱尔根九度应和“阿里吉扎托尔！”（Arigizator!）。礼成之后，朱尔根皇帝与新娘同饮了一盏以曼陀罗与芝麻菜调和的暖汤合卺酒，众恶魔方才识趣地退避而去。

自此朱尔根留居地狱，遵奉此邦俗例，一时倒也颇感心满意足。此刻朱尔根同弗洛里梅尔共享那处幽静岩隙——这原是她仿照少女时代深闺模样布置停当的居所；他们便在深渊城（Barathum）郊野临海之处，过着格外体面的安稳日子。

===Original===

There was, of course, no water in Hell; indeed the importation of water was forbidden, under severe penalties, in view of its possible use for baptismal purposes: this sea was composed of the blood that had been shed by piety in furthering the kingdom of the Prince of Peace, and was reputed to be the largest ocean in existence. And it explained the nonsensical saying which Jurgen had so often heard, as to Hell’s being paved with good intentions.
“For Epigenes of Rhodes is right, after all,” said Jurgen, “in suggesting a misprint: and the word should be ‘laved.’ ”
“Why, to be sure, your majesty,” assented Florimel: “ah, but I always said your majesty had remarkable powers of penetration, quite apart from your majesty’s scholarship.”
For Florimel had this cajoling way of speaking. None the less, all vampires have their foibles, and are nourished by the vigor and youth of their lovers. So one morning Florimel complained of being unwell, and attributed it to indigestion.
Jurgen stroked her head meditatively; then he opened his glittering shirt, and displayed what was plain enough to see.
“I am full of vigor and I am young,” said Jurgen, “but my vigor and my youthfulness are of a peculiar sort, and are not wholesome. So let us have no more of your tricks, or you will quite spoil your vacation by being very ill indeed.”
“But I had thought all emperors were human!” said Florimel, in a flutter of blushing penitence, exceedingly pretty to observe.
“Even so, sweetheart, all emperors are not Jurgens,” he replied, magnificently. “Therefore you will find that not every emperor is justly styled the father of his people, or is qualified by nature to wield the sceptre of Noumaria. I trust this lesson will suffice.”
“It will,” said Florimel, with a wry face.
So thereafter they had no further trouble of this sort, and the wound on Jurgen’s breast was soon healed.
And Jurgen kept away from the damned, of course, because he and Florimel were living respectably. They paid a visit to Jurgen’s father, however, very shortly after they were married, because this was the proper thing to do. And Coth was civil enough, for Coth, and voiced a hope that Florimel might have a good influence upon Jurgen and make him worth his salt, but did not pretend to be optimistic.

===Chinese===

地狱之中自然是滴水皆无的；非但如此，鉴于清水可能被挪作洗礼之用，私运之举受严刑酷法悬禁：眼前这片汪洋，乃是历世历代因恪奉虔敬、光大“和平之君”（Prince of Peace）神国而流淌抛洒的鲜血汇聚而成，号称世间疆域至广之大洋。这恰好印证了朱尔根耳熟能详的那句荒诞俗谚——言道地狱乃是以善意铺就（paved with good intentions）。

“毕竟罗得岛的埃庇革涅斯（Epigenes of Rhodes）终究未曾说错，”朱尔根道，“他指出此处乃是传抄之误：那字眼原本应当是‘浸润’（laved）方对。”

“可不是嘛，陛下，”弗洛里梅尔曲意迎合道，“哎呀，我早说过陛下天纵英明、洞若观火，远非寻常经院学究所能望其项背。”

弗洛里梅尔说话向来怀着这等讨人欢心的柔媚调调。即便如此，凡吸血女妖皆有其天性沉疴，须仰仗情郎的阳刚韶华方得滋养。于是有一日清晨，弗洛里梅尔娇呼周身不适，托辞乃是宿食不化所致。

朱尔根若有所思地摩挲着她的螓首；随即解开闪烁夺目的锦衫，露出了胸前那处清晰可辨的伤痕。

“我固然血气方刚、韶华正盛，”朱尔根说，“然而我的刚健与青春却自有一番异样路数，断非凡品滋补之物。因而你且收起这套把戏，免得当真染上沉疴，彻底搅黄了你这桩消夏休假。”

“可我原以为但凡皇帝皆是凡夫俗子肉身呢！”弗洛里梅尔羞惭改容，满面绯红，神态娇憨煞是动人。

“纵然如此，心肝儿，天下凡百皇帝却非人人皆是朱尔根，”他豪气干云地答道，“故而你当懂得，并非每位皇帝皆配得享天下之父的尊荣，亦非人人皆有秉赋执掌努马利亚（Noumaria）之权杖。想来这番训诫足够你受用了。”

“足够了，”弗洛里梅尔苦着脸娇嗔道。

自此二人再未生出此类衅端，朱尔根胸口的那处创伤亦迅疾愈合如初。

鉴于朱尔根与弗洛里梅尔度日规矩体面，他自然同那些受罚亡魂绝少往还。不过新婚燕尔未几，他们便双双前去探望了朱尔根的生父，盖因这终归是人伦常礼。以科斯（Coth）一贯的脾性而言，他待二人尚算客气周全，且吐露心愿，巴望弗洛里梅尔能给朱尔根施加些良善熏陶、教他不至沦为废物，却并未强装乐观。

===Original===

Yet this visit was never returned, because Coth considered his wickedness was too great for him to be spared a moment of torment, and so would not leave his flame.
“And really, your majesty,” said Florimel, “I do not wish for an instant to have the appearance of criticizing your majesty’s relatives. But I do think that your majesty’s father might have called upon us, at least once, particularly after I offered to have a fire made up for him to sit on any time he chose to come. I consider that your majesty’s father assumes somewhat extravagant airs, in the lack of any definite proof as to his having been a bit more wicked than anybody else: and the childlike candor which has always been with me a leading characteristic prevents concealment of my opinion.”
“Oh, it is just his conscience, dear.”
“A conscience is all very well in its place, your majesty; and I, for one, would never have been able to endure the interminable labor of seducing and assassinating so many fine young fellows if my conscience had not assured me that it was all the fault of my sister-in-law. But, even so, there is no sense in letting your conscience make a slave of you: and when conscience reduces your majesty’s father to ignoring the rules of common civility and behaving like a candlewick, I am sure that matters are being carried too far.”
“And right you are, my dear. However, we do not lack for company. So come now, make yourself fine, and shake the black dog from your back, for we are spending the evening with the Asmodeuses.”
“And will your majesty talk politics again?”
“Oh, I suppose so. They appear to like it.”
“I only wish that I did, your majesty,” observed Florimel, and she yawned by anticipation.
For with the devils Jurgen got on garrulously. The religion of Hell is patriotism, and the government is an enlightened democracy. This contented the devils, and Jurgen had learned long ago never to fall out with either of these codes, without which, as the devils were fond of observing, Hell would not be what it is.
They were, to Jurgen’s finding, simple-minded fiends who allowed themselves to be deplorably overworked by the importunate dead. They got no rest because of the damned, who were such persons as had been saddled with a conscience, and who in consequence demanded interminable torments.

===Chinese===

然而这番回访却终未成行，只因科斯深信自身作恶多端，罪孽深重到容不得少顷刑戮减免，故而执意不肯挪出那团烈焰半步。

“说当真话，陛下，”弗洛里梅尔道，“我丝毫没有在背后编排陛下尊亲的意思。可我确实觉得，陛下的尊大人好歹也该登门回拜一遭，尤其是早先我还许诺过，只要他得空移驾，随他挑拣何时，我都备下柴火供他安坐。依我之见，令尊大人委实有些自视过高了，毕竟至今也拿不出确凿真凭实据证明他比旁人当真更坏上几分：而我生来孩童般的率真秉性，教我实在藏不住心底话。”

“哎，心肝儿，那不过是他的良知在作祟罢了。”

“良知各安其位固然再好不过，陛下；若换作我，倘若不是良知向我打包票说这全是我小姑子的过错，我一人又何尝捱得住经年累月勾引谋害那许多俊美后生的苦工？即便如此，任凭良知将人作践成牛马奴仆亦毫无理路可言：而当良知竟教令尊大人全然不顾寻常礼数、活像一截蜡烛芯般僵硬死板，我敢说事情委实走得太过了。”

“你这话切中要害，我的好娘子。好在我们倒也不愁缺了伴儿。且快快梳妆打扮，抖擞起精神来，今夜咱们要去阿斯莫德（Asmodeus）家作客畅饮呢。”

“陛下莫非又要高谈阔论起政见大事了？”

“嗨，多半免不了。他们似乎偏好听这个。”

“我但愿自己也能听得进去，陛下，”弗洛里梅尔如是叹道，话未说完已先打了个哈欠。

盖因同这帮恶魔交道，朱尔根向来滔滔不绝。地狱的国教乃是爱国热忱，政体则为开明民主。恶魔们对此心满意足，而朱尔根多年前便已学乖，绝不与这双重信条公开龃龉——恶魔们向来挂在嘴边，若无此二者，地狱便成不得如今的地狱了。

在朱尔根眼中，这群魔鬼心思质朴，任由那些纠缠不休的死魂灵将自身盘剥得精疲力竭。受罚之辈生前皆被一副沉重良知所羁绊，死后遂无休无止索求无尽苦刑，搅得恶魔们片刻不得安生。

===Original===

And at the time of Jurgen’s coming into Hell political affairs were in a very bad way, because there was a considerable party among the younger devils who were for compounding the age-old war with Heaven, at almost any price, in order to get relief from this unceasing influx of conscientious dead persons in search of torment. For it was well-known that when Satan submitted to be bound in chains there would be no more death: and the annoying immigration would thus be ended. So said the younger devils: and considered Grandfather Satan ought to sacrifice himself for the general welfare.
Then too they pointed out that Satan had been perforce their presiding magistrate ever since the settlement of Hell, because a change of administration is inexpedient in wartime: so that Satan must term after term be reelected: and of course Satan had been voted absolute power in everything, since this too is customary in wartime. Well, and after the first few thousand years of this the younger devils began to whisper that such government was not ideal democracy.
But their more conservative elders were enraged by these effete and wild new notions, and dealt with their juniors somewhat severely, tearing them into bits and quite destroying them. The elder devils then proceeded to inflict even more startling punishments.
---
So Grandfather Satan was much vexed, because the laws were being violated everywhere: and a day or two after Jurgen’s advent Satan issued a public appeal to his subjects, that the code of Hell should be better respected. But under a democratic government people do not like to be perpetually bothering about law and order, as one of the older and stronger devils pointed out to Jurgen.
Jurgen drew a serious face, and he stroked his chin. “Why, but look you,” says Jurgen, “in deploring the mob spirit that has been manifesting itself sporadically throughout this country against the advocates of peace and submission to the commands of Heaven and other pro-Celestial propaganda﻿—and in warning loyal citizenship that such outbursts must be guarded against, as hurtful to the public welfare of Hell﻿—why, Grandfather Satan should bear in mind that the government, in large measure, holds the remedy of the evil in its own hands.” And Jurgen looked very severely toward Satan.

===Chinese===

而在朱尔根步入地狱之时，此间的政局恰陷于一片泥淖之中：少壮派恶魔中崛起了一股颇具声势的党羽，主张不惜一切代价同天堂缔结和约、息事宁人，好从源源不断涌入索求酷刑的有愧死魂灵大潮中解脱喘息。盖因人尽皆知，一旦撒旦甘愿身受锁链捆缚，天地间便再无死亡；那令人厌烦的移民狂潮便可就此画上句号。少壮派恶魔如是宣称，并认定撒旦祖父（Grandfather Satan）理当为大局做出牺牲。

再者，他们极力陈辞，言道自地狱草创拓荒伊始，撒旦便身兼最高执政法官一职，只因战时频繁易辙殊为不智：于是撒旦连选连任、永无止境；而在一切事务上亦均赋予撒旦绝对大权，盖因这同样合乎战时成例。且说历经了开初数千年这等岁月，少壮派恶魔私下里便窃窃私语，声称这等统御方式断难称作理想的民主。

然而较为保守的元老长辈们闻此等颓废荒诞之新调，无不大发雷霆，遂对后生晚辈痛下辣手，将他们撕得粉碎、彻底铲除。嗣后元老恶魔们更施加了诸多令人瞠目结舌的酷刑严罚。

---

于是撒旦祖父大感焦头烂额，盖因法度纲纪在各处皆遭践踏：在朱尔根抵达地狱的一两天后，撒旦向麾下臣民发布了公开呼吁，号召全境恪遵地狱律法。然而在一个民主国度治下，黎民百姓向来厌烦没完没了被法治纲纪所拘束，正如一位年长且体魄强健的恶魔向朱尔根指出的那般。

朱尔根面色一凛，神色凝重地摩挲着下颏。“哎，诸位且听我一言，”朱尔根说道，“对于全境各处时有发生的针对主和派、针对归顺天堂谕旨之呼声及其他崇天宣传所爆发的自发暴民浪潮，撒旦祖父一面深表痛惜，一面告诫忠诚顺民须防范此类过激宣泄以免大损地狱公益——在此之际，撒旦祖父当牢记在心，平息此类祸患的灵丹妙药，在很大程度上本就操持在官府自己手中。”说罢，朱尔根神色凌厉地凝视着撒旦。

===Original===

“Come now,” says Phlegeton, nodding his head, which was like that of a bear, except for his naked long, red ears, inside each of which was a flame like that of a spirit-lamp: “come now, but this young emperor in the fine shirt speaks uncommonly well!”
“So we spoke together in Pandemonium,” said Belial, wistfully, “in the brave days when Pandemonium was newly built and we were all imps together.”
“Yes, his talk is of the old school, than which there is none better. So pray continue, Emperor Jurgen,” cried the elderly devils, “and let us know what you are talking about.”
“Why, merely this,” says Jurgen, and again he looked severely toward Satan: “I tell you that as long as sentimental weakness marks the prosecution of offences in violation of the laws necessitated by wartime conditions; as long as deserved punishment for overt acts of pro-Celestialism is withheld; as long as weak-kneed clemency condones even a suspicion of disloyal thinking: then just so long will a righteously incensed, if now and then misguided patriotism take into its own hands vengeance upon the offenders.”
“But, still﻿—” said Grandfather Satan.
“Ineffectual administration of the law,” continued Jurgen, sternly, “is the true defence of these outbursts: and far more justly deplorable than acts of mob violence is the policy of condonation that furnishes occasion for them. The patriotic people of Hell are not in a temper to be trifled with, now that they are at war. Conviction for offenses against the nation should not be behedged about with technicalities devised for overrefined peacetime jurisprudence. Why, there is no one of you, I am sure, but has at his tongue’s tip the immortal words of Livonius as to this very topic: and so I shall not repeat them. But I fancy you will agree with me that what Livonius says is unanswerable.”
So it was that Jurgen went on at a great rate, and looking always very sternly at Grandfather Satan.
“Yes, yes!” said Satan, wriggling uncomfortably, but still not thinking of Jurgen entirely: “yes, all this is excellent oratory, and not for a moment would I decry the authority of Livonius. And your quotation is uncommonly apropos and all that sort of thing. But with what are you charging me?”
“With sentimental weakness,” retorted Jurgen.

===Chinese===

“好哇，这才是地道的老派作风！”年长恶魔们大声喝彩，“听听这词锋，这气度！真是讲得不同凡响！”

“昔日万魔殿（Pandemonium）初建、大伙儿全是一般大小的小魔崽子那段意气风发的岁月里，”贝利亚（Belial）怅然追忆道，“我们在万魔殿堂上也是这般论道的。”

“不错，他的辞锋纯系老派风骨，天下再无出其右者。故而恳请朱尔根皇帝务必接着讲下去，”元老恶魔们叫嚷道，“且教我们听个明白，你究竟意欲何为。”

“嗨，无非是这层道理，”朱尔根说道，目光再度威严地投向撒旦，“我告诉诸位，只要在惩处违反战时法令的罪愆之际尚存妇人之仁的软弱妥协；只要对亲天势力的公然罪行迟迟按捺不加重惩；只要软弱无能的宽宥连怀有不忠嫌疑的念头都予以姑息纵容：那么，满怀义愤、纵然偶有失当的爱国热忱，便终将自行挺身而出，亲自向罪人施以私刑复仇！”

“可是，即便如此——”撒旦祖父开口欲辩。

“官府执法软弱无能，”朱尔根声色俱厉地打断道，“才是此类暴民浪潮最坚实的挡箭牌！比起街头暴民行径，更应受到严厉谴责的，乃是为暴行提供温床的姑息政策！值此交战关头，地狱全体爱国臣民断不是好糊弄的！惩治叛国大罪，绝不可被那些为承平岁月量身裁度的迂腐司法繁文缛节所捆住手脚！想来在座诸位无一不能将利沃尼乌斯（Livonius）关于此一论题的不朽箴言倒背如流：我在此便不再赘述。但我料想诸位必赞同我的看法——利沃尼乌斯的定论乃是无可辩驳的！”

朱尔根便这般言辞滔滔、连珠炮似地讲将下去，始终目光炯炯严词逼视着撒旦祖父。

“不错，不错！”撒旦局促不安地扭动着身躯，然而心底依旧未曾全然转到朱尔根这番话上来：“不错，全是大义凛然的高论，我亦绝无贬损利沃尼乌斯泰斗地位之意。你的援引亦是恰如其分、无可挑剔。可是，你究竟在指控我什么呢？”

“指控你妇人之仁、软弱纵容！”朱尔根反唇相讥道。

===Original===

“Was it not only yesterday one of the younger devils was brought before you, upon the charge that he had said the climate in Heaven was better than the climate here? And you, sir, Hell’s chief magistrate﻿—you it was who actually asked him if he had ever uttered such a disloyal heresy!”
“Now, but what else was I to do?” said Satan, fidgeting, and swishing his great bushy tail so that it rustled against his horns, and still not really turning his mind from that ancient thought.
“You should have remembered, sir, that a devil whose patriotism is impugned is a devil to be punished; and that there is no time to be prying into irrevelant questions of his guilt or innocence. Otherwise, I take it, you will never have any real democracy in Hell.”
Now Jurgen looked very impressive, and the devils were all cheering him.
“And so,” says Jurgen, “your disgusted hearers were wearied by such frivolous interrogatories, and took the fellow out of your hands, and tore him into particularly small bits. Now I warn you, Grandfather Satan, that it is your duty as a democratic magistrate just so to deal with such offenders first of all, and to ask your silly questions afterward. For what does Rudigernus say outright upon this point? and Zantipher Magnus, too? Why, my dear sir, I ask you plainly, where in the entire history of international jurisprudence will you find any more explicit language than these two employ?”
“Now certainly,” says Satan, with his bleak smile, “you cite very respectable authority: and I shall take your reproof in good part. I will endeavor to be more strict in the future. And you must not blame my laxity too severely, Emperor Jurgen, for it is a long while since any man came living into Hell to instruct us how to manage matters in time of war. No doubt, precisely as you say, we do need a little more severity hereabouts, and would gain by adopting more human methods. Rudigernus, now?﻿—yes, Rudigernus is rather unanswerable, and I concede it frankly. So do you come home and have supper with me, Emperor Jurgen, and we will talk over these things.”
Then Jurgen went off arm in arm with Grandfather Satan, and Jurgen’s erudition and sturdy common sense were forevermore established among the older and more solid element in Hell.

===Chinese===

“难道就在昨日，一名年轻恶魔被押解至堂前，指控他妄言天堂的气候胜过此地气候？而你，大人，身为地狱首席大法官——竟亲口盘问他是否当真吐露过此等大逆不道之异端邪说！”

“哎，可我除了这么问还能怎办？”撒旦焦躁地扭捏着，巨硕蓬松的魔尾呼呼甩动，拂过两只峥嵘犄角发出沙沙声，心头却依然未曾彻底自那千古旧念中挣脱。

“你早该牢记在心，大人，一个爱国忠诚遭到质疑的恶魔，本身就理当重惩！哪有闲工夫去深究其究竟有罪还是无辜等无关痛痒的琐事！若非如此，依我之见，地狱永无可能建成货真价实的民主！”

此刻朱尔根辞色威严慑人，台下众恶魔掌声雷动、欢呼雀跃。

“于是乎，”朱尔根说，“大人座下那些义愤填膺的听众再难忍受这般荒唐疲沓的审讯，径直自大人手中夺下那厮，将其碎尸万段。如今我在此警告你，撒旦祖父，身为民主法官，你的神圣天职便该先将此类罪囚碎尸万段，嗣后再去盘问你那些荒唐蠢话！盖因关于此点，鲁迪格努斯（Rudigernus）是如何直截了当阐述的？赞蒂弗大帝（Zantipher Magnus）又是如何宣示的？嗨，我亲爱的大人，我坦率问你一句，通观整部国际法学史，你何处寻得见比此二人更为清晰明了的法理昭示？”

“平心而论，”撒旦泛起一抹惨淡凄冷的笑意，“你援引的权威委实令人肃然起敬；老夫自当虚心领受你的规劝。往后我定当严加整饬。不过，朱尔根皇帝，你亦不可对老夫的疏失过分苛责，因世间生魂踏入地狱教导我们如何战时治国，委实已是旷日持久之前的事了。正如你所言，眼下此方水土确乎需要平添几分铁腕，若能采纳更多凡间治民手段必大有裨益。鲁迪格努斯么？不错，鲁迪格努斯的定论确乎无可辩驳，老夫坦率承认便是。既如此，朱尔根皇帝，不妨随老夫回府共进晚膳，咱们再细细切磋详谈。”

言毕朱尔根便与撒旦祖父携手同行、称兄道弟而去，而朱尔根的渊博学识与刚健常识，亦在地狱老成持重的长者群臣中自此树立了不可动摇的崇高声望。

===Original===

And Satan followed Jurgen’s suggestions, and the threatened rebellion was satisfactorily discouraged, by tearing into very small fragments anybody who grumbled about anything. So that all the subjects of Satan went about smiling broadly all the time at the thought of what might befall them if they seemed dejected. Thus was Hell a happier looking place because of Jurgen’s coming.

===Chinese===

撒旦依从了朱尔根的妙策，凡胆敢对任何琐事微词抱怨之人，尽皆当场碎尸万段，一举彻底掐灭了叛乱的苗头。于是乎，撒旦治下的一切臣民整日价皆咧着大嘴满面堆笑，一念及倘若面露沮丧将有何等下场，笑意便愈发灿烂。因朱尔根的驾临，地狱通境瞧来便当真成了一处其乐融融的人间乐土。

## II Assumption of a Noted Garment / 第二章 披上一件赫赫有名的华服
===Original===

The tale tells that all was dark there, and Jurgen could see no one. But the cave stretched straight forward, and downward, and at the far end was a glow of light. Jurgen went on and on, and so came presently to a centaur: and this surprised him not a little, because Jurgen knew that centaurs were imaginary creatures.
Certainly they were curious to look at: for here was the body of a fine bay horse, and rising from its shoulders, the sunburnt body of a young fellow who regarded Jurgen with grave and not unfriendly eyes. The Centaur was lying beside a fire of cedar and juniper wood: near him was a platter containing a liquid with which he was anointing his hoofs. This stuff, as the Centaur rubbed it in with his fingers, turned the appearance of his hoofs to gold.
“Hail, friend,” says Jurgen, “if you be the work of God.”
“Your protasis is not good Greek,” observed the Centaur, “because in Hellas we did not make such reservations. Besides, it is not so much my origin as my destination which concerns you.”
“Well, friend, and whither are you going?”
“To the garden between dawn and sunrise, Jurgen.”
“Surely, now, but that is a fine name for a garden! and it is a place I would take joy to be seeing.”
“Up upon my back, Jurgen, and I will take you thither,” says the Centaur, and heaved to his feet. Then said the Centaur, when the pawnbroker hesitated: “Because, as you must understand, there is no other way. For this garden does not exist, and never did exist, in what men humorously called real life; so that of course only imaginary creatures such as I can enter it.”
“That sounds very reasonable,” Jurgen estimated: “but as it happens, I am looking for my wife, whom I suspect to have been carried off by a devil, poor fellow!”
And Jurgen began to explain to the Centaur what had befallen.
The Centaur laughed. “It may be for that reason I am here. There is, in any event, only one remedy in this matter. Above all devils﻿—and above all gods, they tell me, but certainly above all centaurs﻿—is the power of Koshchei the Deathless, who made things as they are.”
“It is not always wholesome,” Jurgen submitted, “to speak of Koshchei. It seems especially undesirable in a dark place like this.”
“None the less, I suspect it is to him you must go for justice.”
“I would prefer not doing that,” said Jurgen, with unaffected candor.

===Chinese===

故事讲道，洞内一片漆黑，朱尔根（Jurgen）举目不见一人。然而洞穴径直向前、向下延伸，在极深尽头处隐隐泛着一抹光亮。朱尔根一路前行，未几便撞见了一尊半人马（Centaur）：这教他大吃一惊，因朱尔根深知半人马纯系虚构之生灵。

且说这尊生灵瞧来委实奇异：只见一具毛色光鲜的枣红骏马身躯，自马肩处耸立起一个青年后生的古铜色躯干，正以庄重且不失温和的目光凝视着朱尔根。这尊半人马正斜卧在一堆以雪松与杜松木点燃的篝火旁：身旁置有一只陶盘，盛着某种浆液，他正以此涂抹马蹄。那物事经半人马以指尖细细揉搓渗透，竟令他的马蹄焕发出宛若黄金的璀璨光泽。

“向你致意，朋友，”朱尔根说道，“倘若你乃是上帝造化之功。”

“你的前设不合地道的希腊语风，”半人马答道，“盖因在希腊（Hellas），我们向来不做这等瞻前顾后的保留揣测。况且，比起我的来历，真正同你相干的乃是我的去处。”

“唔，朋友，那你意欲何往？”

“去往黎明与日出之间的园囿（garden between dawn and sunrise），朱尔根。”

“妙哉，这委实是个雅致的名字！能一睹其真容，定是桩赏心乐事。”

“跨上我的背脊吧，朱尔根，我载你前去，”半人马说着，翻身直立而起。见这位当铺掌柜略显迟疑，半人马复言道：“只因你当明白，别无他途可循。盖因在世人戏称作现实生活的那方天地里，这座园囿并不存在，亦从未存在过；故而唯有如我这般的虚构生灵方得入内。”

“这话听来倒颇有理路，”朱尔根掂量着答道，“然而机缘不巧，我眼下正在寻觅拙荆，我疑心她被哪个不开眼的魔鬼给掳了去，真是个可怜的魔鬼！”

于是朱尔根向半人马絮絮细述起原委。

半人马朗声大笑。“这或许正是我守候在此的因由。无论如何，在此事上唯有一剂解药。凌驾于一切魔鬼之上——据传亦凌驾于一切神祇之上，但无论如何绝然凌驾于一切半人马之上的——乃是不死者科谢（Koshchei the Deathless）的神威，世间万物的现存法则皆出其手。”

“妄议科谢，”朱尔根怯怯进言，“向来未必稳妥。尤其在这般黑魆魆的洞穴深处，更觉不是时候。”

“即便如此，我料想你唯有投奔他处，方能讨得回公道。”

“我宁可不惹这麻烦，”朱尔根满怀坦荡的诚意如实吐露道。

===Original===

“You have my sympathy: but there is no question of preference where Koshchei is concerned. Do you think, for example, that I am frowzing in this underground place by my own choice? and knew your name by accident?”
Jurgen was frightened, a little. “Well, well! but it is usually the deuce and all, this doing of the manly thing. How, then, can I come to Koshchei?”
“Roundabout,” says the Centaur. “There is never any other way.”
“And is the road to this garden roundabout?”
“Oh, very much so, inasmuch as it circumvents both destiny and common sense.”
“Needs must, then,” says Jurgen: “at all events, I am willing to taste any drink once.”
“You will be chilled, though, traveling as you are. For you and I are going a queer way, in search of justice, over the grave of a dream and through the malice of time. So you had best put on this shirt above your other clothing.”
“Indeed it is a fine snug shining garment, with curious figures on it. I accept such raiment gladly. And whom shall I be thanking for his kindness, now?”
“My name,” said the Centaur, “is Nessus.”
“Well, then, friend Nessus, I am at your service.”
And in a trice Jurgen was on the Centaur’s back, and the two of them had somehow come out of the cave, and were crossing Amneran Heath. So they passed into a wooded place, where the light of sunset yet lingered, rather unaccountably. Now the Centaur went westward. And now about the pawnbroker’s shoulders and upon his breast and over his lean arms glittered like a rainbow the many-colored shirt of Nessus.
For a while they went through the woods, which were composed of big trees standing a goodish distance from one another, with the Centaur’s gilded hoofs rustling and sinking in a thick carpet of dead leaves, all gray and brown, in level stretches that were unbroken by any undergrowth. And then they came to a white roadway that extended due west, and so were done with the woods. Now happened an incredible thing in which Jurgen would never have believed had he not seen it with his own eyes: for now the Centaur went so fast that he gained a little by a little upon the sun, thus causing it to rise in the west a little by a little; and these two sped westward in the glory of a departed sunset. The sun fell full in Jurgen’s face as he rode straight toward the west, so that he blinked and closed his eyes, and looked first toward this side, then the other.

===Chinese===

“我颇同情于你：但在科谢面前，从来容不得你有何偏好抉择。比方说，你莫非以为我潜居在这阴冷地窟之中乃是出于一己之好？知晓你的名姓亦纯系偶然不成？”

朱尔根心头略微发毛。“好哇，好哇！可担当顶天立地的男子汉，向来总是这般倒霉透顶。既如此，我又怎生寻得着科谢呢？”

“曲径通幽，”半人马答道，“从来别无他途。”

“那去往这处园囿的路途亦是曲折迂回的么？”

“哎，曲折得紧呢，因它既绕过了宿命注定，亦规避了凡俗常理。”

“势在必行，那便走一遭吧，”朱尔根言道，“横竖凡是甘霖，我都乐意浅尝一遭。”

“不过，就你眼下这身行头赶路，怕是要受冻着凉的。盖因你我将踏上一条离奇诡谲的迷途以求索公道，踏过美梦的荒冢，穿梭于光阴的恶毒刁难之间。因而你最好在这身衣袍之外，套上这件华服衬衣。”

“果真是一件紧致贴身、流光溢彩的体面袍服，上面还绣着奇异的花纹。这等锦衣我欣然笑纳。敢问我此刻当向哪位尊驾道谢这份盛情？”

“我的名号，”半人马言道，“唤作涅索斯（Nessus）。”

“既如此，涅索斯老友，在下愿听凭差遣。”

眨眼之间，朱尔根已然跃上那半人马的马背，二人不知怎的便奔出了洞穴，径直穿行在阿姆奈兰荒原（Amneran Heath）之上。随后他们没入了一片林木蓊郁之境，落日余晖莫名地久久流连其间。此刻半人马一路向西奔驰。而此刻在那当铺掌柜的肩头、胸膛与精瘦双臂之上，涅索斯的五彩华服（shirt of Nessus）宛若雨后长虹般熠熠生辉。

一时之间二人穿梭林海，林中古木参天，彼此错落有致，半人马那涂金的双蹄踏在平坦无垠、全无灌木荆棘遮挡的灰褐色厚实落叶地毯之上，沙沙作响，陷落又拔起。随即他们步入了一条笔直向西延伸的素白大道，自此别过了苍茫林木。此刻发生了一桩匪夷所思的奇事，若非朱尔根亲眼所见，无论如何断难置信：只见半人马疾驰如电，速度竟一点一滴追赶上了太阳，从而使得落日竟然在西方一点一滴逆向升腾而起；二人便迎着那已然逝去的落日万道霞光，向着西方飞驰电掣。当朱尔根向西纵横飞驰之际，夕阳的金辉正正照在他面庞之上，直晃得他目眩神迷，连连眨眼闭目，顾盼左右。

===Original===

Thus it was that the country about him, and the persons they were passing, were seen by him in quick bright flashes, like pictures suddenly transmuted into other pictures; and all his memories of this shining highway were, in consequence, always confused and incoherent.
He wondered that there seemed to be so many young women along the road to the garden. Here was a slim girl in white teasing a great brown and yellow dog that leaped about her clumsily; here a girl sat in the branches of a twisted and gnarled tree, and back of her was a broad muddied river, copper-colored in the sun; and here shone the fair head of a tall girl on horseback, who seemed to wait for someone: in fine, the girls along the way were numberless, and Jurgen thought he recollected one or two of them.
But the Centaur went so swiftly that Jurgen could not be sure.

===Chinese===

正是由于这般缘由，周遭的山川形胜以及二人沿途擦肩而过的身影，在他眼帘之中皆化作了倏忽即逝的耀目光斑，宛若一幅画卷突兀幻化为另一幅画卷；因而对于这条泛着流光的大道，他脑海中的记忆自始至终皆显得迷离凌乱、支离破碎。

他暗自诧异，去往园囿的大道沿途竟伫立着如此繁多的年轻女子。这儿立着一位身着素白衣裙的窈窕少女，正逗弄着一只围着她笨拙跳跃的黄褐相间大犬；那边一位姑娘俏坐在虬曲苍劲的古树枝桠间，身后是一条在艳阳下泛着古铜辉光的宽阔浊流；而在另一头，一位端坐马背之上的高挑姑娘金发粲然，似在痴痴等候着谁人的归期：总而言之，道旁少女不计其数，朱尔根恍惚自忖依稀认得其中的一两位。

然而半人马疾驰如离弦之箭，朱尔根终究不敢确信。

## XLVIII Candid Opinions of Dame Lisa / 第四十八章 丽莎夫人的肺腑之言
===Original===

“Eh, sirs!” observes Koshchei the Deathless, “but some of us are certainly hard to please.” And now Jurgen was already intent to shrug off his display of emotion. “In selecting a wife, sir,” submitted Jurgen, “there are all sorts of matters to be considered﻿—”
Then bewilderment smote him. For it occurred to Jurgen that his previous commerce with these three women was patently unknown to Koshchei.
