He had forgotten about Guenevere. And nobody knows what were that night the thoughts of the young Princess, nor if she offered any prayers, in the deserted Hall of Judgment.
In the morning a sprinkling of persons came to early mass. Jurgen attended with fervor, and started doorward with the others. Just before him a merchant stopped to get a pebble from his shoe, and the merchant’s wife went forward to the holy-water font.
“Madame, permit me,” said a handsome young esquire, and offered her holy water.
“At eleven,” said the merchant’s wife, in low tones. “He will be out all day.”
“My dear,” says her husband, as he rejoined her, “and who was the young gentleman?”
“Why, I do not know, darling. I never saw him before.”
“He was certainly very civil. I wish there were more like him. And a fine looking young fellow, too!”
“Was he? I did not notice,” said the merchant’s wife, indifferently.
And Jurgen saw and heard and regarded the departing trio ruefully. It seemed to him incredible the world should be going on just as it went before he ventured into the Druid forest.
He paused before a crucifix, and he knelt and looked up wistfully. “If one could only know,” says Jurgen, “what really happened in Judea! How immensely would matters be simplified, if anyone but knew the truth about You, Man upon the Cross!”
Now the Bishop of Merion passed him, coming from celebration of the early mass. “My Lord Bishop,” says Jurgen, simply, “can you tell me the truth about this Christ?”
“Why, indeed, Messire de Logreus,” replied the Bishop, “one cannot but sympathize with Pilate in thinking that the truth about Him is very hard to get at, even nowadays. Was He Melchisedek, or Shem, or Adam? or was He verily the Logos? and in that event, what sort of a something was the Logos?

===Chinese===

朱尔根（Jurgen）战战兢兢地走向卡梅利亚德（Cameliard）的圣荆棘大教堂。整整一夜，朱尔根长跪在教堂深处祈祷，并非出于痛改前非的悔悟，而是出于透骨的惊恐。他为他死去的亲族祈祷，祈求他们不至于在虚无中被彻底抹杀磨灭——他唯独为少年时代曾深爱过的那些故去骨肉祈祷，且仅仅为了他们。至于自那之后结识的芸芸男女，他似乎全然漠不关心，至少未曾这般刻骨铭心。不过，他倒也顺道替丽莎夫人（Dame Lisa）献上了一通含混的祷告——“无论我亲爱的浑家身在何方，噢主啊，祈求宽宥恩准我终有与她重逢之日，并蒙她宽恕罢！”他凄然哀号道，心头却暗自嘀咕自己是否当真出于由衷心意。

他早已将吉尼薇尔（Guenevere）抛到了九霄云外。在空寂无人的正义大殿深处，那一夜年轻的公主心头所思何事、是否曾向天祈祷，无人知晓。

清晨时分，三三两两的信众前来赶早弥撒。朱尔根虔敬万分地参与其中，随后随众人一同向殿门踱去。恰在他身前，一位行商停下步履，俯身从履底抠出一粒小石子，商人妇则径自走向盛着圣水的圣水钵。

“夫人，请容在下效劳，”一位仪表堂堂的青年侍从献媚道，恭敬地为她捧上圣水。

“11点（十一点整），”商人妇压低嗓音低语道，“他整日不在府上。”

“贤妻，”商人重折返回妻子身畔，问道，“方才那位年轻君子是哪一位？”

“哎，我哪里知晓呀，夫君。我平生何曾见过他。”

“倒委实是个知书达礼的体面人。世间若能多几位这等后生才好呢。况且生得亦是一表人才！”

“是么？我倒未曾留神，”商人妇漫不经心地答道。

朱尔根冷眼旁观，耳闻目睹这三人离去的背影，心头泛起阵阵苦涩。在他看来，这尘世竟能如他在涉足德鲁伊森林之前那般若无其事地照常流转运转，委实教人难以置信。

他在一尊受难十字架前驻足，屈膝下跪，神色怅惘地仰首凝望。“倘若世人真能知晓，”朱尔根喃喃自语，“犹太旷野里当年究竟发生了何事！倘若世间有人能洞悉关于您的真谛，十字架上的受难之躯啊，世间纷扰该会变得何等澄澈纯粹！”

此时，梅里翁主教（Bishop of Merion）主持完清晨弥撒恰好从他身旁踱过。“主教大人，”朱尔根直截了当地请教道，“尊驾能否指点在下，关于这位基督的真理究竟为何？”

“哎，爵爷（Messire de Logreus），”主教答道，“世人难免同情彼拉多（Pilate）当年的感叹，即便在今日，关于祂的真理委实亦是极难洞悉参透的。祂究竟是麦基洗德（Melchisedek）、是闪（Shem），抑或是亚当？还是说祂真乃无上‘道’（Logos）的化身？若果真如此，那‘道’究竟又是何等存在？

===Original===

Granted He was a god, were the Arians or the Sabellians in the right? had He existed always, co-substantial with the Father and the Holy Spirit, or was He a creation of the Father, a kind of Israelitic Zagreus? Was He the husband of Acharamoth, that degraded Sophia, as the Valentinians aver? or the son of Pantherus, as say the Jews? or Kalakau, as contends Basilidês? or was it, as the Docetês taught, only a tinted cloud in the shape of a man that went from Jordan to Golgotha? Or were the Merinthians right? These are a few of the questions, Messire de Logreus, which naturally arise. And not all of them are to be settled out of hand.”
Thus speaking, the gallant prelate bowed, then raised three fingers in benediction, and so quitted Jurgen, who was still kneeling before the crucifix.
“Ah, ah!” says Jurgen, to himself, “but what a variety of interesting problems are, in point of fact, suggested by religion. And what delectable exercise would the settling of these problems, once for all, afford the mind of a monstrous clever fellow! Come now, it might be well for me to enter the priesthood. It may be that I have a call.”
But people were shouting in the street. So Jurgen rose and dusted his knees. And as Jurgen came out of the Cathedral of the Sacred Thorn the cavalcade was passing that bore away Dame Guenevere to the arms and throne of her appointed husband. Jurgen stood upon the Cathedral porch, his mind in part preoccupied by theology, but still not failing to observe how beautiful was this young princess, as she rode by on her white palfrey, green-garbed and crowned and aglitter with jewels. She was smiling as she passed him, bowing her small tenderly-colored young countenance this way and that way, to the shouting people, and not seeing Jurgen at all.
Thus she went to her bridal, that Guenevere who was the symbol of all beauty and purity to the chivalrous people of Glathion. The mob worshipped her; and they spoke as though it were an angel who passed.
“Our beautiful young Princess!”
“Ah, there is none like her anywhere!”
“And never a harsh word for anyone, they say﻿—!”
“Oh, but she is the most admirable of ladies﻿—!”
“And so brave too, that lovely smiling child who is leaving her home forever!”
“And so very, very pretty!”
“﻿—So generous!”
“King Arthur will be hard put to it to deserve her!”

===Chinese===

即便公认祂确为神明，究竟是阿里乌派（Arians）言之有理，还是撒伯流派（Sabellians）持论公允？祂究竟是亘古永存、与圣父及圣灵同质同体，抑或仅仅是圣父的一记造物，某种犹太风味的萨格柔斯（Zagreus）？正如瓦伦廷派（Valentinians）断言的那样，祂究竟是阿查拉莫斯（Acharamoth）——亦即堕落索菲亚——的夫君？还是正如犹太人所言，乃是潘特拉（Pantherus）的孽子？或是巴西利德派（Basilidês）坚称的卡拉考（Kalakau）？抑或是如幻影派（Docetês）宣讲的那样，不过是一团自约旦河漂泊至髑髅地的具人形的七彩轻云？还是墨林托斯派（Merinthians）所言不虚？凡此种种，洛格鲁斯爵爷，皆是自然而然涌现的疑窦。而凡百谜题，皆非能一蹴而就决断停当的。”

这位风度翩翩的显赫教长言毕微微欠身，继而竖起3根手指施以祝福，随后撇下依旧跪在十字架前的朱尔根，径自离去。

“啊哈，妙极！”朱尔根暗自思忖，“宗教一事所牵扯出的有趣悬案，委实琳琅满目。而将这凡百悬案毕其功于一役彻底厘清，对一个绝顶聪颖的神妙奇才的头脑而言，该是何等赏心悦目的绝妙操练！得了吧，我遁入空门受戒为僧倒不失为一桩美事。说不定我骨子里真承受了某种圣职天召呢。”

然而长街之上突然人声鼎沸、欢声雷动。朱尔根遂站起身来，拍了拍膝头的尘土。当朱尔根跨出圣荆棘大教堂的大门之际，护送吉尼薇尔夫人前往钦定夫君怀抱与王座的浩荡仪仗车队正辚辚驶过。朱尔根伫立在教堂门廊前，心神虽半沉浸于神学幽思之中，却依然不曾遗漏这位年轻公主的绝世风华：只见她骑在一匹素白牝马上，身着一袭翠绿华服，头戴璀璨王冠，周身宝光流转。当车仗自他身前掠过之际，她正笑靥如花，将娇小柔嫩、宛若凝脂的面庞向左向右频频顾盼颔首，回应着夹道欢呼的芸芸众生，浑然未曾瞧见朱尔根的身影。

她就这般踏上了迎亲花路，这位在恪守骑士信条的格拉西昂臣民心中化身为纯洁与至美象征的吉尼薇尔。如潮的黎民顶礼膜拜；众人交口称颂，仿佛方才掠过眼前的是降临凡尘的天使。

“我们美艳绝伦的年轻公主！”

“啊，放眼寰宇，再无何人能同她平分秋色！”

“且传闻她对天下任何人从未吐露过半句厉色！”

“噢，她当真是世间最无可挑剔的尊贵淑女——！”

“且是何等坚毅英勇啊，那娇笑如花的纯真孩子，竟要为了天下社稷永远挥别故土！”

“生得是这般、这般标致俏丽！”

“——心地又是何等菩萨般宽仁！”

“亚瑟王（King Arthur）若想配得上她，怕是要大费周章啦！”

===Original===

Said Jurgen: “Now it is droll that to these truths I have but to add another truth in order to have large paving-stones flung at her! and to have myself tumultuously torn into fragments, by those unpleasantly sweaty persons who, thank Heaven, are no longer jostling me!”
For the Cathedral porch had suddenly emptied, because as the procession passed heralds were scattering silver among the spectators.
“Arthur will have a very lovely queen,” says a soft lazy voice.
And Jurgen turned and saw that beside him was Dame Anaïtis, whom people called the Lady of the Lake.
“Yes, he is greatly to be envied,” says Jurgen, politely. “But do you not ride with them to London?”
“Why, no,” says the Lady of the Lake, “because my part in this bridal was done when I mixed the stirrup-cup of which the Princess and young Lancelot drank this morning. He is the son of King Ban of Benwick, that tall young fellow in blue armor. I am partial to Lancelot, for I reared him, at the bottom of a lake that belongs to me, and I consider he does me credit. I also believe that Madame Guenevere by this time agrees with me. And so, my part being done to serve my creator, I am off for Cocaigne.”
“And what is this Cocaigne?”
“It is an island wherein I rule.”
“I did not know you were a queen, madame.”
“Why, indeed there are a many things unknown to you, Messire de Logreus, in a world where nobody gets any assuredness of knowledge about anything. For it is a world wherein all men that live have but a little while to live, and none knows his fate thereafter. So that a man possesses nothing certainly save a brief loan of his own body: and yet the body of man is capable of much curious pleasure.”
“I believe,” said Jurgen, as his thoughts shuddered away from what he had seen and heard in the Druid forest, “that you speak wisdom.”
“Then in Cocaigne we are all wise: for that is our religion. But of what are you thinking, Duke of Logreus?”
“I was thinking,” says Jurgen, “that your eyes are unlike the eyes of any other woman that I have ever seen.”
Smilingly the dark woman asked him wherein they differed, and smilingly he said he did not know. They were looking at each other warily. In each glance an experienced gamester acknowledged a worthy opponent.
“Why, then you must come with me into Cocaigne,” says Anaïtis, “and see if you cannot discover wherein lies that difference.

===Chinese===

朱尔根暗忖道：“滑稽至极的是，面对这凡百颂词，我只需再抛出一桩铁证如山的真相，便足以引得漫天沉重铺路石朝她劈头盖脸砸去！并教我自己被眼前这帮浑身散发着恶臭汗酸气的乌合之众当场撕成肉泥——谢天谢地，这帮莽汉眼下总算不再往我身上挤搡推搡了！”

盖因车仗队伍驶过之际，传令官正沿街向围观者大把抛撒白银碎币，教堂门廊前的人潮刹那间被席卷一空。

“亚瑟王即将坐拥一位娇媚绝伦的王后呢，”一道慵懒轻柔的温软嗓音自耳畔悠悠响起。

朱尔根蓦然回首，赫然瞧见伫立身侧的正是阿娜伊蒂斯夫人（Dame Anaïtis）——亦即世人尊奉为湖中仙女（Lady of the Lake）的那位佳人。

“千真万确，他的艳福委实令人艳羡，”朱尔根彬彬有礼地应和道。“可夫人为何不随车仗仪仗一同策马奔赴伦敦？”

“哎，何须多此一举，”湖中仙女答道，“只因今日清晨，当公主同年轻的兰斯洛特（Lancelot）共饮下我亲手调配的那樽饯行酒时，我在这桩大婚中的使命便已告大功告成。他是班威克的班王（King Ban of Benwick）的公子，正是那位身披湛蓝重铠的英武高挑少年。我向来偏疼兰斯洛特，因是我在自家的深湖之底一手将他抚养成人，且我自忖他委实未曾辱没我的调教。我亦笃信，吉尼薇尔夫人此时此刻必定对我的眼光心悦诚服。故而我既已克尽厥职服侍了我的造物主，自当启程折返科凯恩（Cocaigne）。”

“那科凯恩究竟是何等所在？”

“乃是一座由我全权统辖的世外仙岛。”

“在下此前竟不知夫人原是一国女王。”

“哎，在这么一个任谁对天下事皆无从洞悉确凿真知的荒唐尘世里，洛格鲁斯爵爷（Messire de Logreus），你所不知晓的隐秘之事委实多如牛毛。盖因在这茫茫人世间，凡肉身凡胎之辈苟活于世不过须臾片刻，而死后宿命究竟归于何方却无一人知晓。因而一个凡夫确凿无疑所能拥有的，无非是对自家肉身的一场短暂暂借；然而人类的血肉之躯，却偏偏能领略凡百不可名状的神妙欢愉。”

“在下深信，”朱尔根思及自己在德鲁伊森林深处的所见所闻，周身不由得泛起一阵寒噤，“夫人此言字字珠玑、深合至理。”

“如此说来，在科凯恩境内我们人人皆是智者：因那便是我们的无上信仰。可你此刻究竟在沉吟何事，洛格鲁斯公爵？”

“在下适才在想，”朱尔根说道，“夫人的那一双秋水明眸，同在下平生所见过的任何女子的眸子皆截然不同。”

黑肤美人嫣然一笑，垂询究竟有何殊异；朱尔根亦相视一笑，推说自己一时参详不透。二人警惕机敏地互相对视打量。在彼此流转的眼波之中，两位情场老手心照不宣地认出了棋逢对手的强劲敌手。

“既然这般，你何不随我一同去往科凯恩，”阿娜伊蒂斯言道，“好教你亲自参透那番差别究竟何在。

===Original===

For it is not a matter I would care to leave unsettled.”
“Well, that seems only just to you,” says Jurgen. “Yes, certainly I must deal fairly with you.”
Then they left the Cathedral of the Sacred Thorn, walking together. The folk who went toward London were now well out of sight and hearing, which possibly accounts for the fact that Jurgen was now in no wise thinking of Guenevere. So it was that Guenevere rode out of Jurgen’s life for a while: and as she rode she talked with Lancelot.

===Chinese===

只因关于此等事体，我向来不喜半途而废、留下一桩悬而未决的无头悬案。”

“哎，这待夫人而言方称得上合情合理，”朱尔根答道。“千真万确，在下理当公允体面地对待夫人。”

言毕，二人结伴迈步离开圣荆棘大教堂。去往伦敦的滚滚人流此时早已行远，人喧马嘶尽皆湮没在远方，这大抵足以解释何以此刻朱尔根的脑海中对吉尼薇尔毫无半分挂碍。吉尼薇尔就这般暂时策马走出了朱尔根的生命轨迹：她一边策马徐行，一边同身畔的兰斯洛特款款倾谈。

## XII Excursus of Yolande’s Undoing / 第十二章 约兰德沦落之附记
===Original===

Now Jurgen, self-appointed Duke of Logreus, abode at the court of King Gogyrvan. The month of May passed quickly and pleasantly: but the monstrous shadow which followed Jurgen did not pass. Still, no one noticed it: that was the main thing. For himself, he was not afraid of shadows, and the queerness of this one was not enough to distract his thoughts from Guenevere, nor from his lovemaking with Guenevere.
For these were quiet times in Glathion, now that the war with Rience of Northgalis was satisfactorily ended: and lovemaking was now everywhere in vogue. By way of diversion, gentlemen hunted and fished and rode a-hawking and amicably slashed and battered one another in tournaments: but their really serious pursuit was lovemaking, after the manner of chivalrous persons, who knew that the King’s trumpets would presently be summoning them into less softly furnished fields of action, from one or another of which they would return feet foremost on a bier. So Jurgen sighed and warbled and made eyes with many excellent fighting-men: and the Princess listened with many other ladies whose hearts were not of flint. And Gogyrvan meditated.
Now it was the kingly custom of Gogyrvan when his dinner was spread at noontide, not to go to meat until all such as demanded justice from him had been furnished with a champion to redress the wrong. One day as the gaunt old King sat thus in his main hall, upon a seat of green rushes covered with yellow satin, and with a cushion of yellow satin under his elbow, and with his barons ranged about him according to their degrees, a damsel came with a very heartrending tale of the oppression that was on her.
Gogyrvan blinked at her, and nodded. “You are the handsomest woman I have seen in a long while,” says he, irrelevantly. “You are a woman I have waited for. Duke Jurgen of Logreus will undertake this adventure.”
There being no help for it, Jurgen rode off with this Dame Yolande, not very well pleased: but as they rode he jested with her. And so, with much laughter by the way, Yolande conducted him to the Green Castle, of which she had been dispossessed by Graemagog, a most formidable giant.
“Now prepare to meet your death, sir knight!” cried Graemagog, laughing horribly, and brandishing his club; “for all knights who come hither I have sworn to slay.”

===Chinese===

话说自封为洛格鲁斯公爵（Duke of Logreus）的朱尔根（Jurgen），在戈吉尔万国王（King Gogyrvan）的宫廷里住了下来。时值5月（五月），光阴流逝得轻快惬意；唯有紧紧追随朱尔根的那道庞大阴影却始终未曾消散。所幸的是，周遭无一人曾留意到它的存在：这才是至关紧要之处。至于朱尔根自家嘛，他平生向来不惧怕幽影，且这道阴影的古怪离奇，尚不足以分散他对吉尼薇尔（Guenevere）的无尽挂念，更无从阻遏他同吉尼薇尔之间的卿卿我我、如胶似漆。

盖因彼时的格拉西昂（Glathion）境内正值海晏河清的升平日月，同北加利斯的里恩斯（Rience of Northgalis）的战事已然圆满敉平：寻欢作乐、调情求爱之风一时在四方风靡盛行。诸位达官贵胄借由行猎、捕鱼、放鹰狩猎以消遣闲愁，并在骑士比武场上亲睦友善地彼此挥剑劈砍、敲敲打打；然而他们真正念兹在兹的正经行当，却是依循骑士道风范去调情偷欢——因他们心知肚明，国王的召集号角迟早会将他们重新征召入那片绝非这般锦衣玉食的杀伐疆场，而他们终归要从某处沙场上横尸于灵柩之上由人抬回。于是朱尔根同众多身手不凡的虎将猛士一道，长吁短叹、宛转清歌、频送秋波；而公主则同许多心肠绝非铁石铸就的深闺贵妇一道，侧耳倾听。戈吉尔万则冷眼旁观，暗自沉吟。

且说戈吉尔万恪守着一国之君的皇家旧俗：每逢正午盛筵铺排开来之际，凡未曾向每一位御前鸣冤求伸正义者指派勇士去雪耻雪冤之前，国王断不动筷进膳。一日，这位清瘦枯槁的老国王正端坐于大殿之上，身下垫着铺设明黄缎面的翠绿灯心草蒲团，肘下倚着明黄缎面的软枕，群臣诸爵依着尊卑爵位雁翅排开；恰在此刻，一位妙龄少女款步上殿，如泣如诉地控诉起自己所蒙受的惨绝人寰的沉冤迫害。

戈吉尔万眨巴着老眼打量着她，微微颔首。“你乃是老夫长久以来所见过的最标致的尤物，”他前言不搭后语地赞叹道，“老夫苦苦盼望的正是你这般绝色。洛格鲁斯公爵朱尔根当领命承办此番除暴探险。”

事已至此无可转圜，朱尔根只得同这位约兰德夫人（Dame Yolande）一同策马启程，心下虽难免有些不悦；然而二人并辔徐行之际，他却不住向她打情骂俏、逗弄取乐。一路欢声笑语，约兰德便引着他来到了绿堡（Green Castle）——这处基业此前正是遭凶悍无比的巨人格雷马戈格（Graemagog）强行霸占夺走的。

“受死吧，骑士老爷！”格雷马戈格发出令人毛骨悚然的长笑，凶神恶煞般挥舞着巨棒咆哮道；“凡涉足此地的骑士，老子曾立誓定斩不饶！”

===Original===

“Well, if truth-telling were a sin you would be a very virtuous giant,” says Jurgen, and he flourished Thragnar’s sword, resistless Caliburn.
Then they fought, and Jurgen killed Graemagog. Thus was the Green Castle restored to Dame Yolande, and the maidens who attended her aforetime were duly released from the cellarage. They were now maidens by courtesy only, but so tender is the heart of women that they all wept over Graemagog.
Yolande was very grateful, and proffered every manner of reward.
“But, no, I will take none of these fine jewels, nor money, nor lands either,” says Jurgen. “For Logreus, I must tell you, is a fairly well-to-do duchy, and the killing of giants is by way of being my favorite pastime. He is well paid that is well satisfied. Yet if you must reward me for such a little service, do you swear to do what you can to get me the love of my lady, and that will suffice.”
Yolande, without any particular enthusiasm, consented to attempt this: and indeed Yolande, at Jurgen’s request, made oath upon the Four Evangelists that she would do everything within her power to aid him.
“Very well,” said Jurgen, “you have sworn, and it is you whom I love.”
Surprise now made her lovely. Yolande was frankly delighted at the thought of marrying the young Duke of Logreus, and offered to send for a priest at once.
“My dear,” says Jurgen, “there is no need to bother a priest about our private affairs.”
She took his meaning, and sighed. “Now I regret,” said she, “that I made so solemn an oath. Your trick was unfair.”
“Oh, not at all,” said Jurgen: “and presently you will not regret it. For indeed the game is well worth the candle.”
“How is that shown, Messire de Logreus?”
“Why, by candlelight,” says Jurgen﻿—“naturally.”
“In that event, we will talk no further of it until this evening.”
So that evening Yolande sent for him. She was, as Gogyrvan had said, a remarkably handsome woman, sleek and sumptuous and crowned with a wealth of copper-colored hair. Tonight she was at her best in a tunic of shimmering blue, with a surcote of gold embroidery, and with gold embroidered pendent sleeves that touched the floor. Thus she was when Jurgen came to her.
“Now,” says Yolande, frowning, “you may as well come out straightforwardly with what you were hinting at this morning.”

===Chinese===

“哎，倘若实话实说算得上一桩罪孽，你倒真算得上个德行无可挑剔的正人君子巨人了，”朱尔根说着，刷的一声拔出斯拉格纳（Thragnar）那柄势不可挡的宝剑王者之剑（Caliburn）。

二人随即短兵相接厮杀在一处，朱尔根剑起刀落干净利落地结果了格雷马戈格。绿堡就这般物归原主、重新奉还给约兰德夫人，昔日随侍她身侧的深闺侍女们亦顺理成章地自地牢深处被解救脱困。因着受过非人屈辱，她们如今大抵唯有在虚礼名分上勉强算得冰清玉洁的少女，然而妇道人家的心肠向来这般娇柔善感，众人竟齐刷刷围着气绝身亡的格雷马戈格痛哭流涕起来。

约兰德千恩万谢，倾其所有献上凡百珍宝以作酬谢。

“不过在下可断然不收这些罕世美玉，更不论金银铜钱或封邑疆土，”朱尔根推辞道。“只因在下不得不通禀一句，洛格鲁斯公国乃是个颇为富庶肥美的锦绣领地，且斩妖除巨怪不过是在下平生最钟爱的消遣闲趣罢了。知足常乐者自得无上厚酬。然而倘若夫人执意要为这等举手之劳打赏在下，尊驾只需起誓竭尽所能助在下赢得我意中佳人的芳心便足矣。”

约兰德虽无多少非比寻常的狂热兴致，倒也欣然应允一试；且在朱尔根的恳请之下，约兰德果真按着手心对着四位福音使徒（Four Evangelists）郑重起誓，誓言必定竭尽一己之力相助功成。

“甚好，”朱尔根嘴角泛起笑意，“夫人既已对天起誓，那么在下平生所苦恋者，恰恰正是夫人尊驾。”

突如其来的错愕与娇羞令她愈发艳光四射。一念及即将下嫁这位年轻英挺的洛格鲁斯公爵，约兰德由衷喜不自胜，立时便张罗着去请神父前来主持大婚。

“我的好人儿，”朱尔根温存地劝阻道，“何苦拿我们男女私密间的体己细务去搅扰圣洁的神父呢。”

她立时参透了他的弦外之音，长叹一声。“早知如此，”她怨嗔道，“我真后悔立下这等庄严毒誓。你这道圈套委实太有失公允。”

“哎，万万不能这般讲，”朱尔根巧舌如簧：“少顷你便断不会再怀半点悔意。盖因这场风月博弈，委实值得上几支彻夜烛火。”

“何以见得呢，洛格鲁斯爵爷？”

“哎，在蜡烛光底下自见分晓，”朱尔根调笑道——“理所当然嘛。”

“既然如此，今夜之前我们休要再多费唇舌。”

当晚，约兰德遣人传唤朱尔根入闺。正如戈吉尔万昔日所赞叹的那样，她确是个绝色尤物：肌肤胜雪，体态丰腴华贵，满头如瀑红铜色青丝璀璨夺目。今夜她更是盛装打扮、风华绝代：身披一袭微光流转的湛蓝长袍，外罩金丝绣纹罩衫，垂地金绣水袖轻拂石阶。朱尔根踏入香闺之际，映入眼帘的便是这般一番绝色春光。

“眼下嘛，”约兰德微微蹙起黛眉，娇嗔道，“你大可开门见山，老老实实将今晨吞吞吐吐的勾当吐露清楚了。”

===Original===

But first Jurgen looked about the apartment, and it was lighted by a tall gilt stand whereon burned candles.
He counted these, and he whistled. “Seven candles! upon my word, sweetheart, you do me great honor, for this is a veritable illumination. To think of it, now, that you should honor me, as people do saints, with seven candles! Well, I am only mortal, but none the less I am Jurgen, and I shall endeavor to repay this sevenfold courtesy without discount.”
“Oh, Messire de Logreus,” cried Dame Yolande, “but what incomprehensible nonsense you talk! You misinterpret matters, for I can assure you I had nothing of that sort in mind. Besides, I do not know what you are talking about.”
“Indeed, I must warn you that my actions often speak more unmistakably than my words. It is what learned persons term an idiosyncrasy.”
“﻿—And I certainly do not see how any of the saints can be concerned in this. If you had said the Four Evangelists now﻿—! For we were talking of the Four Evangelists, you remember, this morning﻿—Oh, but how stupid it is of you, Messire de Logreus, to stand there grinning and looking at me in a way that makes me blush!”
“Well, that is easily remedied,” said Jurgen, as he blew out the candles, “since women do not blush in the dark.”
“What do you plan, Messire de Logreus?”
“Ah, do not be alarmed!” said Jurgen. “I shall deal fairly with you.”
And in fact Yolande confessed afterward that, considering everything, Messire de Logreus was very generous. Jurgen confessed nothing: and as the room was profoundly dark nobody else can speak with authority as to what happened there. It suffices that the Duke of Logreus and the Lady of the Green Castle parted later on the most friendly terms.
“You have undone me, with your games and your candles and your scrupulous returning of courtesies,” said Yolande, and yawned, for she was sleepy; “but I fear that I do not hate you as much as I ought to.”
“No woman ever does,” says Jurgen, “at this hour.” He called for breakfast, then kissed Yolande﻿—for this, as Jurgen had said, was their hour of parting﻿—and he rode away from the Green Castle in high spirits.
“Why, what a thing it is again to be a fine young fellow!” said Jurgen. “Well, even though her big brown eyes protrude too much﻿—something like a lobster’s﻿—she is a splendid woman, that Dame Yolande: and it is a comfort to reflect I have seen justice was done her.”

===Chinese===

然而朱尔根并未急于答话，而是先机警地环顾寝殿四周：室内由一座高耸的描金烛台映照得通明，其上正荧荧燃烧着多支蜡烛。

他细细清点了一番，不由得撮口吹了一声轻浮口哨。“整整7支蜡烛（七支蜡烛）！指着我的良心起誓，心肝肉儿，你待我当真盛情隆重至极，这简直是一场名副其实的火树银花大庆典。谁能料到，你竟如同世人供奉得道圣徒那般，奉上7支蜡烛来恩宠于我！哎，我朱尔根虽不过是一介凡夫肉胎，然而我终归是朱尔根，我定当殚精竭虑、毫无折扣地如数奉还这7倍（七倍）的缱绻厚谊！”

“哎呀，洛格鲁斯爵爷，”约兰德夫人娇呼道，“你嘴里吐出的尽是些教人捉摸不透的荒唐胡话！你委实想歪了心思，因我敢向你保证我压根儿未曾起过那等念头。况且，我根本参不透你究竟在胡扯些什么。”

“千真万确，在下不得不正告夫人，我的实际行止往往比言辞表白更干脆直白、更不由分说。这便是不学无术的酸儒们口中所谓的情性癖好。”

“——且我压根儿瞧不出哪位天庭圣徒能同这等苟且事体扯上半点干系。你方才若是搬出四位福音使徒倒还罢了——！只因你该记得，今晨我们正在嚼着四位福音使徒的舌根呢——哎呀，洛格鲁斯爵爷，你像个登徒子似的大张着嘴傻笑、拿这等教人面红耳赤的下流眼神死死盯着我，究竟是何居心！”

“哎，那倒极易排解，”朱尔根说着，顺势一口气吹熄了所有蜡烛，“盖因妇道人家在漆黑一团里是决计不会脸红的。”

“你意欲何为，洛格鲁斯爵爷？”

“啊，休要惊慌！”朱尔根安抚道。“在下必定公平体面地伺候夫人。”

事实上，约兰德事后亦娇羞坦承：通盘权衡之下，这位洛格鲁斯爵爷委实称得上宽宏大度、体贴备至。至于朱尔根自家嘛，他平生向来三缄其口、绝无半句泄密；而鉴于室内当时漆黑如墨伸手不见五指，世间自然亦无人能以确凿见证者的身份去断言该处究竟发生了何等颠鸾倒凤。总而言之，洛格鲁斯公爵与绿堡女主在拂晓依依惜别之际，彼此情谊端的是亲密无间、如漆似胶。

“你借着你的风月博弈、你的融融蜡烛、以及你那丝丝入扣的厚意奉还，彻底教我身心沦落了，”约兰德娇慵地打了个哈欠，满面倦容春意；“可我暗自发慌，自己似乎并未能如理所应当的那般去痛恨你。”

“天下妇人皆是这般，”朱尔根笑道，“挑在拂晓这个骨节眼上。”他吩咐备下早膳，随后温存亲吻了约兰德——盖因正如朱尔根所言，此乃分别之时——随后意气风发、精神抖擞地策马离开了绿堡。

“天哪，重获风华青春当真是一桩天大的幸事！”朱尔根喜不自胜。“哎，哪怕她那双棕色大眼珠子凸得有些过火——活脱脱像只煮熟的大龙虾——可这位约兰德夫人依然是个千娇百媚的极品妇人；而一想到自己已然公允妥帖地替她讨还了公道，心头委实感到莫大的宽慰。”

===Original===

Then he rode back to Cameliard, singing with delight in the thought that he was riding toward the Princess Guenevere, whom he loved with his whole heart.

===Chinese===

随即他纵马折返卡梅利亚德，一路引吭高歌、满心欢喜，只因一念及自己正策马奔赴吉尼薇尔公主的身畔，奔向那位他全心全意钟爱着的金枝玉叶，他便觉这人世间万般美好莫过于此。

## XXXIII Farewell to Chloris / 第三十三章 诀别克洛丽斯
===Original===

Now the Philistines led out their prisoners, and made ready to inflict the doom which was decreed. And they permitted the young King of Eubonia to speak with Chloris.
“Farewell to you now, Jurgen!” says Chloris, weeping softly. “It is little I care what foolish words these priests of Philistia may utter against me. But the big-armed axemen are felling my tree yonder, to get them timber to make a bedstead for the Queen of Philistia: for that is what this Queen Dolores ordered them to do the first thing this morning.”
And Jurgen raised his hands. “You women!” he said. “What man would ever have thought of that?”
“So when my tree is felled I must depart into a sombre land wherein there is no laughter at all; and where the puzzled dead go wandering futilely through fields of scentless asphodel, and through tall sullen groves of myrtle﻿—the puzzled quiet dead, who may not even weep as I do now, but can only wonder what it is that they regret. And I too must taste of Lethê, and forget all I have loved.”
“You should give thanks to the imagination of your forefathers, my dear, that your doom is no worse. For I am going into a more barbaric limbo, into the Hell of a people who thought entirely too much about flames and pitchforks,” says Jurgen, ruefully. “I tell you it is the deuce and all, to come of morbid ancestry.” And he kissed Chloris, upon the brow. “My dear, dear girl,” he said, with a gulp, “as long as you remember me, do so with charity.”
“Jurgen”﻿—and she clung close to him﻿—“you were not ever unkind, not even for a moment. Jurgen, you have not ever spoken one harsh word to me or any other person, in all the while we were together. O Jurgen, whom I have loved as you could love nobody, it was not much those other women had left me to worship!”
“Indeed, it is a pity that you loved me, Chloris, for I was not worthy.” And for the instant Jurgen meant it.
“If any other person said that, Jurgen, I would be very angry. And even to hear you say it troubles me, because there was never a hamadryad between two hills that had a husband one-half so clever-foolish as he made light of time and chance, with his sleek black head cocked to one side, and his mischievous brown eyes a-twinkle.”

===Chinese===

此时非利士人（Philistines）押解出他们擒获的囚徒，准备执行先前宣判的极刑。他们恩准年轻的尤波尼亚国王（King of Eubonia）与克洛丽斯（Chloris）话别。

“永别了，朱尔根（Jurgen）！”克洛丽斯轻声呜咽道。“非利士这帮祭司究竟借由何等荒诞诳语构陷于我，我全不在乎。可那边那些膀大腰圆的刽子手樵夫，此刻正挥斧砍伐我的古树，意欲伐取木料好为非利士王后打制一张绣榻：全因那位多洛雷斯王后（Queen Dolores）今晨头一桩旨意便是这般吩咐他们的。”

朱尔根闻言颓然摊开双手。“你们这帮妇人啊！”他长叹道。“世间哪个汉子能想得出这等狠绝手段？”

“所以待到我的古树轰然倒伏之际，我便必须启程远赴一片绝无半点欢声笑语的阴森疆土；在那儿，茫然无措的亡魂徒劳无益地游荡在毫无芬芳的日照百合花丛中，穿行于高大阴郁的香桃木林间——那些茫然死寂的亡魂，甚至无从如我此刻这般痛哭流涕，唯能暗自困惑自己心中究竟在留恋哀悼着何物。而我也注定要饮下忘川（Lethê）之水，将平生所爱尽皆遗忘。”

“你倒当真该感念令祖先辈的瑰丽遐想，我的心肝，好在你的宿命未曾沦落得更惨烈。因我即将坠入一处野蛮残酷得多的虚无夹缝，坠入一个脑子里成日价尽想着熊熊烈焰与噬人铁叉的部族的地狱（Hell），”朱尔根神情凄楚地说道。“我正告你，投胎在生性阴郁病态的列祖列宗门下，委实是一桩倒霉透顶的祸事。”说着，他俯身在克洛丽斯光洁的额头上印下一吻。“我最亲爱、最温柔的姑娘，”他喉头哽咽着说道，“往后岁月里只要你尚且忆起我，万望心怀仁慈宽宥。”

“朱尔根”——她死死依偎进他的怀中——“你生平何曾薄情寡义过，甚至连片刻的薄幸都不曾有过。朱尔根，在相濡以沫的悠长岁月里，你何曾对我抑或世间任何人吐露过半句厉色粗言。噢，朱尔根啊，我深爱着你，这般深情是你断无可能去给予任何人的；那些先前的女子留给我的，本就无非是一副残缺破败的肉身皮囊以供我顶礼膜拜罢了！”

“诚然，你竟会钟情于我，克洛丽斯，委实太不值当，因我根本配不上你的深情。”在这一刹那，朱尔根字字句句皆发自肺腑。

“倘若是旁人敢吐露这等言语，朱尔根，我定会雷霆大怒。可纵然是亲耳听你吐露此言，亦教我心如刀绞；盖因放眼群山丘壑之间，何曾有哪位树精（hamadryad）的夫君，能及得上眼前这位一半的机智幽默与大智若愚？他侧着那颗油亮乌黑的脑袋，闪烁着顽黠淘气的棕色眸子，在岁月与机缘面前谈笑风生、戏谑人间。”

===Original===

And Jurgen wondered that this should be the notion Chloris had of him, and that a gesture should be the things she remembered about him: and he was doubly assured that no woman bothers to understand the man she elects to love and cosset and slave for.
“O woman dear,” says Jurgen, “but I have loved you, and my heart is water now that you are taken from me: and to remember your ways and the joy I had in them will be a big and grinding sorrow in the long time to come. Oh, not with any heroic love have I loved you, nor with any madness and high dreams, nor with much talking either; but with a love befitting my condition, with a quiet and cordial love.”
“And must you be trying, while I die, to get your grieving for me into the right words?” she asks him, smiling very sadly. “No matter: you are Jurgen, and I have loved you. And I am glad that I shall know nothing about it when in the long time to come you will be telling so many other women about what was said by Zorobasius and Ptolemopiter, and when you will be posturing and romancing for their delight. For presently I shall have tasted Lethê: and presently I shall have forgotten you, King Jurgen, and all the joy I had in you, and all the pride, and all the love I had for you, King Jurgen, who loved me as much as you were able.”
“Why, and will there be any lovemaking, do you think, in Hell?” he asks her, with a doleful smile.
“There will be lovemaking,” she replied, “wherever you go, King Jurgen. And there will be women to listen. And at the last there will be a bean-pole of a woman, in a wig.”
“I am sorry﻿—” he said. “And yet I have loved you, Chloris.”
“That is my comfort now. And presently there will be Lethê. I put the greater faith in Lethê. And still, I cannot help but love you, Jurgen, in whom I have no faith at all.”
He said, again: “I am not worthy.”
They kissed. Then each of them was conveyed to an appropriate doom.
And tears were in the eyes of Jurgen, who was not used to weep: and he thought not at all of what was to befall him, but only of this and that small trivial thing which would have pleased his Chloris had Jurgen done it, and which for one reason or another Jurgen had left undone.
“I was not ever unkind to her, says she! ah, but I might have been so much kinder.

===Chinese===

朱尔根不由得暗自惊诧，克洛丽斯心目中的朱尔根竟是这般模样，而她念念不忘的竟然不过是某一个轻佻姿态；这令他愈发深信不疑：天下绝无任何妇人会费心去真正读懂她决意去深爱、去溺爱、去为之当牛做马的那个汉子。

“噢，我亲爱的人儿，”朱尔根说道，“可我确曾深深爱过你，如今眼睁睁瞧着你被生生夺走，我的心碎得宛若一汪春水：而在漫长无尽的往后岁月里，忆及你的一颦一笑以及你曾赐予我的无上欢欣，将化作钝刀割肉般的滔天剧痛。噢，我爱你的方式绝非英雄式的惊天动地，亦不曾伴随狂热执念与崇高美梦，更不曾借由滔滔不绝的花言巧语；而是以一种契合我当下残躯的安分方式，以一份安详、温存的真挚深情去深爱着你。”

“而在我即将香消玉殒的关头，你难道依然在苦苦搜肠刮肚、非要把你对我的悼词拼凑得辞藻华美才肯罢休吗？”她望着他，脸上泛起一抹凄艳哀婉的浅笑。“罢了，无所谓了：你终归是朱尔根，而我终归曾深爱过你。且我甚感欣慰的是，往后余生里，当你向无数其他女子津津乐道于佐罗巴修斯（Zorobasius）与托勒密匹特（Ptolemopiter）的高论微言之际，当你为了博取她们的欢心而卖弄风情、大编罗曼史之际，我已然深陷九泉一无所知了。因少顷我便将饮下忘川之水：少顷我便将把你忘得一干二净，朱尔根国王，将你曾赐予我的全部欢欣、全部骄傲，以及我曾倾注于你的全部深情尽皆遗忘，朱尔根国王啊，你已然在你力所能及的极限里深深爱过了我。”

“哎，你觉得在地狱深处，难道还会有寻欢作乐的调情私会么？”他满面凄苦地反问道。

“凡你朱尔根国王涉足之所，”她答道，“便少不了调情寻欢。且总会有痴心女子洗耳恭听。而在尘埃落定的最终关头，等待着你的将是一个戴着假发、瘦骨嶙峋如豆秸秆般的泼悍妇人。”

“我很抱歉……”他喃喃道。“然而我确曾深爱过你，克洛丽斯。”

“这便是我此刻唯一的慰藉。少顷便有忘川水相伴。我对忘川怀有更坚定的笃信。然而尽管如此，我依然身不由己地深爱着你，朱尔根，纵然我对你毫无半分信赖。”

他再度低语道：“我根本配不上你。”

二人深情相吻。随后，他们各自被押解着奔赴各自钦定的刑场绝地。

朱尔根那双素来不轻弹泪珠的眼眶中溢满了热泪：他全然未曾作想降临自身的惨烈厄运，脑海中唯独盘旋着这桩那桩昔日但凡朱尔根顺手施为便能博得克洛丽斯欢欣雀跃的微末细琐小事，而他却因着这个那个荒唐由头未曾替她办到。

“‘他生平何曾薄情寡义过’，她是这般评价我的！啊，可我昔日明明本该待她体贴温存胜过千万倍啊。

===Original===

And now I shall not ever see her any more, nor ever any more may I awaken delight and admiration in those bright tender eyes which saw no fault in me! Well, but it is a comfort surely that she does not know how I devoted the last night she was to live to teaching mathematics.”
And then Jurgen wondered how he would be despatched into the Hell of his fathers? And when the Philistines showed him in what manner they proposed to inflict their sentence he wondered at his own obtuseness.
“For I might have surmised this would be the way of it,” said Jurgen. “And yet as always there is a simplicity in the methods of the Philistines which is unimaginable by really clever fellows. And as always, too, these methods are unfair to us clever fellows. Well, I am willing to taste any drink once: but this is a very horrible device, none the less; and I wonder if I have the pluck to endure it?”
Then as he stood considering this matter, a man-at-arms came hurrying. He brought with him three great rolled parchments, with seals and ribbons and everything in order: and these were Jurgen’s pardon and Jurgen’s nomination as Poet Laureate of Philistia and Jurgen’s appointment as Mathematician Royal.
The man-at-arms brought also a letter from Queen Dolores, and this Jurgen read with a frown.
“Do you consider now what fun it would be to hoodwink everybody by pretending to conform to our laws!” said this letter, and it said nothing more: Dolores was really a wise woman. Yet there was a postscript. “For we could be so happy!” said the postscript.
And Jurgen looked toward the Woods, where men were sawing up a great oak-tree. And Jurgen gave a fine laugh, and with fine deliberateness he tore up the Queen’s letter into little strips. Then statelily he took the parchments, and found they were so tough he could not tear them. This was uncommonly awkward, for Jurgen’s ill-advised attempt to tear the parchments impaired the dignity of his magnanimous self-sacrifice: he even suspected one of the guards of smiling. So there was nothing for it but presently to give up that futile tugging and jerking, and to compromise by crumpling these parchments.
“This is my answer,” said Jurgen heroically, and with some admiration of himself, but still a little dashed by the uncalled-for toughness of the parchments.

===Chinese===

而如今我往后余生再也无缘得见她的倩影，再也无法在她那双瞧不出我半点瑕疵的温柔明眸中唤起欢欣与钦慕了！哎，可令人宽慰的是，她至死亦不知晓，在她阳寿的最后一夜，我究竟是如何专心致志地传授‘算术学’的。”

随即朱尔根不由得暗自纳罕：自己究竟会被依循何等方式押解进祖先的地狱之中？而当非利士人向他当面揭示他们打算以何等酷刑行刑之际，他不由得为自家的愚钝大惊失色。

“我早该料到这帮人的手段必是这般，”朱尔根叹道。“然而正如往昔一般，非利士人行事的质朴平铺，委实远非真正聪明绝顶的妙人儿所能想象。且同样如往昔一般，这等手段对我们这帮聪明绝顶的智者而言委实太失公允。罢啦，天下杯中物凡是甘霖朱尔根皆愿尝上一尝：然而无论如何，这道刑具构想终归可怖至极；我倒真犯嘀咕自己究竟有没有硬撑到底的骨气？”

恰在他凝神沉思之际，一名披甲侍卫飞奔而至。侍卫怀抱着3卷（三卷）扎着火漆缎带、手续完备的硕大羊皮文书：这赫然便是对朱尔根的无罪赦免令、册封朱尔根为非利士桂冠诗人的诏书，以及钦点朱尔根为皇家首席算术大师的任命状。

侍卫同时呈上了一封多洛雷斯王后的亲笔御函，朱尔根展信蹙眉细读。

“你且想一想，假意顺从我们的法度，把天下所有人尽皆蒙在鼓里耍得团团转，该是何等快活的一桩乐事啊！”信中唯有这么一句轻佻言语，除此以外别无长篇大论：多洛雷斯委实是个绝顶聪明的世故妇人。然而文末尚有一行附言。“因我们本可以过得这般如胶似漆、快活无边！”附言如是写道。

朱尔根抬眼望向森林深处，在那儿，成群的莽汉正挥锯将一株苍劲大橡树截成数段。朱尔根忽然放声朗笑，以极其优雅沉稳的从容派头，将王后的亲笔御信撕扯成道道碎纸屑。随后他仪态尊贵地拾起那些羊皮公文，却尴尬发觉那些羊皮纸质地坚韧异常，凭他的手劲竟然硬生生撕扯不动。这番场面委实尴尬至极，只因朱尔根那番鲁莽撕扯羊皮纸的滑稽举动，彻底败坏了他慷慨就义的大义凛然；他甚至疑心身旁的某个守卫已在掩口窃笑。于是无可奈何之下，他唯有放弃了那番徒劳的拉扯猛拽，退而求其次地将这几张羊皮公文狠狠揉搓成一团废纸。

“这便是我的答复，”朱尔根神采奕奕、大义凛然地宣称，心头对自己颇为顾影自怜，却难免因羊皮纸那番不解风情的坚韧而略感扫兴。

===Original===

Then Jurgen cried farewell to fallen Leukê; and scornfully he cried farewell to the Philistines and to their devices. Then he submitted to their devices. Thus, it was without making any special protest about it that Jurgen was relegated to limbo, and was despatched to the Hell of his fathers, two days before Christmas.

===Chinese===

随后，朱尔根向沦陷的留刻岛（Leukê）高呼诀别；他以轻蔑至极的姿态，向非利士人及其凡百机巧酷刑高呼诀别。随后他俯首就范，任凭这帮人施展刑具。就这般，未曾提出任何格外激烈的抗辩抗争，在距离圣诞节前2天（圣诞节前两天）的凛冬时分，朱尔根被逐入阴阳夹缝，押解发配往他列祖列宗世代沦落的地狱深处。

## XXI How Anaïtis Voyaged / 第二十一章 阿娜伊蒂斯航海记
===Original===

Now the tale tells that Jurgen and this Lady of the Lake came presently to the wharves of Cameliard, and went aboard the ship which had brought Anaïtis and Merlin into Glathion. This ship was now to every appearance deserted: yet all its saffron colored sails were spread, as though in readiness for the ship’s departure.
“The crew are scrambling, it may be, for the largesse, and fighting over Gogyrvan’s silver pieces,” says Anaïtis, “but I think they will not be long in returning. So we will sit here upon the prow, and await their leisure.”
“But already the vessel moves,” says Jurgen, “and I hear behind us the rattling of silver chains and the flapping of shifted saffron-colored sails.”
“They are roguish fellows,” says Anaïtis, smiling. “Evidently, they hid from us, pretending there was nobody aboard. Now they think to give us a surprise when the ship sets out to sea as though it were of itself. But we will disappoint these merry rascals, by seeming to notice nothing unusual.”
So Jurgen sat with Anaïtis in the two tall chairs that were in the prow of the vessel, under a canopy of crimson stuff embroidered with gold dragons, and just back of the ship’s figurehead, which was a dragon painted with thirty colors: and the ship moved out of the harbor, and so into the open sea. Thus they passed Enisgarth.
“And it is a queer crew that serve you, Anaïtis, who are Queen of Cocaigne: for I can hear them talking, far back of us, and their language is all a cheeping and a twittering, as though the mice and the bats were holding conference.”
“Why, you must understand that these are outlanders who speak a dialect of their own, and are not like any other people you have ever seen.”
“Indeed, now, that is very probable, for I have seen none of your crew. Sometimes it is as though small flickerings passed over the deck, and that is all.”
“It is but the heat waves rising from the deck, for the day is warmer than you would think, sitting here under this canopy. And besides, what call have you and I to be bothering over the pranks of common mariners, so long as they do their proper duty?”
“I was thinking, O woman with unusual eyes, that these are hardly common mariners.”
“And I was thinking, Duke Jurgen, that I would tell you a tale of the Old Gods, to make the time speed more pleasantly as we sit here untroubled as a god and a goddess.”

===Chinese===

话说故事讲到，朱尔根（Jurgen）与这位湖中仙女（Lady of the Lake）未几便行抵卡梅利亚德（Cameliard）的码头，登上了昔日将阿娜伊蒂斯（Anaïtis）与梅林（Merlin）载至格拉西昂（Glathion）的那艘海船。此刻放眼望去，这艘船周遭空无一人，仿佛早已人去船空；然而其上凡百面藏红花色（saffron）的帆篷却尽皆张挂铺展，仿佛随时准备起锚出海。

“水手们大抵正在疯抢打赏，正为戈吉尔万的碎银子打得不可开交呢，”阿娜伊蒂斯说道，“但我料想他们不多时便会回转。我们不妨在船头并肩而坐，静候他们闲暇尽兴。”

“可这船明明已然在缓缓移动了，”朱尔根讶异道，“我分明听见身后银链叮当作响，藏红花色的帆篷正被风向拨弄得猎猎飞扬。”

“他们净是些调皮狡黠的无赖汉，”阿娜伊蒂斯抿嘴浅笑道。“显而易见，他们故意躲着我们，假装船上空无一人。眼下他们自以为能给我们制造一场虚惊，教这船瞧来宛若在自行扬帆出海。可我们偏要教这帮快活的小混蛋大失所望，权当没瞧出半点异样。”

于是朱尔根同阿娜伊蒂斯并肩端坐在船头的两把高背大椅上，头顶撑着一顶绣着金龙的金红丝绒华盖，身前紧挨着雕饰为涂抹着整整30种（三十种）绚烂油彩巨龙的船首像：海船缓缓滑出港湾，径自驶入茫茫万顷碧波。他们就这般轻舟掠过了埃尼斯加斯（Enisgarth）。

“服侍您的这帮水手委实古怪离奇得很，身为科凯恩女王的阿娜伊蒂斯：只因我能听见他们远远在我们身后窃窃私语，那言语听来尽是一片吱吱唧唧、悉悉索索的怪响，活脱脱像是一群蝙蝠与耗子在聚首密谋。”

“哎，你自当体谅，他们是一伙说着自家族裔土语的异邦番民，同你平生所见过的任何族裔皆大相径庭。”

“千真万确，这倒极有可能，因我压根儿连贵属水手的半个影子都未曾瞧见过。有时候，甲板上倒像是有一簇簇微小的流光火星飘掠而过，除此以外别无长物。”

“那不过是甲板上腾起的热浪光晕罢了，坐在华盖底下你自是不知，今日的天气可比你所想的要炎热得多。况且，只要这帮粗鄙水手克尽厥职，你我又何苦为这等下贱仆役的恶作剧挂怀劳神呢？”

“在下适才在想，噢，生着非凡异眸的美人，他们瞧来委实算不得寻常水手。”

“而我适才在想，朱尔根公爵，我倒不妨为你讲一则关于上古诸神（Old Gods）的风月旧闻，好教我们在船头如神明与女神般安然高坐之际，教这水上光阴消磨得更显怡人惬意。”

===Original===

Now they had passed Camwy: and Anaïtis began to narrate the history of Anistar and Calmoora and of the unusual concessions they granted each other, and of how Calmoora contented her five lovers: and Jurgen found the tale perturbing.
While Anaïtis talked the sky grew dark, as though the sun were ashamed and veiled his shame with clouds: and they went forward in a gray twilight which deepened steadily over a tranquil sea. So they passed the lights of Sargyll, most remote of the Red Islands, while Anaïtis talked of Procris and King Minos and Pasiphaë. As color went out of the air new colors entered into the sea, which now assumed the varied gleams of water that has long been stagnant. And a silence brooded over the sea, so that there was no noise anywhere except the sound of the voice of Anaïtis, saying, “All men that live have but a little while to live, and none knows his fate thereafter. So that a man possesses nothing certainly save a brief loan of his own body; and yet the body of man is capable of much curious pleasure.”
They came thus to a low-lying naked beach, where there was no sign of habitation. Anaïtis said this was the land they were seeking, and they went ashore.
“Even now,” says Jurgen, “I have seen none of the crew who brought us hither.”
And the beautiful dark woman shrugged, and marveled why he need perpetually be bothering over the doings of common sailors.
They went forward across the beach, through sand hills, to a moor, seeing no one, and walking in a gray fog. They passed many gray fat sluggish worms and some curious gray reptiles such as Jurgen had never imagined to exist, but Anaïtis said these need not trouble them.
“So there is no call to be fingering your charmed sword as we walk here, Duke Jurgen, for these great worms do not ever harm the living.”
“For whom, then, do they lie here in wait, in this gray fog, wherethrough the green lights flutter, and wherethrough I hear at times a thin and far-off wailing?”
“What is that to you, Duke Jurgen, since you and I are still in the warm flesh? Surely there was never a man who asked more idle questions.”
“Yet this is an uncomfortable twilight.”
“To the contrary, you should rejoice that it is a fog too heavy to be penetrated by the Moon.”
“But what have I to do with the Moon?”
“Nothing, as yet.

===Chinese===

说话间，二人已然穿过了卡姆威（Camwy）：阿娜伊蒂斯随即娓娓道来阿尼斯塔尔（Anistar）与卡尔穆拉（Calmoora）的风流轶事，讲述他们如何向彼此恩准了骇人听闻的纵容让步，以及卡尔穆拉如何心满意足地同时侍奉她那5位（五位）情郎：朱尔根听得心旌摇曳、神魂不宁。

伴随着阿娜伊蒂斯的娓娓倾谈，天色逐渐晦暗下来，仿佛艳阳亦感到羞愧难当，不得不拉拢浓云遮掩自家的赧颜；他们就这般在宁谧海面上徐徐前行，穿过一片在海天之间不断浓重的苍茫暮霭。他们穿过了红岛诸境（Red Islands）最为孤悬绝远的萨吉尔（Sargyll）的渔火孤灯，期间阿娜伊蒂斯一路津津乐道于普罗克里斯（Procris）、米诺斯国王（King Minos）与帕西淮（Pasiphaë）的隐秘淫靡。随着流光自天际褪尽，全新的怪异色彩却悄然浸润入海波之中，令海水浮现出腐水长年死寂停滞后才有的斑驳磷光。死一般的寂静笼罩了汪洋，四下里万籁俱寂，唯有阿娜伊蒂斯那道温软嗓音在虚空中幽幽回荡：“凡肉身凡胎之辈苟活于世不过须臾片刻，而死后宿命究竟归于何方却无一人知晓。因而一个凡夫确凿无疑所能拥有的，无非是对自家肉身的一场短暂暂借；然而人类的血肉之躯，却偏偏能领略凡百不可名状的神妙欢愉。”

他们就这般行抵了一处荒僻平缓、毫无人烟生息的裸露浅滩。阿娜伊蒂斯宣称这正是他们寻访的世外疆土，二人遂联袂登岸。

“直至此时此刻，”朱尔根环顾四周道，“我依然未曾得见护送我们抵临此间的水手尊容。”

绝色黑肤佳人耸了耸肩，惊诧于他为何总这般没完没了地对寻常水手的行径咬住不放。

二人踏着沙滩前行，穿过沙丘，步入一片荒凉旷野，途中不见半个人影，唯在苍茫迷雾中踽踽独行。沿途穿过了无数肥硕黏腻、蠕动迟缓的灰色巨虫，以及某些朱尔根平生何曾想象过的古怪灰色爬虫，然而阿娜伊蒂斯宽慰言道无需为此大惊小怪。

“穿行此间，朱尔根公爵，尊驾大可不必神经兮兮地摩挲那柄受过符咒的神剑，因这些大虫向来不伤活人分毫。”

“那么它们潜伏于此究竟意在伺候何人呢？在这片翻滚着幽绿鬼火、且不时能听闻渺远凄厉悲泣之声的苍茫浓雾之中？”

“那同尊驾又有何干涉，朱尔根公爵，只要你我二人依然拥有温热鲜活的血肉之躯？平生所见，当真从未见过哪个汉子如你这般刨根问底、尽问些废话闲篇。”

“可这片暮霭暮色委实教人浑身不舒坦。”

“恰恰相反，你倒该拍手称快才是，因这等浓重雾气，哪怕是天上的月亮亦休想穿透其分毫。”

“可月亮同我又有何相干？”

“眼下自是全无相干。

===Original===

And that is as well for you, Duke Jurgen, since it is authentically reported you have derided the day which is sacred to the Moon. Now the Moon does not love derision, as I well know, for in part I serve the Moon.”
“Eh?” says Jurgen: and he began to reflect.
So they came to a wall that was high and gray, and to the door which was in the wall.
“You must knock two or three times,” says Anaïtis, “to get into Cocaigne.”
Jurgen observed the bronze knocker upon the door, and he grinned in order to hide his embarrassment.
“It is a quaint fancy,” said he, “and the two constituents of it appear to have been modeled from life.”
“They were copied very exactly from Adam and Eve,” says Anaïtis, “who were the first persons to open this gateway.”
“Why, then,” says Jurgen, “there is no earthly doubt that men degenerate, since here under my hand is the proof of it.”
With that he knocked, and the door opened, and the two of them entered.

===Chinese===

而这对你朱尔根公爵而言倒真算得万幸，因据确凿密报，你曾公然嘲弄讥刺过奉献给月亮的圣日。月亮向来眼里揉不得讥嘲，关于这一点我心知肚明，盖因我在某种位阶上亦在侍奉着月亮。”

“嗯？”朱尔根一愣，随即陷入了沉思。

说话间，二人已然行抵一座高耸灰暗的围墙跟前，围墙正中横亘着一扇厚重大门。

“要想跨入科凯恩（Cocaigne），”阿娜伊蒂斯吩咐道，“你必须在门上敲击2、3次（两三次）。”

朱尔根打量着门扉上的青铜门环，随即咧嘴讪笑，借以遮掩内心的惊惶与尴尬。

“这道造物构想委实精巧诡谲，”他打趣道，“这上面雕铸的一对造物，瞧来分明是照着活人真身原样拓模铸就的。”

“它们正是分毫不差地拓印自亚当与夏娃（Adam and Eve）的真身，”阿娜伊蒂斯从容道，“他们乃是昔日头一遭叩开这重门户的始祖。”

“哎，既然这般，”朱尔根笑道，“人世间的子孙后代一代不如一代，骨殖日渐退化萎缩，世间对此断无半点可疑之处了，因在下手心底下正握着铁证如山的铁证呢。”

言毕，他扬手叩门，大门应声洞开，二人遂并肩跨入其中。

## XXXIV How Emperor Jurgen Fared Infernally / 第三十四章 朱尔根皇帝在地狱的际遇
===Original===

Now the tale tells how the devils of Hell were in one of their churches celebrating Christmas in such manner as the devils observe that day; and how Jurgen came through the trapdoor in the vestry-room; and how he saw and wondered over the creatures which inhabited this place. For to him after the Christmas services came all such devils as his fathers had foretold, and in not a hair or scale or talon did they differ from the worst that anybody had been able to imagine.
“Anatomy is hereabouts even more inconsequent than in Cocaigne,” was Jurgen’s first reflection. But the first thing the devils did was to search Jurgen very carefully, in order to make sure he was not bringing any water into Hell.
“Now, who may you be, that come to us alive, in a fine shirt of which we never saw the like before?” asked Dithican. He had the head of a tiger, but otherwise the appearance of a large bird, with shining feathers and four feet: his neck was yellow, his body green, and his feet black.
“It would not be treating honestly with you to deny that I am the Emperor of Noumaria,” said Jurgen, somewhat advancing his estate.
Now spoke Amaimon, in the form of a thick suet-colored worm going upright upon his tail, which shone like the tail of a glowworm. He had no feet, but under his chops were two short hands, and upon his back were bristles such as grow upon hedgehogs.
“But we are rather overrun with emperors,” said Amaimon, doubtfully, “and their crimes are a great trouble to us. Were you a very wicked ruler?”
“Never since I became an emperor,” replied Jurgen, “has any of my subjects uttered one word of complaint against me. So it stands to reason I have nothing very serious with which to reproach myself.”
“Your conscience, then, does not demand that you be punished?”
“My conscience, gentlemen, is too well-bred to insist on anything.”
“You do not even wish to be tortured?”
“Well, I admit I had expected something of the sort. But none the less, I will not make a point of it,” said Jurgen, handsomely. “No, I shall be quite satisfied even though you do not torture me at all.”
And then the mob of devils made a great to-do over Jurgen.
“For it is exceedingly good to have at least one unpretentious and undictatorial human being in Hell.

===Chinese===

话说故事讲到，地狱里的群魔正在自家的某座教堂里，依循魔鬼们惯常的仪式庆贺着圣诞节；朱尔根（Jurgen）猛然从更衣室地面的活板门中一跃而出；他瞪大双眼，对着盘踞此间的妖魔鬼怪啧啧称奇。只因在圣诞弥撒礼拜礼毕之后，昔年他列祖列宗曾预言过的凡百魔鬼纷纷迎面围拢而来，无论是在毫毛、鳞甲或利爪上，尽皆同世人所能构想的最狰狞模样分毫不差。

“此方的解剖骨骼构造，瞧来竟比科凯恩（Cocaigne）还要不合常理，”这乃是朱尔根闪过的头一道念头。然而这帮魔鬼上前的头一桩举动，却是极为周密地搜查朱尔根周身，以确保他未曾私自带半滴清水潜入地狱（Hell）。

“那么，阁下究竟是何方神圣，竟敢以大活人之躯擅闯此间，且身披这件我等平生见所未见的流光锦缎华服？”迪蒂坎（Dithican）尖声盘问道。此怪生着一副斑斓虎头，身躯却是一只生着光洁翎羽与4只足（四只脚）的硕大怪鸟：生着鹅黄脖颈、碧绿身躯，足爪却如焦炭般漆黑。

“若对尊驾有所隐瞒，未免有失公允，在下实乃努马里亚皇帝（Emperor of Noumaria），”朱尔根顺水推舟，将自家的门阀身价略作了一番拔高。

此时阿玛蒙（Amaimon）幽幽开口——此怪化身作一条粗壮如油脂凝脂的肥硕长虫，正用闪烁着宛若萤火虫般磷光的尾巴直立支撑着身躯。它没有腿足，然而在下颌处却生有2只短手，脊背上则倒竖着宛若刺猬一般的尖锐硬刺。

“可我们地狱里的皇帝早已泛滥成灾了，”阿玛蒙狐疑地嘟囔道，“且他们身上的罪愆委实给我们添了无穷烦扰。敢问尊驾在阳世可曾是个十恶不赦的暴君？”

“自打在下黄袍加身荣登大宝以来，”朱尔根答道，“在下的万民臣民从未曾有一人对我吐露过半句微词。由此推论，在下骨子里委实找不出什么值得痛自检讨的弥天大罪。”

“这么说来，尊驾的良心并未苛求本处对你施以惩处？”

“实不相瞒，诸位君子，在下的良心教养极好，素来不屑于强人所难、任性苛求。”

“你甚至压根儿不想受严刑拷打折磨？”

“哎，在下坦承此前确曾料想过这等场面。然而尽管如此，在下断不会对此斤斤计较、咬住不放，”朱尔根大度体面地宣称。“不，纵然诸位尊驾完全免去严刑拷打，在下亦会甘之如饴、心满意足。”

此言一出，群魔立时炸开了锅，齐刷刷对着朱尔根大加殷勤奉承。

“因在这暗无天日的地狱深处，能撞见哪怕一个质朴谦逊、全无颐指气使做派的凡夫俗子，当真是天大的祥瑞大善事啊！

===Original===

Nobody as a rule drops in on us save inordinately proud and conscientious ghosts, whose self-conceit is intolerable, and whose demands are outrageous.”
“How can that be?”
“Why, we have to punish them. Of course they are not properly punished until they are convinced that what is happening to them is just and adequate. And you have no notion what elaborate tortures they insist their exceeding wickedness has merited, as though that which they did or left undone could possibly matter to anybody. And to contrive these torments quite tires us out.”
“But wherefore is this place called the Hell of my fathers?”
“Because your forefathers builded it in dreams,” they told him, “out of the pride which led them to believe that what they did was of sufficient importance to merit punishment. Or so at least we have heard: but if you want the truth of the matter you must go to our Grandfather at Barathum.”
“I shall go to him, then. And do my own grandfathers, and all the forefathers that I had in the old time, inhabit this gray place?”
“All such as are born with what they call a conscience come hither,” the devils said. “Do you think you could persuade them to go elsewhere? For in that event, we would be deeply obliged to you. Their self-conceit is pitiful: but it is also a nuisance, because it prevents our getting any rest.”
“Perhaps I can help you to obtain justice, and certainly to attempt to secure justice for you is my imperial duty. But who governs this country?”
They told him how Hell was divided into principalities that had for governors Lucifer and Beelzebub and Belial and Ascheroth and Phlegeton: but that over all these was Grandfather Satan, who lived in the Black House at Barathum.
“Well, I prefer,” says Jurgen, “to deal directly with your principal, especially if he can explain the polity of this insane and murky country. Do some of you conduct me to him in such state as becomes an emperor!”
So Cannagosta fetched a wheelbarrow, and Jurgen got into it, and Cannagosta trundled him away. Cannagosta was something like an ox, but rather more like a cat, and his hair was curly.
And as they came through Chorasma, a very uncomfortable place where the damned abide in torment, whom should Jurgen see but his own father, Coth, the son of Smoit and Steinvor, standing there chewing his long moustaches in the midst of an especially tall flame.
“Do you stop now for a moment!”

===Chinese===

平日里投奔此间的，无非是些狂妄自大、死脑筋的顽固鬼魂，他们的孤芳自赏委实教人不堪忍受，口中提出的苛求更是荒唐得令人发指。”

“这究竟从何说起？”

“哎，我们不得不惩办他们哪。可唯有当他们心悦诚服地认定降临自身的酷刑乃是公允体面、分毫不差的报应之际，他们方才算得上真正受了惩戒。而你压根儿想象不出，这帮顽固死鬼究竟会如何穷凶极恶地坚称自家的滔天罪孽理当配得上何等巧夺天工、千刀万剐的极刑，仿佛他们生前的所作所为抑或怠慢未尽之责，当真值得全天下人去挂怀动容似的。为了构想调配这凡百酷刑，当真把我们这帮魔鬼累得筋疲力竭。”

“可此地为何偏偏被称作我列祖列宗的地狱呢？”

“皆因这处疆土乃是令祖先辈在幻梦深处一手缔造的，”群魔如实相告，“全凭着一股盲目的傲慢狂妄，令他们坚信自身的所作所为在天地间拥有足以配得上无尽惩处的分量。坊间传闻大抵如此；可你若想参透个中终极真谛，唯有亲去巴拉苏姆（Barathum）拜谒我们的撒旦祖父（Grandfather Satan）。”

“既然如此，我自当前去拜谒他老人家。那么我自家的列祖先辈，昔日古老光阴中我的所有祖先，皆幽禁栖身于这片晦暗灰蒙的幽冥之中吗？”

“凡是生来便怀着他们口中所谓‘良心’这等累赘的冤家，尽皆汇聚于此，”魔鬼们诉苦道。“尊驾能否设法劝劝他们另谋高就、迁居别处？倘若真能办到，我等当真感恩戴德、感激涕零。他们的孤芳自赏自命不凡固然令人心生怜悯，可亦是一桩教人不堪其扰的麻烦祸事，搅得我们连片刻清净都不得安生。”

“在下或许能助诸位讨还一番公道，且替诸位主持公道本就是皇家的天职所在。可这方天地究竟由谁统御执掌？”

群魔便向他细细剖白，讲述地狱如何划分为诸多封国公邦，分别由卢奇菲罗（Lucifer）、别西卜（Beelzebub）、贝利亚（Belial）、阿斯切罗特（Ascheroth）与弗莱格顿（Phlegeton）各自总督分治；然而凌驾于诸侯之上的至高主宰，唯有高居巴拉苏姆黑宫深处的撒旦祖父。

“哎，在下向来偏爱，”朱尔根言道，“直接同你们的东家大掌柜打交道，尤其是倘若他能替在下剖析清楚这片荒腔走板、阴森晦暗国土的治国方略。且请二位依循契合一国之君的显赫排场，护送在下前去面圣吧！”

于是坎纳戈斯塔（Cannagosta）推来了一辆独轮手推小车，朱尔根大模大样地坐了进去，坎纳戈斯塔便欢欢喜喜地推着他骨碌碌上路。这坎纳戈斯塔模样略带几分像耕牛，可神态举止却更肖似家猫，满头鬈毛煞是滑稽。

当车仗穿行霍拉斯马（Chorasma）——一处罚入地狱的沉沦受难亡魂在剧痛中煎熬度日的极不舒坦绝地——之际，朱尔根抬眼一瞧，不是旁人，赫然正是自家生身老父考斯（Coth）——斯莫伊特国王与施泰因沃尔的骨肉！——此刻正伫立在一道格外高耸的熊熊烈焰正中，咬牙切齿地不住咀嚼着自家那两撇浓密长髯。

“快且给老子刹住脚步稍候片刻！”

===Original===

says Jurgen, to his escort.
“Oh, but this is the most vexatious person in all Hell!” cried Cannagosta; “and a person whom there is absolutely no pleasing!”
“Nobody knows that better than I,” says Jurgen.
And Jurgen civilly bade his father good day, but Coth did not recognize this spruce young Emperor of Noumaria, who went about Hell in a wheelbarrow.
“You do not know me, then?” says Jurgen.
“How should I know you when I never saw you before?” replied Coth, irritably.
And Jurgen did not argue the point: for he knew that he and his father could never agree about anything. So Jurgen kept silent for that time, and Cannagosta wheeled him through the gray twilight, descending always deeper and yet deeper into the lowlands of Hell, until they had come to Barathum.

===Chinese===

朱尔根冲着推车的侍从厉声吩咐道。

“哎呀呀，这老死鬼可是全地狱最教人头疼煞星的头号刺头啊！”坎纳戈斯塔尖声哀叹道；“且是个全天下压根儿无从取悦的难缠主儿！”

“关于这一点，全天下断无第二个人比老子更心知肚明，”朱尔根叹道。

朱尔根遂客客气气向生父道了一声清安，然而考斯压根儿未曾将眼前这位衣着光鲜挺拔、大模大样坐着独轮手推小车游逛地狱的年轻努马里亚皇帝认将出来。

“这么说来，你老人家竟认不出我了？”朱尔根问道。

“老子生平何曾见过你，又怎生认得出你这厮！”考斯暴躁不耐地啐道。

朱尔根识趣地并未在此节骨眼上多作纠缠：因他心知肚明，在天地间凡百人事上，他与他父亲从来未曾达成过半点共识契合。于是朱尔根只得三缄其口，由着坎纳戈斯塔推着小车穿行于灰蒙蒙的幽冥暮霭之中，一路盘旋向着地狱愈发幽深、愈发冰冷的平原腹地不断滑落，直至二人行抵巴拉苏姆的深渊府门。

## XLIV In the Manager’s Office / 第四十四章 在掌柜公事房里
===Original===

The tale tells that all was dark there, and Jurgen could see no one. But the cave stretched straight forward, and downward, and at the far end was a glow of light. Jurgen went on and on, and so came to the place where Nessus had lain in wait for Jurgen. Again Jurgen stooped, and crawled through the opening in the cave’s wall, and so came to where lamps were burning upon tall iron stands. Now, one by one, these lamps were going out, and there were now no women here: instead, Jurgen trod inch deep in fine white ashes, leaving the print of his feet upon them.
He went forward as the cave stretched. He came to a sharp turn in the cave, with the failing lamplight now behind him, so that his shadow confronted Jurgen, blurred but unarguable. It was the proper shadow of a commonplace and elderly pawnbroker, and Jurgen regarded it with approval.
Jurgen came then into a sort of underground chamber, from the roof of which was suspended a kettle of quivering red flames. Facing him was a throne, and back of this were rows of benches: but here, too, was nobody. Resting upright against the vacant throne was a triangular white shield: and when Jurgen looked more closely he could see there was writing upon it. Jurgen carried this shield as close as he could to the kettle of flames, for his eyesight was now not very good, and besides, the flames in the kettle were burning low: and Jurgen deciphered the message that was written upon the shield, in black and red letters.
“Absent upon important affairs,” it said. “Will be back in an hour.” And it was signed, “Thragnar R.”
“I wonder now for whom King Thragnar left this notice?” reflected Jurgen﻿—“certainly not for me. And I wonder, too, if he left it here a year ago or only this evening? And I wonder if it was Thragnar’s head I removed in the black and silver pavilion? Ah, well, there are a number of things to wonder about in this incredible cave, wherein the lights are dying out, as I observe with some discomfort. And I think the air grows chillier.”
Then Jurgen looked to his right, at the stairway which he and Guenevere had ascended; and he shook his head. “Glathion is no fit resort for a respectable pawnbroker. Chivalry is for young people, like the late Duke of Logreus. But I must get out of this place, for certainly there is in the air a deathlike chill.”

===Chinese===

故事讲到，洞穴深处漆黑如墨，朱尔根（Jurgen）举目四望不见半个人影。然而洞窟笔直向前且一路向下延伸，在极遥远的尽头泛着一抹微光。朱尔根步履不停向前走去，就这般抵临了昔日涅索斯（Nessus）伏击朱尔根的故地。朱尔根再次俯首弓腰，从洞壁的豁口中匍匐爬了过去，来到了油灯高挑于铁架之上的所在。此刻，那些油灯正一盏接一盏地接连熄灭，此间亦再无任何妇人的踪影：取而代之的是，朱尔根深一脚浅一脚地踩在厚达一英寸的细腻白灰之中，在灰烬上印下了清晰的足迹。

他顺着洞窟的延伸继续前行。他走到了洞穴的一处急转弯处，微弱黯淡的残灯灯火此刻落在了他身后，以致他的阴影正正投映在朱尔根眼前，模糊却无可辩驳。那赫然是一个平庸无奇的老迈典当掌柜所该有的周正阴影，朱尔根颇带赞许地端详着它。

随后朱尔根迈步踏入了一间形同地窖的大厅，顶棚正中悬挂着一口翻滚着颤巍巍赤红烈焰的铁釜。正对着他的是一座王座，王座背后整饬地排布着几排长凳：然而此处同样空无一人。倚靠在空荡王座上的是一面三角形素白盾牌：待朱尔根凑近细瞧，发觉其上赫然铭刻着文字。朱尔根将这面盾牌尽可能端至铁釜火焰近旁，因他当下的眼力已然大不如前，况且铁釜里的残焰正渐显微弱：朱尔根借着微光，吃力地辨认出盾牌上用红黑双色墨迹书就的留言。

“因要务公干外出，”上面写道，“1个小时（一个时辰）内回转。”署名是：“斯拉格纳王（Thragnar R.）。”

“我倒真纳了闷了，斯拉格纳国王留下这通告示究竟是给谁瞧的？”朱尔根暗自琢磨——“左右绝非留给我的。我亦不知他究竟是一年前留在此处、抑或今晚才刚刚留下？且在黑银凉亭里被我一剑枭首的，究竟是不是斯拉格纳的头颅？唉，罢啦，在这座不可思议的怪异洞窟深处，值得纳罕琢磨的事体委实太多了；四下里的灯火正在幽幽熄灭，瞧得我心头泛起阵阵发毛。且我发觉此间的阴风正变得愈发冰寒刺骨。”

随即朱尔根侧目望向右侧，望向昔日他同吉尼薇尔曾拾级而上的石阶；他摇了摇头。“格拉西昂断非一个体面正派的当铺掌柜该涉足的适宜之所。骑士道风流向来是属于年轻后生的玩闹，正如那位业已作古的洛格鲁斯公爵。我必须及早抽身脱离此地，只因这空气中分明弥漫着一股死寂一般的阴森透骨寒气。”

===Original===

So Jurgen went on down the aisle between the rows of benches wherefrom Thragnar’s warriors had glared at Jurgen when he was last in this part of the cave. At the end of the aisle was a wooden door painted white. It was marked, in large black letters, “Office of the Manager﻿—Keep Out.” So Jurgen opened this door.
He entered into a notable place illuminated by six cresset lights. These lights were the power of Assyria, and Nineveh, and Egypt, and Rome, and Athens, and Byzantium: six other cressets stood ready there, but fire had not yet been laid to these. Back of all was a large blackboard with much figuring on it in red chalk. And here, too, was the black gentleman, who a year ago had given his blessing to Jurgen, for speaking civilly of the powers of darkness. Tonight the black gentleman wore a black dressing-gown that was embroidered with all the signs of the Zodiac. He sat at a table, the top of which was curiously inlaid with thirty pieces of silver: and he was copying entries from one big book into another. He looked up from his writing pleasantly enough, and very much as though he were expecting Jurgen.
“You find me busy with the Stellar Accounts,” says he, “which appear to be in a fearful muddle. But what more can I do for you, Jurgen?﻿—for you, my friend, who spoke a kind word for things as they are, and furnished me with one or two really very acceptable explanations as to why I had created evil?”
“I have been thinking, Prince﻿—” begins the pawnbroker.
“And why do you call me a prince, Jurgen?”
“I do not know, sir. But I suspect that my quest is ended, and that you are Koshchei the Deathless.”
The black gentleman nodded. “Something of the sort. Koshchei, or Ardnari, or Ptha, or Jaldalaoth, or Abraxas﻿—it is all one what I may be called hereabouts. My real name you never heard: no man has ever heard my name. So that matter we need hardly go into.”
“Precisely, Prince. Well, but it is a long way that I have traveled roundabout, to win to you who made things as they are. And it is eager I am to learn just why you made things as they are.”
Up went the black gentleman’s eyebrows into regular Gothic arches. “And do you really think, Jurgen, that I am going to explain to you why I made things as they are?”
“I fail to see, Prince, how my wanderings could have any other equitable climax.”
“But, friend, I have nothing to do with justice.

===Chinese===

于是朱尔根顺着长凳之间的过道继续前行——昔年他上一次涉足此间洞穴时，斯拉格纳麾下的彪悍武士正是坐在那些长凳上恶狠狠地怒目瞪着朱尔根。在过道的尽头横亘着一扇漆成白色的木门。门上用粗黑墨字赫然醒目地标着：“掌柜公事房——闲人免进（Office of the Manager—Keep Out）。”朱尔根遂伸手推开了这扇门。

他踏入了一处由6盏（六盏）吊盆油灯映照得通明的奇特密室。这6盏油灯分别象征着亚述、尼尼微、埃及、罗马、雅典与拜占庭的赫赫国运威能：另外6盏吊盆油灯亦在旁整装待发，唯独尚未曾引火点燃。在凡百陈设背后是一块巨大的黑板，上面用红粉笔密密麻麻地勾勒着繁复运算。且在此间端坐着的，赫然正是那位一年前因朱尔根对幽暗冥界的公允美言而向他赐福的黑衣绅士。今夜这位黑衣绅士身披一袭绣着黄道十二宫（Zodiac）全套星象符文的黑色晨袍。他端坐在一张几案之后，案面上极其神妙地镶嵌着30枚（三十枚）斑驳银币：他正专心致志地将账目从一本大部头账册腾抄录入另一本之中。他从笔耕中抬起头来，神情颇为和蔼可亲，活脱脱仿佛早已料定朱尔根今夜必会造访一般。

“你瞧老夫正忙着梳理这本‘星象大账’呢，”他开口说道，“里头瞧来简直是一团烂账乱麻。不过老夫还能为你朱尔根效劳些什么呢？——为你，我亲爱的朋友，你曾替万物的本来面目说过公道话，并曾向老夫奉献过一两通关于老夫因何创设罪恶的极教人受用的精辟开脱解说。”

“在下一直在琢磨，亲王殿下——”典当掌柜试探着开腔道。

“可你为何偏偏称呼老夫为‘亲王’，朱尔根？”

“在下亦参不透个中缘由，先生。但在下疑心自己的苦苦求索已然到了终局，而您尊驾正是那位不死者科什切（Koshchei the Deathless）。”

黑衣绅士微微颔首。“大抵确是这等名堂吧。科什切也好，阿德纳里（Ardnari）也罢，抑或是普塔（Ptha）、雅勒达鲍斯（Jaldalaoth）乃至阿布拉克萨斯（Abraxas）——此间世人究竟如何称呼老夫皆无甚差别。老夫真正的名讳你生平从未得闻：天地间凡夫肉胎无一人曾得闻老夫的真名。故而这桩细枝末节我们大可不必深究。”

“千真万确，亲王殿下。哎，为了寻访创设万物如其所是的您，在下当真兜兜转转跋涉过了万水千山。在下心急如焚地渴望领教，您当初究竟因何将万物创设为如今这般本来面目。”

黑衣绅士的眉毛立时高高挑起，弯成了一道标准的哥特式尖拱拱门。“难道你当真痴心妄想以为，朱尔根，老夫会向你剖白自己因何创设万物如其所是？”

“在下委实瞧不出，亲王殿下，在下的茫茫远游若无这等公允交代，还能有何等完满终局。”

“可我的好朋友，老夫同所谓正义从来不沾半点干系。

===Original===

To the contrary, I am Koshchei who made things as they are.”
Jurgen saw the point. “Your reasoning, Prince, is unanswerable. I bow to it. I should even have foreseen it. Do you tell me, then, what thing is this which I desire, and cannot find in any realm that man has known nor in any kingdom that man has imagined.”
Koshchei was very patient. “I am not, I confess, anything like as well acquainted with what has been going on in this part of the universe as I ought to be. Of course, events are reported to me, in a general sort of way, and some of my people were put in charge of these stars, a while back: but they appear to have run the constellation rather shiftlessly. Still, I have recently been figuring on the matter, and I do not despair of putting the suns hereabouts to some profitable use, in one way or another, after all. Of course, it is not as if it were an important constellation. But I am an Economist, and I dislike waste﻿—”
Then he was silent for an instant, not greatly worried by the problem, as Jurgen could see, but mildly vexed by his inability to divine the solution out of hand. Presently Koshchei said:
“And in the meantime, Jurgen, I am afraid I cannot answer your question on the spur of the moment. You see, there appears to have been a great number of human beings, as you call them, evolved upon﻿—oh, yes!﻿—upon Earth. I have the approximate figures over yonder, but they would hardly interest you. And the desires of each one of these human beings seem to have been multitudinous and inconstant. Yet, Jurgen, you might appeal to the local authorities, for I remember appointing some, at the request of a very charming old lady.”
“In fine, you do not know what thing it is that I desire,” said Jurgen, much surprised.
“Why, no, I have not the least notion,” replied Koshchei. “Still, I suspect that if you got it you would protest it was a most unjust affliction. So why keep worrying about it?”
Jurgen demanded, almost indignantly: “But have you not then, Prince, been guiding all my journeying during this last year?”
“Now, really, Jurgen, I remember our little meeting very pleasantly. And I endeavored forthwith to dispose of your most urgent annoyance. But I confess I have had one or two other matters upon my mind since then. You see, Jurgen, the universe is rather large, and the running of it is a considerable tax upon my time.

===Chinese===

恰恰相反，老夫乃是创设万物本来面目的科什切。”

朱尔根参透了个中要害。“您的立论，亲王殿下，委实教人无可辩驳。在下甘拜下风。在下甚至早该料及此节。那么且请您明示在下，我心中苦苦渴求的究竟是何物——为何在凡人所知晓的任何疆土、在凡人所构想的任何国度之中，我皆寻觅不到它的踪迹？”

科什切展现出了极大的涵养与耐心。“老夫坦白承认，对于这方宇宙疆域里近来发生的凡百流转，老夫的知晓程度委实远不及理当达到的那般透彻。当然，各方动静自会向老夫作个大致纲要汇报，前些日子老夫麾下的某些部属曾被指派掌管这批星辰：可他们打理起这片星座来似乎颇显漫不经心、敷衍了事。不过老夫近来正重新拨弄算盘，对于将此间的几颗太阳在往后经由某种途径派充更有利可图的用场，老夫倒并未彻底绝望。当然，它终归算不上什么举足轻重的显赫星座。然而老夫是个讲求经世节用的大掌柜，素来最厌恶暴殄天物——”

说到此处，他默然沉吟了片刻，正如朱尔根所见，他并未真正被这道算题困扰，无非是因无从随手参破谜底而略感愠怒烦扰。少顷科什切说道：

“而在眼下当口，朱尔根，老夫恐怕无从脱口答复你这道难题。你瞧，在这片被你们称作——噢，对啦！——被称作‘地球’的尘埃之上，似乎繁衍演化出了难以计数的芸芸众生。老夫在那边搁着大致的统计筹算底册，不过那些干瘪数目你大抵毫无兴致。而这芸芸众生中每一个凡人的欲念，瞧来皆是千头万绪、朝三暮四。不过朱尔根，你倒不妨向本处的官府衙门请旨鸣冤，因老夫依稀记得，昔年曾应一位慈眉善目的老太太之恳请，在此间委任过几位执事神明。”

“总而言之，您压根儿不知晓我心中所渴望的究竟是何物，”朱尔根大感惊诧道。

“哎，可不是嘛，老夫毫无半点头绪，”科什切答道。“然而老夫隐隐疑心，纵然有朝一日你果真得偿所愿，你亦必定会拍着大腿指天画地痛骂那是一场极不公道的飞来横祸。既然如此，你又何苦为此成日价愁肠百结呢？”

朱尔根近乎义愤填膺地质问道：“可在这过去的一整年里，亲王殿下，难道冥冥之中不是您尊驾一直在指引着我的一路云游么？”

“哎呀朱尔根，讲真格的，老夫对昔日你我二人的萍水相逢至今记忆犹新、甚感快慰。且老夫当时立时便雷厉风行地替你打发掉了最迫在眉睫的烦心事。但老夫坦白承认，自那之后老夫脑子里挂念的要务委实多如牛毛。你该懂得，朱尔根，这浩瀚宇宙委实太大太大，操持运转它耗费了老夫海量的光阴精力。

===Original===

I cannot manage to see anything like as much of my friends as I would be delighted to see of them. And so perhaps, what with one thing and another, I have not given you my undivided attention all through the year﻿—not every moment of it, that is.”
“Ah, Prince, I see that you are trying to spare my feelings, and it is kind of you. But the upshot is that you do not know what I have been doing, and you did not care what I was doing. Dear me! but this is a very sad comedown for my pride.”
“Yes, but reflect how remarkable a possession is that pride of yours, and how I wonder at it, and how I envy it in vain﻿—I, who have nothing anywhere to contemplate save my own handiwork. Do you consider, Jurgen, what I would give if I could find, anywhere in this universe of mine, anything which would make me think myself one-half so important as you think Jurgen is!” And Koshchei sighed.
But instead, Jurgen considered the humiliating fact that Koshchei had not been supervising Jurgen’s travels. And of a sudden Jurgen perceived that this Koshchei the Deathless was not particularly intelligent. Then Jurgen wondered why he should ever have expected Koshchei to be intelligent? Koshchei was omnipotent, as men estimate omnipotence: but by what course of reasoning had people come to believe that Koshchei was clever, as men estimate cleverness? The fact that, to the contrary, Koshchei seemed well-meaning, but rather slow of apprehension and a little needlessly fussy, went far toward explaining a host of matters which had long puzzled Jurgen. Cleverness was, of course, the most admirable of all traits: but cleverness was not at the top of things, and never had been. “Very well, then!” says Jurgen, with a shrug; “let us come to my third request and to the third thing that I have been seeking. Here, though, you ought to be more communicative. For I have been thinking, Prince, my wife’s society is perhaps becoming to you a trifle burdensome.”
“Eh, sirs, I am not unaccustomed to women. I may truthfully say that as I find them, so do I take them. And I was willing to oblige a fellow rebel.”
“But I do not know, Prince, that I have ever rebelled. Far from it, I have everywhere conformed with custom.”
“Your lips conformed, but all the while your mind made verses, Jurgen. And poetry is man’s rebellion against being what he is.”
“﻿—And besides, you call me a fellow rebel.

===Chinese===

放眼所及，老夫根本无暇去如愿多照拂照拂诸位老朋友。因而多半是因着百务缠身，老夫在过去这一整年里并未曾将全副心神全情倾注于你一身——至少，绝非每一分每一秒皆在照拂你。”

“啊哈，亲王殿下，我瞧得出您是在设法顾全我的颜面，这委实是您的大度体贴。可说到底，您根本不知晓我这一年里究竟干了些什么勾当，且您压根儿毫不关心我的所作所为。天哪！这待我朱尔根素来的傲骨尊严而言，委实是一记令人伤感至极的当头棒喝。”

“话虽如此，可你且反思反思，你身上那股自命不凡的傲骨是何等罕世的至宝啊！连老夫都对其啧啧称奇，甚至徒劳地心生艳羡——老夫放眼寰宇，除了自家的造物手笔之外再无任何外物可供驻足端详！你且想一想，朱尔根，倘若在这浩瀚宇宙的任何角落，老夫能寻觅到任何能让老夫自视有你自视朱尔根之一半这般举足轻重的事物，老夫该愿意倾尽何等代价啊！”说着，科什切长叹了一口气。

然而朱尔根心头盘旋着的，却是科什切未曾亲自关照朱尔根云游这一令人无地自容的难堪事实。朱尔根冷不防猛然醒悟：眼前这位不死者科什切，似乎并不怎么格外聪颖过人。随即朱尔根不由得暗自纳罕，自己生平为何会一厢情愿指望科什切是个绝顶聪明的智者呢？依世人对通天全能的估量，科什切固然是全能的；可世人究竟是凭着何等荒唐的推论，认定科什切必如凡人所标榜的那般精明狡黠呢？事实恰恰相反，科什切瞧来心存善念，可心智理解却颇显迟钝迟缓，且不免有几分小题大做、瞎忙活的琐碎，而这一真相恰恰极圆满地解释通了长久以来盘桓在朱尔根心头的无数悬案谜题。机敏聪慧固然是世间最教人叹服的无上禀赋：然而机智聪颖却从来未曾真正登顶执掌万物的乾坤之巅。“既然这般，甚好！”朱尔根耸了耸肩说道；“那我们便不妨直奔在下的第三桩请旨、直奔在下一路苦苦寻觅的第三桩物事吧。不过在这一节骨眼上，您理当更推心置腹些才是。因在下一直在琢磨，亲王殿下，拙荆的常伴身侧，对您尊驾而言大抵已渐成一桩微不足道却挥之不去的烦扰累赘了吧。”

“哎呀诸位先生，老夫对妇道人家向来习以为常。老夫大可凭良心讲，凡是碰上的妇人，老夫向来兵来将挡顺其自然。且老夫昔日亦乐于顺手周全一位同道叛逆同侪的难处。”

“可在下并不知晓自己平生何曾有过谋逆造反的行径，亲王殿下。恰恰相反，在下无论走到何处皆恪守遵从世俗礼法。”

“你嘴唇上固然顺从礼法，可你脑子里却成日价在赋诗填词，朱尔根。而诗歌，本就是人类对自己肉身皮囊局限的不甘谋逆抗争。”

“——况且，尊驾方才称在下为同道叛逆同侪。

===Original===

Now, how can it be possible that Koshchei, who made all things as they are, should be a rebel? unless, indeed, there is some power above even Koshchei. I would very much like to have that explained to me, sir.”
“No doubt: but then why should I explain it to you, Jurgen?” says the black gentleman.
“Well, be that as it may, Prince! But﻿—to return a little﻿—I do not know that you have obliged me in carrying off my wife. I mean, of course, my first wife.”
“Why, Jurgen,” says the black gentleman, in high astonishment, “do you mean to tell me that you want the plague of your life back again!”
“I do not know about that either, sir. She was certainly very hard to live with. On the other hand, I had become used to having her about. I rather miss her, now that I am again an elderly person. Indeed, I believe I have missed Lisa all along.”
The black gentleman meditated. “Come, friend,” he says, at last. “You were a poet of some merit. You displayed a promising talent which might have been cleverly developed, in any suitable environment. Now, I repeat, I am an Economist: I dislike waste: and you were never fitted to be anything save a poet. The trouble was”﻿—and Koshchei lowered his voice to an impressive whisper﻿—“the trouble was your wife did not understand you. She hindered your art. Yes, that precisely sums it up: she interfered with your soul-development, and your instinctive need of self-expression, and all that sort of thing. You are very well rid of this woman, who converted a poet into a pawnbroker. To the other side, as is with point observed somewhere or other, it is not good for man to live alone. But, friend, I have just the wife for you.”
“Well, Prince,” said Jurgen, “I am willing to taste any drink once.”
So Koshchei waved his hand: and there, quick as winking, was the loveliest lady that Jurgen had ever imagined.

===Chinese===

既然如此，创设万物如其所是的堂堂科什切，又怎生可能自身亦是个叛逆之徒呢？除非，在这天地之间，果真尚有某种凌驾于科什切之上的更高无上主宰。关于此节，先生，在下委实渴望能蒙您指点迷津。”

“那是自然：可老夫为何偏偏要向你朱尔根剖白这些呢？”黑衣绅士反问道。

“哎，姑且不论此节，亲王殿下！然而——让我们略微重返方才的由头——在下倒真摸不准，您替在下把浑家掳走究竟算不算得上周全了在下。在下所指的，自然是我的结发妻子。”

“天哪，朱尔根，”黑衣绅士大惊失色道，“难道你兜了这么大一圈，竟意欲告诉我你想把那桩生平最大的灾星再领回去不成！”

“关于这一点在下亦心中无底，先生。同她过日子确实难熬得紧。然而从另一方面讲，在下早已习惯了身边有这么个人絮絮叨叨。如今重归老迈风霜之年，在下反倒对她颇生怀念。事实上，在下私心以为，这一路行来我自始至终皆在牵挂着丽莎（Lisa）。”

黑衣绅士陷入了长考。“得了吧，好朋友，”他终于开腔道。“你昔年曾是个颇有造诣的文人诗人。你曾展露过令人刮目相看的过人天赋，但凡换作任何适宜的水土，本皆能大加施展栽培。如今老夫重申一句，老夫是个讲求经世节用的大掌柜：老夫最厌恶虚掷浪费：而你生来除了当个诗人之外，根本不配充当任何旁的行当。昔日的大患症结在于”——说到此处，科什切压低嗓音，凑近附耳吐露出一阵沉重威严的耳语——“症结在于你浑家压根儿读不懂你。她扼杀了你的诗文艺术。没错，这便是对症下药的盖棺定论：她横加阻挠了你灵魂深处的造化升华，践踏了你本能中抒发自我的天性渴望，以及诸如此类的凡百雅事。能将这个把你从一代诗雄生生逼成个当铺掌柜的泼悍妇人打发得一干二净，本是你天大的造化气数。而从另一则讲，正如某处前贤极精辟剖白过的那样，孤身独居向来非汉子之福。不过好朋友，老夫手头恰好有一位极契合你的绝色佳偶。”

“哎，亲王殿下，”朱尔根答道，“天下杯中物凡是甘霖，朱尔根皆愿尝上一尝。”

于是科什切大袖一挥：眨眼工夫，朱尔根平生所能构想的最明艳动人的绝代佳人，赫然亭亭玉立在眼前。

## XXVI In Time’s Hourglass / 第二十六章 在时光的沙漏里
===Original===

“Well, well!” said Jurgen, when he had taken off all that foolish ironmongery, and had made himself comfortable in his shirt; “well, beyond doubt, the situation is awkward. I was content enough in Cocaigne, and it is unfair that I should be thus ousted. Still, a sensible person will manage to be content anywhere. But whither, pray, am I expected to go?”
“Into whatever land you may elect, my dear,” said Anaïtis, fondly. “That much at least I can manage for you: and the interpretation of your legend can be arranged afterward.”
“But I grow tired of all the countries I have ever seen, dear Anaïtis, and in my time I have visited nearly all the lands that are known to men.”
“That too can be arranged: and you can go instead into one of the countries which are desired by men. Indeed there are a number of such realms which no man has ever visited except in dreams, so that your choice is wide.”
“But how am I to make a choice without having seen any of these countries? It is not fair to be expecting me to do anything of the sort.”
“Why, I will show them to you,” Anaïtis replied.
The two of them then went together into a small blue chamber, the walls of which were ornamented with gold stars placed helter-skelter. The room was entirely empty save for an hourglass near twice the height of a man.
“It is Time’s own glass,” said Anaïtis, “which was left in my keeping when Time went to sleep.”
Anaïtis opened a little door of carved crystal that was in the lower half of the hourglass, just above the fallen sands. With her fingertips she touched the sand that was in Time’s hourglass, and in the sand she drew a triangle with equal sides, she who was strangely gifted and perverse. Then she drew just such another figure so that the tip of it penetrated the first triangle. The sand began to smoulder there, and vapors rose into the upper part of the hourglass, and Jurgen saw that all the sand in Time’s hourglass was kindled by a magic generated by the contact of these two triangles. And in the vapors a picture formed.
“I see a land of woods and rivers, Anaïtis. A very old fellow, regally crowned, lies asleep under an ash-tree, guarded by a watchman who has more arms and hands than Jigsbyed.”
“It is Atlantis you behold, and the sleeping of ancient Time﻿—Time, to whom this glass belongs﻿—while Briareus watches.”

===Chinese===

“哎呀呀！”当朱尔根（Jurgen）卸下浑身那些愚蠢可笑的破铜烂铁甲胄、穿上衬衫重新落得一身舒坦惬意之际，他开口说道；“哎呀，毫无疑问，当下的处境委实尴尬至极。我在科凯恩（Cocaigne）本过得心满意足，这般将我扫地出门未免有失公允。不过，一个通情达理的明智之人，走到天涯海角皆能设法随遇而安。可敢问心肝，我究竟理当被发配往何方神圣之境？”

“凡是你挑中合意的任何疆土，我的心肝肉儿，”阿娜伊蒂斯（Anaïtis）温存脉脉地答道。“至少这一节我尚能为你张罗周全：至于关于你的神话传说的阐释穿凿，大可容后再慢慢编排。”

“可对于我平生所见过的所有凡尘俗邦，我早已腻烦得无以复加，我亲爱的阿娜伊蒂斯；且在昔年岁月里，凡人所知晓的世间疆土，我几乎已踏遍了个底朝天。”

“那一节倒亦极好打发：你索性转道前往那些世人苦苦渴求的仙邦福地便罢。实不相瞒，这等人迹罕至、唯有在虚无幻梦中方得一游的梦幻王国不胜枚举，足可供你随心挑选。”

“可在未曾亲眼得见那些国度之前，我又怎生拿得定主意？指望我盲人摸象般凭空抉择，委实有失公允。”

“哎，我亲自为你逐一展示便了，”阿娜伊蒂斯答道。

二人遂联袂步入一间素雅的蔚蓝密室，室内的四壁零零星星、错落有致地点缀着金光闪闪的星辰。整座殿室空空如也，唯独正中矗立着一座将近两人多高的巍峨沙漏。

“这正是时光之神（Time）自家的本命沙漏，”阿娜伊蒂斯说道，“昔年时光之神陷入沉睡之际，特托付于我手中好生看管。”

阿娜伊蒂斯伸手拉开嵌在沙漏下半截、恰位于积落沙砾上方的一扇雕花水晶小门。她以葱白玉指轻轻拨弄着时光沙漏中的沙砾，在沙面上勾勒出一个三边相等的三角形——她向来禀赋神异且行事乖张。随即她依样画葫芦又勾勒出另一个形状肖似的三角形，令其尖端恰好刺入第一个三角形的心腹。沙砾随即在交接处冒出青烟、隐隐灼烧起来，缕缕轻烟升腾入沙漏的上半截，朱尔根惊异地瞧见，时光沙漏深处的整片沙海，尽皆被这2个等边三角形相交所催生出的神异法力点燃了。在弥漫的轻烟缭绕之中，一幅画卷徐徐幻化成形。

“我瞧见了一片古木参天、千帆竞渡的浩瀚疆土，阿娜伊蒂斯。一位头戴巍峨王冠的龙钟老朽正酣睡在一株梣树之下，身畔伫立着一位比吉格斯比耶德（Jigsbyed）还要生出更多臂膀巨手的守夜守卫。”

“你所窥见的乃是亚特兰蒂斯（Atlantis），以及太古时光之神的安详酣眠——这座沙漏的主人时光之神——百臂巨人布里阿柔斯（Briareus）此刻正忠心耿耿地守望在侧。”

===Original===

“Time sleeps quite naked, Anaïtis, and, though it is a delicate matter to talk about, I notice he has met with a deplorable accident.”
“So that Time begets nothing any more, Jurgen, the while he brings about old happenings over and over, and changes the name of what is ancient, in order to persuade himself he has a new plaything. There is really no more tedious and wearing old dotard anywhere, I can assure you. But Atlantis is only the western province of Cocaigne. Now do you look again, Jurgen!”
“Now I behold a flowering plain and three steep hills, with a castle upon each hill. There are woods wherein the foliage is crimson: shining birds with white bodies and purple heads feed upon the clusters of golden berries that grow everywhere: and people go about in green clothes, with gold chains about their necks, and with broad bands of gold upon their arms, and all these people have untroubled faces.”
“That is Inislocha: and to the south is Inis Daleb, and to the north Inis Ercandra. And there is sweet music to be listening to eternally, could we but hear the birds of Rhiannon, and there is the best of wine to drink, and there delight is common. For thither comes nothing hard nor rough, and no grief, nor any regret, nor sickness, nor age, nor death, for this is the Land of Women, a land of many-colored hospitality.”
“Why, then, it is no different from Cocaigne. And into no realm where pleasure is endless will I ever venture again of my own free will, for I find that I do not enjoy pleasure.”
Then Anaïtis showed him Ogygia, and Tryphême, and Sudarsana, and the Fortunate Islands, and Æaea, and Caer-Is, and Invallis, and the Hesperides, and Meropis, and Planasia, and Uttarra, and Avalon, and Tir-nam-Beo, and Thelême, and a number of other lands to enter which men have desired: and Jurgen groaned.
“I am ashamed of my fellows,” says he: “for it appears their notion of felicity is to dwell eternally in a glorified brothel. I do not think that as a self-respecting young Prince I would care to inhabit any of these earthly paradises, for were there nothing else, I would always be looking for an invasion by the police.”
“There remains, then, but one other realm, which I have not shown you, in part because it is an obscure little place, and in part because, for a reason that I have, I shall not assist you to go thither.

===Chinese===

“时光之神睡得赤条条一丝不挂呢，阿娜伊蒂斯；且虽说这是件难登大雅之堂的隐秘体己话，可我留神到他老人家似乎曾遭逢过一场惨绝人寰的去势飞来横祸。”

“故而时光之神往后余生再无从孕育点化出任何崭新造物了，朱尔根；他唯有一圈又一圈地将前尘旧事周而复始地重演，给陈年腐物换上一副副崭新名目，借以哄骗自个儿仿佛坐拥了崭新的玩物。放眼寰宇，再无何人比这个讨人嫌的糊涂老迈昏愦老朽更教人腻烦透顶了，这一点我大可向你担保。不过亚特兰蒂斯充其量不过是科凯恩西陲的一个行省罢了。你且定睛再瞧，朱尔根！”

“眼下我瞧见了一处繁花似锦的锦绣平原，以及3座险峻山峦（三座山峦），每座山巅之上皆矗立着一座巍峨古堡。树林间的枝叶如枫火般猩红璀璨：周身素白、头戴紫冠的斑斓异鸟正贪婪啄食着遍地垂挂的金黄浆果：四下里的人群皆身披翠绿锦衣，项悬金链，臂挽宽阔的金镯，且所有人的面庞上尽皆洋溢着毫无挂碍的恬淡安详。”

“那处仙境唤作伊尼斯洛哈（Inislocha）：其南邻伊尼斯达勒布（Inis Daleb），北接伊尼斯埃坎德拉（Inis Ercandra）。在那儿，倘若我们能有幸聆听里安农之鸟（birds of Rhiannon）的天籁啼鸣，便有永生不绝的仙乐悦耳动听，更有天下至醇的琼浆美酒任凭痛饮，四处流淌着人尽可享的无上欢愉。因任何苦涩粗粝、悲戚悔恨、疾疫衰朽抑或死亡阴影皆绝无可能涉足该处，全因那便是名扬四海的‘女人之国’（Land of Women）——一片洋溢着斑斓好客温存的温柔乡。”

“哎，既然这般，那它同科凯恩又有何两样？凡是欢愉永无止境的淫乐国度，我朱尔根凭着自家的清醒心智，往后断不愿再去涉足半步；因我早已看破，我生平根本无福消受这等无休无止的极乐。”

随即阿娜伊蒂斯又引他饱览了俄吉吉亚（Ogygia）、特里菲姆（Tryphême）、苏达沙纳（Sudarsana）、福地群岛（Fortunate Islands）、艾埃亚（Æaea）、凯尔-伊斯（Caer-Is）、因瓦利斯（Invallis）、赫斯珀里得斯金苹果园（Hesperides）、梅罗皮斯（Meropis）、普拉纳西亚（Planasia）、乌塔拉（Uttarra）、阿瓦隆（Avalon）、金生之国（Tir-nam-Beo）与德雷姆（Thelême），以及芸芸凡夫苦苦求索向往的无数世外洞天：朱尔根看得连连哀声长叹。

“我真替我凡尘俗世的同侪同胞脸红害臊，”他叹道：“因世人脑海中对终极极乐的全部构想，无非是能在一方披着璀璨金光的豪华销金窑青楼里生生世世厮混下去。我自忖身为一个自尊自爱的翩翩青年王夫（Prince），断不屑于常驻在这凡百人间乐园之中；哪怕别无挂碍，我亦会终日提心吊胆，成日价防备着巡城捕快衙役登门查抄呢。”

“如此说来，普天之下便唯独只剩最后一处疆土未曾向你展露了；其一因它不过是个名不见经传的偏狭弹丸之地，其二则因着我私心里的由头，我断不愿襄助你转赴该处。

===Original===

Still, there is Leukê, where Queen Helen rules: and Leukê it is that you behold.”
“But Leukê seems like any other country in autumn, and appears to be reasonably free from the fantastic animals and overgrown flowers which made the other paradises look childish. Come now, there is an attractive simplicity about Leukê. I might put up with Leukê if the local bylaws allowed me a rational amount of discomfort.”
“Discomfort you would have full measure. For the heart of no man remains untroubled after he has once viewed Queen Helen and the beauty that is hers. It is for that reason, Jurgen, I shall not help you to go into Leukê: for in Leukê you would forget me, having seen Queen Helen.”
“Why, what nonsense you are talking, my darling! I will wager she cannot hold a candle to you.”
“See for yourself!” said Anaïtis, sadly.
Now through the rolling vapors came confusedly a gleaming and a surging glitter of all the loveliest colors of heaven and earth: and these took order presently, and Jurgen saw before him in the hourglass that young Dorothy who was not Heitman Michael’s wife. And long and wistfully he looked at her, and the blinding tears came to his eyes for no reason at all, and for the while he could not speak.
Then Jurgen yawned, and said, “But certainly this is not the Helen who was famed for beauty.”
“I can assure you that it is,” said Anaïtis: “and that it is she who rules in Leukê, whither I do not intend you shall go.”
“Why, but, my darling! this is preposterous. The girl is nothing to look at twice, one way or the other. She is not actually ugly, I suppose, if one happens to admire that washed-out blonde type, as of course some people do. But to call her beautiful is out of reason; and that I must protest in simple justice.”
“Do you really think so?” says Anaïtis, brightening.
“I most assuredly do. Why, you remember what Calpurnius Bassus says about all blondes?”
“No, I believe not. What did he say, dear?”
“I would only spoil the splendid passage by quoting it inaccurately from memory. But he was quite right, and his opinion is mine in every particular. So if that is the best Leukê can offer, I heartily agree with you I had best go into some other country.”
“I suppose you already have your eyes upon some minx or other?”

===Chinese===

然而，世间终归尚有一处留刻岛（Leukê），乃是由海伦王后（Queen Helen）统御执掌：可放眼四方，并无何人能抵御海伦王后及其那副倾国倾城的天香国色。正是因着这层忌惮，朱尔根，我决计不愿放你涉足留刻岛半步：因一旦行抵留刻岛、亲眼瞻仰了海伦王后的芳容，你顷刻间便会将我抛诸脑后忘得一干二净。”

“哎呀心肝肉儿，你这是在扯哪门子胡言乱语！我敢同你打赌，凭她的造化姿色，替你端茶掌灯提鞋都不配。”

“你不妨亲眼端详端详！”阿娜伊蒂斯满面凄楚地叹道。

说话间，自翻滚升腾的轻烟深处，迷离朦胧地泛起一阵令人目眩神迷的流光溢彩，尽聚了九天十地凡百至艳至美之色：未几诸色渐渐分明定格，朱尔根在沙漏深处的迷雾中，赫然瞧见了当年那位尚未委身下嫁盖特曼·迈克尔（Heitman Michael）的年轻多萝西（Dorothy）！他神魂颠倒、如痴如醉地久久凝望着她，双目不知怎的竟莫名涌满了滚沸的热泪，在这一刹那，他喉头哽咽竟吐不出半个字来。

随即朱尔根大大咧咧打了个哈欠，故作轻佻道：“可这断然不可能是那位名满天下的绝代佳人海伦呀。”

“我敢向你打包票正是她本尊，”阿娜伊蒂斯说道：“且正是统领留刻岛的那位女主，我绝不容许你涉足半步的绝地。”

“哎，我的心肝！这委实太荒唐可笑了。不论打哪头瞧，这丫头根本不值当教人多看第二眼。倘若谁人偏生犯贱偏爱这等寡淡苍白的金发小家碧玉——世间自然少不了这等俗胚——她倒大抵算不得丑八怪。可要把她吹嘘成绝代佳人，未免太丧失理智了；出于至纯至正的公道良心，我断然要向这等谬论提出抗辩。”

“你果真这般作想？”阿娜伊蒂斯闻言，黛眉微展，眸子顿时一亮。

“那是千真万确。哎，难道你忘了卡尔普尼乌斯·巴苏斯（Calpurnius Bassus）当年评述凡百金发娇娃的那段警句名言了么？”

“不曾读过，亲爱的。他老人家当年究竟怎生评说来着？”

“凭着我粗陋残缺的记性若背诵得走样，只会玷污了那段精妙绝伦的篇章。但他老人家持论精辟至极，他的高论在方方面面尽皆深得我心。故而倘若这便是留刻岛所能呈奉的最高成色，我十二分赞同夫人的高见，我顶好还是另谋高就转投别处乐土为妙。”

“我料想你眼下那对贼眼珠子，早就盯上哪家的狐媚骚蹄子了吧？”

===Original===

“Well, my love, those girls in the Hesperides were strikingly like you, with even more wonderful hair than yours: and the girl Aillê whom we saw in Tir-nam-Beo likewise resembled you remarkably, except that I thought she had the better figure. So I believe in either of those countries I could be content enough, after a while. Since part from you I must,” said Jurgen, tenderly, “I intend, in common fairness to myself, to find a companion as like you as possible. You conceive I can pretend it is you at first: and then as I grow fonder of her for her own sake, you will gradually be put out of my mind without my incurring any intolerable anguish.”
Anaïtis was not pleased. “So you are already hankering after those hussies! And you think them better looking than I am! And you tell me so to my face!”
“My darling, you cannot deny we have been married all of three whole months: and nobody can maintain an infatuation for any woman that long, in the teeth of having nothing refused him. Infatuation is largely a matter of curiosity, and both of these emotions die when they are fed.”
“Jurgen,” said Anaïtis, with conviction, “you are lying to me about something. I can see it in your eyes.”
“There is no deceiving a woman’s intuition. Yes, I was not speaking quite honestly when I pretended I had as lief go into the Hesperides as to Tir-nam-Beo: it was wrong of me, and I ask your pardon. I thought that by affecting indifference I could manage you better. But you saw through me at once, and very rightly became angry. So I fling my cards upon the table, I no longer beat about the bushes of equivocation. It is Aillê, the daughter of Cormac, whom I love, and who can blame me? Did you ever in your life behold a more enticing figure, Anaïtis?﻿—certainly I never did. Besides, I noticed﻿—but never mind about that! Still I could not help seeing them. And then such eyes! twin beacons that light my way to comfort for my not inconsiderable regret at losing you, my darling. Oh, yes, assuredly it is to Tir-nam-Beo I elect to go.”
“Whither you go, my fine fellow, is a matter in which I have the choice, not you. And you are going to Leukê.”
“My love, now do be reasonable! We both agreed that Leukê was not a bit suitable. Why, were there nothing else, in Leukê there are no attractive women.”
“Have you no sense except book-sense! It is for that reason I am sending you to Leukê.”

===Chinese===

“哎，我最亲爱的人儿，赫斯珀里得斯金苹果园里的那些姑娘生得同你肖似得紧，甚至满头青丝秀发比你的还要风姿绰约；而在金生之国（Tir-nam-Beo）我们撞见的那个名叫艾勒（Aillê）的姑娘，同样神似你得惊人，唯独在我瞧来她的身段腰肢似乎更胜一筹。故而我笃信在那两个国度之中的任何一处，假以时日我皆能安居乐业。既然你我注定要分道扬镳，”朱尔根深情款款地说道，“出于对自家的公允体面，我打定主意要寻觅一位尽可能同你肖似的佳人常伴身侧。你当能体谅，起初我大可自欺欺人权当她便是你；而待到往后因着她自身的娇美而日渐生情之际，你在我脑海中的印记便会悄然随风淡去，而不至于教我承受撕心裂肺的钻心剧痛。”

阿娜伊蒂斯闻言勃然变色，心头大为光火。“好你个负心汉，原来你早就对那些狐媚贱婢垂涎欲滴了！且你竟敢当着老娘的面，指斥她们比我生得更为标志！甚至敢当着我的面大言不惭！”

“我心肝肉儿，你断无法否认我们结为秦晋之好已然足足有整整3个月（三个月）之久；而在任何百依百顺、予取予求的妇人面前，世间断无任一个汉子能将痴迷狂热维系如此之久。所谓神魂颠倒，大半皆源于猎奇的好奇心，而这两味心绪一旦饱餐餍足，顷刻间便会香消玉殒。”

“朱尔根，”阿娜伊蒂斯斩钉截铁地喝道，“你在某些事体上正冲着我信口雌黄。我单凭你那对眼珠子便能将你看个透穿！”

“妇道人家的通灵直觉委实无从蒙蔽。千真万确，方才我假装去赫斯珀里得斯抑或去金生之国皆全无所谓之际，我确乎未曾倾吐肺腑之言：这委实是在下的过失，在下向夫人告罪赔罪。我原本自忖借由故作冷淡能更易驾驭拿捏于你。然而你顷刻间便戳穿了我的伎俩，且理所当然地雷霆震怒。既然如此，我索性将手里的底牌一股脑儿摊在桌面上，再不闪烁其词顾左右而言他了。我平生真真切切所倾心苦恋者，乃是科马克之女艾勒（Aillê, the daughter of Cormac），天下谁人又能苛责我的痴心？阿娜伊蒂斯，你平生何曾见过更教人魂不守舍的曼妙身段？——天地良心，我朱尔根平生何曾见过。况且，我适才还留神到——罢啦，体己细处休要再提！可我终究身不由己撞见了。更有那一双秋水明眸！宛若指引我航程的一对并蒂灯塔，足可告慰我痛失夫人之余那深不见底的滔天痛惜。噢，千真万确，我决意转道奔赴金生之国。”

“你究竟该被发配去往何方绝地，我的俊后生，乃是由老娘挑肥拣瘦说了算，轮不着你自作主张！你给老娘滚去留刻岛！”

“心肝肉儿，尊驾行行好发发慈悲，讲点常理吧！方才我们明明一致断定，留刻岛万万去不得。哎，哪怕别无挂碍，留刻岛上压根儿连半个姿色入眼的妇人都没有啊！”

“除了死读书读出的呆气，你脑子里难道全装满了浆糊不成！恰恰因着这层缘由，老娘偏要把你发配去留刻岛！”

===Original===

And thus speaking, Anaïtis set about a strong magic that hastened the coming of the Equinox. In the midst of her charming she wept a little, for she was fond of Jurgen.
And Jurgen preserved a hurt and angry face as well as he could: for at the sight of Queen Helen, who was so like young Dorothy la Désirée, he had ceased to care for Queen Anaïtis and her diverting ways, or to care for aught else in the world save only Queen Helen, the delight of gods and men. But Jurgen had learned that Anaïtis required management.
“For her own good,” as he put it, “and in simple justice to the many admirable qualities which she possesses.”

===Chinese===

说话间，阿娜伊蒂斯立时施展出一道霸道无匹的大法术，硬生生催动加速了秋分点（Equinox）的骤降。在念念有词施展咒语的当口，她眼眶中禁不住滚落了几滴清泪，盖因她对朱尔根终归深情款款。

而朱尔根则竭尽所能维持着满面愤懑与委屈的怨嗔之色：只因自打方才亲睹海伦王后——那位同昔日年轻的众望所归的多萝西（Dorothy la Désirée）生得一般无二的绝代佳人——的真容那一刹那起，阿娜伊蒂斯女王及其那些花样百出的销魂手腕，便顷刻间化作了过眼云烟，整个人世间万物皆再入不得朱尔根的法眼，唯独只剩那位教诸神与凡夫心醉神迷的海伦王后。然而朱尔根早已深谙，伺候阿娜伊蒂斯这等妇人，全凭心机拿捏。

正如他心中暗自自得的那样：“全是为了她自个儿的好，且待她周身那诸多教人钦佩叹服的美德而言，亦不过是至为公允体面的成全罢了。”

## XXXVII Invention of the Lovely Vampire / 第三十七章 绝美吸血女妖的现身
===Original===

So again Coth parted with his son in anger, and Jurgen returned again toward Barathum; and, whether or not it was a coincidence, Jurgen met precisely the vampire of whom he had inveigled his father into thinking. She was the most seductively beautiful creature that it would be possible for Jurgen’s father or any other man to imagine: and her clothes were orange-colored, for a reason sufficiently well known in Hell, and were embroidered everywhere with green fig-leaves.
“A good morning to you, madame,” says Jurgen, “and whither are you going?”
