“Why, to no place at all, good youth. For this is my vacation, granted yearly by the Law of Kalki﻿—”
“And who is Kalki, madame?”
“Nobody as yet: but he will come as a stallion. Meanwhile his Law precedes him, so that I am spending my vacation peacefully in Hell, with none of my ordinary annoyances to bother me.”
“And what, madame, can they be?”
“Why, you must understand that it is little rest a vampire gets on earth, with so many fine young fellows like yourself going about everywhere eager to be destroyed.”
“But how, madame, did you happen to become a vampire if the life does not please you? And what is it that they call you?”
“My name, sir,” replied the vampire, sorrowfully, “is Florimel, because my nature no less than my person was as beautiful as the flowers of the field and as sweet as the honey which the bees (who furnish us with such admirable examples of industry) get out of these flowers. But a sad misfortune changed all this. For I chanced one day to fall ill and die (which, of course, might happen to anyone), and as my funeral was leaving the house the cat jumped over my coffin. That was a terrible misfortune to befall a poor dead girl so generally respected, and in wide demand as a seamstress; though, even then, the worst might have been averted had not my sister-in-law been of what they call a humane disposition and foolishly attached to the cat. So they did not kill it, and I, of course, became a vampire.”
“Yes, I can understand that was inevitable. Still, it seems hardly fair. I pity you, my dear.” And Jurgen sighed.
“I would prefer, sir, that you did not address me thus familiarly, since you and I have omitted the formality of an introduction; and in the absence of any joint acquaintances are unlikely ever to meet properly.”
“I have no herald handy, for I travel incognito.

===Chinese===

于是考斯（Coth）再次在盛怒之中同亲生儿子分道扬镳，朱尔根（Jurgen）遂折返走向巴拉苏姆（Barathum）；不知是无巧不成书抑或另有玄机，朱尔根迎面撞见的，恰恰正是他方才诱使老父在脑海中遐想的那只吸血女妖。她是朱尔根之父抑或世间任何凡夫肉胎所能想象出的最勾魂摄魄的绝色尤物：她身披一袭橙黄罗裳——因着地狱（Hell）深处人尽皆知的某个隐秘缘由——且罗裳上各处皆密密麻麻地刺绣着翠绿无花果叶。

“清晨安好，夫人，”朱尔根致意道，“敢问尊驾意欲何往？”

“哎，无处可去，好后生。盖因眼下正值迦尔吉之法（Law of Kalki）依循惯例恩准给我的年度休假——”

“敢问夫人，这位迦尔吉究竟是何方神圣？”

“眼下尚且查无此人：不过有朝一日他将化身为一匹雄性烈马降临。然而他的律法却先他而至，故而我正在地狱深处安享休假，全无阳世那些寻常烦扰来败坏兴致。”

“敢问夫人，阳世究竟有何等烦扰？”

“哎，你自当体谅，吸血鬼在凡间哪里得享安宁，放眼四方，尽是些同你一般英挺倜傥、巴不得引颈受戮自取灭亡的年轻后生。”

“可夫人倘若对这等营生心生厌烦，当初又是怎生沦落为吸血女妖的呢？世人又怎生称呼尊驾？”

“在下芳名，”女妖凄然叹道，“唤作弗洛里梅尔（Florimel），盖因昔年的我无论心性品格抑或婀娜身段，皆如旷野繁花般明媚芬芳，如蜜蜂——那些为凡人树立了何等勤勉典范的生灵——从花蕊中采集的琼浆蜜糖般甘美。然而一场飞来横祸却教凡百美好尽化飞灰。昔日我偶然染恙一命呜呼——世间凡人皆难免有此一劫——而正当出殡灵柩抬出庭院的骨节眼上，一只黑猫冷不防跃过了我的棺木。对一个平日里深蒙四邻敬重、且各方争相延请的俏丽女裁缝而言，这委实是一桩骇人听闻的灭顶之灾；不过，纵然到了那步田地，最惨烈的厄运本亦能化解，奈何我的寡嫂偏生怀着一副世人所谓的‘菩萨心肠’，且愚不可及地对那只黑猫溺爱有加。于是众人未曾将那孽畜乱棍打死，而我，自然而然便化作了吸血女妖。”

“千真万确，在下完全能体谅这乃是势在必行的宿命。然而这待夫人而言委实太失公允。在下由衷怜惜夫人的际遇，我的好人儿。”朱尔根不由得长叹一声。

“在下倒宁愿先生休要这般套近乎，因你我之间尚未循过引荐之礼；且在缺乏共同旧相识的情形下，往后恐亦再难有堂堂正正结交的良机。”

“在下手头眼下未带传令官，因在下向来隐姓埋名出行。

===Original===

However, I am that Jurgen who recently made himself Emperor of Noumaria, King of Eubonia, Prince of Cocaigne, and Duke of Logreus; and of whom you have doubtless heard.”
“Why, to be sure!” says she, patting her hair straight. “And who would have anticipated meeting your highness in such a place!”
“One says ‘majesty’ to an emperor, my dear. It is a detail, of course: but in my position one has to be a little exigent.”
“I perfectly comprehend, your majesty; and indeed I might have divined your rank from your lovely clothes. I can but entreat you to overlook my unintentional breach of etiquette: and I make bold to add that a kind heart reveals the splendor of its graciousness through the interest which your majesty has just evinced in my disastrous history.”
“Upon my word,” thinks Jurgen, “but in this flow of words I seem to recognize my father’s imagination when in anger.”
Then Florimel told Jurgen of her horrible awakening in the grave, and of what had befallen her hands and feet there, the while that against her will she fed repugnantly, destroying first her kindred and then the neighbors. This done, she had arisen.
“For the cattle still lived, and that troubled me. When I had put an end to this annoyance, I climbed into the church belfry, not alone, for one went with me of whom I prefer not to talk; and at midnight I sounded the bell so that all who heard it would sicken and die. And I wept all the while, because I knew that when everything had been destroyed which I had known in my first life in the flesh, I would be compelled to go into new lands, in search of the food which alone can nourish me, and I was always sincerely attached to my home. So it was, your majesty, that I forever relinquished my sewing, and became a lovely peril, a flashing desolation, and an evil which smites by night, in spite of my abhorrence of irregular hours: and what I do I dislike extremely, for it is a sad fate to become a vampire, and still to sympathize with your victims, and particularly with their poor mothers.”
So Jurgen comforted Florimel, and he put his arm around her.
“Come, come!” he said, “but I will see that your vacation passes pleasantly. And I intend to deal fairly with you, too.”
Then he glanced sidewise at his shadow, and whispered a suggestion which caused Florimel to sigh.

===Chinese===

不过，在下正是那位近来荣登努马里亚皇帝（Emperor of Noumaria）、尤波尼亚国王、科凯恩亲王兼洛格鲁斯公爵的朱尔根；想必尊驾对此早有耳闻。”

“哎呀，当真失敬！”女妖说着，赶忙抬手将耳畔青丝理得齐齐整整。“天下谁能料到，竟能在此等晦暗绝地恭逢陛下尊颜！”

“面对一位皇帝，心肝肉儿，理当尊称‘陛下’。这固然是区区细枝末节：但在下身居九五之尊，总难免要在虚礼上略加苛求一番。”

“臣妾完全省得，陛下；且依着陛下身上的这件璀璨华服，臣妾本早该参透陛下的尊贵门阀。臣妾唯能恳祈陛下宽宥臣妾的无心失礼；且臣妾斗胆进言，陛下适才对臣妾这番凄楚家史所垂询垂怜的深切关切，端的是教一颗仁慈圣心在隆恩浩荡中尽显无上光辉。”

“凭良心讲，”朱尔根暗自嘀咕道，“在这番滔滔不绝的花言巧语里，我仿佛认出了我那老子盛怒之下的大脑遐想手笔。”

随后，弗洛里梅尔向朱尔根娓娓倾诉起自己在坟茔地底那场惨绝人寰的复苏苏醒，讲述自己的手足在棺木中遭遇了何等骇人异化变迁，讲述自己如何在违背本心的痛苦煎熬中，以令人作呕的贪婪狼吞虎咽，先是将自家骨肉满门尽数吮吸殆尽，继而又将邻里乡亲屠戮一空。凡此种种办妥之后，她破土而出。

“因乡里的大牲口耕牛依然苟活，这教我心神不宁。待我将这桩烦心事一并料理干净之后，我爬上了教堂的钟楼——并非孑然一身，因身畔尚有一位我不愿提及名讳的同行恶煞；在午夜子时，我奋力敲响了铜钟，以致凡闻听钟声之人皆染病暴毙。而我自始至终皆在痛哭流涕，因我心知肚明，一旦我生前在肉身凡胎中所熟识的一切尽皆灰飞烟灭，我便不得不背井离乡远走异域，去苦苦寻觅那唯一能滋养残躯的血肉血浆，可我平生对自家桑梓家园向来怀着最真挚赤诚的眷恋啊。就这般，陛下，臣妾永远抛却了手中的绣花针线，化作了一桩娇美绝伦的致命祸水、一抹光华四射的倾覆绝境，以及一道在夜色中噬人性命的黑暗孽障——尽管臣妾平生最厌恶作息无常；凡我所作所为，我自家皆嫌恶透顶，因沦落为吸血女妖，心头却偏偏割舍不下对受害生灵的深切同情——尤其是对他们那些可怜的老母亲——这委实是人世间最惨痛不过的宿命啊。”

朱尔根遂温存抚慰着弗洛里梅尔，顺势揽住了她纤细的腰肢。

“好啦，好啦！”他柔声道，“有朱尔根在，定教夫人的休假过得快活如意。且在下向来打定主意要公平体面地侍奉夫人。”

随即他侧眼斜睨了一眼自家的阴影，伏在女妖耳畔窃窃私语吐露了一记提议，引得弗洛里梅尔长叹连连。

===Original===

“By the terms of my doom,” said she, “at no time during the nine lives of the cat can I refuse. Still, it is a comfort you are the Emperor of Noumaria and have a kind heart.”
“Oh, and a many other possessions, my dear! and I again assure you that I intend to deal fairly with you.”
So Florimel conducted Jurgen, through the changeless twilight of Barathum, like that of a gray winter afternoon, to a quiet cleft by the Sea of Blood, which she had fitted out very cosily in imitation of her girlhood home; and she lighted a candle, and made him welcome to her cleft. And when Jurgen was about to enter it he saw that his shadow was following him into the vampire’s home.
“Let us extinguish this candle!” says Jurgen, “for I have seen so many flames today that my eyes are tired.”
So Florimel extinguished the candle, with a goodwill that delighted Jurgen. And now they were in utter darkness, and in the dark nobody can see what is happening. But that Florimel now trusted Jurgen and his Noumarian claims was evinced by her very first remark.
“I was in the beginning suspicious of your majesty,” said Florimel, “because I had always heard that every emperor carried a magnificent sceptre, and you then displayed nothing of the sort. But now, somehow, I do not doubt you any longer. And of what is your majesty thinking?”
“Why, I was reflecting, my dear,” says Jurgen, “that my father imagines things very satisfactorily.”

===Chinese===

“依循老天惩处我的戒律，”她幽幽叹道，“在黑猫享有的9条命（九条命）的岁月轮回里，凡有所求，臣妾断无拒绝之权。不过，思及陛下乃是努马里亚皇帝且怀有一副菩萨心肠，心头终归算得上一桩莫大的慰藉。”

“噢，在下所坐拥的身家福泽尚多着呢，心肝肉儿！且在下再度向你担保，在下必定公允体面地伺候夫人。”

于是弗洛里梅尔引着朱尔根，穿过巴拉苏姆那如灰蒙蒙凛冬午后般亘古不变的永恒薄暮，行抵了血海（Sea of Blood）畔一处幽僻恬静的岩石裂隙——此处已被她依循着少女时代的故土闺房拾掇得极其温馨雅致；她点亮了一支红烛，柔情脉脉地迎奉朱尔根步入裂隙洞府。而当朱尔根正欲抬足迈入之际，赫然瞧见自家的那道阴影正紧随其后、一道钻进吸血女妖的香闺。

“我们且将这支蜡烛熄了吧！”朱尔根提议道，“因在下今日目睹了太多的熊熊烈焰，双目已然疲惫酸涩得紧。”

弗洛里梅尔依言吹熄了蜡烛，其顺从温存的乖巧之态令朱尔根心花怒放。刹那间二人陷入了伸手不见五指的纯粹漆黑之中，而在漆黑一团里，世间自然无人能窥见其间究竟在上演何等风云变幻。然而弗洛里梅尔此刻对朱尔根及其努马里亚皇权位阶的心悦诚服，单凭她吐露的头一句话便已然展露无遗。

“起初臣妾对陛下尚存几分疑窦，”弗洛里梅尔娇喘微微道，“皆因臣妾自幼便听闻，每一位皇帝皆随身执掌着一柄硕大无朋的巍峨权杖，而陛下先前身上却分明未曾展露半点端倪。然而此刻此时，不知怎的，臣妾对陛下的九五之尊再无半分怀疑了。可陛下眼下究竟在沉思何等国家大计呢？”

“哎，在下适才在琢磨，心肝肉儿，”朱尔根心满意足地叹道，“我家老爷子凭空遐想的造化能耐，委实精妙得令人无可挑剔。”

## XXIV Of Compromises in Cocaigne / 第二十四章 在科凯恩的妥协
===Original===

Thus Jurgen abode for a little over two months in Cocaigne, and complied with the customs of that country. Nothing altered in Cocaigne: but in the world wherein Jurgen was reared, he knew, it would by this time be September, with the leaves flaring gloriously, and the birds flocking southward, and the hearts of Jurgen’s fellows turning to not unpleasant regrets. But in Cocaigne there was no regret and no variability, but only an interminable flow of curious pleasures, illumined by the wandering star of Venus Mechanitis.
“Why is it, then, that I am not content?” said Jurgen. “And what thing is this which I desire? It seems to me there is some injustice being perpetrated upon Jurgen, somewhere.”
Meanwhile he lived with Anaïtis the Sun’s daughter very much as he had lived with Lisa, who was daughter to a pawnbroker. Anaïtis displayed upon the whole a milder temper: in part because she could confidently look forward to several centuries more of life before being explained away by the Philologists, and so had less need than Dame Lisa to worry over temporal matters; and in part because there was less to ruin one’s disposition in two months than in ten years of Jurgen’s company. Anaïtis nagged and sulked for a while when her Prince Consort slackened in the pursuit of strange delights, as he did very soon, with frank confession that his tastes were simple and that these outlandish refinements bored him. Later Anaïtis seemed to despair of his ever becoming proficient in curious pleasures, and she permitted Jurgen to lead a comparatively normal life, with only an occasional and halfhearted remonstrance.
What puzzled Jurgen was that she did not seem to tire of him: and he would often wonder what this lovely myth, so skilled and potent in arts wherein he was the merest bungler, could find to care for in Jurgen. For now they lived together like any other humdrum married couple, and their occasional exchange of endearments was as much a matter of course as their meals, and hardly more exciting.
“Poor dear, I believe it is simply because I am a monstrous clever fellow. She distrusts my cleverness, she very often disapproves of it, and yet she values it as queer, as a sort of curiosity. Well, but who can deny that cleverness is truly a curiosity in Cocaigne?”

===Chinese===

话说朱尔根（Jurgen）就这般在科凯恩（Cocaigne）羁留了足有2个多月（两个多月）之久，且随乡入俗地恪守着该国的凡百习俗礼法。科凯恩境内万物亘古不变：然而在朱尔根生长的凡尘阳世，他深知此时早已是枫红似火的9月（九月），候鸟成群结队翩跹南迁，朱尔根同胞凡夫的心头亦泛起并不教人难堪的脉脉闲愁。然而在科凯恩境内，既无闲愁亦无变迁，唯有一汪在工巧维纳斯（Venus Mechanitis）游离流光辉映下的无尽奇巧淫乐之流。

“可我究竟因何未得安宁满足呢？”朱尔根自言自语道。“而我心中苦苦渴求的究竟又是何物？依我看来，冥冥之中定有谁人正在向我朱尔根施加某种不公不义。”

在此期间，他同太阳之女阿娜伊蒂斯（Anaïtis）同居度日的光景，同他昔日与当铺掌柜的闺女丽莎夫人（Dame Lisa）的过日子别无两样。阿娜伊蒂斯的脾性大体更为温存克制：其一因她大可泰然自若地指望在被语文学大师们以学术考据一笔勾销之前尚有数个世纪的漫长阳寿，因而无需如丽莎夫人那般为尘世琐碎成日价焦躁烦扰；其二则因同朱尔根厮混2个月（两个月），比起同他同甘共苦10年（十年）光阴，要少去不知多少败坏妇人性情的窝囊气。起初，当自家这位王夫（Prince Consort）对光怪陆离的极乐钻研渐显懈怠之际，阿娜伊蒂斯难免会唠叨生闷气；而朱尔根很快便老实坦承自家的品味质朴平庸，且对这等光怪陆离的异邦精微绝活腻烦得紧。后来阿娜伊蒂斯似乎对他有朝一日能在奇巧极乐上登堂入室彻底绝了望，便听任朱尔根过上相对寻常的生活，唯有偶尔半心半意地略加微词抗辩两句。

教朱尔根百思不得其解的是，她似乎从不曾对他心生厌倦：他时常不由得暗自纳罕，这位在风月玄功上炉火纯青、而朱尔根在其手下不过是个十足笨拙外行的绝色神话尤物，究竟在朱尔根身上瞧上了哪一点。因他们眼下厮守在一处，同世间凡百平庸无奇的寻常夫妻别无二致，偶尔交换的温存体己话，亦如按部就班的一日三餐般成了家常便饭，激不起半点惊心动魄的波澜。

“可怜的冤家，我料想无非是因我乃是个绝顶聪颖的神妙奇才。她疑心我的聪慧机巧，她时常鄙薄不屑于我的聪慧，可她却偏偏因其古怪离奇而将其视作一桩罕世奇珍。哎，可谁又能否认，在科凯恩境内，机敏聪慧确乎称得上是一桩前所未见的罕见西洋镜呢？”

===Original===

So Anaïtis petted and pampered her Prince Consort, and took such open pride in his queerness as very nearly embarrassed him sometimes. She could not understand his attitude of polite amusement toward his associates and the events which befell him, and even toward his own doings and traits. Whatever happened, Jurgen shrugged, and, delicately avoiding actual laughter, evinced amusement. Anaïtis could not understand this at all, of course, since Asian myths are remarkably destitute of humor. To Jurgen in private she protested that he ought to be ashamed of his levity: but none the less, she would draw him out, when among the bestial and grim nature myths, and she would glow visibly with fond pride in Jurgen’s queerness.
“She mothers me,” reflected Jurgen. “Upon my word, I believe that in the end this is the only way in which females are capable of loving. And she is a dear and lovely creature, of whom I am sincerely fond. What is this thing, then, that I desire? Why do I feel life is not treating me quite justly?”
So the summer had passed; and Anaïtis travelled a great deal, being a popular myth in every land. Her sense of duty was so strong that she endeavored to grace in person all the peculiar festivals held in her honor, and this, now the harvest season was at hand, left her with hardly a moment disengaged. Then, too, the mission of Anaïtis was to divert; and there were so many people whom she had personally to visit﻿—so many notable ascetics who were advancing straight toward canonization, and whom her underlings were unable to divert﻿—that Anaïtis was compelled to pass night after night in unwholesomely comfortless surroundings, in monasteries and in the cells and caves of hermits.
“You are wearing yourself out, my darling,” Jurgen would say: “and does it not seem, after all, a game that is hardly worth the candle? I know that, for my part, before I would travel so many miles into a desert, and then climb a hundred foot pillar, just to whisper diverting notions into an anchorite’s very dirty ear, I would let the gaunt rascal go to Heaven. But you associate so much with saintly persons that you have contracted their incapacity for seeing the humorous side of things. Well, you are a dear, even so. Here is a kiss for you: and do you come back to your adoring husband as soon as you conveniently can without neglecting your duty.”

===Chinese===

于是阿娜伊蒂斯百般娇纵溺爱自家这位王夫，且对他的古怪离奇流露出教他有时都深感难为情的昭彰自豪。对于朱尔根面对同侪部属、面对降临周身的凡百际遇、乃至面对自家的言行品性所流露出的那份文雅从容的戏谑自嘲做派，她全然参不透个中奥妙。不论发生何事，朱尔根总不过是耸了耸肩，极有分寸地避开粗鄙大笑，唯嘴角噙着一抹戏谑。阿娜伊蒂斯对此自然一窍不通，盖因亚细亚神话向来极其匮乏幽默风趣。私下里她常向朱尔根抗议，斥责他不该这般放肆轻浮：然而一旦步入那些凶蛮狞恶的自然神话禽兽之中，她却又忍不住刻意逗引他开腔，且满面容光焕发，因朱尔根的古怪机锋而洋溢着慈母般的溺爱骄傲。

“她简直是在如母亲般照拂着我，”朱尔根暗自思忖。“指着我的良心起誓，我终归看破，这大抵是天下妇道人家唯一懂得的深爱方式。而她是个温存娇媚的尤物，我对她亦由衷怀着真切的喜爱。既然这般，我心头苦苦渴求的究竟是何等物事？我为何偏偏觉得这苍天人世待我委实不够公道？”

暑去秋来，夏日消逝；阿娜伊蒂斯四处周游巡幸，因她在天南海北凡百疆土皆是极孚众望的显赫神话。她的职分心性是如此强烈，以致她竭力亲身莅临主持每一处专为供奉她而设立的荒诞祭典，而随着秋收时令接踵而至，她几乎再抽不出半点闲暇。况且，阿娜伊蒂斯的天职乃是抚慰引诱凡夫；天下有如此众多的苦修隐士亟待她亲身登门点化——如此众多的得道高僧正昂首阔步迈向册封圣徒的关头，而她麾下的随从仆役根本无力使之动摇破戒——以致阿娜伊蒂斯不得不接连数夜歇宿在阴冷不适的苦寒绝地，在修道院深处、在苦行僧幽暗潮湿的石窟穴居之中。

“你这是在糟践摧残自家的身子骨呀，心肝肉儿，”朱尔根劝诫道：“且仔细一琢磨，这等营生难道当真值当得上一夜烛火？依我之见，若教我长途跋涉数百里深入不毛大漠，继而费力攀上一座百英尺高（一百英尺）的石柱，仅仅为了将几句挑逗风月耳语吹入一个苦修隐士污秽不堪的脏耳朵里，我宁可由着那个骨瘦如柴的泼皮径自升入天堂去。然而你成日同圣徒打交道太甚，以致连你也染上了他们参不透天地万物幽默诙谐那一面的顽疾。不过即便如此，你终归是我心爱的人儿。来，香一个：且在不耽搁公干职守的前提下，尽早回转你这位痴心汉子的怀抱罢。”

===Original===

“They report that this Stylites is very far gone in rectitude,” said Anaïtis, absentmindedly, as she prepared for the journey, “but I have hopes for him.”
Then Anaïtis put purple powder on her hair, and hastily got together a few beguiling devices, and went into the Thebaid. Jurgen went back to the Library, and the *System of Worshipping a Girl*, and the unique manuscripts of Astyanassa and Elephantis and Sotadês, and the Dionysiac Formulae, and the Chart of Postures, and the *Litany of the Centre of Delight*, and the Spintrian Treatises, and the *Thirty-Two Gratifications*, and innumerable other volumes which he found instructive.
The Library was a vaulted chamber, having its walls painted with the twelve Asan of Cyrenê; the ceiling was frescoed with the arched body of a woman, whose toes rested upon the cornice of the east wall, and whose outstretched fingertips touched the cornice of the western wall. The clothing of this painted woman was remarkable: and to Jurgen her face was not unfamiliar.
“Who is that?” he inquired, of Anaïtis.
Looking a little troubled, Anaïtis told him this was Æsred.
“Well, I have heard her called otherwise: and I have seen her in quite other clothing.”
“You have seen Æsred!”
“Yes, with a kitchen towel about her head, and otherwise unostentatiously appareled﻿—but very becomingly, I can assure you!” Here Jurgen glanced sidewise at his shadow, and he cleared his throat. “Oh, and a most charming and a most estimable old lady I found this Æsred to be, I can assure you also.”
“I would prefer to know nothing about it,” said Anaïtis, hastily, “I would prefer, for both our sakes, that you say no more of Æsred.” Jurgen shrugged.
Now in the Library of Cocaigne was garnered a record of all that the nature myths had invented in the way of pleasure. And here, with no companion save his queer shadow, and with Æsred arched above and bleakly regarding him, Jurgen spent most of his time, rather agreeably, in investigating and meditating upon the more curious of these recreations. The painted Asan were, in all conscience, food for wonder: but over and above these dozen surprising pastimes, the books of Anaïtis revealed to Jurgen, without disguise or reticence, every other far-fetched frolic of heathenry.

===Chinese===

“据探子密报，那位柱头修士西缅·斯蒂利特（Stylites）在清心寡欲正道上一意孤行陷得太深，”阿娜伊蒂斯在打点行装时心不在焉地答道，“不过我对他倒颇怀胜算。”

随即阿娜伊蒂斯在青丝发端敷上紫色香粉，匆忙罗致了几样勾魂摄魄的秘法器物，便启程奔赴底比斯旷野（Thebaid）。朱尔根则折返回藏书楼，去研读那部《少女崇拜仪轨》（*System of Worshipping a Girl*），以及阿斯蒂亚纳萨（Astyanassa）、埃勒凡蒂斯（Elephantis）与索塔德斯（Sotadês）的罕世秘卷，酒神狂饮秘诀，房中体态秘谱，《极乐中枢祈愿经》（*Litany of the Centre of Delight*），斯宾特里亚秘典，以及《32种极乐考》（《三十二乐图》，*Thirty-Two Gratifications*），以及无数他大觉受益匪浅的长卷宏篇。

这座藏书楼乃是一间穹顶广厦，四壁彩绘着昔兰尼的12种阿散（十二种阿散秘姿，twelve Asan of Cyrenê）；穹顶壁画则是一位呈拱桥倒悬之姿的裸身妇人，其玉趾踏在东墙的飞檐之上，舒展的手指恰好触及西墙的挑檐。壁画上妇人的穿戴打扮极教人侧目：且对朱尔根而言，她的容貌竟似曾相识。

“那女子是哪一位？”他曾向阿娜伊蒂斯探问。

阿娜伊蒂斯神色微显惶恐，低声告之这乃是埃斯雷德（Æsred）。

“哎，我可曾听闻世人唤她旁的名号；且我所见过的她，身上披裹的行头可截然不同。”

“你竟曾目睹过埃斯雷德真容？！”

“千真万确，头上扎着一块厨房擦布包头巾，身上穿戴亦极其朴素平实——不过委实得体标致得很，这一点我大可向你担保！”说到此处朱尔根侧眼斜睨了一眼自家的阴影，清了清嗓子。“噢，且我发觉这位埃斯雷德老太太端的是一位绝顶慈眉善目、令人肃然起敬的可敬长者，这一点我亦大可向你打包票。”

“我宁愿对此充耳不闻一无所知，”阿娜伊蒂斯仓促打断道，“为了你我二人的福祉，我宁愿你往后休要再吐露埃斯雷德半个字。”朱尔根耸了耸肩。

科凯恩的藏书楼里，巨细靡遗地汇聚典藏了自然神话诸神在淫乐一道上所开创的凡百奇巧秘法。在此地，除了那道古怪的阴影常伴身侧、以及头顶埃斯雷德冰冷俯瞰的倒悬壁画之外再无任何闲杂打扰，朱尔根将大半光阴打发在此，颇为自得其乐地钻研沉思着这些光怪陆离的消遣秘法。画壁上的阿散秘姿固然教人叹为观止：然而凌驾于这12种令人惊愕的秘戏之上，阿娜伊蒂斯的秘典更毫无掩饰保留地向朱尔根展示了异教神祇凡百匪夷所思的狂放嬉戏。

===Original===

Hitherto unheard-of forms of diversion were unveiled to him, and every recreation which ingenuity had been able to contrive, for the gratifying of the most subtle and the most strong-stomached tastes. No possible sort of amusement would seem to have been omitted, in running the quaint gamut of refinements upon nature which Anaïtis and her cousins had at odd moments invented, to satiate their desire for some more suave or more strange or more sanguinary pleasure. Yet the deeper Jurgen investigated, and the longer he meditated, the more certain it seemed to him that all such employment was a peculiarly unimaginative pursuit of happiness.
“I am willing to taste any drink once. So I must give diversion a fair trial. But I am afraid these are the games of mental childhood. Well, that reminds me I promised the children to play with them for a while before supper.”
So he came out, and presently, brave in the shirt of Nessus, and mimicked in every action by that incongruous shadow, Prince Jurgen was playing tag with the three little Eumenidês, the daughters of Anaïtis by her former marriage with Acheron, the King of Midnight.
Anaïtis and the dark potentate had parted by mutual consent. “Acheron meant well,” she would say, with a forgiving sigh, “and that in the Moon’s absence he occasionally diverted travellers, I do not deny. But he did not understand me.”
And Jurgen agreed that this tragedy sometimes befell even the irreproachably diverting.
The three Eumenidês at this period were half-grown girls, whom their mother was carefully tutoring to drive guilty persons mad by the stings of conscience: and very quaint it was to see the young Furies at practise in the schoolroom, black-robed, and waving lighted torches, and crowned each with her garland of pet serpents. They became attached to Jurgen, who was always fond of children, and who had frequently regretted that Dame Lisa had borne him none.
“It is enough to get the poor dear a name for eccentricity,” he had been used to say.
So Jurgen now made much of his stepchildren: and indeed he found their innocent prattle quite as intelligent, in essentials, as the talk of the full-grown nature myths who infested the palace of Anaïtis.

===Chinese===

前所未闻的欢愉形式在此向他彻底揭开面纱，凡是机巧智谋所能勾勒出的凡百消遣，皆旨在餍足最挑剔精微、以及胃口最沉重凶蛮的嗜好。在阿娜伊蒂斯及其堂表同侪于闲暇偶得时所开创的巧夺天工的自然放浪谱系中，似乎未曾遗漏任何一种欢愉形态，无一不是为了填补他们对更温存、更离奇抑或更血腥极乐的无底欲望。然而朱尔根越是往深处钻研、越是沉思冥想，便越发十二分笃定：凡此种种消遣勾当，本质上无非是对欢愉极乐一种极度匮乏想象力的笨拙追逐罢了。

“天下杯中物凡是甘霖朱尔根皆愿尝上一尝。故而我必须向欢愉消遣施以公允考量。然而我深恐这凡百游戏，终归不过是心智幼童的小儿把戏。哎，这倒提醒了我，我答应过孩子们在晚膳前陪她们玩闹片刻呢。”

于是他掩卷迈出书斋，少顷，身披那袭流光溢彩的涅索斯之衫（shirt of Nessus）、一举一动皆被那道扞格不入的滑稽阴影分毫不差模仿着的朱尔根亲王，正欢欢喜喜地同3位（三位）小厄里倪厄斯（Eumenidês）玩着抓人捉迷藏——这三位千金乃是阿娜伊蒂斯同前夫午夜之王阿刻戎（Acheron）所生的血脉骨肉。

阿娜伊蒂斯同那位冥界大君乃是协议和离。“阿刻戎本心倒是不坏的，”她曾怀着宽宥的长叹感慨道，“且在月亮隐没之际，他偶尔亦能引诱开释行旅路人，这一点我倒并不否认。然而他根本读不懂我。”

朱尔根由衷赞同，这等悲剧有时候纵然降临在无可挑剔的尤物身上亦在所难免。

此时这三位小复仇女神尚不过是半大少女，她们的生母正严加管教调教她们，好教她们日后能借由良心的啃噬将罪大恶极之辈生生逼疯：瞧着这帮年轻的复仇女神在闺阁学堂里演练操演，委实古怪风趣至极——一个个身披黑袍，挥舞着熊熊火把，额头各自盘绕着自幼圈养当宠物的一圈毒蛇花冠。她们对朱尔根格外依恋亲昵，而朱尔根向来喜爱孩童，且昔日时常暗自哀叹丽莎夫人未曾替他诞下一男半女。

“这足以教那可怜的冤家落得个性格孤僻乖戾的话柄，”他昔日总爱这般自嘲。

于是朱尔根眼下对这几个继女百般宠溺呵护：且事实上，他发觉她们天真烂漫的童言稚语，在根本要害上，丝毫不逊色于在阿娜伊蒂斯宫殿里出没的那些成年自然神话的虚妄阔论。

===Original===

And the four of them﻿—Jurgen, and critical Alecto, and grave Tisiphonê, and fairy-like little Megæra﻿—would take long walks, and play with their dolls (though Alecto was a trifle condescending toward dolls), and romp together in the eternal evening of Cocaigne; and discuss what sort of dresses and trinkets Mother would probably bring them when she came back from Ecbatana or Lesbos, and would generally enjoy themselves.
Rather pathetically earnest and unimaginative little lasses, Jurgen found the young Eumenidês: they inherited much of their mother’s narrow-mindedness, if not their father’s brooding and gloomy tendencies; but in them narrow-mindedness showed merely as amusing. And Jurgen loved them, and would often reflect what a pity it was that these dear little girls were destined when they reached maturity, to spend the rest of their lives in haunting criminals and adulterers and parricides and, generally, such persons as must inevitably tarnish the girls’ outlook upon life, and lead them to see too much of the worst side of human nature.
So Jurgen was content enough. But still he was not actually happy, not even among the endless pleasures of Cocaigne.
“And what is this thing that I desire?” he would ask himself, again and again.
And still he did not know: he merely felt he was not getting justice: and a dim sense of this would trouble him even while he was playing with the Eumenidês.

===Chinese===

他们4人（四人）——朱尔根、吹毛求疵的小阿莱克托（Alecto）、神色肃穆的小提西福涅（Tisiphonê），以及宛若精灵般的小墨盖拉（Megæra）——会长途漫步远足，摆弄泥偶布娃娃（虽说阿莱克托对待娃娃的态度略带几分屈尊傲慢），在科凯恩亘古不变的永恒黄昏中摸爬滚打、嬉闹玩耍；并津津乐道于母亲待从埃克巴塔那（Ecbatana）或莱斯博斯（Lesbos）巡幸回朝时究竟会替她们捎带何等斑斓罗裙与精巧首饰，大体上过得端的是其乐融融。

朱尔根发觉，这几个小复仇女神乃是心思格外赤诚、却颇显缺乏想象力的可怜小丫头：她们骨子里承袭了母亲狭隘偏执的习性，所幸未曾遗传生父那般阴郁暴戾的戾气；且在她俩身上，这份狭隘偏执瞧来反倒显出几分孩童的娇憨滑稽。朱尔根由衷疼爱她们，且时常暗自叹惋：这几个惹人怜爱的小姑娘，待到及笄成人之际，注定要将往后余生虚掷于没完没了地追索纠缠江洋大盗、奸夫淫妇与弑父逆子之中，而这等污秽下作的恶煞凶顽，势必会彻底玷污姑娘们看待世相人生的纯澈眼眸，迫使她们过早洞悉目睹人类天性中最为丑陋不堪的一面。

于是朱尔根算得上随遇而安、自得其乐。然而即便置身于科凯恩这永无休止的凡百极乐深处，他却依然未曾领略到真真正正的欢欣喜乐。

“我心中所苦苦渴求的，究竟是何等物事？”他一次又一次在心头扪心自问。

可他依然参详不透：他唯独隐隐感到自己未曾获得至公至正的待遇；而这份若隐若现的烦扰，甚至在他同小复仇女神们嬉戏玩闹之际，亦会如鲠在喉般教他心神不宁。

## XV Of Compromises in Glathion / 第十五章 在格拉西昂的妥协
===Original===

The tale records that it was not a great while before, in simple justice to Guenevere, Duke Jurgen had afforded her the advantage of frank conversation in actual privacy. For conventions have to be regarded, of course. Thus the time of a princess is not her own, and at any hour of day all sorts of people are apt to request an audience just when some most improving conversation is progressing famously: but the Hall of Judgment stood vacant and unguarded at night.
“But I would never consider doing such a thing,” said Guenevere: “and whatever must you think of me, to make such a proposal!”
“That too, my dearest, is a matter which I can only explain in private.”
“And if I were to report your insolence to my father﻿—”
“You would annoy him exceedingly: and from such griefs it is our duty to shield the aged.”
“And besides, I am afraid.”
“Oh, my dearest,” says Jurgen, and his voice quavered, because his love and his sorrow seemed very great to him: “but, oh, my dearest, can it be that you have not faith in me! For with all my body and soul I love you, as I have loved you ever since I first raised your face between my hands, and understood that I had never before known beauty. Indeed, I love you as, I think, no man has ever loved any woman that lived in the long time that is gone, for my love is worship, and no less. The touch of your hand sets me to trembling, dear; and the look of your gray eyes makes me forget there is anything of pain or grief or evil anywhere: for you are the loveliest thing God ever made, with joy in the new skill that had come to His fingers. And you have not faith in me!”
Then the Princess gave a little sobbing laugh of content and repentance, and she clasped the hand of her grief-stricken lover. “Forgive me, Jurgen, for I cannot bear to see you so unhappy!”
“Ah, and what is my grief to you!” he asks of her, bitterly.
“Much, oh, very much, my dear!” she whispered.
So in the upshot Jurgen was never to forget that moment wherein he waited behind the door, and through the crack between the half-open door and the doorframe saw Guenevere approach irresolutely, a wavering white blur in the dark corridor. She came to talk with him where they would not be bothered with interruptions: but she came delightfully perfumed, in her night-shift, and in nothing else. Jurgen wondered at the way of these women even as his arms went about her in the gloom.

===Chinese===

故事记载道，不多时日之后，出于待吉尼薇尔（Guenevere）至公至正的体面考量，朱尔根公爵（Duke Jurgen）便顺理成章地向她呈奉了在深闺私密处推心置腹秉烛夜谈的无上恩泽。盖因世俗礼法自然不得不顾及。一国公主的白昼光阴终归不属于她自家，在青天白日的任何骨节眼上，恰逢某些极富教益的雅致阔论渐入佳境之际，凡百闲杂人等皆随时可能请旨觐见；然而到了沉沉深夜，整座正义大殿（Hall of Judgment）却空寂无人且无人值守。

“可我断不会做出这等大逆不道的事来，”吉尼薇尔娇嗔道：“且你竟敢提出这等非分之想，心底里究竟把人家当成何等轻浮女子了！”

“关于那一节，我最心爱的人儿，亦唯有在私密无人之处我方能为你细细剖解。”

“倘若我将你的狂妄无礼径自回禀父王——”

“那定会教他老人家烦闷透顶：而替风烛残年的长辈遮蔽这等忧戚，本是你我的孝道天职。”

“况且，人家害怕嘛。”

“噢，我最亲爱的人儿，”朱尔根说着，嗓音微微发颤，因在他看来，自家的深情与哀恸委实宏大无匹：“噢，我最心爱的人儿，难道你当真对我毫无半分信赖不成！我倾尽自家的全副身心与灵魂深爱着你，自昔日我头一遭用双手捧起你的玉容、参透自己生平从未领略过绝世之美的那一刹那起，我便这般矢志不渝地深爱着你。千真万确，在漫长逝去的旧日光阴里，世间从未曾有哪位汉子能如我深爱于你这般去深爱过凡尘女子，因我的深情乃是顶礼膜拜，绝无半点瑕疵玷污。触碰你的柔荑教我周身战栗，亲爱的；凝眸你的秋水明眸，教我将天地间凡百剧痛、悲戚抑或罪愆忘得一干二净：因你是造物主在领悟了双手全新造化神技的狂喜之中所缔造出的至美尤物。可你竟对我毫无信赖！”

公主听罢，在满足与懊悔中发出了一声带着抽泣的温存浅笑，随即紧紧握住了眼前这位悲戚情郎的双手。“宽恕我吧，朱尔根，我委实瞧不得你这般伤心欲绝！”

“唉，我的哀恸于你又有何相干！”他凄然苦涩地反问道。

“重若千钧，噢，重若万钧呀，我亲爱的人儿！”她耳语呢喃道。

于是最终的结果，乃是朱尔根此生永难磨灭那一瞬的记忆：他屏息伫立在门扉之后，透过半掩门扉与门框间的缝隙，眼见吉尼薇尔在昏暗的走廊中迈着游移迟疑的碎步款款而来，宛若一道在暗夜中摇曳不定的素白轻云。她依约前来与他幽叙，在这不受凡俗打扰的清净幽境；然而她却沐浴得通体幽香，身上唯披裹着一袭薄如蝉翼的贴身素白睡袍，除此以外周身一丝不挂。正当他在幽暗中张开双臂揽她入怀之际，朱尔根心头不由得暗自惊叹于天下妇人这等不可名状的风月机巧。

===Original===

He remembered always the feel of that warm and slender and yielding body, naked under the thin fabric of the shift, as his arms first went about her: of all their moments together that last breathless minute before either of them had spoken stayed in his memory as the most perfect.
And yet what followed was pleasant enough, for now it was to the wide and softly cushioned throne of a king, no less, that Guenevere and Jurgen resorted, so as to talk where they would not be bothered with interruptions. The throne of Gogyrvan was perfectly dark, under its canopy, in the unlighted hall, and in the dark nobody can see what happens.
Thereafter these two contrived to talk together nightly upon the throne of Glathion: but what remained in Jurgen’s memory was that last moment behind the door, and the six tall windows upon the east side of the hall, those windows which were of commingled blue and silver, but were all an opulent glitter, throughout that time in the night when the moon was clear of the treetops and had not yet risen high enough to be shut off by the eaves. For that was all which Jurgen really saw in the Hall of Judgment. There would be a brief period wherein upon the floor beneath each window would show a narrow quadrangle of moonlight: but the windows were set in a wall so deep that this soon passed. On the west side were six windows also, but about these was a porch; so no light ever came from the west.
Thus in the dark they would laugh and talk with lowered voices. Jurgen came to these encounters well primed with wine, and in consequence, as he quite comprehended, talked like an angel, without confining himself exclusively to celestial topics. He was often delighted by his own brilliance, and it seemed to him a pity there was no one handy to take it down: so much of his talking was necessarily just a little over the head of any girl, however beautiful and adorable.
And Guenevere, he found, talked infinitely better at night. It was not altogether the wine which made him think that, either: the girl displayed a side she veiled in the day time. A girl, far less a princess, is not supposed to know more than agrees with a man’s notion of maidenly ignorance, she contended.
“Nobody ever told me anything about so many interesting matters.

===Chinese===

他此生始终铭记着双臂初次将她揽入怀中的销魂触感：那具温热、纤细且顺从的娇躯，在轻薄睡袍的蝉翼织物下宛若赤身裸裎；在二人共处的所有良辰美景之中，在彼此尚未启齿吐露半句言语之前的那最后一分钟窒息凝睇，在他记忆中始终定格为至臻至美的无上巅峰。

然而紧随其后的际遇亦极尽销魂，只因眼下吉尼薇尔同朱尔根所落座幽叙之处，赫然是一国至尊国王那宽阔柔软、铺设锦褥的宝座，正可在不受任何闲杂打扰的境地里款款倾谈。在黑灯瞎火的大殿深处，在深垂的华盖之下，戈吉尔万的王座陷入了一片纯粹的漆黑，而在漆黑一团里，世间自然无人能窥见究竟在上演何等风月。

自此之后，二人夜夜相约在这座格拉西昂的王座之上秉烛密谈：然而深深烙印在朱尔根记忆深处的，却是门扉后那最后的屏息一瞬，以及大殿东侧的那6扇（六扇）高耸长窗——那6扇融汇了深蓝与纯银质地的瑰丽窗棂，在皓月升离树梢、尚未被殿檐遮蔽的良宵时分，尽皆流转着富丽堂皇的耀目光辉。因那乃是朱尔根在正义大殿深处真真切切所能目睹的全部光景。每逢此时，在每扇窗下的石板地面上，皆会短暂投映出一抹狭长的四角月影；然而长窗嵌在极深邃的厚重石墙之中，这片光斑转瞬即逝。西侧同样砌着6扇长窗，然而窗外围筑着外廊；故而西面从未曾透进半点月华清辉。

于是二人便在黑暗中压低嗓音，窃窃私语、吃吃窃笑。赴约之前，朱尔根总要借着美酒饱饮微醺，因而正如他自家心知肚明的那样，谈吐间口若悬河宛若天使下凡，且绝不画地自限唯论天界玄谈。他时常为自家的机敏锋芒心花怒放，心头不由遗憾身旁竟无人能将这珠玑妙语随手笔录成卷：只因他的高谈阔论，往往难免略微超出了任何妙龄少女的理解头脑——不论她生得何等天香国色、惹人怜爱。

且他发觉，吉尼薇尔在深夜里的谈吐远比白昼高明胜过千万倍。令他作此感想的绝非单纯是杯中佳酿的晕眩：这姑娘在夜色中展露出了她在朗朗白日里刻意深藏的另一副面目。依她辩解，一个寻常姑娘——更遑论一国贵胄公主——断不该懂得逾越汉子心目中所标榜的‘闺阁无知’之限度。

“凡百有趣的大千人事，昔年何曾有人向我吐露过半句微言。

===Original===

Why, I remember﻿—” And Guenevere narrated a quaintly pathetic little story, here irrelevant, of what had befallen her some three or four years earlier. “My mother was living then: but she had never said a word about such things, and frightened as I was, I did not go to her.”
Jurgen asked questions.
“Why, yes. There was nothing else to do. I cannot talk freely with my maids and ladies even now. I cannot question them, that is: of course I can listen as they talk among themselves. For me to do more would be unbecoming in a princess. And I wonder quietly about so many things!” She educed instances. “After that I used to notice the animals and the poultry. So I worked out problems for myself, after a fashion. But nobody ever told me anything directly.”
“Yet I dare say that Thragnar﻿—well, the Troll King, being very wise, must have made zoology much clearer.”
“Thragnar was a skilled enchanter,” says a demure voice in the dark; “and through the potency of his abominable arts, I can remember nothing whatever about Thragnar.”
Jurgen laughed, ruefully. Still, he was tolerably sure about Thragnar now.
So they talked: and Jurgen marvelled, as millions of men had done aforetime, and have done since, at the girl’s eagerness, now that barriers were down, to discuss in considerable detail all such matters as etiquette had previously compelled them to ignore. About her ladies in waiting, for example, she afforded him some very curious data: and concerning men in general she asked innumerable questions that Jurgen found delicious.
Such innocence combined﻿—upon the whole﻿—with a certain moral obtuseness, seemed inconceivable. For to Jurgen it now appeared that Guenevere was behaving with not quite the decorum which might fairly be expected of a princess. Contrition, at least, one might have looked for, over this hole and corner business: whereas it worried him to note that Guenevere was coming to accept affairs almost as a matter of course. Certainly she did not seem to think at all of any wickedness anywhere: the utmost she suggested was the necessity of being very careful. And while she never contradicted him in these private conversations, and submitted in everything to his judgment, her motive now appeared to be hardly more than a wish to please him. It was almost as though she were humoring him in his foolishness. And all this within six weeks!

===Chinese===

哎，我还记得呢——”吉尼薇尔随即讲述了一则凄楚动人、却同当下风马牛不相及的闺中秘事，乃是大约3、4年前（三四年前）降临周身的懵懂际遇。“家母当年尚在人世；可她老人家何曾对这等体己事吐露过半个字，而我纵然吓得魂飞魄散，亦不敢冒冒失失跑去向她请教。”

朱尔根遂连连追问究竟。

“可不是嘛。除此以外我又能如何呢。即便在今日，我也断无法同身旁的侍女贵妇推心置腹坦诚相谈。也就是说，我不能屈尊盘问她们：当然她们私下嚼舌根时我自可从旁侧耳倾听。若逾越此规，未免有伤一国公主的风骨体统。可我一个人暗地里对天下那么多的奇巧人事浮想联翩呢！”她随口列举了数则例证。“自那之后，我常悄悄留神牲畜走兽与禽鸟家雏。于是依着自家的揣摩，多多少少把个中症结推演了个八九不离十。然而却从未曾有人堂堂正正当面向我指点过迷津。”

“不过我敢打赌，斯拉格纳（Thragnar）——那位见多识广的巨魔山怪之王（Troll King），定然曾将个中的走兽繁衍之道剖解得通透得多。”

“斯拉格纳乃是个法力通天的妖术巫师，”黑暗中响起一道娴静端庄的娇声；“且仰仗他那身可憎的通天妖法，关于斯拉格纳的凡百往事，我脑海中如今早已是一片空白忘得一干二净。”

朱尔根闻言干笑了一声，心头泛起阵阵苦涩。不过关于斯拉格纳当年的行径，他此刻已然心明眼亮、八九不离十了。

二人就这般耳鬓厮磨窃窃私语：而朱尔根一如古往今来成千上万的痴心汉子那般，暗自惊诧于这姑娘在卸除心防藩篱之后，竟会如此如饥似渴、事无巨细地热切研讨起昔日依循宫廷礼法不得不视若无睹的凡百隐秘。譬如关于她身畔的诸位随侍女官，她便向他爆料了诸多耸人听闻的奇闻逸事；且关于天下男儿的种种癖好，她更抛出了无数教朱尔根大呼过瘾的俏皮提问。

这般冰清玉洁的天真烂漫，大体上与某种道德上的麻木迟钝奇妙地融汇一炉，委实教人难以置信。因在当下的朱尔根眼中，吉尼薇尔的举手投足，似乎并未能恪守一国公主理当严加秉持的矜持体统。面对这等暗度陈仓的私相授受，世人本该指望她至少流露出几分悔疚自责才是；然而教朱尔根心生忧戚的是，吉尼薇尔竟日渐将这凡百苟且视作了理所当然的家常便饭。显而易见，她脑子里压根儿未曾想及半点伤风败俗之罪；她至多不过是软语提醒万事必须万分谨慎罢了。且在这等私密幽会之中，她从未曾当面反驳过他半句微词，在凡事上皆对他的一言九鼎百依百顺，然而她当下的动机，瞧来却不过是在曲意逢迎讨他欢心罢了。那神态，活脱脱仿佛是在大度纵容迁就着他的小儿胡闹。而这一切变迁，竟然全发生在短短6个星期（六个星期）之内！

===Original===

reflected Jurgen: and he nibbled his fingernails, with a mental side-glance toward the opinions of King Gogyrvan Gawr.
But in daylight the Princess remained unchanged. In daylight Jurgen adored her, but with no feeling of intimacy. Very rarely did occasion serve for them to be actually alone in the day time. Once or twice, though, he kissed her in open sunlight: and then her eyes were melting but wary, and the whole affair was rather flat. She did not repulse him: but she stayed a princess, appreciative of her station, and seemed not at all the invisible person who talked with him at night in the Hall of Judgment.
Presently, by common consent, they began to avoid each other by daylight. Indeed, the time of the Princess was now preoccupied: for now had come into Glathion a ship with saffron colored sails, and having for its figurehead a dragon that was painted with thirty colors. Such was the ship which brought Messire Merlin Ambrosius and Dame Anaïtis, the Lady of the Lake, with a great retinue, to fetch young Guenevere to London, where she was to be married to King Arthur.
First there was a week of feasting and tourneys and high mirth of every kind. Now the trumpets blared, and upon a scaffolding that was gay with pennons and smart tapestries King Gogyrvan sat nodding and blinking in his brightest raiment, to judge who did the best: and into the field came joyously a press of dukes and earls and barons and many famous knights, to contend for honor and a trumpery chaplet of pearls.
Jurgen shrugged, and honored custom. The Duke of Logreus acquitted himself with credit in the opening tournament, unhorsing Sir Dodinas le Sauvage, Earl Roth of Meliot, Sir Epinogris, and Sir Hector de Maris: then Earl Damas of Listenise smote like a whirlwind, and Jurgen slid contentedly down the tail of his fine horse. His part in the tournament was ended, and he was heartily glad of it. He preferred to contemplate rather than share in such festivities: and he now followed his bent with a most exquisite misery, because he considered that never had any other poet occupied a situation more picturesque.
By day he was the Duke of Logreus, which in itself was a notable advance upon pawnbroking: after nightfall he discounted the peculiar privileges of a king.

===Chinese===

朱尔根暗自思忖，一边心神不宁地啃咬着指甲，脑海中不由自主地向戈吉尔万·高尔国王（King Gogyrvan Gawr）的评判立场投去了一记心虚的斜睨。

然而在朗朗白日之下，公主却依旧宛若换了个人一般圣洁威严。在白昼的艳阳下，朱尔根对她顶礼膜拜，却泛不起半点私密亲昵之感。在白日里，二人几乎难得觅得真正独处的机缘。虽说在光天化日之下，他也曾有一两遭偷得浮生亲吻了她：彼时她的明眸中春水流转却戒备森严，整场亲昵反倒显得索然无味。她倒并未将他生硬推开：可她自始至终端着一国公主的尊严仪态，深谙自家的九五门阀，浑然不像深夜在正义大殿里同他幽叙的那个隐形佳人。

未几，心照不宣之下，二人在白昼里开始心照不宣地彼此避嫌。事实上，公主的白昼光阴如今早已被凡百国事填得满满当当：盖因一艘张挂着藏红花色帆篷的海船已然驶抵格拉西昂，船首赫然耸立着涂抹着整整30种（三十种）绚烂油彩的巨龙船首像。正是这艘海船，载着梅林·安布罗休斯爵爷（Messire Merlin Ambrosius）与湖中仙女阿娜伊蒂斯夫人（Dame Anaïtis），率领着浩浩荡荡的显赫仪仗队，专程前来迎奉年轻的吉尼薇尔前往伦敦，去同亚瑟王完婚大典。

首先铺排开来的，乃是整整一周的盛宴、比武与凡百喧嚣狂欢。角号齐鸣，在彩旗猎猎、织锦璀璨的高台之上，戈吉尔万国王身披最明艳的王袍端坐其上，频频颔首眨眼，亲自裁决谁拔得头筹；大批公爵、伯爵、男爵与无数名震天下的赫赫骑士欢呼雀跃涌入沙场，争夺无上荣光与一顶区区微不足道的珍珠桂冠。

朱尔根耸了耸肩，随乡入俗恪守旧制。洛格鲁斯公爵在开幕比武中身手矫健、战功赫赫，干净利落地挑落了野蛮的多迪纳斯爵士（Sir Dodinas le Sauvage）、梅利奥特的罗斯伯爵（Earl Roth of Meliot）、埃皮诺格里斯爵士（Sir Epinogris）与马里斯的赫克托爵士（Sir Hector de Maris）：随后利斯泰尼斯的达马斯伯爵（Earl Damas of Listenise）如狂风骤雨般呼啸杀至，朱尔根遂顺水推舟、心满意足地顺着自家族骏马的尾巴滑落马下。他在比武场上的使命就此功德圆满，他心头由衷额手称庆。比起亲身投身于这等喧嚣狂欢，他向来更乐于作壁上观：眼下他沉浸在一股极精致雅致的感伤愁绪之中，因在他自忖看来，世间文人墨客何曾有人身处于如此极具画意的奇妙境遇之中。

在白昼，他是堂堂洛格鲁斯公爵，比起昔日的典当行掌柜已然是天翻地覆的无上显赫；而夜幕低垂之后，他更是在暗中尽享一国君王专属的隐秘特权。

===Original===

It was the secrecy, the deluding of everybody, which he especially enjoyed: and in the thought of what a monstrous clever fellow was Jurgen, he almost lost sight of the fact that he was miserable over the impending marriage of the lady he loved.
Once or twice he caught the tail-end of a glance from Gogyrvan’s bright old eye. Jurgen by this time abhorred Gogyrvan, as a person of abominably unjust dealings.
“To take no better care of his own daughter,” Jurgen considered, “is infamous. The man is neglecting his duties as a father, and to do that is not fair.”

===Chinese===

正是这份天衣无缝的隐秘、这份将全天下人尽皆玩弄于股掌之中的蒙蔽感，教他咂摸出无上的快慰；在一念及朱尔根乃是个何等绝顶聪颖的神妙奇才的自鸣得意中，他几乎将自己对意中人即将下嫁他人的凄凉苦楚彻底抛诸脑后了。

偶尔一两次，他自戈吉尔万那双炯炯有神的老眼余光中捕捉到了一抹意味深长的神色。直至此时，朱尔根对戈吉尔万已然嫌恶透顶，断定他是个行事极不公道的恶劣之徒。

“对自家亲生骨肉竟这般疏于管教照拂，”朱尔根义愤填膺地暗自思忖，“委实大逆不道。为人父者竟这般玩忽职守，这待天下公理而言未免太失公允。”

## XLI Of Compromises in Heaven / 第四十一章 在天堂的妥协
===Original===

Jurgen then went unhindered to where the God of Jurgen’s grandmother sat upon a throne, beside a sea of crystal. A rainbow, made high and narrow like a window frame, so as to fit the throne, formed an archway in which He sat: at His feet burned seven lamps, and four remarkable winged creatures sat there chaunting softly, “Glory and honor and thanks to Him Who liveth forever!” In one hand of the God was a sceptre, and in the other a large book with seven red spots on it.
There were twelve smaller thrones, without rainbows, upon each side of the God of Jurgen’s grandmother, in two semicircles: upon these inferior thrones sat benignant-looking elderly angels, with long white hair, all crowned, and clothed in white robes, and having a harp in one hand, and in the other a gold flask, about pint size. And everywhere fluttered and glittered the multicolored wings of seraphs and cherubs, like magnified parakeets, as they went softly and gaily about the golden haze that brooded over Heaven, to a continuous sound of hushed organ music and a remote and undistinguishable singing.
Now the eyes of this God met the eyes of Jurgen: and Jurgen waited thus for a long while, and far longer, indeed, than Jurgen suspected.
“I fear You,” Jurgen said, at last: “and, yes, I love You: and yet I cannot believe. Why could You not let me believe, where so many believed? Or else, why could You not let me deride, as the remainder derided so noisily? O God, why could You not let me have faith? for You gave me no faith in anything, not even in nothingness. It was not fair.”
And in the highest court of Heaven, and in plain view of all the angels, Jurgen began to weep.
“I was not ever your God, Jurgen.”
“Once very long ago,” said Jurgen, “I had faith in You.”
“No, for that boy is here with Me, as you yourself have seen. And today there is nothing remaining of him anywhere in the man that is Jurgen.”
“God of my grandmother! God Whom I too loved in boyhood!” said Jurgen then: “why is it that I am denied a God? For I have searched: and nowhere can I find justice, and nowhere can I find anything to worship.”
“What, Jurgen, and would you look for justice, of all places, in Heaven?”
“No,” Jurgen said; “no, I perceive it cannot be considered here. Else You would sit alone.”

===Chinese===

朱尔根（Jurgen）随后畅行无阻地行抵自家族祖母的神明端坐的宝座前，王座正临着一片宛若水晶的玻璃海。一道宛若窗框般高耸狭长的七色彩虹，恰好贴合着宝座的轮廓，构筑成祂端坐其间的拱门：在祂足下，7盏灯（七盏明灯）熊熊燃烧，4只非同寻常的六翼活物蹲伏在侧，轻声宛转吟咏着：“荣耀、尊贵与感恩，皆归于永生亘古的至尊主宰！”在神明的一只手里执掌着一柄王权金杖，另一只手里则握着一部封印着7个红点（七个红印）的厚重大书。

在朱尔根祖母的神明两侧，各有12座（十二座）未置彩虹的稍小宝座，排布成2个半圆（两个半圆形）：在这些次等王座之上，端坐着一位位慈眉善目、生着如瀑银白长髯的年迈天使，尽皆头戴金冠，身披素白长袍，一手轻抚着竖琴，另一手则托着一只约莫一品脱大小的金酒壶。四下里，炽天使与智天使的斑斓双翼如放大无数倍的锦雉般流光溢彩、翩跹翱翔，在笼罩天堂的金色氤氲迷雾中轻盈快活地穿梭往来，伴随着若隐若现、宛转不绝的管风琴圣乐与渺远难辨的天籁清歌。

此时，这位神明的圣目撞上了朱尔根的目光：朱尔根就这般驻足静候了良久良久，其久远委实远超乎朱尔根自家的揣摩。

“我敬畏您，”朱尔根终于启齿道：“且是的，我深爱着您：然而我却终归无法笃信。为何在这芸芸众生皆笃信不疑的天地里，您偏偏不能恩准我亦如他们一般深信不疑呢？抑或退一步讲，为何您偏偏不能恩准我如其余众人那般喧嚣嘈杂地大肆嘲弄呢？噢神啊，您为何偏偏不肯赐予我半点信仰？因您未曾赐予我对世间任何事物的笃信，甚至未曾赐予我对虚无本身的笃信。这委实太失公允。”

在这天堂至高无上的天庭圣殿之上，在诸位天使的众目睽睽之下，朱尔根不由自主地泪如泉涌。

“朱尔根，我从未曾是你的神明。”

“在极为久远的往昔岁月里，”朱尔根哽咽道，“我确曾对您深信不疑。”

“不曾有过，因当年那个笃信的少年此刻正同我常伴身侧，正如你适才亲眼所见。而时至今日，在化身为朱尔根的这个汉子体内，当年的少年已然荡然无存了。”

“我祖母的神明啊！我少年时代亦曾深爱过的至尊神啊！”朱尔根随即呼喊道：“为何我偏偏被剥夺了坐拥一位神明的福泽？只因我踏遍天涯海角苦苦求索：放眼四方我寻不见公理正义，放眼四方我寻觅不到任何值得我顶礼膜拜的事物。”

“何出此言，朱尔根，难道你竟妄图在天堂这等所在去寻觅公理正义不成？”

“不，”朱尔根答道；“不，我如今已然看破，公理正义在天庭亦是容纳不下的。如若不然，您此刻便唯有形单影只孤坐于此了。”

===Original===

“And for the rest, you have looked to find your God without, not looking within to see that which is truly worshipped in the thoughts of Jurgen. Had you done so, you would have seen, as plainly as I now see, that which alone you are able to worship. And your God is maimed: the dust of your journeying is thick upon him; your vanity is laid as a napkin upon his eyes; and in his heart is neither love nor hate, not even for his only worshipper.”
“Do not deride him, You Who have so many worshippers! At least, he is a monstrous clever fellow,” said Jurgen: and boldly he said it, in the highest court of Heaven, and before the pensive face of the God of Jurgen’s grandmother.
“Ah, very probably. I do not meet with many clever people. And as for My numerous worshippers, you forget how often you have demonstrated that I was the delusion of an old woman.”
“Well, and was there ever a flaw in my logic?”
“I was not listening to you, Jurgen. You must know that logic does not much concern us, inasmuch as nothing is logical hereabouts.”
And now the four winged creatures ceased their chaunting, and the organ music became a far-off murmuring. And there was silence in Heaven. And the God of Jurgen’s grandmother, too, was silent for a while, and the rainbow under which He sat put off its seven colors and burned with an unendurable white, tinged bluishly, while the God considered ancient things. Then in the silence this God began to speak.
Some years ago (said the God of Jurgen’s grandmother) it was reported to Koshchei that scepticism was abroad in his universe, and that one walked therein who would be contented with no rational explanation. “Bring me this infidel,” says Koshchei: so they brought to him in the void a little bent gray woman in an old gray shawl. “Now, tell me why you will not believe,” says Koshchei, “in things as they are.”
Then the decent little bent gray woman answered civilly; “I do not know, sir, who you may happen to be. But, since you ask me, everybody knows that things as they are must be regarded as temporary afflictions, and as trials through which we are righteously condemned to pass, in order to attain to eternal life with our loved ones in Heaven.”
“Ah, yes,” said Koshchei, who made things as they are; “ah, yes, to be sure! and how did you learn of this?”

===Chinese===

“至于其余种种，你始终执拗于向身外去寻觅你的神明，却从未曾反观内照、去审视朱尔根神思意念深处真真正正所顶礼膜拜的究竟是何物。倘若你曾这般内省，你便会如我此刻这般洞若观火，清清楚楚瞧见那唯一能教你俯首膜拜的真身。而你的神明早已残缺不全：你平生跋涉的万丈红尘厚厚覆盖其身；你的虚荣自命不凡化作一块餐巾紧紧蒙蔽了他的双眼；且在他冰冷的心窍之中既无深爱亦无仇恨，甚至对他唯一的信徒亦毫无半点悲悯温存。”

“休要嘲弄于他，坐拥如此浩瀚信众的至尊神啊！至少，他乃是个绝顶聪颖的神妙奇才，”朱尔根辩解道：在这天堂至高无上的天庭圣殿之上，当着朱尔根祖母神明那沉郁悲悯的面庞，他大义凛然地吐露了这番言辞。

“啊，大抵确是这般吧。我平生见识过的机敏聪慧之人委实寥寥无几。至于我门下的芸芸信徒嘛，你莫非忘却了，你生平曾多少次言之凿凿论证我不过是一个村妪老妇的一厢情愿虚妄幻想罢了。”

“哎，可我当年的逻辑推演何曾有过半点漏洞瑕疵？”

“我适才并未曾细听你的宏论，朱尔根。你自当明晓，世俗逻辑同我等并无多大干系，皆因在这方天庭境地，万物从来不讲究任何逻辑。”

此时那4只六翼神兽止住了低吟浅唱，管风琴乐音亦化作了渺远的窃窃微风。天庭刹那间陷入了一片庄严的死寂。朱尔根祖母的神明亦默然长考了片刻，祂头顶的那道彩虹褪尽了7种色彩（七彩），化作了一道令人目眩神迷、隐隐泛着青蓝幽光的刺目纯白，神明陷入了对太古苍茫往事的回溯沉思。随后，在万籁俱寂之中，这位神明徐徐启齿道来。

数年之前（朱尔根祖母的神明娓娓叙述道），有密报呈奏至创设万物如其所是的科什切（Koshchei）案前，言道在其浩瀚宇宙之中竟有怀疑逆乱之风在暗中滋长，且有一人游荡其间，无论何等合乎理性的至理陈情皆无法教其心悦诚服。“将那个异端狂徒押至老夫案前，”科什切吩咐道：于是手下仆役自虚无混沌中，为他领来了一位身上裹着褪色灰毛披肩、身形佝偻矮小的白发老妪。“老实交代，你为何偏偏不肯信服万物的本来面目，”科什切盘问道。

这位端庄质朴、身形佝偻的小老太太客客气气地答道：“老妇人不知晓大老爷究竟是哪位天尊。然而既然老爷动问，天下人尽皆晓得，万物的本来面目无非是暂时的苦难磨砺，是我们命中注定该当受领的苦役考验，好教我们在百年之后得以飞升入天，同我们至亲至爱的骨肉在天堂永享天伦永生。”

“啊哈，正是这般，”创设万物本来面目的科什切若有所思道；“啊，千真万确！可你究竟是从何处打探得知这等名堂的？”

===Original===

“Why, every Sunday morning the priest discoursed to us about Heaven, and of how happy we would be there after death.”
“Has this woman died, then?” asked Koshchei.
“Yes, sir,” they told him﻿—“recently. And she will believe nothing we explain to her, but demands to be taken to Heaven.”
“Now, this is very vexing,” Koshchei said, “and I cannot, of course, put up with such scepticism. That would never do. So why do you not convey her to this Heaven which she believes in, and thus put an end to the matter?”
“But, sir,” they told him, “there is no such place.”
Then Koshchei reflected. “It is certainly strange that a place which does not exist should be a matter of public knowledge in another place. Where does this woman come from?”
“From Earth,” they told him.
“Where is that?” he asked: and they explained to him as well as they could.
“Oh, yes, over that way,” Koshchei interrupted. “I remember. Now﻿—but what is your name, woman who wish to go to Heaven?”
“Steinvor, sir: and if you please I am rather in a hurry to be with my children again. You see, I have not seen any of them for a long while.”
“But stay,” said Koshchei: “what is that which comes into this woman’s eyes as she speaks of her children?” They told him it was love.
“Did I create this love?” says Koshchei, who made things as they are. And they told him, no: and that there were many sorts of love, but that this especial sort was an illusion which women had invented for themselves, and which they exhibited in all dealings with their children. And Koshchei sighed.
“Tell me about your children,” Koshchei then said to Steinvor: “and look at me as you talk, so that I may see your eyes.”
So Steinvor talked of her children: and Koshchei, who made all things, listened very attentively. Of Coth she told him, of her only son, confessing Coth was the finest boy that ever lived﻿—“a little wild, sir, at first, but then you know what boys are,”﻿—and telling of how well Coth had done in business and of how he had even risen to be an alderman. Koshchei, who made all things, seemed properly impressed. Then Steinvor talked of her daughters, of Imperia and Lindamira and Christine: of Imperia’s beauty, and of Lindamira’s bravery under the mishaps of an unlucky marriage, and of Christine’s superlative housekeeping. “Fine women, sir, every one of them, with children of their own!

===Chinese===

“哎，每逢主日清晨，堂里的神父便会在祭坛上向我们讲经布道，细说天堂的光景，以及我们在死后升天该是何等欢欣喜乐。”

“这么说来，这位妇人如今已然作古咽气了？”科什切动问左右。

“启禀老爷，”仆役们回奏道——“正是近些日子的事。且任凭我等如何向她分说大义，她一概充耳不闻，硬是哭着喊着非要立刻动身前往天堂不可。”

“这委实令人烦扰恼怒得紧，”科什切喃喃道，“且老夫自然断无法容忍这等顽固的怀疑逆论。这断断不成体统。那么尔等为何不索性将她送去她心心念念的那个天堂，好将这桩公案一笔勾销呢？”

“启禀老爷，”左右战战兢兢回禀道，“天地间压根儿没有那么一处所在啊。”

科什切陷入了沉思。“一个根本查无实据的虚无之境，竟然能在另一个角落化作家喻户晓的人间常识，这委实透着蹊跷古怪。这位妇人究竟是打哪处乡土来的？”

“打地球来的，”左右奏道。

“那是何等所在？”他探问道：左右侍从遂殚精竭虑向他剖白描摹了一番。

“噢，对啦，在那个方向，”科什切打断道。“老夫依稀记起来了。且慢——这位一心要去天堂的老妇人，你姓甚名谁？”

“老妇人名叫施泰因沃尔（Steinvor），老爷：倘若老爷行好，老妇人眼下赶着去同我的儿女们重聚呢。您老不知，我已然好长好长日子未曾得见我的孩子们了。”

“且慢，”科什切蓦地止住她：“当提及自家骨肉之际，这位妇人眸子里泛起的那抹流光究竟是何等物事？”左右回奏道，那唤作‘慈爱’。

“这所谓慈爱乃是老夫亲手创设的么？”创设万物如其所是的科什切探问道。左右回奏道，断非老爷所造：世间的爱意琳琅满目，唯独这种特别的母爱，乃是妇道人家凭空为自家生造出来的某种虚幻执念，且在照拂抚育儿女的一举一动中展露无遗。科什切听罢，长长叹息了一声。

“同老夫讲讲你的儿女们吧，”科什切遂向施泰因沃尔吩咐道：“同老夫叙话之际且直视老夫的眼眸，好教老夫好生端详端详你的双眼。”

于是施泰因沃尔便絮絮叨叨讲起了自家的儿女：而缔造万物的科什切，自始至终皆听得专心致志。她向他讲起考斯（Coth），讲起她那唯一的独子，赌咒发誓考斯乃是天下最无可挑剔的英武好儿郎——“起初性子难免略带几分毛躁野气，老爷，可年轻汉子哪个不是这副德行呀，”——讲起考斯在行商买卖上何等飞黄腾达，甚至一路荣登市镇议员的显赫高位。缔造万物的科什切似乎适时流露出了钦佩动容之色。随后施泰因沃尔又讲起她的闺女们，讲起因佩里亚（Imperia）、琳达米拉（Lindamira）与克里斯蒂娜（Christine）：讲起因佩里亚倾国倾城的绝色天香，讲起琳达米拉在不幸姻缘坎坷中何等坚毅勇毅，讲起克里斯蒂娜持家过日子的巧夺天工。“个顶个皆是出类拔萃的体面妇人，老爷，且每一个皆诞下了一窝活泼可爱的儿女！

===Original===

and to me they still seem such babies, bless them!” And the decent little bent gray woman laughed. “I have been very lucky in my children, sir, and in my grandchildren, too,” she told Koshchei. “There is Jurgen, now, my Coth’s boy! You may not believe it, sir, but there is a story I must tell you about Jurgen﻿—” So she ran on very happily and proudly, while Koshchei, who made all things, listened, and watched the eyes of Steinvor.
Then privately Koshchei asked, “Are these children and grandchildren of Steinvor such as she reports?”
“No, sir,” they told him privately.
So as Steinvor talked Koshchei devised illusions in accordance with that which Steinvor said, and created such children and grandchildren as she described. Male and female he created them standing behind Steinvor, and all were beautiful and stainless: and Koshchei gave life to these illusions.
Then Koshchei bade her turn about. She obeyed: and Koshchei was forgotten.
Well, Koshchei sat there alone in the void, looking not very happy, and looking puzzled, and drumming upon his knee, and staring at the little bent gray woman, who was busied with her children and grandchildren, and had forgotten all about him. “But surely, Lindamira,” he hears Steinvor say, “we are not yet in Heaven.”﻿—“Ah, my dear mother,” replies her illusion of Lindamira, “to be with you again is Heaven: and besides, it may be that Heaven is like this, after all.”﻿—“My darling child, it is sweet of you to say that, and exactly like you to say that. But you know very well that Heaven is fully described in the Book of Revelations, in the Bible, as the glorious place that Heaven is. Whereas, as you can see for yourself, around us is nothing at all, and no person at all except that very civil gentleman to whom I was just talking; and who, between ourselves, seems woefully uninformed about the most ordinary matters.”
“Bring Earth to me,” says Koshchei. This was done, and Koshchei looked over the planet, and found a Bible. Koshchei opened the Bible, and read the Revelation of St. John the Divine, while Steinvor talked with her illusions. “I see,” said Koshchei. “The idea is a little garish. Still﻿—!” So he replaced the Bible, and bade them put Earth, too, in its proper place, for Koshchei dislikes wasting anything.

===Chinese===

而在老妇人眼里，她们瞧来依旧宛若襁褓里的稚子婴儿一般，愿老天保佑她们！”这位端庄质朴、身形佝偻的灰发老妪放声欢笑起来。“在儿女身上，老妇人委实是享尽了天大的福分，老爷，且在孙辈身上亦是福泽绵长，”她向科什切炫耀道。“就说朱尔根吧，我那考斯家的小子！尊驾或许难免不信，关于朱尔根，有一桩奇事老妇人定要向您老说道说道——”她就这般滔滔不绝、满面欢喜骄傲地倾诉着，而创设万物的科什切默然聆听着，双目死死凝视着施泰因沃尔那双泛着流光的眼眸。

随后科什切暗自私下探询左右：“施泰因沃尔口中的这些子嗣孙儿，真如她所言这般出类拔萃么？”

“回禀老爷，绝无此事，”左右私下密奏道。

于是趁着施泰因沃尔滔滔不绝叙谈之际，科什切依循着施泰因沃尔口中的描述随手点化生造出重重幻境，亲手缔造出了同她描摹一般无二的儿女孙辈。男男女女皆被他随手塑造、整饬地伫立在施泰因沃尔身后，个个冰清玉洁、宛若天人：科什切吹了一口仙气，赋予了这些虚妄幻象以活脱脱的真切生机。

随后科什切吩咐她转过身去。她依言回首：刹那间科什切被她彻底抛诸脑后忘得一干二净。

话说科什切形单影只孤坐在虚空混沌之中，神情瞧来并不怎么痛快，满面困惑地用手指在膝头轻轻叩击，呆呆凝望着那个正忙着同儿孙抱头痛哭、将他忘得干干净净的佝偻老妇人。“可千真万确呀，琳达米拉，”他依稀听见施泰因沃尔诧异道，“我们眼下分明尚未抵临真正的天堂呢。”——“啊呀，我最亲爱的母亲，”琳达米拉的幻象温存宽慰道，“能重新依偎在母亲怀抱，此处便已然是无上天堂了；况且说不定所谓天堂，本就是这般光景呢。”——“我心爱的心肝肉儿，你能吐露这等体己话委实贴心至极，且活脱脱是你的心性。可你心知肚明，《圣经》里的《启示录》（Book of Revelations）明明将天堂描摹得何等金碧辉煌、宏阔庄严！而眼下你自个儿瞧瞧，四下里光秃秃空无一物，除了方才同我絮叨的那位知书达礼的和气绅士之外连半个人影都没有；且恕老妇人私下嚼舌根，那位大老爷对于凡尘俗世最寻常不过的家常细务，似乎无知得令人扼腕呢。”

“把地球给老夫端过来，”科什切吩咐道。左右依言奉上，科什切将那颗蔚蓝行星托在掌心翻覆打量，顺藤摸瓜翻出了一本《圣经》。科什切翻开《圣经》，就着施泰因沃尔同子孙幻象欢叙的当口，一字一句细细研读起《使徒圣约翰之启示录》。“原来如此，”科什切评述道，“这道建筑构想未免略显俗艳张扬。不过话虽如此——！”于是他合拢《圣经》，吩咐手下将地球重新安置归位于宿命轨道，盖因科什切向来最嫌恶虚掷浪费任何微末之物。

===Original===

Then Koshchei smiled and created Heaven about Steinvor and her illusions, and he made Heaven just such a place as was described in the book.
“And so, Jurgen, that was how it came about,” ended the God of Jurgen’s grandmother. “And Me also Koshchei created at that time, with the seraphim and the saints and all the blessed, very much as you see us: and, of course, he caused us to have been here always, since the beginning of time, because that, too, was in the book.”
“But how could that be done?” says Jurgen, with brows puckering. “And in what way could Koshchei juggle so with time?”
“How should I know, since I am but the illusion of an old woman, as you have so frequently proved by logic? Let it suffice that whatever Koshchei wills, not only happens, but has already happened beyond the ancientest memory of man and his mother. How otherwise could he be Koshchei?”
“And all this,” said Jurgen, virtuously, “for a woman who was not even faithful to her husband!”
“Oh, very probably!” said the God: “at all events, it was done for a woman who loved. Koshchei will do almost anything to humor love, since love is one of the two things which are impossible to Koshchei.”
“I have heard that pride is impossible to Koshchei﻿—”
The God of Jurgen’s grandmother raised His white eyebrows. “What is pride? I do not think I ever heard of it before. Assuredly it is something that does not enter here.”
“But why is love impossible to Koshchei?”
“Because Koshchei made things as they are, and day and night he contemplates things as they are. How, then, can Koshchei love anything?”
But Jurgen shook his sleek black head. “That I cannot understand at all. If I were imprisoned in a cell wherein was nothing except my verses I would not be happy, and certainly I would not be proud: but even so, I would love my verses. I am afraid that I fall in more readily with the ideas of Grandfather Satan than with Yours; and without contradicting You, I cannot but wonder if what You reveal is true.”
“And how should I know whether or not I speak the truth?” the God asked of him, “since I am but the illusion of an old woman, as you have so frequently proved by logic.”
“Well, well!” said Jurgen, “You may be right in all matters, and certainly I cannot presume to say You are wrong: but still, at the same time﻿—! No, even now I do not quite believe in You.”

===Chinese===

随即科什切嘴角泛起一抹淡然笑意，亲手在施泰因沃尔及其子孙幻象周遭辟出了浩瀚天堂，依循着书卷上的描摹，将天堂塑造成了分毫不差的金碧辉煌之所。

“正因如此，朱尔根，一切便这般应运而生了，”朱尔根祖母的神明作结道。“而我本尊，亦是在那个骨节眼上被科什切顺手创造出来的，连同这漫天六翼炽天使、四方得道圣徒与芸芸蒙福圣灵一道，恰如你此刻眼中所见；且自然而然地，祂令我等显得自太古创世之初便已然永驻于此，盖因那卷圣册之上，亦赫然白纸黑字这般记载着。”

“可这等神迹究竟怎生可能办到？”朱尔根眉头拧成了一个疙瘩，追问道。“科什切又凭何能将时光岁月这般玩弄于股掌之间？”

“我不过是一个老妇人的一厢情愿虚幻幻象罢了，正如你昔日借由缜密逻辑千百次论证昭示的那般，我又怎生知晓个中奥秘？天下事但凡科什切心念所动，不仅立时应验成真，且早在世人及其先祖的最古老记忆之前便已然亘古成真。如若不然，他又何以为科什切呢？”

“而这凡百惊天造化，”朱尔根神色严肃、义正词严地叹道，“竟然仅仅是为了成全一个昔年对自家夫君甚至算不得从一而终的不贞妇人！”

“哎，大抵确是这般吧！”神明从容道：“无论如何，这一切乃是为了一位深怀大爱的妇人而破例缔造。凡是为了成全抚慰深情大爱，科什切几乎愿意赴汤蹈火纵容一切，皆因在这苍茫宇宙之间，深爱乃是科什切通天造化中唯二无从办到的绝壁天堑之一。”

“我曾听闻，傲慢亦是科什切无从领略的绝壁——”

朱尔根祖母的神明挑了挑雪白长眉。“傲慢究竟是何等物事？我似乎平生未曾听闻。毫无疑问，这等秽物断无法涉足天庭半步。”

“可为何深爱于科什切而言亦是绝无可能之物呢？”

“只因科什切亲手创设了万物的本来面目，且日日夜夜端详凝视着万物的本来面目。既然如此，科什切又怎生可能去深爱任何物事呢？”

朱尔根摇了摇那颗油亮乌黑的脑袋。“这我委实全然无法参透。倘若我被幽禁在一间除了自家的诗文残卷之外空无一物的囚室深处，我绝不会感到欢欣快慰，且我定然不会自命不凡傲骨嶙峋：然而纵然如此，我依然会深深钟爱我自家写就的诗行。我深恐自己更容易心悦诚服于撒旦祖父（Grandfather Satan）的高见，而非俯首于您的圣意；且在不欲忤逆顶撞您的前提下，我禁不住暗自纳罕尊驾所向我开示的究竟是否为铁证真理。”

“我又怎生知晓自己口中吐露的是否为真理呢？”神明反问道，“我不过是一个老妇人的一厢情愿虚幻妄念罢了，正如你昔日借由缜密逻辑千百次论证昭示的那样。”

“哎呀呀！”朱尔根叹道，“在万事万物上尊驾或许字字全中，且我自然断不敢狂妄指斥您有何差错：然而与此同时——！不，纵然到了此时此刻，我依然无法完完全全深信于您。”

===Original===

“Who could expect it of a clever fellow, who sees so clearly through the illusions of old women?” the God asked, a little wearily.
And Jurgen answered:
“God of my grandmother, I cannot quite believe in You, and Your doings as they are recorded I find incoherent and a little droll. But I am glad the affair has been so arranged that You may always now be real to brave and gentle persons who have believed in and have worshipped and have loved You. To have disappointed them would have been unfair: and it is right that before the faith they had in You not even Koshchei who made things as they are was able to be reasonable.
“God of my grandmother, I cannot quite believe in You; but remembering the sum of love and faith that has been given You, I tremble. I think of the dear people whose living was confident and glad because of their faith in You: I think of them, and in my heart contends a blind contrition, and a yearning, and an enviousness, and yet a tender sort of amusement colors all. Oh, God, there was never any other deity who had such dear worshippers as You have had, and You should be very proud of them.
“God of my grandmother, I cannot quite believe in You, yet I am not as those who would come peering at You reasonably. I, Jurgen, see You only through a mist of tears. For You were loved by those whom I loved greatly very long ago: and when I look at You it is Your worshippers and the dear believers of old that I remember. And it seems to me that dates and manuscripts and the opinions of learned persons are very trifling things beside what I remember, and what I envy!”
“Who could have expected such a monstrous clever fellow ever to envy the illusions of old women?” the God of Jurgen’s grandmother asked again: and yet His countenance was not unfriendly.
“Why, but,” said Jurgen, on a sudden, “why, but my grandmother﻿—in a way﻿—was right about Heaven and about You also. For certainly You seem to exist, and to reign in just such estate as she described. And yet, according to Your latest revelation, I too was right﻿—in a way﻿—about these things being an old woman’s delusions. I wonder now﻿—?”
“Yes, Jurgen?”
“Why, I wonder if everything is right, in a way? I wonder if that is the large secret of everything? It would not be a bad solution, sir,” said Jurgen, meditatively.
The God smiled.

===Chinese===

“对一个早已将老妇人的虚妄执念彻底洞若观火的绝顶聪明人而言，谁又能苛求他盲从笃信呢？”神明神情略显疲惫地反问道。

朱尔根肃穆答道：

“我祖母的神明啊，我终归无法完完全全深信于您，且史册经卷上关于您老人家的凡百行止记载，在我瞧来前言不搭后语且略显荒诞滑稽。然而我由衷额手称庆，整桩公案被这般慈悲周全地安顿妥帖，以致往后千秋万代，在那些深信您、膜拜您、深爱您的英勇柔顺仁人志士眼中，您得以永远化作活生生的真切救主。若教他们的痴心化作泡影，未免太失公允；且合情合理的是，面对世人对您所怀抱的这份赤诚信仰，连创设万物如其所是的科什切本尊，亦再无法去恪守冰冷刻板的理性常理了。

“我祖母的神明啊，我终归无法完完全全深信于您；然而思及世世代代倾注于您尊前的浩瀚大爱与赤诚信仰，我周身不由得战栗战栗。我忆及那些因着对您的深信不疑而在红尘中从容笃定、欢欣行路的至亲至爱之人：一念及他们，盲目的悔疚、刻骨的眷恋、深切的艳羡，便在我的心坎深处翻江倒海交织厮杀，然而一抹温存怜惜的戏谑，却给凡此种种心绪镀上了一层微妙的微光。噢神啊，放眼寰宇诸天，何曾有哪尊神明能坐拥如您门下这般令人怜爱的赤诚信众，您当真理当为他们感到由衷的骄傲与自豪。

“我祖母的神明啊，我终归无法完完全全深信于您，然而我绝非那些妄图借由冰冷理性对您指手画脚刨根问底的狂徒。我朱尔根，唯能透过一片滚沸朦胧的泪雾去仰望尊颜。只因昔年深爱着您的那些慈亲，恰恰正是我在极久远的旧日岁月中曾刻骨铭心深爱过的至亲骨肉；每当我凝眸仰望于您，浮现在我眼前的，尽是那些早已作古的至诚信徒与深爱您的昔日冤家。且在我看来，凡百生辰纪年、考据古卷以及饱学宿儒的阔论高见，比起我心头所铭记的这份温存、比起我心坎深处所艳羡的这份赤诚，皆不过是些微不足道的草芥微尘罢了！”

“谁能料到，这么一个绝顶聪颖的神妙奇才，有朝一日竟然会深深艳羡起老妇人的一厢情愿虚幻执念来呢？”朱尔根祖母的神明再度反问道：然而祂的面容上却并未流露出丝毫愠色与冷漠。

“哎，可话说回来，”朱尔根冷不防脱口而出，“这么说来，我祖母——在某种位阶上——关于天堂乃至关于尊驾，其实说得全都不错。只因您尊驾分明活生生君临于此，且依循着她口中描摹的仪仗排场统御着浩瀚乾坤。然而依循您方才的无上开示，我昔年关于这一切纯系村妪老妇痴心妄念的论断——在某种位阶上——其实亦全都没错。我眼下倒真纳了闷了——？”

“你想到了何处，朱尔根？”

“哎，我在琢磨，是否天地间凡百人事，在某种位阶上皆是对的？这莫非便是大千世界背后深藏的无上终极秘辛？这倒委实不失为一道绝妙完满的破局之道呢，先生，”朱尔根若有所思地沉吟道。

神明嘴角泛起了一抹高深莫测的微笑。

===Original===

Then suddenly that part of Heaven was vacant, except for Jurgen, who stood there quite alone. And before him was the throne of the vanished God and the sceptre of the God, and Jurgen saw that the seven spots upon the great book were of red sealing-wax.
Jurgen was afraid: but he was particularly appalled by his consciousness that he was not going to falter. “What, you who have been duke and prince and king and emperor and pope! and do such dignities content a Jurgen? Why, not at all,” says Jurgen.
So Jurgen ascended the throne of Heaven, and sat beneath that wondrous rainbow: and in his lap now was the book, and in his hand was the sceptre, of the God of Jurgen’s grandmother.
Jurgen sat thus, for a long while regarding the bright vacant courts of Heaven. “And what will you do now?” says Jurgen, aloud. “Oh, fretful little Jurgen, you that have complained because you had not your desire, you are omnipotent over Earth and all the affairs of men. What now is your desire?” And sitting thus terribly enthroned, the heart of Jurgen was as lead within him, and he felt old and very tired. “For I do not know. Oh, nothing can help me, for I do not know what thing it is that I desire! And this book and this sceptre and this throne avail me nothing at all, and nothing can ever avail me: for I am Jurgen who seeks he knows not what.”
So Jurgen shrugged, and climbed down from the throne of the God, and wandering at adventure, came presently to four archangels. They were seated upon a fleecy cloud, and they were eating milk and honey from gold porringers: and of these radiant beings Jurgen inquired the quickest way out of Heaven.
“For hereabouts are none of my illusions,” said Jurgen, “and I must now return to such illusions as are congenial. One must believe in something. And all that I have seen in Heaven I have admired and envied, but in none of these things could I believe, and with none of these things could I be satisfied. And while I think of it, I wonder now if any of you gentlemen can give me news of that Lisa who used to be my wife?”
He described her; and they regarded him with compassion.
But these archangels, he found, had never heard of Lisa, and they assured him there was no such person in Heaven.

===Chinese===

刹那间，那片天庭圣境变得空空如也，唯独剩下朱尔根一人孤零零伫立在原地。在他眼前，赫然横陈着已然隐没消逝的神明的巍峨宝座与王权金杖，且朱尔根清晰瞧见，那部宏伟巨册之上的7个火漆印（七个印记），尽皆是由鲜红的封蜡封印而成的。

朱尔根心生敬畏惶恐：然而最教他惊骇欲绝的，却是他清晰省悟到自家竟然毫无半分退缩战栗之意。“怎么着，你朱尔根生平曾领略过公爵、亲王、国王、皇帝乃至教宗的显赫尊荣！难道区区凡俗九五便能教一个朱尔根心满意足了么？嘿，断然不能，”朱尔根朗声道。

于是朱尔根拾级而上步入天庭宝座，端坐在那道巧夺天工的神妙彩虹之下：神明的无上法典厚厚落在他膝头，神明的王权金杖稳稳执掌在朱尔根祖母神明昔日所握的朱尔根手心。

朱尔根就这般巍然端坐，久久凝视着天庭那明净空旷的殿堂。“而眼下你究竟打算作何行止呢？”朱尔根大声自问自答道。“噢，多愁善感、成日价怨天尤人的小朱尔根啊，你先前总因欲壑难填未得遂愿而满腹牢骚，眼下你对这人间凡尘及其凡百人事已然手握通天全能。那么此时此刻，你心坎深处的终极渴望究竟为何？”就这般君临天庭至尊宝座，朱尔根的心头却沉重得宛若灌铅，整个人只觉苍老疲惫得无以复加。“皆因我一无所知。噢，天地间再无任何神力能拯救我了，只因我压根儿不知晓我心中所渴望的究竟是何物！而这部宝卷、这柄权杖、这座宝座，待我而言毫无半点用处，且天地间任何造化皆永生无从救赎于我：因我是朱尔根，一个连自家究竟在寻觅何物皆懵懂不知的苦难游魂。”

于是朱尔根耸了耸肩，信步走下神明的巍峨王座，漫无目的地随波逐流漫步游荡，未几便在云端撞见了4位大天使（四大天使）。他们正端坐在如羊毛般松软的祥云之上，用纯金钵盂津津有味地品尝着牛乳与蜂蜜：朱尔根遂向这几位容光焕发的光明圣灵垂询逃离天堂的最迅疾归途。

“只因在这方水土之上，寻不见我朱尔根自家的半点虚妄幻象，”朱尔根叹道，“我眼下必须折返回那些同我情投意合的尘世幻象之中。人这一辈子总归得信服点什么。而在天堂所目睹的凡百神迹，我无一不叹为观止、无一不艳羡万分，然而凡此种种神迹，我却无一能全心全意深信不疑，亦无一能教我由衷心满意足。且趁着我尚且记挂在心，我倒暗自琢磨诸位先生能否向在下通禀一声，昔日我那名为丽莎（Lisa）的浑家如今可曾在此间落脚？”

他细细描摹了丽莎的形容相貌；诸位天使听罢，皆满目怜悯地凝视着他。

然而他发觉，诸位大天使生平何曾听闻过丽莎的大名，且他们向他赌咒发誓保证，在这巍峨天堂之中，压根儿查无此人。

===Original===

For Steinvor had died when Jurgen was a boy, and so she had never seen Lisa; and in consequence, had not thought about Lisa one way or the other, when Steinvor outlined her notions to Koshchei who made things as they are.
Now Jurgen discovered, too, that, when his eyes first met the eyes of the God of Jurgen’s grandmother, Jurgen had stayed motionless for thirty-seven days, forgetful of everything save that the God of his grandmother was love.
“Nobody else has willingly turned away so soon,” Zachariel told him: “and we think that your insensibility is due to some evil virtue in the glittering garment which you are wearing, and of which the like was never seen in Heaven.”
“I did but search for justice,” Jurgen said: “and I could not find it in the eyes of your God, but only love and such forgiveness as troubled me.”
“Because of that should you rejoice,” the four archangels said; “and so should all that lives rejoice: and more particularly should we rejoice that dwell in Heaven, and hourly praise our Lord God’s negligence of justice, whereby we are permitted to enter into this place.”

===Chinese===

盖因施泰因沃尔早在朱尔根尚是垂髫少年时便已溘然长逝，因而她生平何曾见过丽莎半面；由此推论，昔日施泰因沃尔在虚空深处向创设万物如其所是的科什切描摹自家骨肉的蓝图轮廓之际，脑海中压根儿未曾起过关于丽莎的一丝一念。

此时朱尔根更惊异发觉，当他的目光头一遭同自家族祖母的神明圣目相接的那一刹那，朱尔根竟然已在天庭原地笔挺僵立了整整37天（三十七天）之久，将周身凡百杂念尽皆抛诸脑后，唯余一个念头：祖母的神明便是至纯的大爱。

“放眼古今天庭，何曾有人能在如此短暂的光阴里便主动抽身回转，”扎卡里尔（Zachariel）向他感慨道：“且我等私忖，你的这般麻木不仁，大抵是拜你周身所披裹的那袭璀璨华服深处所潜藏的某种邪门法力所赐，在天庭胜境，世人何曾见过这等妖冶的衣裳。”

“在下不过是在苦苦追寻公理正义罢了，”朱尔根叹道：“而在贵派神明的圣目深处，在下寻不见半点正义公道，唯独只有大爱，以及某种令在下心神不宁的浩瀚宽恕。”

“正因如此，你倒该欢呼雀跃才是，”4位大天使异口同声宣称道；“大千世界凡有生灵皆该为此欢呼雀跃：而长驻在这天堂深处的我等更该欢欣鼓舞，每时每刻皆在赞美颂扬我主神圣对公理正义的玩忽职守与宽宏大度，全凭着这份不讲公道的网开一面，我等方得以大模大样迈入这方极乐仙境啊。”

## XXXIX Of Compromises in Hell / 第三十九章 在地狱的妥协
===Original===

Now Grandfather Satan’s wife was called Phyllis: and apart from having wings like a bat’s, she was the loveliest little slip of devilishness that Jurgen had seen in a long while. Jurgen spent this night at the Black House of Barathum, and two more nights, or it might be three nights: and the details of what Jurgen used to do there, after supper, when he would walk alone in the Black House Gardens, among the artfully colored cast-iron flowers and shrubbery, and would so come to the grated windows of Phyllis’s room, and would stand there joking with her in the dark, are not requisite to this story.
Satan was very jealous of his wife, and kept one of her wings clipped and held her under lock and key, as the treasure that she was. But Jurgen was accustomed to say afterward that, while the gratings over the windows were very formidable, they only seemed somehow to enhance the piquancy of his commerce with Dame Phyllis. This queen, said Jurgen, he had found simply unexcelled at repartee.
Florimel considered the saying cryptic: just what precisely did his majesty mean?
“Why, that in any and all circumstances Dame Phyllis knows how to take a joke, and to return as good as she receives.”
“So your majesty has already informed me: and certainly jokes can be exchanged through a grating﻿—”
“Yes, that was what I meant. And Dame Phyllis appeared to appreciate my ready flow of humor. She informs me Grandfather Satan is of a cold dry temperament, with very little humor in him, so that they go for months without exchanging any pleasantries. Well, I am willing to taste any drink once: and for the rest, remembering that my host had very enormous and intimidating horns, I was at particular pains to deal fairly with my hostess. Though, indeed, it was more for the honor and the glory of the affair than anything else that I exchanged pleasantries with Satan’s wife. For to do that, my dear, I felt was worthy of the Emperor Jurgen.”
“Ah, I am afraid your majesty is a sad scapegrace,” replied Florimel: “however, we all know that the sceptre of an emperor is respected everywhere.”
“Indeed,” says Jurgen, “I have often regretted that I did not bring with me my jewelled sceptre when I left Noumaria.”
She shivered at some unspoken thought: it was not until some while afterward that Florimel told Jurgen of her humiliating misadventure with the absentminded Sultan of Garçao’s sceptre.

===Chinese===

话说撒旦祖父（Grandfather Satan）的浑家唤作菲莉丝（Phyllis）：除了背后生有一双蝙蝠般的肉翅之外，她委实是朱尔根（Jurgen）好长好长光阴未曾得见的一只最娇媚玲珑的恶魔小尤物。朱尔根在巴拉苏姆（Barathum）的黑宫（Black House）里歇宿了这一夜，继而又盘桓了2个乃至3个夜晚（两三个夜晚）：至于晚膳过后，当朱尔根独自一人在黑宫花园中漫步、穿行于巧夺天工涂抹着油彩的铸铁花卉与灌木丛之间、悄然踱至菲莉丝深闺安有铁栅栏的窗棂前，并在沉沉夜色中伫立于铁窗外同她高谈阔论逗弄取乐的凡百细枝末节，在此处自当无需一一赘述。

撒旦向来对自家浑家妒火中烧，硬生生剪短了她的一截肉翅，并以铁锁重关将她深锁闺阁，权当她是一桩绝世奇珍。然而朱尔根事后常津津乐道：虽说窗棂上的铁栅栏森严可怖，不知怎的却反倒教他同菲莉丝夫人的暗度陈仓更添了几分勾魂摄魄的辛辣风月之趣。朱尔根赞叹道，这位地狱王后在机锋对答的风月口才上委实天下无双。

弗洛里梅尔（Florimel）对此番评价大惑不解：陛下口中所指究竟为何？

“哎，无非是指在任何境遇之下，菲莉丝夫人皆深谙如何领会玩笑戏谑，且绝不甘落人下风、必定如数奉还。”

“这一点陛下先前已然通禀过臣妾；隔着铁栅栏，倒委实足可交换几句玩笑戏谑——”

“千真万确，在下所指的正合此意。且菲莉丝夫人似乎极赏识我这奔涌不绝的绝妙风趣。据她向在下诉苦，撒旦祖父性情冰冷枯燥，周身毫无半点幽默细胞，以致二人常常一连数月互不吐露半句体己玩笑。哎，天下杯中物凡是甘霖朱尔根皆愿尝上一尝；至于其余种种，思及东家掌柜生着那般庞大骇人的巍峨尖角，在下自然格外费心费力，打定主意要公允周全地伺候好女主人。虽说归根结底，在下同撒旦的浑家打情骂俏，大抵更多是图个荣耀威名的名分气数罢了。因能够办到这一桩，心肝肉儿，在下自忖方不辱没我朱尔根皇帝的九五风采。”

“啊呀，臣妾深恐陛下乃是个风流薄幸的浪荡子呢，”弗洛里梅尔巧笑嫣然道：“不过天下人尽皆晓得，一国皇帝的大权金杖在四海八荒皆受人顶礼膜拜。”

“千真万确，”朱尔根叹道，“在下常常悔恨昔日逃离努马里亚（Noumaria）之际，怎未曾将镶嵌满罕世奇珍的宝钻权杖随身带出。”

弗洛里梅尔为某道未曾吐露的隐秘遐想打了个寒噤；直至许久之后，弗洛里梅尔方才向朱尔根吞吞吐吐吐露了昔年关于那位健忘的加尔桑苏丹（Sultan of Garçao）的权杖所招致的惨痛难堪飞来横祸。

===Original===

Now she only replied that jewels might, conceivably, seem ostentatious and out of place.
Jurgen agreed to this truism: for of course they were living very quietly, and Jurgen was splendid enough for any reasonable wife’s requirements, in his glittering shirt.
So Jurgen got on pleasantly with Florimel. But he never became as fond of her as he had been of Guenevere or Anaïtis, nor one-tenth as fond of her as he had been of Chloris. In the first place, he suspected that Florimel had been invented by his father, and Coth and Jurgen had never any tastes in common: and in the second place, Jurgen could not but see that Florimel thought a great deal of his being an emperor.
“It is my title she loves, not me,” reflected Jurgen, sadly, “and her affection is less for that which is really integral to me than for imperial orbs and sceptres and suchlike external trappings.”
And Jurgen would come out of Florimel’s cleft considerably dejected, and would sit alone by the Sea of Blood, and would meditate how inequitable it was that the mere title of emperor should thus shut him off from sincerity and candor.
“We who are called kings and emperors are men like other men: we are as rightly entitled as other persons to the solace of true love and affection: instead, we live in a continuous isolation, and women offer us all things save their hearts, and we are a lonely folk. No, I cannot believe that Florimel loves me for myself alone: it is my title which dazzles her. And I would that I had never made myself the emperor of Noumaria: for this emperor goes about everywhere in a fabulous splendor, and is, very naturally, resistless in his semi-mythical magnificence. Ah, but these imperial gewgaws distract the thoughts of Florimel from the real Jurgen; so that the real Jurgen is a person whom she does not understand at all. And it is not fair.”
Then, too, he had a sort of prejudice against the way in which Florimel spent her time in seducing and murdering young men. It was not possible, of course, actually to blame the girl, since she was the victim of circumstances, and had no choice about becoming a vampire, once the cat had jumped over her coffin. Still, Jurgen always felt, in his illogical masculine way, that her vocation was not nice.

===Chinese===

此时此刻，她唯独温存应和道，深山幽居之间，穿戴金玉珠宝瞧来未免略显俗艳张扬、不合时宜。

朱尔根对这番颠扑不破的大实话深以为然：盖因二人眼下度日清幽安宁，且身披那袭流光溢彩的璀璨衬衫，朱尔根的气派足以餍足世间任何通情达理妻房的尊荣了。

于是朱尔根同弗洛里梅尔厮守得其乐融融。然而他却从未曾如迷恋吉尼薇尔（Guenevere）或阿娜伊蒂斯（Anaïtis）那般钟情于她，更及不上他对克洛丽斯（Chloris）之深情的十分之一。其一，他暗自疑心弗洛里梅尔乃是他父亲脑海中凭空生造出的虚妄尤物，而考斯（Coth）与朱尔根生平何曾有过半点心性品味的契合；其二，朱尔根不由自主地瞧破，弗洛里梅尔心底里真正在意看重的，乃是他身为九五至尊的皇帝门阀。

“她所深爱者唯有我的尊贵头衔，而非我朱尔根本尊，”朱尔根怅惘哀叹道，“且她的脉脉温存，与其说是倾注于我朱尔根本身不可分割的真性情，毋宁说是奔着皇权宝球、巍峨王杖与诸如此类的虚荣皮相招牌而去的。”

于是朱尔根常常心事重重、神情落寞地踱出弗洛里梅尔的岩穴裂隙，形单影只孤坐在血海（Sea of Blood）畔，对这等荒唐世相深思默想：区区一个皇帝的虚衔，竟生生将他与坦诚赤诚隔绝开来，这待他而言是何等有失公允。

“我等受命被称为君王与皇帝者，肉身骨子里同凡尘匹夫何曾有半分殊异：我等理当如天下芸芸众生一般，有权得享真挚爱恋与骨肉体贴的温存慰藉；然而反之，我等却活在无休止的绝顶孤绝之中，妇人奉献给我们的涵盖了天地万物唯独除了一颗真心，以致我等终归成了天底下最伶仃落寞的苦命人。不，我断无法相信弗洛里梅尔单单钟爱我自家本身：迷乱她心智的不过是我的皇帝尊衔罢了。我真悔不该自封为努马里亚皇帝：因这位皇帝无论走到何方皆笼罩在虚幻的万丈荣光之中，在其半人半神的光辉照耀下自然无往而不胜。唉，可这凡百浮华俗物，却生生分散了弗洛里梅尔的思绪，令她无从得见真真正正的朱尔根；以致真真正正的朱尔根在她眼中成了一个她压根儿读不懂的陌生人。这委实太失公道。”

况且，对于弗洛里梅尔成日将大好韶华虚掷于勾引、残害年轻后生的营生，他骨子里隐隐怀着一股难以名状的芥蒂。当然，苛责这姑娘委实毫无道理，因她不过是世事弄人的无辜遭难者，自打那只黑猫跃过她的灵柩棺木那一刹那起，沦落为吸血女妖便成了无从逃遁的铁定宿命。尽管如此，依循汉子向来不讲道理的顽固心性，朱尔根总不由觉得，她的这门行当终究算不得十分体面风光。

===Original===

And equally in the illogical way of men, did he persist in coaxing Florimel to tell him of her vampiric transactions, in spite of his underlying feeling that he would prefer to have his wife engaged in some other trade: and the merry little creature would humor him willingly enough, with her purple eyes a-sparkle, and with her vivid lips curling prettily back, so as to show her tiny white sharp teeth quite plainly.
She was really very pretty thus, as she told him of what happened in Copenhagen when young Count Osmund went down into the blind beggar-woman’s cellar, and what they did with bits of him; and of how one kind of serpent came to have a secret name, which, when cried aloud in the night, with the appropriate ceremony, will bring about delicious happenings; and of what one can do with small unchristened children, if only they do not kiss you, with their moist uncertain little mouths, for then this thing is impossible; and of what use she had made of young Sir Ganelon’s skull, when he was through with it, and she with him; and of what the young priest Wulfnoth had said to the crocodiles at the very last.
“Oh, yes, my life has its amusing side,” said Florimel: “and one likes to feel, of course, that one is not wholly out of touch with things, and is even, in one’s modest way, contributing to the suppression of folly. But even so, your majesty, the calls that are made upon one! the things that young men expect of you, as the price of their bodily and spiritual ruin! and the things their relatives say about you! and, above all, the constant strain, the irregular hours, and the continual effort to live up to one’s position! Oh, yes, your majesty, I was far happier when I was a consumptive seamstress and took pride in my buttonholes. But from a sister-in-law who only has you in to tea occasionally as a matter of duty, and who is prominent in churchwork, one may, of course, expect anything. And that reminds me that I really must tell your majesty about what happened in the hayloft, just after the abbot had finished undressing﻿—”
So she would chatter away, while Jurgen listened and smiled indulgently. For she certainly was very pretty. And so they kept house in Hell contentedly enough until Florimel’s vacation was at an end: and then they parted, without any tears but in perfect friendliness.

===Chinese===

且同样依循着汉子不讲常理的执拗癖好，尽管他打心底里巴不得自家浑家能改弦更张换门正经营生，却偏偏成日价诱哄着弗洛里梅尔，非要她向他巨细靡遗地倾吐她那些吸血戕人的风流勾当；而这位活泼俏丽的小尤物亦欣然应允曲意逢迎，一双紫罗兰色的妖眸流光溢彩，两瓣殷红朱唇娇艳地微微翻卷，将内里那一排洁白细碎尖锐的贝齿展露无遗。

当她如数家珍地向他娓娓叙述哥本哈根昔日的惨剧之际，她的神态委实美艳绝伦：讲述年轻的奥斯蒙德伯爵（Count Osmund）如何潜入盲眼乞丐婆的地窖深处，而众人又是如何将他的碎肉残肢物尽其用；讲述某种毒蛇如何坐拥一个隐秘不传的通灵密咒，一旦在子夜时分依循密仪高声诵出，便能催生出凡百销魂极乐；讲述倘若那些尚未受洗的襁褓婴儿未曾用温热湿润的小嘴去亲吻你（因一旦如此这桩法术便立时告破），世人究竟能拿他们炼就何等神异奇效；讲述年轻的加内隆爵士（Sir Ganelon）在阳寿终了之后，她如何将其头骨打发充作趁手器物；以及年轻的沃尔夫诺斯（Wulfnoth）神父在弥留之际对着鳄鱼吐露的最后诀别。

“噢，那是自然，臣妾的生活亦有其妙趣横生的一面呢，”弗洛里梅尔娇声道：“且人总喜欢体味到自己并未彻底脱离尘世流转，甚至借由自家的微薄绵力，亦在为荡平人间的愚顽痴念添砖加瓦。可纵然如此，陛下，压在弱女子肩头的重担委实太沉重啦！那些年轻后生以肉身与灵魂尽皆沉沦为代价，苛求臣妾去呈奉凡百纵容！他们府上的三姑六婆又在背后如何嚼舌根编排臣妾！尤其是那份无休止的精神损耗、作息无常的日夜颠倒，以及成日价为了配得上自家名分地位而硬撑的苦苦煎熬！噢，陛下，昔日我当个肺痨咯血的小裁缝、为自家缝出的精致纽扣眼暗自得意之际，过得远比眼下快活胜过千万倍。不过，摊上一个唯有逢年过节出于虚礼应酬才偶邀你登门饮茶、且在教会公事中出尽风头的寡嫂，世人对这等境遇自然早该料到一切后果。哎，这倒提醒了臣妾，关于老修道院院长刚褪尽衣衫之后在干草棚里上演的那桩奇事，臣妾当真非得向陛下好生分说一番不可——”

她就这般喋喋不休娇嗔絮语，而朱尔根则侧耳倾听，嘴角噙着宽纵宠溺的微笑。因她生得委实太惹人怜爱了。二人就这般在地狱深处夫唱妇随安分过日子，其乐融融，直至弗洛里梅尔的年度休假终于届满终局：随后二人执手道别，未曾洒下一滴离人泪，唯有一派亲睦融洽的依依惜别之情。

===Original===

And Jurgen always remembered Florimel most pleasantly, but not as a wife with whom he had ever been on terms of actual intimacy.
Now when this lovely vampire had quitted him, the Emperor Jurgen, in spite of his general popularity and the deference accorded his political views, was not quite happy in Hell.
“It is a comfort, at any rate,” said Jurgen, “to discover who originated the theory of democratic government. I have long wondered who started the notion that the way to get a wise decision on any conceivable question was to submit it to a popular vote. Now I know. Well, and the devils may be right in their doctrines; certainly I cannot go so far as to say they are wrong: but still, at the same time﻿—!”
For instance, this interminable effort to make the universe safe for democracy, this continual warring against Heaven because Heaven clung to a tyrannical form of autocratic government, sounded both logical and magnanimous, and was, of course, the only method of insuring any general triumph for democracy: yet it seemed rather futile to Jurgen, since, as he knew now, there was certainly something in the Celestial system which made for military efficiency, so that Heaven usually won. Moreover, Jurgen could not get over the fact that Hell was just a notion of his ancestors with which Koshchei had happened to fall in: for Jurgen had never much patience with antiquated ideas, particularly when anyone put them into practice, as Koshchei had done.
“Why, this place appears to me a glaring anachronism,” said Jurgen, brooding over the fires of Chorasma: “and its methods of tormenting conscientious people I cannot but consider very crude indeed. The devils are simple-minded and they mean well, as nobody would dream of denying, but that is just it: for hereabouts is needed some more pertinacious and efficiently disagreeable person﻿—”
And that, of course, reminded him of Dame Lisa: and so it was the thoughts of Jurgen turned again to doing the manly thing. And he sighed, and went among the devils tentatively looking and inquiring for that intrepid fiend who in the form of a black gentleman had carried off Dame Lisa. But a queer happening befell, and it was that nowhere could Jurgen find the black gentleman, nor did any of the devils know anything about him.

===Chinese===

在往后的岁月里，朱尔根始终对弗洛里梅尔怀着极惬意温暖的念想，却从未曾将她视作一个曾与他水乳交融、推心置腹的真正结发之妻。

当这位娇美绝伦的吸血女妖飘然离去之后，朱尔根皇帝纵然享尽了万民拥戴、且朝廷在凡百政见上皆对他尊崇备至，可他在地狱深处却依然算不得欢欣快活。

“无论如何，心头总算得上一桩慰藉，”朱尔根自嘲道，“总算参透了民选民主政体究竟是谁人开创发明的。长久以来我始终纳罕，究竟是哪个奇才想出的馊主意，认定凡事若想裁夺出公允英明的决断、唯一之途唯有交由贩夫走卒全民投票。眼下我总算大彻大悟了。哎，这帮魔鬼的高论经义或许全对；我自然断不敢指斥他们有何差错：然而与此同时——！”

譬如，这帮魔鬼为了将浩瀚宇宙改造为适宜民主的乐土而做出的旷日持久的劳顿折腾，为了反抗天庭顽固恪守专制独裁而向天庭发动的无休止连年征战，听来固然既合乎逻辑又大义凛然，且自然是确保民主大获全胜的唯一坦途：然而在朱尔根瞧来，这一切折腾却未免显得徒劳无益，皆因他此刻已然心知肚明，天庭那一套神圣体制之中，偏生坐拥某种在军伍战阵上极其凌厉高效的必胜法门，以致天庭在两军对垒中往往所向披靡。况且，朱尔根始终无法释怀的是：这暗无天日的地狱，充其量不过是他列祖列宗昔日的一桩荒唐臆想、而恰巧被科什切随手依葫芦画瓢点化出来罢了；朱尔根向来对食古不化的陈腐教条缺乏耐心，尤其是当有谁当真将其付诸实践之际——正如科什切当年那般。

“依我之见，这处绝地分明是一桩明晃晃的时空错乱谬误，”朱尔根凝视着霍拉斯马（Chorasma）的滚沸烈焰沉思道：“且其惩处折磨赤诚良心的方法手腕，我不得不认为委实粗鄙简陋之至。群魔皆是些心智质朴、本心不坏的善人，天下无人会妄图否认此节，然而症结偏生在此：因在这方水土之上，急需的乃是某个手腕更坚定执拗、行事更令人咬牙切齿嫌恶的真正刺头——”

而这一念头，自然而然教他猛然忆起了丽莎夫人（Dame Lisa）：于是朱尔根的心念再次掉转，打定主意要履行汉子的堂堂天职。他长叹一声，信步穿行于群魔之间，探头探脑四处打探寻访昔年化身黑衣绅士将丽莎夫人掠走的那位胆大包天的无上魔君。然而一桩怪事却横空降临：朱尔根翻遍地狱各处，竟寻不见那位黑衣绅士的半点踪迹，且群魔之中无一人知晓其究竟为何方神圣。

===Original===

“From what you tell us, Emperor Jurgen,” said they all, “your wife was an acidulous shrew, and the sort of woman who believes that whatever she does is right.”
“It was not a belief,” says Jurgen: “it was a mania with the poor dear.”
“By that fact, then, she is forever debarred from entering Hell.”
“You tell me news,” says Jurgen, “which if generally known would lead many husbands into vicious living.”
“But it is notorious that people are saved by faith. And there is no faith stronger than that of a bad-tempered woman in her own infallibility. Plainly, this wife of yours is the sort of person who cannot be tolerated by anybody short of the angels. We deduce that your Empress must be in Heaven.”
“Well, that sounds reasonable. And so to Heaven I will go, and it may be that there I shall find justice.”
“We would have you know,” the fiends cried, bristling, “that in Hell we have all kinds of justice, since our government is an enlightened democracy.”
“Just so,” says Jurgen: “in an enlightened democracy one has all kinds of justice, and I would not dream of denying it. But you have not, you conceive, that lesser plague, my wife; and it is she whom I must continue to look for.”
“Oh, as you like,” said they, “so long as you do not criticize the exigencies of wartime. But certainly we are sorry to see you going into a country where the benighted people put up with an autocrat Who was not duly elected to His position. And why need you continue seeking your wife’s society when it is so much pleasanter living in Hell?”
And Jurgen shrugged. “One has to do the manly thing sometimes.”
So the fiends told him the way to Heaven’s frontiers, pitying him. “But the crossing of the frontier must be your affair.”
“I have a cantrap,” said Jurgen; “and my stay in Hell has taught me how to use it.”
Then Jurgen followed his instructions, and went into Meridie, and turned to the left when he had come to the great puddle where the adders and toads are reared, and so passed through the mists of Tartarus, with due care of the wild lightning, and took the second turn to his left﻿—“always in seeking Heaven be guided by your heart,” had been the advice given him by devils﻿—and thus avoiding the abode of Jemra, he crossed the bridge over the Bottomless Pit and the solitary Narakas.

===Chinese===

“依朱尔根皇帝适才所言描摹，”群魔齐声评述道，“尊驾的原配浑家分明是个尖酸刻薄、不可理喻的泼悍恶妇，且是那种骨子里坚信自己无论干出何事皆天经地义大获全胜的难缠主儿。”

“那已然不能算作一份信念了，”朱尔根叹道：“对那可怜的冤家而言，那简直成了一桩根深蒂固的癫狂病症。”

“既然如此，单凭这一条铁律，她便被地狱的大门永远逐出在外了。”

“诸位倒是向在下通报了一条罕世奇闻，”朱尔根苦笑道，“这消息倘若在阳世走漏风声，怕是会诱使成千上万的当家汉子放纵堕落、无恶不作呢。”

“然而天下尽皆晓得，因信得称义得救赎。世间断无任何信仰，能比一个泼妇对其自身的颠扑不破绝对无谬更为坚若磐石。显而易见，尊驾的那位浑家，乃是除了九天天使之外世间断无任何人能容忍包容的绝世恶煞。我等由衷推论，陛下的皇后此刻必定高居天堂之中。”

“哎，这话听来倒颇通情达理。既然如此，老子索性便杀上天堂，说不定在天庭我能寻觅到公理正义呢。”

“我等必须提醒尊驾，”众恶煞魔鬼闻言纷纷倒竖尖毛，忿忿抗辩道，“在地狱深处我们坐拥凡百公理正义，因我们的朝廷乃是开明启蒙的至高民主！”

“千真万确，”朱尔根打圆场道：“在一处开明启蒙的民主胜境，凡人自得尽享凡百公理正义，在下断不敢有一丝否认。然而诸位手头，却唯独未曾坐拥在下那桩名为‘结发之妻’的较小灾星；而她正是在下必须披荆斩棘继续寻觅的下落。”

“随尊驾尊便罢，”魔鬼们说道，“只要你休要妄议战时戒严体制便成。不过眼睁睁瞧着你迈入一个万民俯首顺从一位未经民主公推选任的独裁霸主所统辖的蒙昧之乡，我等由衷替你感到惋惜。且既然置身地狱是这般快活如意，你又何苦非要去同你的浑家重修旧好呢？”

朱尔根耸了耸肩。“身为堂堂男儿，有时候总归不得不挺身而出干出点顶天立地的事体来。”

于是众恶煞满目怜悯地为他指明了通往天庭边境的坦途。“然而强渡边界天堑，却全凭尊驾自求多福了。”
