Oh, but this is a very dangerous heresy, let me tell you, an Inquisition matter, a consistory business! Yet, luckily, upon his own contention, this Pedro Juliani﻿—”
“And that was his name, too, for he told me! You evidently know all about it, messire,” said the young angel, visibly impressed.
“Of course, I know all about it. Well, I repeat, upon his own contention this man is nonexistent, and so, whatever he may say amounts to nothing. For he tells you there was never any Pope John the Twentieth: and either he is lying or he is telling you the truth. If he is lying, you, of course, ought not to believe him: yet, if he is telling you the truth, about there never having been any Pope John the Twentieth, why then, quite plainly, there was never any Pope John the Twenty-first, so that this man asserts his own nonexistence; and thus is talking nonsense, and you, of course, ought not to believe in nonsense.

===Chinese===

我查阅了名册——您知道的，所有教皇一经离世便会立即获准升入此地，从不过问其私事，以免惹出什么不幸的丑闻——我们名册上共载有二十三位若望教皇。果然不出所料，为若望二十世预备的宅邸正空置着呢。他似乎是唯一一位未在天国的教皇。”
“哎，那是自然，”朱尔根洋洋得意地说，“既然你亲眼见着我这位昔日的罗马主教、上帝众仆之仆，此刻正伫立在这下方的灰烬堆上。”
“话虽如此，但同名序列里的其他人似乎没一个认得出您。若望十九世说他从未听说过您，还叫人别在他练竖琴的当口打扰他——”
“他自然是在我登基之前便已亡故了。”
“——而若望二十一世则说，他觉得大概是不知怎么数错了数，世上根本就不曾有过什么若望二十世。他说您必定是个冒牌货。”
“唉，同行相轻的嫉妒啊！”朱尔根叹道，“哎呀呀，这可真令人痛心，直叫人对人性的阴暗大失所望。那么孩子，我公公道道问你一句，倘若没有第二十任，又何来第二十一任？当一位教皇承认自己在初等算术上犯了糊涂，那至高无上的教皇无谬论又将置于何地？噢，我得告诉你，这可是极其凶险的异端邪说，理应交由宗教裁判所严加查办，交由枢机主教会议审理！不过万幸的是，照他自己的论调，这位佩德罗·朱利亚尼（Pedro Juliani）——”
“他确实叫这个名字，正是他亲口对我说的！爵爷，您显然对这一切了如指掌，”年轻的天使显得大为信服地说。
“那是自然，我全都一清二楚。好啦，我且重申，依照他自己的论调，此人根本就不存在，因此无论他说什么都不过是一纸空谈。因为他宣称世上从未有过教皇若望二十世：要么他在撒谎，要么他在讲真话。倘若他在撒谎，你自然不该信他；但倘若他在讲真话，真如他所言从未有过若望二十世，那么显而易见，世上也断不可能有什么若望二十一世，由此便等于此人在宣称他自己根本不存在；因而纯系一派胡言，而你自然绝不该听信胡言乱语。

===Original===

Even did we grant his insane contention that he is nobody, you are too well brought up, I am sure, to dispute that nobody tells lies in Heaven: it follows that in this case nobody is lying; and so, of course, I must be telling the truth, and you have no choice save to believe me.”
“Now, certainly that sounds all right,” the younger Jurgen conceded: “though you explain it so quickly it is a little difficult to follow you.”
“Ah, but furthermore, and over and above this, and as a tangible proof of the infallible particularity of every syllable of my assertion,” observes the elder Jurgen, “if you will look in the garret of Heaven you will find the identical ladder upon which I descended hither, and which I directed them to lay aside until I was ready to come up again. Indeed, I was just about to ask you to fetch it, inasmuch as my business here is satisfactorily concluded.”
Well, the boy agreed that the word of no pope, whether in Hell or Heaven, was tangible proof like a ladder: and again he was off. Jurgen waited, in tolerable confidence.
It was a matter of logic. Jacob’s Ladder must from all accounts have been far too valuable to throw away after one night’s use at Beth-El; it would come in very handy on Judgment Day: and Jurgen’s knowledge of Lisa enabled him to deduce that anything which was being kept because it would come in handy some day would inevitably be stored in the garret, in any establishment imaginable by women. “And it is notorious that Heaven is a delusion of old women. Why, the thing is a certainty,” said Jurgen; “simply a mathematical certainty.”
And events proved his logic correct: for presently the younger Jurgen came back with Jacob’s Ladder, which was rather cobwebby and obsolete looking after having been lain aside so long.
“So you see you were perfectly right,” then said this younger Jurgen, as he lowered Jacob’s Ladder into Hell. “Oh, Messire John, do hurry up and have it out with that old fellow who slandered you!”

===Chinese===

纵使我们姑且采纳他那疯癫荒谬的论调，承认他纯属无名小卒，但我确信，以你受过的良好教养，绝不会质疑天国之中绝无谎言：由此推断，在这种情形下便没有谁在撒谎；因此，讲出真话的自然必定是我，而你除了相信我之外别无选择。”
“听起来倒确实很有道理，”年轻的朱尔根承认道，“虽然您解释得太快，让人有些跟不上。”
“啊，不仅如此，更进一步而言，作为证实我句句属实、毫无谬误的确凿凭据，”年长的朱尔根评述道，“你若去天国的阁楼里瞧上一眼，便能找到当年我下来时踩着的那架一模一样的梯子，当时我吩咐他们将其搁置一旁，好待我准备重返天界时使用。实不相瞒，由于我在下界的公干已然圆满结束，我方才正打算请你帮我把它取来呢。”
少年赞同道，任何教皇的话语，不论是在地狱还是在天国，都比不上梯子这般实打实的凭证：于是他又飞奔而去。朱尔根胸有成竹地候在原地。
这全凭逻辑推断。依照各方记述，雅各的天梯（Jacob’s Ladder）在伯特利（Beth-El）用过一夜之后，断不至于因过于珍贵而被随手扔掉；到了最后的审判日（Judgment Day）它必定大派用场；而朱尔根对丽莎的了解使他推知，在任何女人所能想象出的家当规制里，凡是因日后用得上而保留下来的物件，毫无例外都会被堆在阁楼上。“况且世人皆知，天国本就是老妇人的痴心妄想。哎，这简直是十拿九稳的事，”朱尔根说，“纯粹是数学般的必然。”
事实证明他的逻辑完全正确：不多时，年轻的朱尔根便扛着雅各的天梯返了回来，由于闲置太久，梯子上结满了蛛网，显得颇为陈旧过时。
“您瞧，您说得一点儿不错，”年轻的朱尔根将雅各的天梯垂入地狱，说道。“噢，若望爵爷，快爬上来，去同那个诽谤您的老家伙好好理论理论！”

===Original===

Thus it came about that Jurgen clambered merrily from Hell to Heaven upon a ladder of unalloyed, time-tested gold: and as he climbed the shirt of Nessus glittered handsomely in the light which shone from Heaven: and by this great light above him, as Jurgen mounted higher and yet higher, the shadow of Jurgen was lengthened beyond belief along the sheer white wall of Heaven, as though the shadow were reluctant and adhered tenaciously to Hell. Yet presently Jurgen leaped the ramparts: and then the shadow leaped too; and so his shadow came with Jurgen into Heaven, and huddled dispiritedly at Jurgen’s feet.
“Well, well!” thinks Jurgen, “certainly there is no disputing the magic of the Master Philologist when it is correctly employed. For through its aid I am entering alive into Heaven, as only Enoch and Elijah have done before me: and moreover, if this boy is to be believed, one of the very handsomest of Heaven’s many mansions awaits my occupancy. One could not ask more of any magician fairly. Aha, if only Lisa could see me now!”
That was his first thought. Afterward Jurgen tore up the cantrap and scattered its fragments as the Master Philologist had directed. Then Jurgen turned to the boy who aided Jurgen to get into Heaven.
“Come, youngster, and let us have a good look at you!”
And Jurgen talked with the boy that he had once been, and stood face to face with all that Jurgen had been and was not any longer. And this was the one happening which befell Jurgen that the writer of the tale lacked heart to tell of.
So Jurgen quitted the boy that he had been. But first had Jurgen learned that in this place his grandmother Steinvor (whom King Smoit had loved) abode and was happy in her notion of Heaven; and that about her were her notions of her children and of her grandchildren. Steinvor had never imagined her husband in Heaven, nor King Smoit either.
“That is a circumstance,” says Jurgen, “which heartens me to hope one may find justice here. Yet I shall keep away from my grandmother, the Steinvor whom I knew and loved, and who loved me so blindly that this boy here is her notion of me. Yes, in mere fairness to her, I must keep away.”
So he avoided that part of Heaven wherein were his grandmother’s illusions: and this was counted for righteousness in Jurgen. That part of Heaven smelt of mignonette, and a starling was singing there.

===Chinese===

就这样，朱尔根踏着纯粹且历经岁月洗礼的纯金天梯，欢天喜地自地狱攀向天国：当他向上攀登时，涅索斯（Nessus）的衬衫在天国倾泻的光芒中熠熠生辉；仰仗着头顶上这片浩瀚光华，随着朱尔根越爬越高，朱尔根的影子在天国陡峭洁白的城墙上被拉得不可思议之长，仿佛那影子恋恋不舍，顽固地依附着地狱。然而须臾之间，朱尔根便跃过了壁垒：那影子随即也跟着跃了过来；于是他的影子随同朱尔根一道迈入了天国，垂头丧气地蜷缩在朱尔根脚边。
“好哇，好哇！”朱尔根心想，“倘若运用得当，语文学大师的法术当真无可匹敌。借助其威力，我竟得以肉身活着升入天国，此前唯得以诺（Enoch）与以利亚（Elijah）曾获此殊荣；况且，倘若这孩子所言不虚，天国林立的宅邸中最华美的一所正虚位以待呢。平心而论，对任何魔法师的要求也莫过于此了。哈哈，要是丽莎眼下能瞧见我该多好！”
那是他的首要念头。随后朱尔根撕毁了符咒，遵照语文学大师的叮嘱将碎片随风撒去。接着朱尔根转向协助朱尔根登入天国的那个少年。
“来吧，小家伙，让我好好打量打量你！”
于是朱尔根同昔日的那个少年攀谈起来，同朱尔根曾经拥有的、如今却已不复存在的一切面面相对。而这也是降临在朱尔根身上的诸般境遇中，故事的作者狠不下心来诉诸笔端的一幕。
就这样，朱尔根辞别了昔日的自己。但他首先获悉，他的祖母斯坦沃尔（斯莫伊特国王［King Smoit］曾钟爱过她）便安居在此处，沉浸在她自己设想的天国之中自得其乐；而环绕着她的，乃是她对自己儿孙的种种设想。斯坦沃尔从未设想过自己的丈夫会在天国，也从未设想过斯莫伊特国王会在这里。
“这番情形，”朱尔根说，“倒激励我怀揣在此处觅得公道的希望。然而我必须远离我的祖母，远离那位我所熟识且深爱、又如此盲目疼爱着我的斯坦沃尔——以至于眼前的这少年便是她心目中对我的设想。不错，哪怕单单本着对她的公允，我也必须敬而远之。”
于是他避开了天国中存留着他祖母幻象的那片区域：这在朱尔根身上被算作一件义举。天国的那个角落弥漫着木犀草的幽香，一只八哥正在那里婉转啼鸣。

## XIX The Brown Man with Queer Feet / 第十九章 长着怪脚的棕肤汉子
===Original===

Early in the following morning Jurgen left Cameliard, traveling toward Carohaise, and went into the Druid forest there, and followed Merlin’s instructions.
“Not that I for a moment believe in such nonsense,” said Jurgen: “but it will be amusing to see what comes of this business, and it is unjust to deny even nonsense a fair trial.”
So he presently observed a sun-browned brawny fellow, who sat upon the bank of a stream, dabbling his feet in the water, and making music with a pipe constructed of seven reeds of irregular lengths. To him Jurgen displayed, in such a manner as Merlin had prescribed, the token which Merlin had given. The man made a peculiar sign, and rose. Jurgen saw that this man’s feet were unusual.
Jurgen bowed low, and he said, as Merlin had bidden: “Now praise be to thee, thou lord of the two truths! I have come to thee, O most wise, that I may learn thy secret. I would know thee, and would know the forty-two mighty ones who dwell with thee in the hall of the two truths, and who are nourished by evildoers, and who partake of wicked blood each day of the reckoning before Wennofree. I would know thee for what thou art.”
The brown man answered: “I am everything that was and that is to be. Never has any mortal been able to discover what I am.”
Then this brown man conducted Jurgen to an open glen, at the heart of the forest.
“Merlin dared not come himself, because,” observed the brown man, “Merlin is wise. But you are a poet. So you will presently forget that which you are about to see, or at worst you will tell pleasant lies about it, particularly to yourself.”
“I do not know about that,” says Jurgen, “but I am willing to taste any drink once. What are you about to show me?”
The brown man answered: “All.”
So it was near evening when they came out of the glen. It was dark now, for a storm had risen. The brown man was smiling, and Jurgen was in a flutter.
“It is not true,” Jurgen protested. “What you have shown me is a pack of nonsense. It is the degraded lunacy of a so-called Realist. It is sorcery and pure childishness and abominable blasphemy. It is, in a word, something I do not choose to believe. You ought to be ashamed of yourself!”
“Even so, you do believe me, Jurgen.”
“I believe that you are an honest man and that I am your cousin: so there are two more lies for you.”

===Chinese===

次日清晨，朱尔根（Jurgen）离开卡梅利亚德（Cameliard），动身前往卡罗海斯（Carohaise），走入那里的德鲁伊森林，遵照梅林（Merlin）的指示行事。
“我倒不是真会哪怕相信一星半点这等胡话，”朱尔根说，“不过看看这桩事能搞出什么名堂倒挺有意思，哪怕是对胡话，不给个公道的求证机会也是不公平的。”
不久，他便瞧见一个晒得黝黑的壮汉正坐在一道溪流岸边，双脚在溪水里戏水，手中把玩着一支由长短不一的七根苇管拼成的排箫，吹奏出悠扬的乐声。朱尔根按照梅林吩咐的方式，向他亮出了梅林给的信物。那汉子打了个奇异的手势，随即站起身来。朱尔根瞧见这汉子的双脚异于常人。
朱尔根深深鞠了一躬，按照梅林的叮嘱说道：“赞美归于您，双重真理之主（lord of the two truths）！至睿至智者啊，我来到您面前，是为了得悉您的奥秘。我渴望认识您，渴望认识与您一同栖息在双重真理之殿（hall of the two truths）里的四十二位大能者——他们以作恶者为食，在温诺弗里（Wennofree）面前清算的每一日痛饮邪恶之血。我渴望认清您的真面目。”
棕肤汉子答道：“我即曾存在与将存在之万物。凡夫俗子从未有人参透我为何物。”
随后，棕肤汉子领着朱尔根走入森林腹地的一处开阔峡谷。
“梅林自己不敢来，皆因，”棕肤汉子评述道，“梅林是个聪明人。但你是个诗人。因此你很快便会把即将见识到的一切忘个干净，最不济你也会就此编造悦耳的谎言，尤其用来欺瞒你自己。”
“这我可吃不准，”朱尔根说，“但我乐意对任何美酒浅尝一口。你究竟要让我瞧些什么？”
棕肤汉子答道：“万象一切。”
待他们走出峡谷时，天色已近黄昏。此刻风暴已起，天地间一片晦暗。棕肤汉子面带微笑，朱尔根却心惊肉跳、慌乱不安。
“这不是真的，”朱尔根抗辩道，“你让我瞧的纯粹是一派胡言。这不过是所谓实在论者卑劣下作的发疯妄想。这是巫术，是彻头彻尾的儿戏，是不可饶恕的亵渎。总而言之，这是我不乐意去相信的东西。你真该为自己感到羞耻！”
“纵然如此，朱尔根，你心里终究是信了我的。”
“我还信你是个老实人、信我是你的堂兄弟呢：得，又平白多奉送你两句谎话。”

===Original===

The brown man said, still smiling: “Yes, you are certainly a poet, you who have borrowed the apparel of my cousin. For you come out of my glen, and from my candor, as sane as when you entered. That is not saying much, to be sure, in praise of a poet’s sanity at any time. But Merlin would have died, and Merlin would have died without regret, if Merlin had seen what you have seen, because Merlin receives facts reasonably.”
“Facts! sanity! and reason!” Jurgen raged: “why, but what nonsense you are talking! Were there a bit of truth in your silly puppetry this world of time and space and consciousness would be a bubble, a bubble which contained the sun and moon and the high stars, and still was but a bubble in fermenting swill! I must go cleanse my mind of all this foulness. You would have me believe that men, that all men who have ever lived or shall ever live hereafter, that even I am of no importance! Why, there would be no justice in any such arrangement, no justice anywhere!”
“That vexed you, did it not? It vexes me at times, even me, who under Koshchei’s will alone am changeless.”
“I do not know about your variability: but I stick to my opinion about your veracity,” says Jurgen, for all that he was upon the verge of hysteria. “Yes, if lies could choke people that shaggy throat would certainly be sore.”
Then the brown man stamped his foot, and the striking of his foot upon the moss made a new noise such as Jurgen had never heard: for the noise seemed to come multitudinously from every side, at first as though each leaf in the forest were tinily cachinnating; and then this noise was swelled by the mirth of larger creatures, and echoes played with this noise, until there was a reverberation everywhere like that of thunder. The earth moved under their feet very much as a beast twitches its skin under the annoyance of flies. Another queer thing Jurgen noticed, and it was that the trees about the glen had writhed and arched their trunks, and so had bended, much as candles bend in very hot weather, to lay their topmost foliage at the feet of the brown man. And the brown man’s appearance was changed as he stood there, terrible in a continuous brown glare from the low-hanging clouds, and with the forest making obeisance, and with shivering and laughter everywhere.
“Make answer, you who chatter about justice!

===Chinese===

棕肤汉子依旧微笑着说：“不错，你确然是个诗人，竟还借用了我堂兄的行头。因为你从我的峡谷中走出来、见识了我的坦诚相待之后，竟同走进去时一样清醒如初。当然啦，无论在何时，夸赞诗人的神智清醒都算不上什么褒奖之词。但倘若梅林见识了你方才目睹的景象，梅林必定当场暴毙，且会死而无憾，盖因梅林向来理性地接纳事实。”
“事实！清醒！理性！”朱尔根勃然大怒，“哎呀，你讲的都是些什么胡言乱语！若是你那些愚蠢的木偶把戏真有半点真实成分，这个由时间、空间与知觉构成的世界便不过是个气泡，一个包容了日月星辰、却终究不过是发酵泔水里翻腾的一颗气泡！我得赶紧去洗净心头的这股污秽。你竟妄图让我相信：世人、有史以来活过的以及往后将要降生的一切世人，甚至连我都毫无分量！哎呀，倘若世间当真是这般格局，便谈不上任何公道，天下哪里还有公道可言！”
“这惹恼了你，不是么？有时这也会惹恼我，甚至连我——唯独遵照科谢（Koshchei）的旨意才恒久不移的我——也会心生烦扰。”
“我不管你变是不变：反正我认定你嘴里没有半句实话，”朱尔根说，尽管他此刻几乎已处于歇斯底里的边缘。“是啊，要是谎话能把人噎死，你那长毛的喉咙早该痛得发溃了。”
这时棕肤汉子顿了顿脚，他的足底踏在苔藓上，发出一种朱尔根从未听闻过的奇异声响：那声浪仿佛排山倒海般自四面八方席卷而来，起初恰似林间的每一片树叶都在细细嗤嗤发笑；继而这声响又因更大生灵的狂笑而骤然壮大，回声与之应和交织，直至周遭宛若滚滚雷鸣般轰隆震荡。他们脚下的土地剧烈蠕动，活像一头被苍蝇叮扰难耐的野兽在抽搐颤动表皮。朱尔根还察觉到了另一桩异象：峡谷周遭的树木纷纷扭曲弯拱起树干，直挺挺弯下身来，宛如烈日酷暑下弯折的蜡烛，将树梢顶端的枝叶匍匐垂在棕肤汉子的脚下。站在那里的棕肤汉子形貌骤变，在低垂密云所透出的连绵褐色光芒中显得可怖骇人，整个森林尽皆俯首称臣，四下里充斥着战栗与狂笑。
“回答我，你这个满嘴喋喋不休嚷着公道的家伙！

===Original===

how if I slew you now,” says the brown man﻿—“I being what I am?”
“Slay me, then!” says Jurgen, with shut eyes, for he did not at all like the appearance of things. “Yes, you can kill me if you choose, but it is beyond your power to make me believe that there is no justice anywhere, and that I am unimportant. For I would have you know I am a monstrous clever fellow. As for you, you are either a delusion or a god or a degraded Realist. But whatever you are, you have lied to me, and I know that you have lied, and I will not believe in the insignificance of Jurgen.”
Chillingly came the whisper of the brown man: “Poor fool! O shuddering, stiff-necked fool! and have you not just seen that which you may not ever quite forget?”
“None the less, I think there is something in me which will endure. I am fettered by cowardice, I am enfeebled by disastrous memories; and I am maimed by old follies. Still, I seem to detect in myself something which is permanent and rather fine. Underneath everything, and in spite of everything, I really do seem to detect that something. What role that something is to enact after the death of my body, and upon what stage, I cannot guess. When fortune knocks I shall open the door. Meanwhile I tell you candidly, you brown man, there is something in Jurgen far too admirable for any intelligent arbiter ever to fling into the dustheap. I am, if nothing else, a monstrous clever fellow: and I think I shall endure, somehow. Yes, cap in hand goes through the land, as the saying is, and I believe I can contrive some trick to cheat oblivion when the need arises,” says Jurgen, trembling, and gulping, and with his eyes shut tight, but even so, with his mind quite made up about it. “Of course you may be right; and certainly I cannot go so far as to say you are wrong: but still, at the same time﻿—”
“Now but before a fool’s opinion of himself,” the brown man cried, “the Gods are powerless. Oh, yes, and envious, too!”
And when Jurgen very cautiously opened his eyes the brown man had left him physically unharmed. But the state of Jurgen’s nervous system was deplorable.

===Chinese===

倘若我此刻将你诛杀，”棕肤汉子说道，“——以我这般身份——又当如何？”
“那就杀了我吧！”朱尔根紧闭着双眼说道，因为他一点儿也不喜欢眼前的这副景象。“是啊，你若乐意，尽管杀了我便是，但你休想迫使我相信世间毫无公道可言、休想让我承认自己微不足道。因为我倒要让你明白，我是一个绝顶聪明的家伙。至于你，你要么是一桩幻象，要么是一位神明，要么是个卑劣下作的实在论者。但无论你是何方神圣，你都在对我撒谎，我心知肚明你说了谎，我绝不相信朱尔根会无足轻重。”
棕肤汉子冷冰冰地低语道：“可怜的蠢货！噢，瑟瑟发抖、冥顽不灵的蠢货！难道你方才未曾目睹那令你至死难忘的一切吗？”
“纵然如此，我依然坚信我自身蕴含着某种恒久长存的东西。我虽受制于怯懦，消沉于痛苦的回忆，残缺于往昔的愚蠢，但我依然仿佛在自身之中觉察到某种永恒而美好的东西。在万事万物底层，抛开一切不谈，我真真切切仿佛察觉到了那点东西。那点东西在我的肉身消亡之后将要在何种舞台上扮演何种角色，我不得而知。待机运敲门，我自会迎门相接。其间我坦白告诉你，棕肤汉子，朱尔根体内蕴含着某种过于值得赞许的东西，任何明智的裁决者都绝不忍将其弃入尘埃垃圾堆。哪怕别无长处，我也终归是个绝顶聪明的家伙：而且我相信无论如何我都能挺立不倒。是啊，正如俗话所说，脱帽致敬行天下，我相信待到紧要关头，我定能巧施妙计蒙蔽遗忘，”朱尔根发着抖，吞咽着唾沫，紧闭着双眼，可即便是这般模样，他的心意却已打定。“当然，你兴许是对的；我也断不至于硬说你是错的：然而，与此同时——”
“而在一个蠢货对自身的盲目自负面前，”棕肤汉子高呼道，“诸神亦无可奈何。噢，不错，而且艳羡不已！”
当朱尔根战战兢兢睁开双眼时，棕肤汉子并未伤他一毫。然而朱尔根的精神状况却已狼狈不堪。

## XLVI The Desire of Anaïtis / 第四十六章 阿娜伊蒂斯的渴望
===Original===

And again Koshchei waved his hand. Then came to Jurgen a woman who was strangely gifted and perverse. Her dark eyes glittered: upon her head was a network of red coral, with branches radiating downward, and her tunic was of two colors, being shot with black and crimson curiously mingled.
And Anaïtis also had forgotten Jurgen, or else she did not recognize him in this man of forty and something: and again belief awoke in Jurgen’s heart that this was the only woman whom Jurgen had really loved, as he listened to Anaïtis and to her talk of marvelous things.
Of the lore of Thaïs she spoke, and of the schooling of Sappho, and of the secrets of Rhodopê, and of the mourning for Adonis: and the refrain of all her talking was not changed. “For we have but a little while to live, and none knows his fate thereafter. So that a man possesses nothing certainly save a brief loan of his own body: and yet the body of man is capable of much curious pleasure. As thus and thus,” says she. And the bright-colored pensive woman spoke with antique directness of matters that Jurgen, being no longer a scapegrace of twenty-one, found rather embarrassing.
“Come, come!” thinks he, “but it will never do to seem provincial. I believe that I am actually blushing.”
Aloud he said: “Sweetheart, there was﻿—why, not a half-hour since!﻿—a youth who sought quite zealously for the overmastering frenzies you prattle about. But, candidly, he could not find the flesh whose touch would rouse insanity. The lad had opportunities, too, let me tell you! Hah, I recall with tenderness the glitter of eyes and hair, and the gay garments, and the soft voices of those fond foolish women, even now. But he went from one pair of lips to another, with an ardor that was always half-feigned, and with protestations which were conscious echoes of some romance or other. Such escapades were pleasant enough: but they were not very serious, after all. For these things concerned his body alone: and I am more than an edifice of viands reared by my teeth. To pretend that what my body does or endures is of importance seems rather silly nowadays. I prefer to regard it as a necessary beast of burden which I maintain, at considerable expense and trouble. So I shall make no more pother about it.”

===Chinese===

科谢（Koshchei）再次挥了挥手。随后，一位天赋异禀、性情乖张的妇人向朱尔根（Jurgen）走来。她的黑眸闪烁着幽光，头上戴着一具红珊瑚编织的发网，珊瑚枝桠向下蔓延铺展，她的束腰短袍兼具双色，由黑红相间的奇异丝线交织而成。
阿娜伊蒂斯（Anaïtis）也同样忘却了朱尔根，抑或在这位四十出头的男子身上未能认出他；当朱尔根倾听阿娜伊蒂斯谈论种种神异之事时，心底再次涌起一股信念，认定这才是朱尔根唯一真正深爱过的女人。
她讲起泰伊丝（Thaïs）的逸闻，萨福（Sappho）的教谕，罗多庇斯（Rhodopê）的秘辛，以及为阿多尼斯（Adonis）所唱的哀歌：而她所有高谈阔论的主旨始终未变。“因为凡人一生转瞬即逝，死后前程谁也无法预料。因此除了短暂借用一具肉身之外，人实则一无所有；然而人的躯体却能领略诸多奇妙的欢愉。譬如这般那般，”她说。这位明艳而沉静的妇人带着古风般的直率，侃侃而谈那些令朱尔根颇感局促的话题，毕竟朱尔根已不再是当年那个二十一岁放荡不羁的浪荡子了。
“得了吧，得了吧！”他暗想，“但显出一副少见多怪的乡巴佬模样可万万不行。我觉着自己甚至都在脸红了。”
他朗声说道：“亲爱的，方才——嗨，甚至还不到半小时前！——有个年轻人还曾极其狂热地追寻你口中喋喋不休的那些销魂狂喜呢。可是坦白讲，他终究未能寻得一具仅凭触碰便能激起癫狂的肉体。我得告诉你，那小伙子可不乏良机！哈，直到此刻，我仍温柔地记挂着那些痴情愚蠢的女人们明眸皓齿的光彩、秀发的流光、艳丽的罗裳与轻柔的软语。但他辗转于一双双唇瓣之间，那份热情却向来半真半假，吐露的山盟海誓也不过是有意附和着某些风流传奇。这等寻欢作乐虽说惬意快活，但终究算不上多么要紧。因为这诸般事宜仅与他的肉身相关：而我绝不仅仅是一座由牙齿咀嚼鱼肉搭建而成的皮囊建筑。事到如今，若还要假装我这躯壳的所作所为或所承受的一切至关重要，未免显得有些可笑。我宁愿将它视作一头由我耗费颇多资财与精力供养的必需役畜。因此我绝不会再为此瞎费心思了。”

===Original===

But then again Queen Anaïtis spoke of marvelous things; and he listened, fair-mindedly; for the Queen spoke now of that which was hers to share with him.
“Well, I have heard,” says Jurgen, “that you have a notable residence in Cocaigne.”
“But that is only a little country place, to which I sometimes repair in summer, in order to live rustically. No, Jurgen, you must see my palaces. In Babylon I have a palace where many abide with cords about them and burn bran for perfume, while they await that thing which is to befall them. In Armenia I have a palace surrounded by vast gardens, where only strangers have the right to enter: they there receive a hospitality that is more than gallant. In Paphos I have a palace wherein is a little pyramid of white stone, very curious to see: but still more curious is the statue in my palace at Amathus, of a bearded woman, which displays other features that women do not possess. And in Alexandria I have a palace that is tended by thirty-six exceedingly wise and sacred persons, and wherein it is always night: and there folk seek for monstrous pleasures, even at the price of instant death, and win to both of these swiftly. Everywhere my palaces stand upon high places near the sea: so they are beheld from afar by those whom I hold dearest, my beautiful broad-chested mariners, who do not fear even me, but know that in my palaces they will find notable employment. For I must tell you of what is to be encountered within these places that are mine, and of how pleasantly we pass our time there.” Then she told him.
Now he listened more attentively than ever, and his eyes were narrowed, and his lips were lax and motionless and foolish-looking, and he was deeply interested. For Anaïtis had thought of some new diversions since their last meeting: and to Jurgen, even at forty and something, this queen’s voice was all a horrible and strange and lovely magic. “She really tempts very nicely, too,” he reflected, with a sort of pride in her.
Then Jurgen growled and shook himself, half angrily: and he tweaked the ear of Queen Anaïtis.
“Sweetheart,” says he, “you paint a glowing picture: but you are shrewd enough to borrow your pigments from the daydreams of inexperience. What you prattle about is not at all as you describe it. You forget you are talking to a widely married man of varied experience.

===Chinese===

然而阿娜伊蒂斯王后随即再次讲起那些神异奇幻之事；他心平气和地倾听着，皆因王后此刻所言，乃是她乐意同他共享的一切。
“唔，我曾听闻，”朱尔根说，“你在科凯恩（Cocaigne）有一处赫赫有名的宅邸。”
“可那不过是一处乡间小所，我夏天偶尔去小住避暑，过过田园生活罢了。不，朱尔根，你必须去瞧瞧我的诸座宫殿。在巴比伦（Babylon），我有一座宫殿，许多人身上束着绳索住在那里，焚烧麦麸充当香料，静候降临在他们身上的宿命。在亚美尼亚（Armenia），我有一座被广袤花园环绕的宫殿，唯有异邦外客才有权入内：他们在那里受到的款待远胜风流殷勤。在帕福斯（Paphos），我有一座宫殿，里面矗立着一座白石小金字塔，瞧着极其奇异别致；但更奇特的，当属我在阿马苏斯（Amathus）宫殿里的一尊长须妇人雕像，那雕像甚至展露出了女人所不具备的其余特征。在亚历山大里亚（Alexandria），我有一座由三十六位至圣极智之人服侍的宫殿，那里终年宛如长夜：世人在那里追寻穷凶极欲的狂欢，哪怕以当场毙命为代价也在所不惜，且能飞速兼得二者。我的宫殿无一不矗立在临海的高崖之上：因而那些被我视作挚爱的人——我那些英武宽阔胸膛的水手们——自远方便能遥遥望见它们，他们甚至连我都不惧怕，只晓得在我的宫殿里定能觅得非凡的差事。因为我必须向你诉说在我的这些居所里将要遭遇的一切，以及我们在那里消磨时光是何等惬意。”随后她便一五一十向他细细道来。
此刻他听得比以往任何时候都要专注，双眼微微眯起，嘴唇微张僵直，神情看似呆滞，内心却兴致盎然。盖因自他们上次相聚以来，阿娜伊蒂斯又想出了一些崭新的消遣花样：在朱尔根听来，哪怕年逾四十，这位王后的嗓音依然充斥着一种可怖、奇异而迷人的魔力。“她这番诱惑当真巧妙至极，”他暗自寻思，心中竟对她生出一股由衷的赞叹。
随后朱尔根低吼一声，半带恼意地晃了晃身子：他伸手拧了拧阿娜伊蒂斯王后的耳朵。
“亲爱的，”他说，“你描摹的景象确实绚丽动人：但你精明得很，借用的颜料全出自不谙世事的白日遐想。你口中喋喋不休的一切，压根不像你描述的那般美妙。你忘了自己正在同一个饱经沧桑、阅女无数的结发老手对话呢。

===Original===

Moreover, I shudder to think of what might happen if Lisa were to walk in unexpectedly. And for the rest, all this to-do over nameless delights and unspeakable caresses and other anonymous antics seems rather naive. My ears are beset by eloquent gray hairs which plead at closer quarters than does that fibbing little tongue of yours. And so be off with you!”
With that Queen Anaïtis smiled very cruelly, and she said: “Farewell to you, then Jurgen, for it is I that am leaving you forever. Henceforward you must fret away much sunlight by interminably shunning discomfort and by indulging tepid preferences. For I, and none but I, can waken that desire which uses all of a man, and so wastes nothing, even though it leave that favored man forever after like wan ashes in the sunlight. And with you I have no more concern, for it is I that am leaving you forever. Join with your graying fellows, then! and help them to affront the clean sane sunlight, by making guilds and laws and solemn phrases wherewith to rid the world of me. I, Anaïtis, laugh, and my heart is a wave in the sunlight. For there is no power like my power, and no living thing which can withstand my power; and those who deride me, as I well know, are but the dead dry husks that a wind moves, with hissing noises, while I harvest in open sunlight. For I am the desire that uses all of a man: and it is I that am leaving you forever.”
Said Jurgen: “I could not see all this in you, not quite all this, because of a shadow that followed me. Now it is too late, and this is a sorrowful thing which is happening. I am become as a puzzled ghost who furtively observes the doings of loud-voiced ruddy persons: and I am compact of weariness and apprehension, for I no longer discern what thing is I, nor what is my desire, and I fear that I am already dead. So farewell to you, Queen Anaïtis, for this, too, is a sorrowful thing and a very unfair thing that is happening.”
Thus he cried farewell to the Sun’s daughter. And all the colors of her loveliness flickered and merged into the likeness of a tall thin flame, that aspired; and then this flame was extinguished.

===Chinese===

更何况，一想到丽莎（Lisa）若是冷不丁推门撞见这番光景，我便不寒而栗。至于其余种种，为了那些不可名状的欢愉、难以言表的抚弄以及其他无名把戏如此大费周章，未免显得有些天真幼稚。我耳畔那些能说会道的白发，比你那巧舌如簧的小舌头更能贴身劝诫我。所以，去你的吧！”
言毕，阿娜伊蒂斯王后极其冷酷地笑了起来，她说：“那么永别了，朱尔根，皆因是我将永远离你而去。从今往后，你只能在一味躲避不适与迁就温吞琐屑的偏好中虚掷漫长日光。因为唯有我，除我之外别无他人，方能唤醒那足以榨干男子全副心神的炽热欲望，丝丝毫留残存，哪怕让那受眷顾之人往后在日光下形同槁木死灰。我同你再无瓜葛，皆因是我将永远离你而去。那就滚去同你那些头发花白的同类为伍吧！去帮着他们创设行会、律法与庄严套话，企图将我从世间驱逐，以此亵渎那澄澈明朗的日光。我阿娜伊蒂斯放声大笑，我的心宛若日光下翻腾的浪涛。因为世间绝无任何伟力能匹敌我的威能，也绝无任何生灵能抗拒我的威力；我很清楚，那些嘲弄我的人，不过是风吹过时沙沙作响的干瘪空壳，而我却在朗朗日光之下尽情收割。因为我即是榨干男子全副身心的欲望：而正是我，将永远离你而去。”
朱尔根说：“只因那道尾随我的阴影，我此前未能看透你身上所有的这般面貌。如今大势已去，眼前正在发生的委实是一桩令人痛心的悲剧。我已化作一个困惑不解的幽灵，鬼祟窥视着那些面色红润、嗓音洪亮之人的行径：我满心疲惫与惶恐，皆因我已辨不清何者为我，亦不知自身所欲为何，我甚至唯恐自己早已是一具行尸走肉。因此永别了，阿娜伊蒂斯王后，皆因眼前正在发生的一切，同样是一桩令人痛心且极不公道的事。”
他就这样同太阳之女道了声永别。她满身绚烂的美色随之摇曳交融，化作一道向上升腾的高挑纤细火焰；转瞬之间，火焰便熄灭了。

## IV The Dorothy Who Did Not Understand / 第四章 懵懂不解的多萝西
===Original===

For now had come to Jurgen and the Centaur a gold-haired woman, clothed all in white, and walking alone. She was tall, and lovely and tender to regard: and hers was not the red and white comeliness of many ladies that were famed for beauty, but rather it had the even glow of ivory. Her nose was large and high in the bridge, her flexible mouth was not of the smallest: and yet whatever other persons might have said, to Jurgen this woman’s countenance was in all things perfect. Perhaps this was because he never saw her as she was. For certainly the color of her eyes stayed a matter never revealed to him: gray, blue or green, there was no saying: they varied as does the sea; but always these eyes were lovely and friendly and perturbing.
Jurgen remembered that: for Jurgen saw this was Count Emmerick’s second sister, Dorothy la Désirée, whom Jurgen very long ago (a many years before he met Dame Lisa and set up in business as a pawnbroker) had hymned in innumerable verses as Heart’s Desire.
“And this is the only woman whom I ever loved,” Jurgen remembered, upon a sudden. For people cannot always be thinking of these matters.
So he saluted her, with such deference as is due to a countess from a tradesman, and yet with unforgotten tremors waking in his staid body. But the strangest was yet to be seen, for he noted now that this was not a handsome woman in middle life but a young girl.
“I do not understand,” he said, aloud: “for you are Dorothy. And yet it seems to me that you are not the Countess Dorothy who is Heitman Michael’s wife.”
And the girl tossed her fair head, with that careless lovely gesture which the Countess had forgotten. “Heitman Michael is well enough, for a nobleman, and my brother is at me day and night to marry the man: and certainly Heitman Michael’s wife will go in satin and diamonds at half the courts of Christendom, with many lackeys to attend her. But I am not to be thus purchased.”
“So you told a boy that I remember, very long ago. Yet you married Heitman Michael, for all that, and in the teeth of a number of other fine declarations.”
“Oh, no, not I,” said this Dorothy, wondering. “I never married anybody. And Heitman Michael has never married anybody, either, old as he is. For he is twenty-eight, and looks every day of it! But who are you, friend, that have such curious notions about me?”

===Chinese===

只因此时向朱尔根与半人马（Centaur）走来了一位金发女子，她通体素白，独自行来。她身形高挑，容颜娇柔动人：她的美并非寻常那些艳帜高张的贵妇所惯有的红白相间，倒透着象牙般温润莹洁的光泽。她鼻梁高挺硕大，线条柔和的嘴唇也算不得精巧玲珑；然而无论旁人如何评头论足，在朱尔根眼里，这女子的面庞在诸般细微处皆是尽善尽美。或许这只是因为他从未看清过她真正的模样。毕竟她眼眸的色泽对他而言始终是个未解之谜：究竟是灰、是蓝还是绿，委实难以言表，那色彩就如大海般变幻莫测；然而那双眸子却始终如一地柔媚、亲切，又教人心绪不宁。
朱尔根忆起了这一切：皆因朱尔根看清了，眼前这位正是埃梅里克伯爵（Count Emmerick）的二妹众望所归的多萝西（Dorothy la Désirée）——早在多年以前（早在朱尔根结识丽莎夫人并开当铺营生之前许多年），朱尔根便曾在数不尽的诗篇中将她歌颂为「衷心所愿」。
“而这正是我平生唯一爱过的女人，”朱尔根猝然记起。毕竟，凡人不可能成日惦记着这些风月旧事。
于是他向她致意，仪态中带着市井小民对伯爵夫人的卑微恭顺，然而在他那副沉稳老成的躯壳里，那些难以忘怀的战栗却悄然苏醒。然而更诡谲的景象还在后头，皆因他此刻惊异地发觉，眼前并非一位徐娘半老的中年妇人，而分明是个妙龄少女。
“我委实不明就里，”他大声说道，“因为你分明是多萝西（Dorothy）。可依我看来，你却又不是那位委身做了盖特曼·迈克尔（Heitman Michael）之妻的多萝西伯爵夫人。”
少女扬了扬她那美丽的金发脑袋，带着伯爵夫人早已忘却的漫不经心与娇俏风致。“对于一个贵族而言，盖特曼·迈克尔倒也过得去，我哥哥日夜催促我嫁给他；而做了盖特曼·迈克尔的夫人，自然能在半个基督教世界的宫廷里锦衣钻饰，前呼后拥。但我可不是随随便便就能被买走的。”
“很久以前，你也曾对一个我记得的少年说过这番话。可到头来你还是嫁给了盖特曼·迈克尔，全然不顾当初那一连串掷地有声的漂亮宣言。”
“噢，才没有呢，我可没有，”这位多萝西诧异道，“我谁也没嫁。而且盖特曼·迈克尔也谁都没娶，尽管他年纪都那么一把了。他已经二十八岁了，看上去也是一脸二十八岁的老气横秋！可朋友，你究竟是何许人，竟对我存着这般古怪的念头？”

===Original===

“That question I will answer, just as though it were put reasonably. For surely you perceive I am Jurgen.”
“I never knew but one Jurgen. And he is a young man, barely come of age﻿—” Then as she paused in speech, whatever was the matter upon which this girl now meditated, her cheeks were tenderly colored by the thought of it, and in her knowledge of this thing her eyes took infinite joy.
And Jurgen understood. He had come back somehow to the Dorothy whom he had loved: but departed, and past overtaking by the fleet hoofs of centaurs, was the boy who had once loved this Dorothy, and who had rhymed of her as his Heart’s Desire: and in the garden there was of this boy no trace. Instead, the girl was talking to a staid and paunchy pawnbroker, of forty-and-something.
So Jurgen shrugged, and looked toward the Centaur: but Nessus had discreetly wandered away from them, in search of four-leafed clovers. Now the east had grown brighter, and its crimson began to be colored with gold.
“Yes, I have heard of this other Jurgen,” says the pawnbroker. “Oh, Madame Dorothy, but it was he that loved you!”
“No more than I loved him. Through a whole summer have I loved Jurgen.”
And the knowledge that this girl spoke a wondrous truth was now to Jurgen a joy that was keen as pain. And he stood motionless for a while, scowling and biting his lips.
“I wonder how long the poor devil loved you! He also loved for a whole summer, it may be. And yet again, it may be that he loved you all his life. For twenty years and for more than twenty years I have debated the matter: and I am as well informed as when I started.”
“But, friend, you talk in riddles.”
“Is not that customary when age talks with youth? For I am an old fellow, in my forties: and you, as I know now, are near eighteen﻿—or rather, four months short of being eighteen, for it is August. Nay, more, it is the August of a year I had not looked ever to see again; and again Dom Manuel reigns over us, that man of iron whom I saw die so horribly. All this seems very improbable.”
Then Jurgen meditated for a while. He shrugged.
“Well, and what could anybody expect me to do about it? Somehow it has befallen that I, who am but the shadow of what I was, now walk among shadows, and we converse with the thin intonations of dead persons.

===Chinese===

“这个问题我自会作答，只当问得还算合情合理。因为你定然瞧得出来，我便是朱尔根。”
“我所认得的朱尔根唯有一人。而他是个年轻小伙子，方才将将及笄成年——”说到此处她蓦地顿住，无论少女此刻脑海中浮现出何等思绪，那念头都教她的双颊泛起了脉脉柔情的红晕，而在了然于心间，她的双眸流露出无尽的欣喜。
朱尔根全然明白了。不知怎的，他竟然重返了他曾深爱过的多萝西身畔：然而，昔日曾倾心恋慕过多萝西、曾在韵律中将她奉为「衷心所愿」的那个少年，却已然远逝，纵使半人马那疾风般的飞蹄亦追之不及；在这座园圃之中，已寻不到那少年的一丝踪迹。取而代之的是，眼前的少女正面对着一个年逾不惑、大腹便便而神情拘谨的当铺掌柜款款而谈。
于是朱尔根耸了耸肩，转头望向半人马：然而涅索斯（Nessus）早已识趣地踱步走远，在一旁寻觅着四叶草。此时东方愈发破晓明亮，那一抹深红开始镀上了璀璨的金辉。
“不错，我倒也听说过另外那个朱尔根，”当铺掌柜说道，“噢，多萝西小姐，然而真正爱恋着你的正是他啊！”
“绝不会胜过我对他的爱意。整整一个夏日，我都深爱着朱尔根。”
获悉眼前的少女吐露的是这般惊人的实情，此时于朱尔根而言，竟化作了一股尖锐如剧痛般的狂喜。他凝立良久，眉头深锁，紧咬着嘴唇。
“我倒不知那个可怜的家伙究竟爱了你多久！或许他也爱了你整整一个夏日。然而退一步讲，或许他也用尽余生爱恋着你。二十年了，二十多年来我始终在推敲琢磨此事：可我如今所明白的，跟当初一开头相比却也没多出分毫。”
“可是，朋友，你的话委实教人如堕五里雾中。”
“年迈者同青春对谈，岂非向来如此？毕竟我是个年过四十的老家伙；而你，正如我如今所知晓的，年将十八——确切地说，离十八岁还差四个月，因为如今正是八月。不仅如此，这更是那一年中的八月，一个我原以为生生世世绝无可能再见的八月；而铁血宰制的曼努埃尔阁下（Dom Manuel）竟又再次统御着我们，那个我曾亲睹他惨死情状的铁石般的人物。这一切看起来委实荒诞不经。”
随后朱尔根沉思片刻。他耸了耸肩。
“罢，谁又能指望我对此有所作为呢？不知怎的竟落得这般境地：我这个不过是昔日自我的残破幽影，如今在诸多虚影间徜徉漫步，而我们彼此交谈，声调浅薄干瘪，宛如亡者的低语。

===Original===

For, Madame Dorothy, you who are not yet eighteen, in this same garden there was once a boy who loved a girl, with such love as it puzzles me to think of now. I believe that she loved him. Yes, certainly it is a cordial to the tired and battered heart which nowadays pumps blood for me, to think that for a little while, for a whole summer, these two were as brave and comely and clean a pair of sweethearts as the world has known.”
Thus Jurgen spoke. But his thought was that this was a girl whose equal for loveliness and delight was not to be found between two oceans. Long and long ago that doubtfulness of himself which was closer to him than his skin had fretted Jurgen into believing the Dorothy he had loved was but a piece of his imaginings. But certainly this girl was real. And sweet she was, and innocent she was, and light of heart and feet, beyond the reach of any man’s inventiveness. No, Jurgen had not invented her; and it strangely contented him to know as much.
“Tell me your story, sir,” says she, “for I love all romances.”
“Ah, my dear child, but I cannot tell you very well of just what happened. As I look back, there is a blinding glory of green woods and lawns and moonlit nights and dance music and unreasonable laughter. I remember her hair and eyes, and the curving and the feel of her red mouth, and once when I was bolder than ordinary﻿—But that is hardly worth raking up at this late day. Well, I see these things in memory as plainly as I now seem to see your face: but I can recollect hardly anything she said. Perhaps, now I think of it, she was not very intelligent, and said nothing worth remembering. But the boy loved her, and was happy, because her lips and heart were his, and he, as the saying is, had plucked a diamond from the world’s ring. True, she was a count’s daughter and the sister of a count: but in those days the boy quite firmly intended to become a duke or an emperor or something of that sort, so the transient discrepancy did not worry them.”
“I know. Why, Jurgen is going to be a duke, too,” says she, very proudly, “though he did think, a great while ago, before he knew me, of being a cardinal, on account of the robes. But cardinals are not allowed to marry, you see﻿—And I am forgetting your story, too! What happened then?”

===Chinese===

皆因，多萝西小姐，你这位尚未年满十八岁的姑娘啊，在同这一座园圃之中，曾几何时有个少年深爱着一个少女，那份爱恋之深切，如今想来反倒令我困惑丛生。我确信那姑娘也曾深爱过他。是的，念及曾几何时，在整整一个夏日里，他们二人曾是世间所知晓的最为勇敢、标致且纯洁的一对璧人，这对于我胸膛中如今勉力泵送血液的疲惫沧桑之心而言，委实是一剂难得的慰藉。”
朱尔根这般说着。但他心中暗想的，却是眼前这个少女的美艳与娇俏，即便行遍重洋两岸也断然寻不出第二人来。很久很久以前，那种比肌肤更贴近自我的自我怀疑，便折磨得朱尔根不由得相信，自己当年所爱过的多萝西不过是一幅出于凭空臆造的虚妄幻象。然而眼前的少女却确凿无疑是真实的。她是那般甜美，那般天真无邪，步履与心境皆是那般轻盈欢畅，远非世间任何男子的匠心巧思所能杜撰。不，朱尔根并未虚构出她来；而了然于此，竟教他心底涌起了一抹异样的欣慰。
“先生，讲讲你的故事吧，”她说，“凡是传奇风月我都爱听。”
“啊，我亲爱的孩子，可当年究竟发生了何事，我却委实难以细细道来。如今回首顾盼，只见一片令人目眩神迷的光辉：青葱的林木与草坪、溶溶的月夜、悠扬的舞曲，以及毫无由头的欢笑。我仍记得她的发丝与双眸，记得她红润朱唇的弧度与温存，还记得某一回我比往常都要大胆放肆——然而事到如今，重翻这些陈年旧账又有何益。总之，我在记忆中看这些光景，就同此刻分明看着你的面庞一般真切：可关于她说过的话，我却几乎全无印象。或许，如今细细思量起来，她本就算不得何等聪颖灵秀，也没说过半句值得铭记的话语。可那个少年深爱着她，且心满意足，皆因她的温存与芳心皆尽归他所有，正如俗语所言，他已然从这尘世的指环上摘取了至璀璨的钻石。诚然，她是一位伯爵的千金，又是一位伯爵的令妹：然而在那些岁月里，那个少年却笃定决意自己定要成为公爵或皇帝或诸如此类的大人物，是以这等暂时的门第差距从不曾教他们挂怀。”
“这我清楚。朱尔根也是打定主意要当大公爵的呀，”她满面骄傲地说道，“虽说在很久以前，在同我相识之前，他因着那些华美的法袍还曾想过去当枢机主教呢。可你瞧，枢机主教是不许娶妻的——哎呀，我光顾着插嘴，都把你的故事给忘了！后来又怎样了呢？”

===Original===

“They parted in September﻿—with what vows it hardly matters now﻿—and the boy went into Gâtinais, to win his spurs under the old Vidame de Soyecourt. And presently﻿—oh, a good while before Christmas!﻿—came the news that Dorothy la Désirée had married rich Heitman Michael.”
“But that is what I am called! And as you know, there is a Heitman Michael who is always plaguing me. Is that not strange! for you tell me all this happened a great while ago.”
“Indeed, the story is very old, and old it was when Methuselah was teething. There is no older and more common story anywhere. As the sequel, it would be heroic to tell you this boy’s life was ruined. But I do not think it was. Instead, he had learned all of a sudden that which at twenty-one is heady knowledge. That was the hour which taught him sorrow and rage, and sneering, too, for a redemption. Oh, it was armor that hour brought him, and a humor to use it, because no woman now could hurt him very seriously. No, never any more!”
“Ah, the poor boy!” she said, divinely tender, and smiling as a goddess smiles, not quite in mirth.
“Well, women, as he knew by experience now, were the pleasantest of playfellows. So he began to play. Rampaging through the world he went in the pride of his youth and in the armor of his hurt. And songs he made for the pleasure of kings, and swordplay he made for the pleasure of men, and a whispering he made for the pleasure of women, in places where renown was, and where he trod boldly, giving pleasure to everybody, in those fine days. But the whispering, and all that followed the whispering, was his best game, and the game he played for the longest while, with many brightly colored playmates who took the game more seriously than he did. And their faith in the game’s importance, and in him and his high-sounding nonsense, he very often found amusing: and in their other chattels too he took his natural pleasure. Then, when he had played sufficiently, he held a consultation with divers waning appetites; and he married the handsome daughter of an estimable pawnbroker in a fair line of business. And he lived with his wife very much as two people customarily live together. So, all in all, I would not say his life was ruined.”
“Why, then, it was,” said Dorothy. She stirred uneasily, with an impatient sigh; and you saw that she was vaguely puzzled.

===Chinese===

“他们在九月分道扬镳——立下了何等誓言，如今看来都无关紧要了——少年奔赴加蒂奈（Gâtinais），投身到老苏瓦耶库尔代理主教（Vidame de Soyecourt）麾下挣取骑士马刺。不久之后——噢，远在圣诞节之前！——便传来了众望所归的多萝西已然下嫁腰缠万贯的盖特曼·迈克尔的消息。”
“可世人正就是这般称呼我的呀！而且正如你所知晓的，眼下正有个盖特曼·迈克尔成天对我死缠烂打。这难道不离奇古怪吗！毕竟你口口声声说这些全是陈年旧事。”
“确实，这桩故事委实古老，早在玛土撒拉（Methuselah）长乳牙时它便已是陈词滥调了。普天之下再没有比这更古老、更寻常的勾当了。按后文的情节，若对你声称这少年的锦绣前程就此毁于一旦，未免更富英雄气概。但我可不觉得当真如此。相反，他猝然领悟了某种在二十一岁时教人头脑发昏的深奥学问。正是在那一刻，他学会了悲痛与狂怒，也学会了以冷嘲热讽作为救赎的解药。噢，正是那一刻为他披上了坚不可摧的铠甲，并赐予他挥洒自如的风趣，皆因从今往后，世间再没有任何妇人能够真正伤他分毫。不，生生世世绝不再有！”
“啊，可怜的少年！”她叹道，神情流露出如神祇般超凡脱俗的温柔，唇边漾着女神般的微笑，却全无半分欢欣之意。
“嗯，凭着亲身阅历，他如今深知妇人原是世间最教人惬意的玩伴。于是他便肆意玩耍起来。他横行天下，依仗着青春年少的傲气与创痛所铸的坚甲招摇过市。在那些风流倜傥的锦绣年华里，他为博君王一笑而裁制颂歌，为娱男儿之兴而挥剑比试，又在声名煊赫之处向妇人们低声耳语，他在四海昂首阔步，教人人心花怒放。然而那些耳语细语，以及耳语过后的一应风月，才是他最得心应手的好把戏，也是他沉溺最久的游戏，周旋于众多光鲜艳丽的玩伴之间，而那些女子往往比他更把这场游戏当真。她们对游戏深意坚信不疑，对他本人及其那些冠冕堂皇的高谈阔论更是笃信不疑，对此他往往觉得滑稽可笑；而从她们其余的私己体己之中，他自然也尽情取乐。后来，待他尽兴玩够之后，他便同自身日渐衰退的诸般欲念好生商榷了一番；最终他迎娶了一位体面当铺掌柜的漂亮千金，其岳丈的典当买卖做得颇为红火。他就这样同妻子过活，与世间两两相伴的凡夫俗子寻常过日子并无二致。因此，总而言之，我绝不会说他的一生就此毁了。”
“可如此说来，他分明就是毁了，”多萝西说道。她烦躁地动了动身子，发出一声不耐烦的叹息；谁都能看出她心头正泛起一阵模糊的困惑。

===Original===

“Oh, but somehow I think you are a very horrible old man: and you seem doubly horrible in that glittering queer garment you are wearing.”
“No woman ever praised a woman’s handiwork, and each of you is particularly severe upon her own. But you are interrupting the saga.”
“I do not see”﻿—and those large bright eyes of which the color was so indeterminable and so dear to Jurgen, seemed even larger now﻿—“but I do not see how there could well be any more.”
“Still, human hearts survive the benediction of the priest, as you may perceive any day. This man, at least, inherited his father-in-law’s business, and found it, quite as he had anticipated, the fittest of vocations for a cashiered poet. And so, I suppose, he was content. Ah, yes; but after a while Heitman Michael returned from foreign parts, along with his lackeys, and plate, and chest upon chest of merchandise, and his fine horses, and his wife. And he who had been her lover could see her now, after so many years, whenever he liked. She was a handsome stranger. That was all. She was rather stupid. She was nothing remarkable, one way or another. This respectable pawnbroker saw that quite plainly: day by day he writhed under the knowledge. Because, as I must tell you, he could not retain composure in her presence, even now. No, he was never able to do that.”
The girl somewhat condensed her brows over this information. “You mean that he still loved her. Why, but of course!”
“My child,” says Jurgen, now with a reproving forefinger, “you are an incurable romanticist. The man disliked her and despised her. At any event, he assured himself that he did. Well, even so, this handsome stupid stranger held his eyes, and muddled his thoughts, and put errors into his accounts: and when he touched her hand he did not sleep that night as he was used to sleep. Thus he saw her, day after day. And they whispered that this handsome and stupid stranger had a liking for young men who aided her artfully to deceive her husband: but she never showed any such favor to the respectable pawnbroker. For youth had gone out of him, and it seemed that nothing in particular happened. Well, that was his saga. About her I do not know. And I shall never know! But certainly she got the name of deceiving Heitman Michael with two young men, or with five young men it might be, but never with a respectable pawnbroker.”

===Chinese===

“噢，不知怎的，我觉得你是个十分可怕的老头子；而穿上你身上那件亮闪闪的怪里怪气袍子，你瞧着更是加倍可怕。”
“女人从不夸赞女人的手艺，且你们每一个人对自家同类的评判尤为苛刻。不过你打断了这篇传奇。”
“我委实瞧不出”——那双色泽变幻莫测、令朱尔根魂牵梦萦的明眸，此刻似乎睁得更大了——“我委实瞧不出这后头还能有什么下文。”
“然而，人心在历经神父的赐福后依旧跳动不休，这一点你随时随地都能瞧得真切。至少这个男人继承了他岳丈的行当，正如他早先料想的那般，他发觉对于一个落魄革职的落第诗人而言，典当营生实乃天下最妥帖不过的归宿。我想，他也算是心满意足了。啊，正是；然而过了一阵子，盖特曼·迈克尔携着大批侍从、银器、一箱又一箱的奇珍异宝、骏马以及他的娇妻，从异国他乡衣锦还乡。而那位曾做过她情郎的故人，历经这许多春秋之后，如今只要愿意，便能随时随地得见她的尊容。她不过是个漂亮的陌路人。仅此而已。她生得相当愚笨。无论从哪方面看，都算不得出类拔萃。这位体面的当铺掌柜看得清清楚楚：日复一日，他在这般清醒的认知下饱受煎熬折磨。因为我不得不告诉你，即便到了如今，只要置身于她面前，他便无法维持从容镇定。不，他生平从未能做到过那般地步。”
少女听罢这番话，微微蹙起了秀眉。“你的意思是说，他心里仍旧爱着她。哎呀，那还用问么！”
“我的好孩子，”朱尔根说着，竖起一根手指以示告诫，“你委实是个不可救药的浪漫狂。那男人打心底厌弃她、鄙夷她。至少无论如何，他自己对此深信不疑。然而即便如此，这个漂亮而愚蠢的陌路妇人依旧牢牢牵动着他的目光，搅乱他的思绪，甚至让他在账本上算错账目；而一旦触碰到她的柔荑，他那一夜便再也无法如往常那般安然入眠。他就这般日复一日地瞧着她。四下里人们私语纷纷，说这位漂亮而愚笨的陌路贵妇偏爱结交年轻小伙，靠着他们的巧计瞒天过海背叛夫君；可她却从未对这位体面的当铺掌柜施舍过分毫这般恩宠。皆因青春已从他身上消逝殆尽，生活仿佛并未泛起什么惊涛骇浪。嗯，这便是他的传奇了。至于关于她的种种，我无从知晓。而且我将生生世世无从知晓！但毫无疑问，她在外头背下了背叛盖特曼·迈克尔的名声，或许是同两个年轻小伙，或许是同五个，但绝不曾包括一个体面的当铺掌柜。”

===Original===

“I think that is an exceedingly cynical and stupid story,” observed the girl. “And so I shall be off to look for Jurgen. For he makes love very amusingly,” says Dorothy, with the sweetest, loveliest meditative smile that ever was lost to heaven.
And a madness came upon Jurgen, there in the garden between dawn and sunrise, and a disbelief in such injustice as now seemed incredible.
“No, Heart’s Desire,” he cried, “I will not let you go. For you are dear and pure and faithful, and all my evil dream, wherein you were a wanton and befooled me, was not true. Surely, mine was a dream that can never be true so long as there is any justice upon earth. Why, there is no imaginable God who would permit a boy to be robbed of that which in my evil dream was taken from me!”
“And still I cannot understand your talking, about this dream of yours﻿—!”
“Why, it seemed to me I had lost the most of myself; and there was left only a brain which played with ideas, and a body that went delicately down pleasant ways. And I could not believe as my fellows believed, nor could I love them, nor could I detect anything in aught they said or did save their exceeding folly: for I had lost their cordial common faith in the importance of what use they made of half-hours and months and years; and because a jill-flirt had opened my eyes so that they saw too much, I had lost faith in the importance of my own actions, too. There was a little time of which the passing might be made endurable; beyond gaped unpredictable darkness: and that was all there was of certainty anywhere. Now tell me, Heart’s Desire, but was not that a foolish dream? For these things never happened. Why, it would not be fair if these things ever happened!”
And the girl’s eyes were wide and puzzled and a little frightened. “I do not understand what you are saying: and there is that about you which troubles me unspeakably. For you call me by the name which none but Jurgen used, and it seems to me that you are Jurgen; and yet you are not Jurgen.”
“But I am truly Jurgen. And look you, I have done what never any man has done before! For I have won back to that first love whom every man must lose, no matter whom he marries. I have come back again, passing very swiftly over the grave of a dream and through the malice of time, to my Heart’s Desire! And how strange it seems that I did not know this thing was inevitable!”

===Chinese===

“我觉得这是个极其愤世嫉俗且愚不可及的故事，”少女评判道，“因此我得动身去寻朱尔根了。毕竟他谈起情说起爱来可有趣极了，”多萝西说道，唇边挂着一抹最甜美、最娇俏的沉思微笑，连天堂失却了这般笑容都要为之逊色。
就在这破晓与拂晓之间的神秘园圃里，一股狂乱猝然席卷了朱尔根，教他断然不信世间竟会有这般教人难以置信的荒唐不公。
“不，心之所愿！”他大声呼喊，“我决不放你走。因为你是这般惹人怜爱、纯洁无瑕而忠贞不渝，而我那场视你为轻浮荡妇并受尽戏弄的噩梦，断然不是真的。天地良心，只要这人世间尚存一丝公道，我的那场噩梦就绝不可能成真。试问天下哪一位能想象得到的神明，会容许一个少年被生生剥夺走我噩梦中所失去的那一切！”
“可我依然听不懂你翻来覆去念叨的这番话，什么你的噩梦——！”
“因为在梦里，我仿佛失落了大半个自己；只剩下一副成日摆弄思绪的脑瓜，以及一副在安逸享乐中从容度日的皮囊。我再也无法如身旁的同侪那般笃信不疑，亦无法去爱慕他们，除了他们满身的荒唐愚昧之外，我从他们的言谈举止中再也瞧不出任何端倪：皆因我已失落了他们那种质朴笃定的信念，不再相信把每分每秒、日月春秋耗费在何处有何深意；更因一个轻浮浪荡的薄情女子替我擦亮了双眼，教我看破了太多真相，我甚至连自身所作所为的分量亦不再坚信。人世不过是偷得浮生片刻，使时光的流逝尚能忍受；在这之外，唯有一片难以测度的深渊黑暗大张着巨口：在天地寰宇之间，除此以外再无任何确定无疑之事。如今你且告诉我，心之所愿，那难道不只是一场荒谬的噩梦么？因为这些事分明从未发生过。试问倘若这等事当真发生过，这世间岂非太不公道！”
少女的双眸睁得大大的，满面困惑，甚至带着一丝隐隐的惶恐。“我完全听不懂你在胡说些什么：可你身上却有种教我说不出的心绪不宁。因为你呼唤我的那个名字，除朱尔根外从未有人这般唤过我，依我看来你仿佛就是朱尔根；可你却分明不是朱尔根。”
“但我确确实实就是朱尔根。你且瞧，我做成了凡间男子从未有人能做成的大事！因为无论男子迎娶何人，总注定要痛失的初恋挚爱，如今却被我重新赢了回来。我跨越了残梦的坟茔，穿透了岁月的刁难恶意，疾驰而归，重返我的心之所愿身畔！而我当初竟不知晓这一切皆是命中注定，如今回想起来该是何等不可思议！”

===Original===

“Still, friend, I do not understand you.”
“Why, but I yawned and fretted in preparation for some great and beautiful adventure which was to befall me by and by, and dazedly I toiled forward. Whereas behind me all the while was the garden between dawn and sunrise, and therein you awaited me! Now assuredly, the life of every man is a quaintly builded tale, in which the right and proper ending comes first. Thereafter time runs forward, not as schoolmen fable in a straight line, but in a vast closed curve, returning to the place of its starting. And it is by a dim foreknowledge of this, by some faint prescience of justice and reparation being given them by and by, that men have heart to live. For I know now that I have always known this thing. What else was living good for unless it brought me back to you?”
But the girl shook her small glittering head, very sadly. “I do not understand you, and I fear you. For you talk foolishness and in your face I see the face of Jurgen as one might see the face of a dead man drowned in muddy water.”
“Yet am I truly Jurgen, and, as it seems to me, for the first time since we were parted. For I am strong and admirable﻿—even I, who sneered and played so long, because I thought myself a thing of no worth at all. That which has been since you and I were young together is as a mist that passes: and I am strong and admirable, and all my being is one vast hunger for you, my dearest, and I will not let you go, for you, and you alone, are my Heart’s Desire.”
Now the girl was looking at him very steadily, with a small puzzled frown, and with her vivid young soft lips a little parted. And all her tender loveliness was glorified by the light of a sky that had turned to dusty palpitating gold.
“Ah, but you say that you are strong and admirable: and I can only marvel at such talking. For I see that which all men see.”
And then Dorothy showed him the little mirror which was attached to the long chain of turquoise matrix about her neck: and Jurgen studied the frightened foolish aged face that he found in the mirror.
Thus drearily did sanity return to Jurgen: and his flare of passion died, and the fever and storm and the impetuous whirl of things was ended, and the man was very weary. And in the silence he heard the piping cry of a bird that seemed to seek for what it could not find.

===Chinese===

“可是，朋友，我依然听不懂你的话。”
“哎呀，此前我打着哈欠、心烦意乱，只为了筹备那迟早会降临到我头上的宏伟绝美的壮举险境，我浑浑噩噩地向前苦苦跋涉。然而在我身后，却始终矗立着这座破晓与拂晓之间的神秘园圃，而你正是在这里翘首等待着我！如今毫无疑问，世间凡夫的一生本就是个架构离奇的故事，最恰切美满的结局往往最先上演。自那以后，岁月滚滚向前，绝非如经院学者所虚构的那般沿着一条笔直的直线延展，而是划出一道巨大的闭合圆弧，最终回归至它起始的原点。正是仰仗着对此事隐约的预知，仰仗着对公义与补偿终将降临的微茫先见，世人才有勇气苟活于世。因为我如今心知肚明，我其实一直都通晓此事。若不能将我带回你的身畔，这苟活的人生又有何意义？”
然而少女却极其哀伤地摇了摇头，金发微颤。“我听不懂你的话，而且我害怕你。因为你说的尽是些胡话，我在你的脸上瞧见了朱尔根的面庞，就像一个人在泥泞浑浊的死水中瞧见一具溺死之人的面孔一般。”
“可我确确实实就是朱尔根，而且依我看，这是自我们分道扬镳以来我头一回真正做回朱尔根。因为我是强健而令人钦佩的——即便是我，这个曾因自视一文不值而长久冷嘲热讽、逢场作戏的家伙。自从你我当年年少相伴以来的种种过往，不过如过眼云烟消散无踪：而如今我是强健而令人叹服的，我的全副身心都化作了对你无边无际的渴求，我最亲爱的人，我决不放你走，因为你，唯有你，才是我的心之所愿。”
此时少女定定地凝视着他，秀眉微蹙，神情困惑，娇艳柔嫩的双唇微微张开。天幕已然化作一抹流转荡漾的金色微光，将她浑身上下柔美娇艳的风致衬托得宛如神迹。
“啊，可你口口声声说自己强健而令人钦佩：对于这般言论我唯有惊诧万分。因为我眼见的，正是天下人人皆能瞧见的光景。”
言毕，多萝西向他出示了那面系在她颈间绿松石母岩长链上的小巧宝镜：朱尔根凝视着镜中那张惶恐、愚顽而苍老的面孔。
理智就这样凄凉落寞地重返朱尔根心头：他心头的炽热烈焰归于熄灭，高烧狂热、狂风骤雨与急促旋转的万般幻象皆烟消云散，这个男人只觉疲惫不堪。在万籁俱寂中，他听见了一声清脆啼鸣的鸟鸣，那飞禽仿佛在苦苦寻觅着它永远也寻不见的物事。

===Original===

“Well, I am answered,” said the pawnbroker: “and yet I know that this is not the final answer. Dearer than any hope of heaven was that moment when awed surmises first awoke as to the new strange loveliness which I had seen in the face of Dorothy. It was then I noted the new faint flush suffusing her face from chin to brow so often as my eyes encountered and found new lights in the shining eyes which were no longer entirely frank in meeting mine. Well, let that be, for I do not love Heitman Michael’s wife.
“It is a grief to remember how we followed love, and found his service lovely. It is bitter to recall the sweetness of those vows which proclaimed her mine eternally﻿—vows that were broken in their making by prolonged and unforgotten kisses. We used to laugh at Heitman Michael then; we used to laugh at everything. Thus for a while, for a whole summer, we were as brave and comely and clean a pair of sweethearts as the world has known. But let that be, for I do not love Heitman Michael’s wife.
“Our love was fair but short-lived. There is none that may revive him since the small feet of Dorothy trod out this small love’s life. Yet when this life of ours too is over﻿—this parsimonious life which can allow us no more love for anybody﻿—must we not win back, somehow, to that faith we vowed against eternity? and be content again, in some fair-colored realm? Assuredly I think this thing will happen. Well, but let that be, for I do not love Heitman Michael’s wife.”
“Why, this is excellent hearing,” observed Dorothy, “because I see that you are converting your sorrow into the raw stuff of verses. So I shall be off to look for Jurgen, since he makes love quite otherwise and far more amusingly.”
And again, whatever was the matter upon which this girl now meditated, her cheeks were tenderly colored by the thought of it, and in her knowledge of this thing her eyes took infinite joy.
Thus it was for a moment only: for she left Jurgen now, with the friendliest light waving of her hand; and so passed from him, not thinking of this old fellow any longer, as he could see, even in the instant she turned from him. And she went toward the dawn, in search of that young Jurgen whom she, who was perfect in all things, had loved, though only for a little while, not undeservedly.

===Chinese===

“好罢，我得到了应得的答复，”当铺掌柜说道，“但我很清楚，这绝非尘埃落定的最终答复。曾几何时，当我初次在多萝西的面庞上窥见那抹崭新而奇妙的风致，心头初次泛起敬畏交加的揣测那一刹那，比起任何通往天堂的希冀都要教人弥足珍贵。正是在那时，我发觉每逢我的双眸与她四目相对，并从她那不再坦荡无迎的盈盈秋波中寻见崭新的光芒时，一层崭新而微薄的红晕便会自下颌漫上她的额头。罢了，随它去吧，因为我根本不爱盖特曼·迈克尔的妻子。
“忆及当年我们追随爱神左右、并在其麾下侍奉得如此欢畅，委实是一桩断肠悲事。重温当年那些宣告她生生世世尽归我所有的誓言之甜美，更是一番苦涩彻骨——那些誓言在许下的当口，便已被那冗长而难忘的缠绵深吻击得粉碎。当年我们总爱拿盖特曼·迈克尔取笑打趣；我们拿世间万物放声嘲弄。就这样，在整整一个夏日里，我们曾是世间所知晓的最为勇敢、标致且纯洁的一对璧人。然而罢了，随它去吧，因为我根本不爱盖特曼·迈克尔的妻子。
“我们的爱恋虽美妙却短命。既然多萝西纤巧的双足早已踏碎了这段微末爱恋的生机，便再无人能教它起死回生。然而当我们这一遭人生同样走到尽头——这吝啬苛刻、不容我们再去爱慕任何旁人的人生——我们难道不应当以某种法子，重新赢回当年对抗永恒时所立下的信念？并在某处色彩绚烂的国度里再次心满意足？毫无疑问，我坚信此事终将上演。然而，随它去吧，因为我根本不爱盖特曼·迈克尔的妻子。”
“哎呀，这听起来可太妙了，”多萝西评判道，“皆因我瞧出你正把满腔悲伤化作裁制诗篇的生宣素材呢。所以我这就得动身去寻朱尔根了，毕竟他谈情说爱的手腕截然不同，而且要有趣得多。”
言至于此，无论少女脑海中浮现出何等思绪，那念头都教她的双颊再次泛起了脉脉柔情的红晕，而在了然于心间，她的双眸流露出无尽的欣喜。
这般光景不过稍纵即逝：皆因她此刻向朱尔根作别，轻柔而极其友善地挥了挥手；就这样离他而去，正如他甚至在她转身离去的那一瞬便能瞧得分明的那样，她脑海里再没有给这个老头子留出分毫余地。她迎着拂晓的曦光信步走去，去寻觅那个年轻的朱尔根——那个诸般细微处皆尽善尽美的少女，虽则不过短短一晌，却也算实至名归曾倾心恋慕过的少年。

## XLV The Faith of Guenevere / 第四十五章 吉尼薇尔的信仰
===Original===

Very fair was this woman to look upon, with her shining gray eyes and small smiling lips, a fairer woman might no man boast of having seen. And she regarded Jurgen graciously, with her cheeks red and white, very lovely to observe. She was clothed in a robe of flame-colored silk, and about her neck was a collar of red gold. And she told him, quite as though she spoke with a stranger, that she was Queen Guenevere.
“But Lancelot is turned monk, at Glastonbury: and Arthur is gone into Avalon,” says she: “and I will be your wife if you will have me, Jurgen.”
And Jurgen saw that Guenevere did not know him at all, and that even his name to her was meaningless. There were a many ways of accounting for this: but he put aside the unflattering explanation that she had simply forgotten all about Jurgen, in favor of the reflection that the Jurgen she had known was a scapegrace of twenty-one. Whereas he was now a staid and knowledgeable pawnbroker.
And it seemed to Jurgen that he had never really loved any woman save Guenevere, the daughter of Gogyrvan Gawr, and the pawnbroker was troubled.
“For again you make me think myself a god,” says Jurgen. “Madame Guenevere, when man recognized himself to be Heaven’s vicar upon earth, it was to serve and to glorify and to protect you and your radiant sisterhood that man consecrated his existence. You were beautiful, and you were frail; you were half goddess and half bric-a-brac. *Ohimé*, I recognize the call of chivalry, and my heartstrings resound: yet, for innumerable reasons, I hesitate to take you for my wife, and to concede myself your appointed protector, responsible as such to Heaven. For one matter, I am not altogether sure that I am Heaven’s vicar here upon earth. Certainly the God of Heaven said nothing to me about it, and I cannot but suspect that Omniscience would have selected some more competent representative.”
“It is so written, Messire Jurgen.”
Jurgen shrugged. “I too, in the intervals of business, have written much that is beautiful. Very often my verses were so beautiful that I would have given anything in the world in exchange for somewhat less sure information as to the author’s veracity. Ah, no, madame, desire and knowledge are pressing me so sorely that, between them, I dare not love you, and still I cannot help it!”
Then Jurgen gave a little wringing gesture with his hands.

===Chinese===

眼前这女子端的是容姿绝代，生着一双明亮烁烁的灰眸与浅笑盈盈的朱唇，凡间男子绝无一人敢夸口曾见过胜过她的美人。她神态端庄宽仁地注视着朱尔根，双颊白里透红，观之教人神魂颠倒。她身着一袭火红色的丝缎华袍，颈项间佩着一圈赤金项圈。她向他款款道来，语调全然宛如在同陌路之人搭话，声称自己乃是吉尼薇尔王后（Queen Guenevere）。
“然而兰斯洛特已在格拉斯顿伯里出家做了修士，亚瑟亦远遁前往阿瓦隆，”她说，“倘若你肯纳我，朱尔根，我愿委身作你的妻子。”
朱尔根看出吉尼薇尔全然不认得他，甚至于他的姓名对她而言亦毫无意趣。对此自可有诸般由头来解说：但他撇开了那等教人颜面扫地的猜测——即她压根儿把朱尔根忘得一干二净——宁愿暗自寻思，只因她当年所认得的朱尔根，不过是个年方二十一岁的浪荡败家子。而如今的他，却已然是个持重练达的当铺掌柜。
朱尔根恍惚觉得，除却戈吉尔万·高尔（Gogyrvan Gawr）的千金吉尼薇尔之外，他此生似乎从未真正深爱过任何妇人，当铺掌柜心头不由得烦乱不堪。
“皆因你又一次让我误以为自己是个神明了，”朱尔根说道，“吉尼薇尔夫人，遥想当年，凡间男子自认是上苍在人间的代行者之际，正是为了侍奉、颂扬并护佑您以及您那一众光彩照人的红颜同类，男子才将自己的整条性命奉为牺牲。你们美丽，却又娇弱；一半是女神，一半是精巧的摆件玩物。呜呼（Ohimé），我听见了骑士风度的号召，我的心弦为之颤动共鸣：然而，因着数不胜数的由头，我却迟疑着不敢娶你为妻，亦不敢自诩为上苍钦定受命、专司护佑你的庇护者。其一，我压根儿不能确定自己究竟算不算上苍派驻人间的代行者。天上的上帝分明从未对我提及只字片语，且我不免暗自揣测，无所不知的至高存在断然会遴选出某位更堪重任的代言人来。”
“经书上正是这般白纸黑字写着的，朱尔根大人（Messire Jurgen）。”
朱尔根耸了耸肩。“在下亦曾在操持典当买卖的间隙，写下过不少美不胜收的华章。往往我的诗篇是如此绝妙，以至于我宁可倾尽人间万物，来换取那关于作者品行真伪的稍显模糊的消息。啊，不可，夫人，欲望与理智正如此苦苦逼迫着我，以至于夹在这二者之间，我既不敢去爱慕你，却又情不自禁！”
言毕，朱尔根无奈地绞了绞双手。

===Original===

His smile was not merry; and it seemed pitiful that Guenevere should not remember him.
“Madame and queen,” says Jurgen, “once long and long ago there was a man who worshipped all women. To him they were one and all of sacred, sweet intimidating beauty. He shaped sonorous rhymes of this, in praise of the mystery and sanctity of women. Then a count’s towheaded daughter whom he loved, with such love as it puzzles me to think of now, was shown to him just as she was, as not even worthy of hatred. The goddess stood revealed, unveiled, and displaying in all things such mediocrity as he fretted to find in himself. That was unfortunate. For he began to suspect that women, also, are akin to their parents; and are no wiser, and no more subtle, and no more immaculate, than the father who begot them. Madame and queen, it is not good for any man to suspect this.”
“It is certainly not the conduct of a chivalrous person, nor of an authentic poet,” says Queen Guenevere. “And yet your eyes are big with tears.”
“Hah, madame,” he replied, “but it amuses me to weep for a dead man with eyes that once were his. For he was a dear lad before he went rampaging through the world, in the pride of his youth and in the armor of his hurt. And songs he made for the pleasure of kings, and sword play he made for the pleasure of men, and a whispering he made for the pleasure of women, in places where renown was, and where he trod boldly, giving pleasure to everybody in those fine days. But for all his laughter, he could not understand his fellows, nor could he love them, nor could he detect anything in aught they said or did save their exceeding folly.”
“Why, man’s folly is indeed very great, Messire Jurgen, and the doings of this world are often inexplicable: and so does it come about that man can be saved by faith alone.”
“Ah, but this boy had lost his fellows’ cordial common faith in the importance of what use they made of half-hours and months and years; and because a jill-flirt had opened his eyes so that they saw too much, he had lost faith in the importance of his own actions, too. There was a little time of which the passing might be made not unendurable; beyond gaped unpredictable darkness; and that was all there was of certainty anywhere. Meanwhile, he had the loan of a brain which played with ideas, and a body that went delicately down pleasant ways.

===Chinese===

他的笑容全无欢欣之意；而吉尼薇尔竟然记不得他，委实教人倍感凄凉。
“夫人兼王后陛下，”朱尔根说道，“很久很久以前，曾有一个男子顶礼膜拜天底下的所有女子。在他眼中，她们无一不生得神圣、甜美，带着令人敬畏的美色。他为此谱写了铿锵有力的诗篇，竭力颂扬女子的奥秘与圣洁。随后，一位他曾倾心恋慕的伯爵浅黄头发的千金——那份爱恋之深切，如今想来反倒令我困惑丛生——向他显露了她真实的本来面目，甚至连教人去憎恨都不配。女神就这样褪去了面纱，原形毕露，并在诸般细微处暴露出种种平庸乏味，而那正是他平日里在自身身上瞧见时便深恶痛绝的平庸。那委实是不幸之极。皆因他自此开始怀疑，女子终究亦逃不过血肉父母的干系；她们既不比生养她们的父辈更聪慧，亦不比父辈更敏锐，更不比父辈更纯洁无瑕。夫人兼王后陛下，凡间男子一旦起了这般疑心，断然不是什么好事。”
“这委实绝非一个崇尚骑士之风者所当为，亦非一个真正的诗人所当为，”吉尼薇尔王后说道，“可你的双眸却分明盈满了泪水。”
“哈，夫人，”他答道，“借着曾经属于某个死者的眼睛，为那个死者哭上一场，倒也别有一番趣味。皆因在他仗着青春年少的傲气与创痛铸就的铠甲横行天下之前，他曾是个极惹人怜爱的少年。在那些风流倜傥的锦绣年华里，他为博君王一笑而裁制颂歌，为娱男儿之兴而挥剑比试，又在声名煊赫之处向妇人们低声耳语，他在四海昂首阔步，教人人心花怒放。然而任凭他如何放声欢笑，他既无法理解身旁的同侪，亦无法去爱慕他们，除了他们满身的荒唐愚昧之外，他从他们的言谈举止中再也瞧不出任何端倪。”
“哎呀，凡人的愚行委实深重，朱尔根大人，尘世间的所作所为往往难以理喻：正因如此，凡夫唯有仰仗纯正的信仰方能得救。”
“啊，可这个少年早已失落了同侪那种质朴笃定的信念，不再相信把每分每秒、日月春秋耗费在何处有何深意；更因一个轻浮浪荡的薄情女子替他擦亮了双眼，教他看破了太多真相，他甚至连自身所作所为的分量亦不再坚信。人世不过是偷得浮生片刻，使时光的流逝尚能忍受；在这之外，唯有一片难以测度的深渊黑暗大张着巨口；在天地寰宇之间，除此以外再无任何确定无疑之事。在此期间，他不过借来了一副成日摆弄思绪的脑瓜，以及一副在安逸享乐中从容度日的皮囊。

===Original===

And so he was never the mate for you, dear Guenevere, because he had not sufficient faith in anything at all, not even in his own deductions.”
Now said Queen Guenevere: “Farewell to you, then, Jurgen, for it is I that am leaving you forever. I was to them that served me the lovely and excellent masterwork of God: in Caerleon and Northgalis and at Joyeuse Garde might men behold me with delight, because, men said, to view me was to comprehend the power and kindliness of their Creator. Very beautiful was Iseult, and the face of Luned sparkled like a moving gem; Morgaine and Enid and Viviane and shrewd Nimuë were lovely, too; and the comeliness of Ettarde exalted the beholder like a proud music: these, going statelily about Arthur’s hall, seemed Heaven’s finest craftsmanship until the Queen came to her dais, as the moon among glowing stars: men then affirmed that God in making Guenevere had used both hands. And it is I that am leaving you forever. My beauty was no human white and red, said they, but an explicit sign of Heaven’s might. In approaching me men thought of God, because in me, they said, His splendor was incarnate. That which I willed was neither right nor wrong: it was divine. This thing it was that the knights saw in me; this surety, as to the power and kindliness of their great Father, it was of which the chevaliers of yesterday were conscious in beholding me, and of men’s need to be worthy of such parentage; and it is I that am leaving you forever.”
Said Jurgen: “I could not see all this in you, not quite all this, because of a shadow that followed me. Now it is too late, and this is a sorrowful thing which is happening. I am become as a rudderless boat that goes from wave to wave: I am turned to unfertile dust which a whirlwind makes coherent, and presently lets fall. And so, farewell to you, Queen Guenevere, for it is a sorrowful thing and a very unfair thing that is happening.”
Thus he cried farewell to the daughter of Gogyrvan Gawr. And instantly she vanished like the flame of a blown out altar-candle.

===Chinese===

因此，亲爱的吉尼薇尔，他生生世世绝配不上做你的伴侣，皆因他对世间万物全无分毫起码的信念，甚至连对自己推导出的定论亦未尝笃信。”
此时吉尼薇尔王后说道：“那么永别了，朱尔根，皆因是我将永远离你而去。对于昔日那些侍奉我的人而言，我曾是上帝至为娇媚精妙的鬼斧神工：在卡尔利恩、在北加利斯，以及在快乐守望堡（Joyeuse Garde），凡夫俗子无不欣喜若狂地瞻仰我，皆因世人皆称，唯有亲睹我的容颜，方能真正领略造物主的威能与仁爱。伊索尔德（Iseult）固然美艳绝伦，卢内德（Luned）的面容亦如灵动美玉般熠熠生辉；摩根（Morgaine）、伊妮德（Enid）、薇薇安（Viviane）以及精明伶俐的尼妙（Nimuë）同样娇美动人；埃塔尔德（Ettarde）的秀色更如昂扬激荡的乐章般震撼着瞻仰者的心神：她们仪态万方地穿行于亚瑟的华殿之内，在上苍的诸般造物中堪称登峰造极，直至王后款款步上宝座台阶，宛若皓月凌驾于繁星之上：世人届时无不感叹，上帝在雕琢吉尼薇尔之际，定然动用了双手。而正是我，将永远离你而去。世人皆言，我的美并非凡俗血肉的红白之色，而是上苍威能的确凿彰显。走近我身畔之际，人们便不由得心系上帝，皆因他们说，祂的光耀正借着我化作肉身。凡我所欲之物，无所谓对错是非：那本就是神圣的旨意。这正是一众骑士在我身上所窥见的光芒；昔日的骑士们在瞻仰我之际，心中真切感受到的，正是对他们伟大天父之威能与慈悲的笃定确信，以及世人必须配得上这等父嗣恩典的由衷渴求；而正是我，将永远离你而去。”
朱尔根说道：“因着那道紧随我的阴影，我此前未能看透你身上所有的这般面貌，未能全然看透。如今大势已去，眼前正在发生的事委实令人痛心疾首。我已化作一叶失却了舵的孤舟，在浪涛之间随波逐流；我已化作不毛之地的尘埃，任由旋风将我卷聚成形，转瞬又任其散落倾覆。因此，永别了，吉尼薇尔王后，皆因眼前正在发生的事，同样是一桩令人痛心且极不公道的事。”
他就这样向戈吉尔万·高尔的千金道了声永别。转瞬之间，她便宛若祭坛上被骤然吹熄的烛火那般，杳然消散了。

## XXXI The Fall of Pseudopolis / 第三十一章 伪城的沦陷
===Original===

So Jurgen abode in Leukê, and complied with the customs of that country; and what with one thing and another, he and Chloris made the time pass pleasantly enough, until the winter solstice was at hand. Now Pseudopolis, as has been said, was at war with Philistia: so it befell that at this season Leukê was invaded by an army of Philistines, led by their Queen Dolores, a woman who was wise but not entirely reliable. They came from the coast, a terrible army insanely clad in such garments as had been commanded by Ageus, a god of theirs; and chaunting psalms in honor of their god Vel-Tyno, who had inspired this crusade: thus they swept down upon Pseudopolis, and encamped before the city.
These Philistines fought in this campaign by casting before them a more horrible form of Greek fire, which consumed whatever was not gray-colored. For that color alone was now favored by their god Vel-Tyno. “And all other colors,” his oracles had decreed, “are forevermore abominable, until I say otherwise.”
So the forces of Philistia were marshalled in the plain before Pseudopolis, and Queen Dolores spoke to her troops. And smilingly she said:﻿—
“Whenever you come to blows with the enemy he will be beaten. No mercy will be shown, no prisoners taken. As the Philistines under Libnah and Goliath and Gershon, and a many other tall captains, made for themselves a name which is still mighty in traditions and legend, even thus today may the name of Realist be so fixed in Pseudopolis, by your deeds today, that no one shall ever dare again even to look askance at a Philistine. Open the door for Realism, once for all!”
Meanwhile within the city Achilles, King of Men, addressed his army:﻿—
“The eyes of all the world will be upon you, because you are in some especial sense the soldiers of Romance. Let it be your pride, therefore, to show all men everywhere, not only what good soldiers you are, but also what good men you are, keeping yourselves fit and straight in everything, and pure and clean through and through. Let us set ourselves a standard so high that it will be a glory to live up to it, and then let us live up to it, and add a new laurel to the crown of Pseudopolis. May the Gods of Old keep you and guide you!”
Then said Thersitês, in his beard: “Certainly Pelidês has learned from history with what weapon a strong man discomfits the Philistines.”

===Chinese===

话说朱尔根留宿在琉克（Leukê），恪守该国风俗礼法；在凡百琐事相伴之下，他同克洛丽斯（Chloris）将光阴打发得颇为惬意，转眼冬至将至。然而正如前文所述，伪城（Pseudopolis）正与非利士（Philistia）交兵鏖战：于是在这个时节，一支由非利士王后多洛雷斯（Queen Dolores）统领的非利士大军悍然入侵了琉克——这位妇人聪慧过人，却全不可靠。他们自海岸席卷而来，这是一支教人毛骨悚然的雄师，身上狂乱地裹着遵照他们供奉的神祇阿吉乌斯（Ageus）律令所制定的衣袍；口中高唱着颂扬他们神明韦尔-泰诺（Vel-Tyno）的赞美诗，正是这位神明授意发起了这场十字军远征：就这样，他们浩浩荡荡扑向伪城，扎营在城门之下。
在这一场征战中，非利士人作战的伎俩是朝前方抛掷一种更为可怖的希腊火，凡非灰色之物，皆被这股烈焰吞噬殆尽。皆因唯有这般色彩，眼下深得他们神明韦尔-泰诺的青睐。“至于其余诸般色彩，”祂的神谕曾断言道，“生生世世皆为可憎之物，直至本神另有谕旨为止。”
就这样，非利士大军在伪城前方的平原上列开阵势，多洛雷斯王后向麾下将士发表演说。她浅笑盈盈地说道：——
“但凡与敌军交锋，敌军必败无疑。我军绝不施舍慈悲，绝不招降纳俘。昔日立拿（Libnah）、歌利亚（Goliath）、革顺（Gershon）以及诸位魁梧骁勇的将领麾下的非利士人，曾为自身赢得了至今在典籍与传奇中依旧威名赫赫的声誉；今时今日，亦祈愿‘现实主义者’之名，借由诸位今日的赫赫功勋，牢牢铭刻在伪城之中，教世人从此再不敢斜眼睥睨非利士人分毫。让我们一劳永逸，为‘现实主义’彻底敞开大门！”
在此期间，城池之内的人王阿喀琉斯（Achilles）亦在向麾下大军发话：——
“举世瞩目的目光皆聚焦在诸位身上，皆因在某种非同寻常的深意上，诸位乃是‘浪漫传奇’的甲士。因此，务必以向天下四海昭示诸位的本色为荣——不仅昭示诸位是何等精锐的兵士，更要昭示诸位是何等高尚的男儿，在凡百行止间皆刚健正直、表里如一地纯洁无瑕。让我们为自身树立起至高无上的标准，教世人以遵从这般准则为无上荣耀，而后恪守这般准则，为伪城的王冠再添一顶崭新的桂冠。祈愿昔日的诸神护佑诸位，指引诸位！”
此时忒耳西忒斯（Thersitês）捋着胡须咕哝道：“珀利得斯（Pelidês）委实深谙青史，深知刚健之士究竟该借由何等兵刃好将非利士人打得溃不成军。”

===Original===

But the other kings applauded, and the trumpet was sounded, and the battle was joined. And that day the forces of Philistia were everywhere triumphant. But they report a queer thing happened: and it was that when the Philistines shouted in their triumph, Achilles and all they who served him rose from the ground like gleaming clouds and passed above the heads of the Philistines, deriding them.
Thus was Pseudopolis left empty, so that the Philistines entered thereinto without any opposition. They defiled this city of blasphemous colors, then burned it as a sacrifice to their god Vel-Tyno, because the color of ashes is gray.
Then the Philistines erected *lithoi* (which were not unlike maypoles), and began to celebrate their religious rites.
---
So it was reported: but Jurgen witnessed none of these events.
“Let them fight it out,” said Jurgen: “it is not my affair. I agree with Silenus: dullness will conquer dullness, and it will not matter. But do you, woman dear, take shelter with your kindred in the unconquerable Woods, for there is no telling what damage the Philistines may do hereabouts.”
“Will you go with me, Jurgen?”
“My dear, you know very well that it is impossible for me ever again to go into the Woods, after the trick I played upon Phobetor.”
“And if only you had kept your head about that bean-pole of a Helen, in her yellow wig﻿—for I have not a doubt that every strand of it is false, and at all events this is not a time to be arguing about it, Jurgen﻿—why, then you would never have meddled with Uncle Phobetor! It simply shows you!”
“Yes,” said Jurgen.
“Still, I do not know. If you come with me into the Woods, Uncle Phobetor in his impetuous way will quite certainly turn you into a boar-pig, because he has always done that to the people who irritated him﻿—”
“I seem to recognise that reason.”
“﻿—But give me time, and I can get around Uncle Phobetor, just as I have always done, and he will turn you back.”
“No,” says Jurgen, obstinately, “I do not wish to be turned into a boar-pig.”
“Now, Jurgen, let us be sensible about this! Of course, it is a little humiliating. But I will take the very best of care of you, and feed you with my own acorns, and it will be a purely temporary arrangement. And to be a pig for a week or two, or even for a month, is infinitely better for a poet than being captured by the Philistines.”
“How do I know that?” says Jurgen.

===Chinese===

然而其余诸位帝王却齐声喝彩，战角随即吹响，战事全面爆发。那一日，非利士大军在各处所向披靡。然而据风传，席间却上演了一桩离奇古怪之事：那便是在非利士人齐声欢呼大捷之际，阿喀琉斯以及麾下将士竟宛如耀目的祥云般拔地而起，从非利士人的头顶飘然而过，尽情嘲弄讥讽着他们。
伪城就这样沦为了一座空城，非利士人长驱直入，未遭遇分毫抵抗。他们先是玷污了这座涂抹着渎神色泽的城池，随后将整座城池付之一炬，作为奉献给其神祇韦尔-泰诺的祭品，皆因灰烬的颜色正是灰色。
随后，非利士人立起了巨石柱（lithoi，形状颇似五月柱），开始举行起宗教仪轨。
---
坊间传闻大抵如此：然而朱尔根却未曾亲眼目睹这当中的任何一桩变故。
“随他们打个你死我活去吧，”朱尔根说道，“这同我毫不相干。我赞同西勒诺斯（Silenus）的评判：乏味终将战胜乏味，而这根本无关紧要。然而我亲爱的女人，你还是快同你的同族一道，躲进那坚不可摧的古林里避难吧，毕竟谁也料不准这帮非利士人在四下里会造下何等祸端。”
“你同我一道走么，朱尔根？”
“亲爱的，自从我在福贝托尔（Phobetor）身上耍了那套把戏之后，我生生世世绝不可能再踏入那片森林半步，这一点你心里再清楚不过了。”
“倘若你当初面对那个戴着黄假发的干瘪豆秸海伦时能保持几分清醒——毕竟我毫不怀疑她满头青丝没一根是真的，无论如何，眼下可不是争辩这些的时候，朱尔根——哎呀，那样你根本就不会去招惹福贝托尔舅父了！这正好给你长个记性！”
“是啊，”朱尔根应道。
“可话虽如此，我却也拿不准。倘若你同我一道进林子，福贝托尔舅父那副性急暴躁的脾气，定会把你变成一头野公猪，皆因他对那些惹恼他的人向来都是这般处置的——”
“我依稀领教过这由头。”
“——但只要宽限我些许工夫，我自能像以往那般把福贝托尔舅父哄得团团转，他自会把你变回人形的。”
“不行，”朱尔根固执己见道，“我可不想被变成一头野公猪。”
“哎呀，朱尔根，咱们在正经事上能不能明智些！诚然，这难免教人有些颜面扫地。但我自会好生照料你，亲手拿我私存的橡子喂你，而且这不过是权宜之计。对于一个诗人而言，变成一头猪熬上一两周，哪怕熬上一个月，也比沦为非利士人的俘虏要强上千百倍。”
“我怎知晓究竟孰优孰劣？”朱尔根说道。

===Original===

“﻿—For it is not, after all, as if Uncle Phobetor’s heart were not in the right place. It is just his way. And besides, you must remember what you did with that gimlet!”
Said Jurgen: “All this is hardly to the purpose. You forget I have seen the hapless swine of Phobetor, and I know how he ameliorates the natural ferocity of his boar-pigs. No, I am Jurgen. So I remain. I will face the Philistines and whatever they may possibly do to me, rather than suffer that which Phobetor will quite certainly do to me.”
“Then I stay too,” said Chloris.
“No, woman dear﻿—!”
“But do you not understand?” says Chloris, a little pale, as he saw now. “Since the life of a hamadryad is linked with the life of her tree, nobody can harm me so long as my tree lives: and if they cut down my tree I shall die, wherever I may happen to be.”
“I had forgotten that.” He was really troubled now.
“﻿—And you can see for yourself, Jurgen, it is quite out of the question for me to be carrying that great oak anywhere, and I wonder at your talking such nonsense.”
“Indeed, my dear,” says Jurgen, “we are very neatly trapped. Well, nobody can live longer in peace than his neighbor chooses. Nevertheless, it is not fair.”
As he spoke the Philistines came forth from the burning city. Again the trumpet sounded, and the Philistines advanced in their order of battle.

===Chinese===

“——毕竟，福贝托尔舅父的心肠终归并不坏。这不过是他平日里的秉性罢了。况且，你可别忘了你当初拿那把钻子干了些什么勾当！”
朱尔根说道：“这些话全扯不到正题上。你忘了我曾亲眼目睹过福贝托尔圈养的那些倒霉猪猡，且我很清楚他是如何‘抚平’公猪骨子里的天然野性的。不，我乃是朱尔根。因此我选择留在此地。我宁愿面对非利士人以及他们可能施加在我身上的凡百行径，也绝不愿去领受福贝托尔十成十必定会对我动下的手段。”
“那我也不走，”克洛丽斯说道。
“不可，亲爱的女人——！”
“可你难道还不明白么？”克洛丽斯说道，此时朱尔根发觉她的面色微微泛白。“由于树精（hamadryad）的性命与本体古树休戚与共，只要我的大树尚存，便无人能伤我分毫；而一旦他们砍倒我的大树，无论我身在何处，皆必死无疑。”
“我倒把这茬给忘了。”他此刻委实烦乱难安起来。
“——而且你自家也瞧得真切，朱尔根，教我把那棵参天巨栎随身扛到任何地方去，皆是无稽之谈，我倒诧异你怎会吐出这等疯话来。”
“确实，亲爱的，”朱尔根说道，“我们当真被困得滴水不漏。罢了，只要恶邻不许，谁也休想长久安生度日。然而无论如何，这未免太不公道了。”
言语之间，非利士人已从那座烈火熊熊的城池中蜂拥而出。号角声再度响起，非利士军列成严整战阵，向前步步逼近。

## III The Garden Between Dawn and Sunrise / 第三章 破晓与拂晓之间的园圃
===Original===

Thus it was that Jurgen and the Centaur came to the garden between dawn and sunrise, entering this place in a fashion which it is not convenient to record. But as they passed over the bridge three fled before them, screaming. And when the life had been trampled out of the small furry bodies which these three had misused, there was none to oppose the Centaur’s entry into the garden between dawn and sunrise.
This was a wonderful garden: yet nothing therein was strange. Instead, it seemed that everything hereabouts was heart-breakingly familiar and very dear to Jurgen. For he had come to a broad lawn which slanted northward to a well-remembered brook: and multitudinous maples and locust-trees stood here and there, irregularly, and were being played with very lazily by an irresolute west wind, so that foliage seemed to toss and ripple everywhere like green spray: but autumn was at hand, for the locust-trees were dropping a Danaë’s shower of small round yellow leaves. Around the garden was an unforgotten circle of blue hills. And this was a place of lucent twilight, unlit by either sun or stars, and with no shadows anywhere in the diffused faint radiancy that revealed this garden, which is not visible to any man except in the brief interval between dawn and sunrise.
“Why, but it is Count Emmerick’s garden at Storisende,” says Jurgen, “where I used to be having such fine times when I was a lad.”
“I will wager,” said Nessus, “that you did not use to walk alone in this garden.”
“Well, no; there was a girl.”
“Just so,” assented Nessus. “It is a local bylaw: and here are those who comply with it.”
For now had come toward them, walking together in the dawn, a handsome boy and girl. And the girl was incredibly beautiful, because everybody in the garden saw her with the vision of the boy who was with her. “I am Rudolph,” said this boy, “and she is Anne.”
“And are you happy here?” asked Jurgen.
“Oh, yes, sir, we are tolerably happy: but Anne’s father is very rich, and my mother is poor, so that we cannot be quite happy until I have gone into foreign lands and come back with a great many lakhs of rupees and pieces of eight.”
“And what will you do with all this money, Rudolph?”
“My duty, sir, as I see it. But I inherit defective eyesight.”
“God speed to you, Rudolph!” said Jurgen, “for many others are in your plight.”

===Chinese===

就这样，朱尔根同半人马（Centaur）来到了破晓与拂晓之间的园圃，他们踏入此间的方式，在此不便秉笔直书。然而当他们跨过木桥之际，有三个身影尖叫着从他们眼前仓皇奔逃。当那三个身影所肆意糟践的毛茸茸的小小躯壳被践踏得生机断绝之后，便再无任何人阻挡半人马步入这片破晓与拂晓之间的园圃了。
这是一座奇妙绝伦的园圃：然而园中万物却并无半点光怪陆离。相反，在朱尔根眼中，周遭的一切皆熟悉得令人心碎，亲切备至。皆因他来到了一片宽阔的草坪前，草坪向北倾斜，蜿蜒伸向一条记忆犹新的潺潺溪流：数不尽的枫树与刺槐错落散布各处，正被一阵犹豫不决的西风懒洋洋地拨弄抚弄，教树冠枝叶宛如漫天碧绿的浪花般在四下里翻涌起伏；然而深秋已然临近，刺槐正簌簌飘落下一场宛如洒向达那厄（Danaë）的金雨般细小浑圆的黄叶。园圃四周环绕着一圈难以忘怀的青蓝色群山。此地笼罩在一片澄澈空明的薄暮微光之中，既无旭日亦无星辰辉映，那展露园圃真容的弥散微光里寻不见半分阴影，而这片园圃，除了在破晓与拂晓之间那稍纵即逝的间隙之外，凡间男子绝无一人能得见其真容。
“哎呀，这分明是斯托里森德（Storisende）埃梅里克伯爵（Count Emmerick）的花园嘛，”朱尔根说道，“当年我少不更事时，曾在此地度过不知凡几的锦绣时光。”
“我敢打赌，”涅索斯（Nessus）说道，“当年你在这座花园里漫步，绝非孑然一身。”
“嗯，确实不是；那时有个姑娘相伴。”
“正是如此，”涅索斯赞同道，“此乃当地的规矩律法：而眼前正是那些恪守这般规矩之人。”
皆因此时正有一对俊美秀丽的少年少女相伴而行，迎着熹微晨光向他们走来。那少女美得不可思议，皆因这座园圃中的每一个人，皆是借着身旁少年的眼眸来瞻仰她的绝色。“我叫鲁道夫（Rudolph），”少年说道，“她是安妮（Anne）。”
“你们在此地过得快活么？”朱尔根问道。
“噢，回先生的话，我们算得上差强人意地快活：只是安妮的父亲腰缠万贯，家母却一贫如洗，因此非得等我远涉异邦、满载着成千上万拉克卢比与八里尔银币衣锦还乡之后，我们方能真正称心快活。”
“等攒了这许多银钱，鲁道夫，你打算拿来做甚？”
“尽我的本分，先生，依我愚见当是如此。只可惜我打娘胎里便承袭了一双视力欠佳的浊眼。”
“祝你一路顺遂，鲁道夫！”朱尔根说道，“皆因世间沦入你这般窘境之人不知凡几。”

===Original===

Then came to Jurgen and the Centaur another boy with the small blue-eyed person in whom he took delight. And this fat and indolent looking boy informed them that he and the girl who was with him were walking in the glaze of the red mustard jar, which Jurgen thought was gibberish: and the fat boy said that he and the girl had decided never to grow any older, which Jurgen said was excellent good sense if only they could manage it.
“Oh, I can manage that,” said this fat boy, reflectively, “if only I do not find the managing of it uncomfortable.”
Jurgen for a moment regarded him, and then gravely shook hands.
“I feel for you,” said Jurgen, “for I perceive that you, too, are a monstrous clever fellow: so life will get the best of you.”
“But is not cleverness the main thing, sir?”
“Time will show you, my lad,” says Jurgen, a little sorrowfully. “And God speed to you, for many others are in your plight.”
And a host of boys and girls did Jurgen see in the garden. And all the faces that Jurgen saw were young and glad and very lovely and quite heart-breakingly confident, as young persons beyond numbering came toward Jurgen and passed him there, in the first glow of dawn: so they all went exulting in the glory of their youth, and foreknowing life to be a puny antagonist from whom one might take very easily anything which one desired. And all passed in couples﻿—“as though they came from the Ark,” said Jurgen. But the Centaur said they followed a precedent which was far older than the Ark.
“For in this garden,” said the Centaur, “each man that ever lived has sojourned for a little while, with no company save his illusions. I must tell you again that in this garden are encountered none but imaginary creatures. And stalwart persons take their hour of recreation here, and go hence unaccompanied, to become aldermen and respected merchants and bishops, and to be admired as captains upon prancing horses, or even as kings upon tall thrones; each in his station thinking not at all of the garden ever any more. But now and then come timid persons, Jurgen, who fear to leave this garden without an escort: so these must need go hence with one or another imaginary creature, to guide them about alleys and bypaths, because imaginary creatures find little nourishment in the public highways, and shun them.

===Chinese===

随后又有一名少年走向朱尔根与半人马，身旁依偎着令他心醉神迷的蓝眸小人儿。这个生得白胖慵懒的少年向他们通报，说他与身旁的少女正漫步在红芥末罐的釉光之中，朱尔根只当这是满嘴胡话；白胖少年又声称，他与少女已决意生生世世绝不再长大一岁，朱尔根对此评判道，倘若他们果真能办得到，倒不失为一番绝妙明智之举。
“噢，我办得到的，”这个白胖少年若有所思地说道，“只要我觉得操持此事不致让人太不舒坦便成。”
朱尔根端详了他片刻，随后神情庄重地同他握了握手。
“我深表同情，”朱尔根说道，“皆因我看得出，你同样是个聪明绝顶的家伙：是以生活迟早会把你收拾得服服帖帖。”
“可聪明机巧难道不是顶顶要紧的么，先生？”
“岁月自会教你明白的，孩子，”朱尔根略带伤感地说道，“祝你一路顺遂，皆因世间沦入你这般窘境之人不知凡几。”
在园圃之中，朱尔根见到了成群结队的少年与少女。在初露的晨曦之中，数不尽的年轻男女向着朱尔根迎面走来并擦肩而过，朱尔根所见到的每一张面庞皆是那般青春明媚、欢畅娇艳，且带着教人心碎的笃定自信：他们无一不沉醉在青春年少的荣耀之中昂首前行，且自以为生活不过是个不堪一击的对手，随随便便就能从它手中予取予求任何心仪之物。所有人都两两结伴而行——“简直宛若刚从方舟（Ark）里走出来一般，”朱尔根感叹道。然而半人马却指出，他们所遵循的先例远比方舟还要古老得多。
“皆因在这座园圃之中，”半人马说道，“古往今来活过的每一个凡夫俗子，皆曾在此短暂停驻，除却自身的幻觉之外别无旁物相伴。我必须再次告诫你，在这座园圃中所邂逅的，无一不是凭空臆造的虚妄生灵。刚健勇毅之人在此偷得一时欢愉，随后便形单影只地抽身离去，去充当市府参事、受人景仰的商贾与主教，或骑在高头大马之上化作令人叹服的统帅，乃至端坐在高耸宝座之上受人朝拜为君王；每个人在各自的位置上，生生世世绝不再念及这座园圃分毫。然而朱尔根啊，时不时总有些怯懦之人，害怕在没有护送的情形下独自离开这座园圃：于是他们必须与某一个臆造的生灵结伴同行，好引着他们在幽暗小巷与偏僻幽径中穿行，皆因臆造的生灵在朗朗通衢大道上难觅滋养，向来对大道避之唯恐不及。

===Original===

Thus must these timid persons skulk about obscurely with their diffident and skittish guides, and they do not ever venture willingly into the thronged places where men get horses and build thrones.”
“And what becomes of these timid persons, Centaur?”
“Why, sometimes they spoil paper, Jurgen, and sometimes they spoil human lives.”
“Then are these accursed persons,” Jurgen considered.
“You should know best,” replied the Centaur.
“Oh, very probably,” said Jurgen. “Meanwhile here is one who walks alone in this garden, and I wonder to see the local bylaws thus violated.”
Now Nessus looked at Jurgen for a while without speaking: and in the eyes of the Centaur was so much of comprehension and compassion that it troubled Jurgen. For somehow it made Jurgen fidget and consider this an unpleasantly personal way of looking at anybody.
“Yes, certainly,” said the Centaur, “this woman walks alone. But there is no help for her loneliness, since the lad who loved this woman is dead.”
“Nessus, I am willing to be reasonably sorry about it. Still, is there any need of pulling quite such a portentously long face? After all, a great many other persons have died, off and on: and for anything I can say to the contrary, this particular young fellow may have been no especial loss to anybody.”
Again the Centaur said, “You should know best.”

===Chinese===

于是这些怯懦之人便只得同他们那些羞怯猜忌的引路向导一道，在暗昧阴影中鬼祟度日，且绝不肯心甘情愿地踏入那些凡夫俗子赢取骏马、筑起王座的熙攘喧嚣之所。”
“那这些怯懦之人的下场又当如何呢，半人马？”
“哎，朱尔根，有时候他们糟蹋纸张，有时候他们糟蹋人命。”
“那这帮人委实该遭天谴，”朱尔根思忖道。
“对此你应当最心知肚明，”半人马答道。
“噢，极有可能，”朱尔根说道，“在此期间，这里却有个在这座园圃里茕茕独立漫步的身影，眼见当地规矩就这般遭人践踏，我倒有些诧异。”
此时涅索斯凝视着朱尔根，良久不发一语：半人马眼眸中流露出的了然与悲悯是如此深切，教朱尔根不由得心神不宁。皆因不知怎的，这教朱尔根坐立难安，觉得这般打量人的眼神委实带着令人不快的私密针对意味。
“不错，确乎如此，”半人马说道，“这名女子的确独自行走。然而她的形单影只却无可救药，皆因曾深爱过这女子的那个少年，早已一命呜呼了。”
“涅索斯，我倒也乐意为之适度感伤一番。可话说回来，当真有必要拉长着这副丧气至极的脸孔么？毕竟隔三差五总有大批旁人撒手人寰；且依我看来，这个特定的年轻后生究竟死没死，对世间任何人而言大抵也算不得什么大不了的损失。”
半人马再次说道：“对此你应当最心知肚明。”

## L The Moment That Did Not Count / 第五十章 算不得数的瞬间
===Original===

And that is really all the story save for the moment Jurgen paused on his way home.
